### [1] [The Future of AI-assisted Physics: Discovering, Verifying and Organizing Results via Formalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14059)
*Sven Krippendorf,Leopoldo Sarra,Joseph Tooby-Smith*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper argues that formalization is essential for trustworthy, reusable AI-driven physics discoveries; AI accelerates formalization but struggles with foundational choices like definitions and library design; current AI speed compromises trust and community ownership; shared, community-reviewed formal libraries (e.g., Physlib) are critical infrastructure; and formalizing physics poses unique challenges—especially ensuring physical fidelity of definitions—not present in mathematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address growing challenges in verifying, trusting, and reusing AI-generated physics results by establishing formalization as a necessary layer bridging AI capabilities and scientific rigor.

Method: Conceptual analysis and position articulation, combining survey of AI's role in physics with arguments grounded in formal methods, library design principles, and epistemology of physics.

Result: Five key claims about the interplay of AI and formalization in physics, including the necessity of formal libraries, AI's asymmetric capabilities (strong in proof generation, weak in definition/library design), and the unique semantic challenge of physical fidelity in formalized definitions.

Conclusion: Formalization—supported by community-curated libraries like Physlib—is indispensable for responsible AI-augmented physics; progress requires coordinated effort across AI developers, physicists, and formal method experts to align computational correctness with physical meaning.

Abstract: Rapid advances in AI have opened a new era for physics, enabling unprecedented capabilities in research and discovery. However, these advances also bring challenges in verifying, trusting, and reusing the results they so rapidly produce. Formalization is a necessary part of the solution to these challenges. In this paper, as well as giving an overview of the role of AI and formalization in physics, we make the case that: (1) formalization is an essential layer for AI-driven discoveries in both computational and theoretical physics; (2) AI is lowering the barrier to, and increasing the efficiency of, formalization in physics, though it remains weaker at choosing definitions and designing libraries than at writing proofs; (3) the speed AI brings to formalization in physics currently comes at the cost of trust, credit, and community; (4) shared, community-reviewed formal libraries such as Physlib should be central in making progress in this field, both as a trusted, reusable foundation for physics results and as the environment in which AI systems can reason about physics; (5) formalizing physics raises challenges beyond those in mathematics, most importantly that a checked proof does not establish that its definitions faithfully capture the intended physics. We also give our perspectives on near-term and long-term directions in these areas.

</details>


### [2] [A Witten effect for global monopoles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14168)
*Pablo Quílez Lasanta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that global monopoles—topological defects arising from spontaneous symmetry breaking—can acquire electric charge via a CP-odd operator in the Goldstone effective theory, analogous to the Witten effect but for global (not gauge) defects; the charge is quantized as Q = −Cn, where C is the Wilson coefficient and n the winding number, stemming from a mixed θ-term between electromagnetism and a composite connection of Goldstone fields; the existence of both the operator and the monopoles is tied to the same group-theoretic condition, and a fermionic UV completion is provided with coefficient fixed by Goldstone–Wilczek current matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain how electrically neutral scalar fields can generate globally charged topological defects (global monopoles), resolving a conceptual gap between global topology and electromagnetic coupling—specifically, extending the Witten effect (normally for gauge monopoles) to global defects.

Method: Analysis of the Goldstone effective field theory, identification and reinterpretation of a CP-odd total-derivative operator as physically relevant due to topological boundary terms, construction of a composite connection from Goldstone fields, derivation of a mixed θ-term coupling it to electromagnetism, group-theoretic consistency check for monopole existence, and fermionic UV completion with matching to the Goldstone–Wilczek current to fix the Wilson coefficient.

Result: Global monopoles acquire quantized electric charge Q = −Cn due to a topologically activated CP-odd operator; this arises from a mixed θ-term between U(1)EM and a composite connection built from Goldstone bosons; the same Lie-algebraic condition (e.g., nontrivial π₂(G/H)) ensuring monopole existence also permits the operator; the Wilson coefficient C is determined unambiguously via fermionic UV completion and Goldstone–Wilczek current matching.

Conclusion: Electric charge on global monopoles is a robust, topologically protected phenomenon governed by effective CP violation in the Goldstone sector—not requiring charged scalars—thereby unifying topological defect physics with anomalous electromagnetic responses in a way that parallels but generalizes the Witten effect.

Abstract: Global monopoles can carry electric charge even when the scalar field that forms them is neutral. We show that a CP-odd operator in the Goldstone effective theory, typically discarded as a total derivative, gives them an electric charge $Q=-Cn$, with $C$ its Wilson coefficient and $n$ the winding number. This is a Witten effect on a global defect, generated by a mixed $θ$-term between electromagnetism and a composite connection built from the Goldstone fields. The same group theory condition that allows the operator also guarantees the existence of monopoles. We also give a fermionic UV completion and determine the coefficient by matching the Goldstone-Wilczek current.

</details>


### [3] [Solutions of Roy equations for variable pion mass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14170)
*Gilberto Colangelo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves Roy equations for ππ S- and P-wave scattering below, at, and above physical pion mass (up to 240 MeV), using chiral perturbation theory inputs and phenomenological data above s_A = 0.84 GeV²; it achieves high-accuracy partial-wave descriptions across the chiral limit, validates consistency via Wanders’ sum rules, and bridges lattice QCD with rigorous S-matrix constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a theoretically rigorous, model-independent description of ππ scattering as a function of pion mass—enabling controlled extrapolation to the chiral limit and facilitating precise comparison with lattice QCD results—while respecting fundamental principles: analyticity, unitarity, and crossing symmetry.

Method: Numerical solution of Roy equations for S- and P-wave ππ scattering amplitudes over 0 ≤ M_π ≤ 240 MeV; uses fixed phenomenological input above s_A = 0.84 GeV², supplemented by chiral perturbation theory predictions for S-wave scattering lengths below physical pion mass; validates self-consistency via Wanders’ sum rules for threshold parameters up to D wave.

Result: Accurate, constrained partial-wave solutions for S and P waves across the full pion mass range; excellent agreement between Wanders’ sum rule predictions and chPT for S-, P-, and D-wave threshold parameters; quantitative consistency with lattice QCD data at M_π = 239 MeV; establishment of a framework for studying quark-mass dependence of σ and ρ resonances.

Conclusion: The Roy equation approach, augmented by chiral input and fixed high-energy data, yields a robust, symmetry-respecting parameterization of ππ scattering across varying light-quark masses—providing a foundational tool for future lattice-QCD analyses and resonance studies in the chiral regime.

Abstract: The Roy equations for the $S$ and $P$ waves are solved for pion
  masses below the physical value in the region between threshold and
  $s_A=0.84\GeV^2$. The analysis is based on the assumption that the
  phenomenological input above $s_A$ can be used essentially
  unchanged. Moreover, for $M_π\le M_{π,\mathrm{phys}}$, the chiral
  perturbation theory predictions for the two $S$-wave scattering lengths
  provide a very reliable additional input to the Roy equations. The
  outcome is an accurate description of the $S$ and $P$ partial waves as
  the light-quark masses decrease towards the chiral limit. As a test of
  the approach, we evaluate the first few threshold parameters of the $S$,
  $P$, and $D$ waves via Wanders' sum rules and find excellent agreement
  with chiral perturbation theory across the entire range $0 \le M_π\le
  M_{π,\mathrm{phys}}$. We further solve the Roy equations for pion masses
  above the physical value (up to $240\MeV$) and show that the resulting
  solutions agree well with existing lattice data at $M_π=239\MeV$. The
  present work paves the way for analyses of lattice data that fully
  incorporate the constraints of analyticity, unitarity, and crossing
  symmetry, and provides the basis for an accurate study of the dependence
  of the $σ$ and $ρ$ resonances on the light-quark masses, both of
  which will be addressed in future work.

</details>


### [4] [Collider and direct detection as key probes of asymmetric dark matter: Signals from a compressed Inert Higgs doublet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14178)
*David Cerdeño,Miguel Escudero,Pietro Ghedini,Guilherme Guedes,Thomas Hambye,Abel Muñoz-Navarro,Elena Pressendo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies an asymmetric dark matter model where a light, compressed inert Higgs doublet $H'$ mediates asymmetry transfer from visible to dark sector; it predicts a long-lived charged component ($H^\pm$), testable at HL-LHC via disappearing/displaced tracks, and links its lifetime to direct detection rates—potentially explaining the LZ high-energy recoil event.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the cosmic particle–antiparticle asymmetry’s possible inheritance by dark matter, and to explore testable signatures of the minimal asymmetric dark matter model featuring an inert Higgs doublet as asymmetry mediator.

Method: Phenomenological and cosmological constraint analysis (including relic density, BBN, electroweak precision), recasting of LHC searches for compressed Higgsinos (disappearing and displaced tracks), and correlation of $H^\pm$ lifetime with dark matter–nucleon scattering cross section for direct detection forecasts.

Result: The inert Higgs doublet must be light (46–580 GeV) and highly compressed ($\Delta M \lesssim 1$ GeV, $\delta m \lesssim 5$ MeV); $H^\pm$ is long-lived on collider scales; current LHC limits are weak but HL-LHC will probe large regions; next-gen direct detection experiments (e.g., LZ, DARWIN) are uniquely sensitive; the small neutral mass splitting naturally accommodates the LZ high-energy nuclear recoil event.

Conclusion: The minimal asymmetric dark matter model with an inert Higgs doublet is phenomenologically viable, highly predictive, and experimentally testable across colliders and direct detection; its compressed, light spectrum and correlated collider/detection signatures make it a compelling target for near-future experiments.

Abstract: The cosmic imbalance of particles over antiparticles may be inherited by the dark matter in a certain class of asymmetric dark matter models. In this article, we thoroughly investigate the signatures of the minimal realization of this idea. In this model a new (stable) inert Higgs, $H'$, plays the role of the mediator that facilitates the asymmetry transfer from the visible to the dark sector. It also constitutes a small dark matter sub-component, while the dominant component stems from a singlet scalar asymmetry. A combination of phenomenological and cosmological constraints requires this new inert Higgs doublet to be quite light $46\,{\rm GeV}<m_{H'}< 580\,{\rm GeV}$ and its spectrum rather compressed, $ΔM = m_{H^\pm}-m_{H_0} \lesssim 1\,{\rm GeV}$ and $δm = m_{A_0}-m_{H_0}\lesssim 5\,{\rm MeV}$. Therefore its charged component can be relatively long-lived on collider length-scales. By recasting searches for compressed Higgsinos at the LHC, we derive new limits on this particle and show that, while at present most of the parameter space for this state is still unconstrained, large and key regions of parameter space will be tested at the HL-LHC by a combination of searches for disappearing charged tracks and displaced soft tracks. Quite remarkably, the lifetime of the charged component of the doublet can be correlated with the dark matter interaction rate at direct detection experiments and we show that the next generation of direct detection experiments has a unique sensitivity to the parameter space in the model. The fact that a small neutral mass splitting is a prediction of the asymmetric setup makes it an interesting candidate to account for the high energy nuclear recoil event recently reported by the LZ experiment. More generally, our detailed phenomenological study holds for any dark matter model involving an inert Higgs doublet with mass below 580 GeV.

</details>


### [5] [Lepton Flavour Universality in Semileptonic $\bar B_{(s)} \to D_{(s)}^{(\ast)}$ Transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17289)
*Yu-Chen Hu,Cong-Cong Ye,Craig D. Roberts,Zhao-Qian Yao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses continuum Schwinger function methods to compute SM predictions for $\bar B_{(s)} \to D_{(s)}^{(\ast)}$ semileptonic form factors without free parameters, yielding $R(D)$ and $R(D^\ast)$ values that deviate from experiment by $2.8\sigma$, and reveal a $2.3\sigma$ SU(3) flavour symmetry violation in $D_{(s)}^\ast$ transitions linked to Higgs–strong interaction couplings and emergent QCD effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve persistent tensions between SM predictions and experimental measurements of $R(D)$ and $R(D^\ast)$, and to probe SU(3) flavour symmetry breaking beyond leading-order approximations using ab initio nonperturbative QCD methods.

Method: Continuum Schwinger function approach within quantum chromodynamics (QCD), enabling parameter-free, pointwise determination of all twelve $b \to c$ semileptonic transition form factors across the full kinematic domain.

Result: $R(D) = 0.290(11)$, $R(D^\ast) = 0.267(5)$, $R(D_s) = 0.283(11)$, $R(D_s^\ast) = 0.251(5)$; $2.8\sigma$ discrepancy with experimental $R(D^{(\ast)})$ averages; $2.3\sigma$ SU(3) breaking observed in $D_{(s)}^\ast$ channels, attributed to Higgs–gluon couplings and emergent strong-interaction dynamics.

Conclusion: The results suggest physics beyond the standard model or unaccounted QCD effects—particularly Higgs-mediated contributions to hadronic currents—and highlight the role of emergent strong-interaction phenomena in flavour observables.

Abstract: Using continuum Schwinger function methods, we deliver parameter-free pointwise predictions for the complete set of twelve Standard Model (SM) $\bar B_{(s)} \to D_{(s)}^{(\ast)}$, i.e., $b\to c$, semileptonic transition form factors on the entire kinematically accessible domain. Therewith, we calculate SM theory values for the decay-rate ratios of $τ$ and $μ$ final states: $\{ R(D) \, , \, R(D)^\ast \} = \{ 0.290(11) \, , \, 0.267(5) \}$, $\{ R(D_{s}) \, , \, R(D_{s}^{\ast}) \} = \{ 0.283(11) \, , \, 0.251(5) \}$. The former is consistent with an existing average of SM estimates, but disagrees with the experimental average by $2.8σ$. Comparing transitions with light quark and strange quark spectators, we find a $2.3σ$ signal for violation of SU$(3)$ flavour symmetry, expressed at the 5% level in $D_{(s)}^\ast$ final states. It is induced by Higgs boson couplings into the strong interaction and modulated by phenomena deriving from emergent features of the strong interaction.

</details>


### [6] [Disappearing Tracks from Electroweak Vector Dark Matter at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14182)
*Fabián Hernández-Pinto,Patricio Escalona,Alfonso Zerwekh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates a spin-1 minimal dark matter candidate (SU(2)_L quintuplet) whose charged component has a macroscopic lifetime due to radiatively induced mass compression, leading to disappearing-track signatures at ATLAS; it recasts Run-2 disappearing-track searches, sets new mass exclusions (up to 1080 GeV), and projects sensitivity to ~1.5–1.7 TeV with future luminosity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test a specific spin-1 minimal dark matter model — the real SU(2)_L quintuplet with vanishing hypercharge — via its distinctive disappearing-track signature arising from radiatively compressed spectrum and long-lived V± decaying hadronically inside trackers.

Method: Analytic recasting of ATLAS Run-2 disappearing-track search (137 fb⁻¹) targeting long-lived charginos; computes tracklet reconstruction efficiency as a function of proper decay length cτ; validates method against pure-wino limits; incorporates model-predicted radiative mass splitting and Higgs-portal coupling constraints.

Result: Excludes quintuplet mass M_V ≤ 1080 GeV at cτ ≈ 250 mm; M_V < 630 GeV for fixed Δm = 166 MeV; and M_V ∈ [120, 240–300] GeV for the theoretically predicted mass splitting with small Higgs-portal coupling; projects reach of 1530–1750 GeV with 3 ab⁻¹.

Conclusion: The disappearing-track signature provides strong, complementary constraints on spin-1 quintuplet dark matter; current LHC data already excludes substantial parameter space, and high-luminosity upgrades will probe multi-TeV masses, making this a viable and testable dark matter scenario.

Abstract: We study the disappearing-track signature of a spin-1 minimal dark matter candidate transforming in the real five-dimensional representation of $SU(2)_L$ with vanishing hypercharge. Radiative electroweak corrections induce a compressed mass spectrum among the multiplet components, allowing the singly charged state $V^\pm$ to attain a macroscopic lifetime and decay via $V^\pm \to V^0 π^\pm$ inside tracking detectors. We recast the ATLAS search for disappearing tracks with the full Run-2 dataset of $137\text{ fb}^{-1}$, originally targeted at long-lived charginos, computing the tracklet reconstruction probability analytically from the published efficiency so that the full lifetime range is covered. The procedure reproduces the ATLAS limits for pure winos within $6\%$ in mass. The Run-2 data exclude quintuplet masses up to $1080\text{ GeV}$ for proper decay lengths $cτ\simeq 250\text{ mm}$, and $M_V < 630\text{ GeV}$ for a mass splitting of $166\text{ MeV}$. For the radiative mass splitting predicted by the model with a small Higgs-portal coupling, masses between $120\text{ GeV}$ and $240\text{--}300\text{ GeV}$ are excluded. With $3\text{ ab}^{-1}$ and a Run-2-like analysis, the sensitivity would extend to $1750\text{ GeV}$ for vanishing background and to $1530\text{ GeV}$ for a linear extrapolation of the instrumental background.

</details>


### [7] [A Comprehensive Interpretation of the LZ High-Energy Recoil Event: A Minimal $U(1)_B$ Dark Sector and Its Experimental Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14188)
*Mattia Di Mauro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an inelastic dark matter (DM) interpretation of the 248 keV nuclear-recoil excess observed by LZ, realized in a gauged baryon-number model with a Z_B mediator; it identifies viable parameter space satisfying relic density, LZ signal, and dijet constraints, and outlines multi-experiment tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explain the 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate from LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) via inelastic DM scattering, embedding it in a theoretically consistent, anomaly-free framework—gauged baryon number—with testable phenomenology.

Method: Constructs a UV-complete, anomaly-free model where baryonic U(1)_B is spontaneously broken, generating masses for a Z_B vector mediator and a two-state DM sector with small mass splitting; performs parameter scans to identify regions simultaneously satisfying the LZ recoil signal, Planck-measured DM relic abundance, and ATLAS/CMS dijet resonance limits.

Result: Identifies viable parameter regions: DM mass m_χ ≈ 0.19–4.5 TeV, Z_B mass M_{Z_B} ≈ 0.05–5.0 TeV, gauge coupling g_B ≈ 0.004–0.74; a benchmark thermal solution has m_χ = 1 TeV, M_{Z_B} = 1.44 TeV, g_B ≈ 0.228, Δm ≈ 300 keV; electroweak mixing enables rapid decay of the heavier DM state, suppressing indirect signals.

Conclusion: The inelastic DM interpretation in the gauged baryon-number model is viable and testable: future LZ/XENONnT data will confirm or refute the recoil excess; Run-3 ATLAS/CMS dijet searches probe the Z_B parameter space; and IceCube can test solar DM capture/annihilation into four-quark final states—providing complementary validation.

Abstract: The $248$ keV nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) motivates an inelastic dark matter (DM) interpretation, in which scattering converts the DM particle into a slightly heavier state. We provide a comprehensive study of this interpretation in an anomaly-free model of gauged baryon number, where a new vector boson, $Z_B$, mediates interactions between DM and quarks. Breaking the baryonic gauge symmetry generates the new particle masses and a small splitting between two DM states, while a residual symmetry keeps the lighter state stable. We identify regions compatible with the LZ candidate, the observed DM abundance, and current ATLAS and CMS dijet constraints. Within the scanned domain, these regions span DM masses $m_χ\simeq0.19$-$4.5$ TeV, mediator masses $M_{Z_B}\simeq0.05$-$5.0$ TeV, and gauge couplings $g_B\simeq0.004$-$0.74$, with correlated values of these parameters. A representative thermal solution has $m_χ=1$ TeV, $M_{Z_B}=1.44$ TeV, $g_B\simeq0.228$, and a mass splitting of $300$ keV. A small electroweak mixing allows the heavier DM state to decay rapidly, suppressing unwanted signals from its surviving population. This interpretation can be tested through complementary measurements: additional LZ and XENONnT data can establish whether the high-energy recoil signal persists, ATLAS and CMS Run-3 dijet searches can probe the mediator mass and coupling, and a dedicated IceCube analysis of four-quark final states can test DM capture and annihilation in the Sun. Together, these measurements can test complementary parts of the surviving region and assess whether the LZ candidate is consistent with this interpretation.

</details>


### [8] [Factorisation theorem and pole cancellation in the resolved $Q_1^q$-$Q_{7γ}$ contribution to $\bar{B}\to X_sγ$ at NLO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14211)
*Riccardo Bartocci,Tobias Hurth*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the next-to-leading order (NLO) QCD correction to the subleading resolved Q1^q–Q7γ contribution in the inclusive B̄→Xsγ decay within a factorisation framework, explicitly verifying cancellation of all 1/ε infrared singularities across hard, hard-collinear, anti-hard-collinear, and soft sectors — notably via a new u-integrated massless two-loop anti-hard-collinear jet function — thereby validating the consistency of the factorisation theorem beyond leading order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To verify the consistency of the SCET-based factorisation theorem for subleading resolved contributions to B̄→Xsγ at NLO, particularly addressing the non-trivial cancellation of infrared divergences across distinct effective-theory sectors.

Method: Uses Soft-Collinear Effective Theory (SCET); computes the u-integrated massless two-loop anti-hard-collinear jet function; determines the hard function at NLO; combines with known hard-collinear jet function and previously determined shape-function renormalisation; performs explicit dimensional-regularisation (1/ε) singularity cancellation among hard, hard-collinear, anti-hard-collinear, and soft contributions.

Result: Complete cancellation of all 1/ε poles is demonstrated; the anti-hard-collinear jet function is computed analytically at two loops; the full NLO subleading resolved Q1^q–Q7γ contribution is consistently factorised; physical irrelevance of certain multi-light-cone soft contributions is confirmed through divergence cancellation.

Conclusion: The analysis provides a non-trivial consistency check of the SCET factorisation framework for subleading resolved contributions to B̄→Xsγ at NLO and establishes the theoretical foundation for a complete phenomenological study of this channel.

Abstract: We study the subleading resolved $Q_1^q$-$Q_{7γ}$ contribution to the inclusive decay $\bar{B}\to X_sγ$ at next-to-leading order (NLO) in $α_s$. We present a formulation of the factorisation theorem which is valid beyond the leading order (LO). On this basis, we explicitly demonstrate the cancellation of all $1/\varepsilon$ singularities between the hard, hard-collinear, anti-hard-collinear and soft contributions. For this purpose, a central result of this work is the computation of the $u$-integrated massless two-loop anti-hard-collinear jet function, which determines the structure of the $1/\varepsilon$ singularities. In addition, we compute the hard function at the required order, combine our results with the hard-collinear jet function known from the literature, and employ the shape function whose renormalisation was determined in our previous work. There, we showed, for inclusive as well as for exclusive modes, that certain contributions to these multi-light-cone soft functions are physically irrelevant and can be consistently omitted; the explicit cancellation of the corresponding divergences provides a non-trivial confirmation of this statement. Overall, our findings constitute a non-trivial consistency check of the factorisation framework for this subleading resolved contribution at NLO and provide the basis for a full phenomenological analysis.

</details>


### [9] [Seeking a possible approach to understanding the origin of the proton mass using twist-4 contributions in inclusive reactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14485)
*Xiaohui Wen,Shinsuke Yoshida*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel theoretical framework to experimentally access the gluon condensate contribution to nucleon mass—traditionally inaccessible—by expressing it via twist-4 lightcone matrix elements and deriving the first complete analytic cross-section formulas for twist-4 contributions (quark, quark-gluon, gluon) in ep scattering at the Electron-Ion Collider.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The origin of nucleon mass from the strong interaction remains a fundamental unsolved problem; the gluon condensate term is especially elusive theoretically and experimentally, and its resolution is a major goal of the upcoming Electron-Ion Collider.

Method: The authors reformulate the gluon condensate term using twist-4 lightcone matrix elements, then derive analytic expressions for the ep scattering cross section incorporating all twist-4 contributions—including pure quark, quark-gluon, and pure gluon operators—within the collinear factorization framework.

Result: They obtain the first complete analytic cross-section formulas at twist-4 order for ep collisions and establish explicit relations between measurable twist-4 observables and the gluon condensate term.

Conclusion: Twist-4 lightcone matrix elements in high-energy ep scattering provide a viable experimental pathway to constrain or extract the gluon condensate contribution to nucleon mass, bridging nonperturbative QCD structure with collider observables.

Abstract: Most of the mass of the visible universe originates from the strong interaction. Quantitatively reproducing the origin of the nucleon mass is one of the important challenges in fundamental science. Since the next-generation Electron-Ion Collider has identified resolving this problem as one of its major goals, further theoretical studies are needed to develop approaches to investigating the origin experimentally. In this paper, we explore the possibility of evaluating the ``gluon condensate term'' which is particularly difficult to determine among the contributions to the nucleon mass, using high-energy scattering experiments. We first express the gluon condensate term in terms of experimentally accessible twist-4 lightcone matrix elements. We then derive, for the first time, analytic formulas for the cross section including all twist-4 contributions from pure quark, quark-gluon, and pure gluon matrix elements in $ep$ collisions, and show how these contributions can be related to the gluon condensate term.

</details>


### [10] [Mixed leptogenesis sources and low-energy neutrino portals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14708)
*Yingnan Xu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how a mixed leptogenesis source and low-energy neutrino portal share a common origin in heavy-particle amplitudes, imposing constraints on matching cancellations and scalar production during reheating; it quantifies implications for neutrino mass textures, vacuum lifetimes, energy partitioning, variance amplification by bath-produced scalars, and derives finite-density transport equations incorporating renormalized scalar mass and quartic couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the shared heavy-particle amplitude origin of mixed leptogenesis and the low-energy neutrino portal constrains model-building—particularly regarding matching conditions, scalar population dynamics during reheating, and consistency with observed baryon asymmetry and neutrino masses.

Method: Analytical quantum field-theoretic matching at finite temperature; invariant-based constraint analysis using lepton-number-violating invariants; flavour texture alignment and continuous parameterization; computation of vacuum lifetimes, energy fractions, and variance contributions from bath-produced scalars; derivation of stimulated emission scaling and Hartree moment equation with renormalized scalar mass and quartic coupling.

Result: An inclusive lepton-number-violating invariant defines a norm domain for the mixed dimension-five coefficient; aligned flavour texture nullifies conventional leptogenesis while equalizing coupling norms; a continuous family preserves neutrino-mass fit but yields unequal light couplings; benchmark vacuum lifetimes are 1.62e12 s and 2.80e11 s; bath scalars carry 4.81e-5 of primary scalar energy but contribute 11.1× its variance; a conditional variance bound arises from the positive production functional; scalar-mass dependence of stimulated emission and Hartree moment equation are derived with renormalized inputs.

Conclusion: The common amplitude origin tightly links high-scale leptogenesis to low-energy neutrino phenomenology and finite-density scalar dynamics; matching constraints translate into testable predictions for vacuum decays, spectral shaping during reheating, and non-equilibrium transport—enabling unified interpretation of baryogenesis, neutrino masses, and scalar thermal history.

Abstract: A mixed leptogenesis source and its low-energy neutrino portal depend on the same heavy-particle amplitudes. We determine how this common origin restricts matching cancellations and the scalar population produced during reheating. For two Majorana mass levels, each containing a degenerate pair, the inclusive lepton-number-violating invariant fixes an allowed norm domain for the mixed dimension-five coefficient at specified threshold strengths. An aligned flavour texture makes the conventional source vanish as a matrix and gives equal coupling norms for the two massive neutrinos. A continuous family preserves the neutrino-mass fit while relating the source to unequal light coupling strengths. The matched couplings give benchmark vacuum lifetimes of $1.62\times10^{12}\,\mathrm{s}$ and $2.80\times10^{11}\,\mathrm{s}$, while reheating normalized to the baryon abundance determines the primary spectrum. In the massless Born approximation with dilute outgoing states, bath-produced scalars carry $4.81\times10^{-5}$ of the primary scalar energy but contribute $11.1$ times its variance. The positive production functional converts the matching constraint into a conditional variance bound. We derive the scalar-mass dependence of stimulated emission and the Hartree moment equation, keeping the scalar mass and quartic as renormalized matching inputs. These results link the leptogenesis source to light-neutrino decay and the finite-density quantities entering subsequent coupled transport.

</details>


### [11] [$χ_{c0}$ production in $\barΛn$ scattering process](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14725)
*Quan-Yun Guo,Zhuo Yu,Dian-Yong Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies χ_{c0} production in the anti-Λn → χ_{c0} K⁰_S reaction using an effective Lagrangian approach, fitting invariant mass spectra to extract coupling constants and identify intermediate N and Λ resonances; predicts cross sections (1.9–8.0 nb at √s = 6 GeV) and forward-peaked differential distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the production mechanism of χ_{c0} in the anti-Λn → χ_{c0} K⁰_S process, motivated by the BESIII observation of χ_{c0} → n K⁰_S anti-Λ decay, and to identify contributing nucleon and hyperon resonances.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach with eight intermediate N and Λ states (including N(938), N(1720), N(1895), Λ(1116), Λ(1600), Λ(1890)); coupling constants fitted to M_{K⁰_S anti-Λ} and M_{n K⁰_S} invariant mass spectra; parameter β varied as 1, 2, 3 to test sensitivity.

Result: Two clear structures observed in both invariant mass spectra, assigned to N(1720)/N(1895) and Λ(1600)/Λ(1890); negligible contributions from ground-state N(938) and Λ(1116); predicted total cross sections of 1.9 nb, 4.2 nb, and 8.0 nb at √s = 6 GeV for β = 1, 2, 3; differential cross sections peak in the forward angular region.

Conclusion: The χ_{c0} production in anti-Λn collisions is dominated by excited N and Λ resonances rather than ground states; the reaction is experimentally accessible at current or near-future facilities (e.g., PANDA/FAIR), and its angular distribution favors forward detection.

Abstract: In the present work, we investigate the production of $χ_{c0}$ in the $\barΛ n \rightarrow χ_{c0} K^{0}_{S}$ process by utilizing an effective Lagrangian approach. Based on the $χ_{c0} \rightarrow n K^{0}_{S} \barΛ$ decay observed by the BESIII Collaboration, the coupling constants are determined by fitting the $M_{K^{0}_{S} \barΛ}$ and $M_{n K^{0}_{S}}$ invariant mass spectra, where eight $N$ and $Λ$ state are considered. Moreover, the parameters $β$ is taken to be 1, 2, 3 as special cases to check the parameter dependence. Our results show that two clear structures can be observed in both $M_{K^{0}_{S} \barΛ}$ and $M_{n K^{0}_{S}}$ invariant mass spectra, which should correspond to $N(1720)/N(1895)$ and $Λ(1600)/Λ(1890)$, respectively. In addition, the contributions from $N(938)$ and $Λ(1116)$ intermediate states are much smaller than those of the above-threshold resonances. With the fitted parameters for $β=1$, 2, 3, the cross sections for $\barΛ n \rightarrow χ_{c0} K^{0}_{S}$ are estimated to be 1.9 nb, 4.2 nb, and 8.0 nb at $\sqrt{s}=6$ GeV, respectively. Furthermore, our estimations indicate that the differential cross sections for $\barΛ n \rightarrow χ_{c0} K^{0}_{S}$ reach a maximum in the forward angle region.

</details>


### [12] [Probing eV-scale sterile neutrinos with neutrinoless double-beta decay and beta decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15060)
*Ruisha Yang,Jiajun Liao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how beta-decay constraints tighten limits on eV-scale sterile Majorana neutrinos in 3+1 neutrino models, where destructive interference between active and sterile contributions can weaken neutrinoless double-beta decay (0νββ) bounds; using updated oscillation data (including JUNO), it derives analytic and geometric conditions on sterile mass, mixing, and Majorana phase α₄ that satisfy both 0νββ and beta-decay limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: eV-scale sterile Majorana neutrinos can suppress neutrinoless double-beta decay (0νββ) via destructive interference with active neutrinos, thereby evading current 0νββ constraints; this work aims to assess whether complementary beta-decay data (via m_β limit) can break this degeneracy and restore sensitivity to sterile parameters.

Method: Combines updated global neutrino oscillation data (including first JUNO results) within a 3+1 framework; performs parameter-space scans for sterile mass m₄ and mixing |U_e4|² under normal/inverted ordering; derives analytic expressions for boundaries of allowed (m₄, α₄) given |m_ββ| and m_β limits; employs complex-plane vector analysis to characterize interference conditions and phase dependence.

Result: Beta-decay upper limit on m_β significantly tightens constraints on sterile-neutrino parameter space compared to 0νββ alone; allowed sterile mass range shrinks, especially for inverted ordering; α₄ is broadly allowed near 0 for small m₄ under normal ordering but constrained near π under inverted ordering; phase window narrows as m₄ approaches its upper bound or |m_ββ| limit tightens.

Conclusion: Complementary beta-decay information breaks the cancellation ambiguity in 0νββ interpretation, restoring robust exclusion power for eV-scale sterile neutrinos; the interplay between m_β and m_ββ imposes nontrivial geometric and phase-dependent constraints, making joint analysis essential for sterile neutrino searches in the 3+1 model.

Abstract: An eV-scale sterile Majorana neutrino can suppress neutrinoless double-beta decay through destructive interference with the active-neutrino contribution, weakening the resulting constraints on sterile-neutrino parameters. We investigate how complementary beta-decay information restricts this cancellation in the 3+1 framework. Using updated oscillation parameters that include the first JUNO results, we determine the allowed sterile-neutrino mass and mixing regions for both normal and inverted ordering. We show that an upper limit on the effective beta-decay mass $m_β$ of the three light neutrinos strengthens the constraints from neutrinoless double-beta decay alone. The beta-decay bound restricts the light-neutrino masses and hence the cancellation between the active and sterile contributions to $m_{ββ}$. We derive analytic expressions for the resulting boundaries and use complex-plane geometry to explain the allowed values of the sterile Majorana phase $α_4$ for a finite $|m_{ββ}|$ limit. For the mixing considered, normal ordering allows a broad range of $α_4$ at small sterile masses, whereas inverted ordering favors values near $π$. The allowed phase range narrows as the sterile mass approaches its upper bound or the limit on $|m_{ββ}|$ becomes more stringent.

</details>


### [13] [Chiral mass corrections to decuplet hidden-charm pentaquark states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15075)
*Yu-Quan Guo,Hao-Song Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates masses of hidden-charm pentaquark states in the SU(3) decuplet using heavy pentaquark chiral perturbation theory (HPChPT) at next-to-leading order (NLO), incorporating one-loop mesonic self-energy corrections; it predicts masses for doubly- and triply-strange members and observes an approximate Gell–Mann–Okubo mass relation persisting at NLO.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study SU(3) flavor symmetry breaking effects in hidden-charm pentaquark multiplets and test the robustness of the Gell–Mann–Okubo (GMO) mass relation beyond leading order.

Method: Heavy pentaquark chiral perturbation theory (HPChPT) at next-to-leading order (NLO); includes one-loop self-energy corrections from π, K, and η mesons; uses P_ψ^N(4312) and P_ψs^Λ(4459) as inputs to fix low-energy constants (LECs).

Result: Predicted masses: P_ψss^N = 4.607 GeV, P_ψsss^Λ = 4.755 GeV; near-constant mass splitting ≈ 0.148 GeV between adjacent strangeness layers; GMO relation holds to good accuracy at NLO.

Conclusion: The equal-spacing mass pattern — a hallmark of the GMO relation — emerges naturally from the analytical structure of NLO chiral corrections, confirming that SU(3) symmetry breaking remains mild and systematically describable within HPChPT.

Abstract: We calculate the masses of the $J^P=\frac{1}{2}^-$ hidden-charm pentaquark states in the $SU(3)$ decuplet within the heavy pentaquark chiral perturbation theory (HPChPT) up to the next-to-leading order (NLO). The one-loop self-energy corrections from the $π$, $K$, and $η$ mesons are evaluated. Taking $P_ψ^N(4312)$ and $P_{ψs}^Λ(4459)$ as inputs, we predict the masses of the doubly-strange and triply-strange decuplet members $P_{ψss}^N$ and $P_{ψsss}^Λ$ to be 4.607 GeV and 4.755 GeV, respectively. The mass splittings between adjacent strangeness layers are nearly constant at approximately 0.148 GeV. This equal-spacing pattern follows directly from the analytical structure of the NLO chiral corrections, while its actual magnitude is set by the low-energy constants (LECs). It indicates that the Gell--Mann--Okubo (GMO) relation, expected at the leading order of SU(3) flavor symmetry breaking, persists to good accuracy at the NLO.

</details>


### [14] [BSMScanner: a lightweight framework for beyond-the-Standard-Model global fits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15096)
*Mohamed Belfkir*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: BSMScanner is a declarative YAML-based framework for BSM parameter scans, enabling specification of parameters, priors, observables, theory constraints, and likelihoods; it compiles to optimized C++ kernels and supports multiple scanning engines and plugin extensibility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simplify and standardize BSM phenomenology scans by replacing imperative code with declarative, human-readable, and validated specifications—reducing implementation errors, improving reproducibility, and accelerating model exploration.

Method: BSMScanner parses YAML specifications defining parameters, mass matrices, diagonalizations, observables, validity conditions, and likelihood terms; constructs a dependency graph; compiles a typed C++ evaluation kernel; and interfaces with modular scanning engines (uniform sampling, differential evolution, basin discovery, MCMC refinement). It provides built-in flavor physics routines and plugin support for non-analytic quantities.

Result: Demonstrated on six benchmark BSM models (4–26 parameters), achieving efficient, validated scans with analytic cross-checks; generated best-fit points and confirmed consistency across engines and implementations.

Conclusion: BSMScanner enables robust, maintainable, and high-performance BSM parameter inference through declarative specification and compiled execution, bridging the gap between theoretical model definition and statistical scanning infrastructure.

Abstract: We present BSMScanner, a framework in which a beyond-the-Standard-Model (BSM) parameter scan is specified as a set of declarative YAML documents rather than written as a program. The user declares the free parameters and their priors, the mass matrices and the diagonalisations that act on them, the observables, the theory-validity conditions and the likelihood terms. The framework validates this specification, resolves it into a dependency graph and compiles it once into a typed C++ evaluation kernel, which is then driven by an independently chosen scanning engine: uniform sampling, two differential-evolution engines, or a staged basin-discovery orchestrator, optionally followed by Markov-chain posterior refinement. Flavour physics matrix diagonalisation, PMNS and CKM construction in fixed conventions, tabulated oscillation likelihoods and a library of common flavour observables are provided by the framework and imported by name, so that a model file contains only model-specific definitions; quantities without a closed form, such as a relic density, enter through a C++ or Python plugin interface. We describe the specification schema, the observable and likelihood library, the scanning engines and their configuration, and demonstrate the framework on six benchmark models with $4$ to $26$ free parameters, giving for each the specification, the scan configuration, the resulting best fit and analytic cross-checks of the result.

</details>


### [15] [Theoretical calculations for $t{\bar t}H$ production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15100)
*Nikolaos Kidonakis,Nodoka Yamanaka*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes higher-order perturbative corrections (up to approximate N^3LO in QCD and NLO in EW) for tt̄H production at the LHC, improving precision and reducing theoretical uncertainties in total and differential cross sections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision and reduce scale dependence in tt̄H production — a key channel for measuring Higgs-top Yukawa coupling — by including higher-order soft-gluon resummation beyond standard fixed-order calculations.

Method: Combines second- and third-order soft-gluon corrections with existing NLO QCD and NLO electroweak results to construct approximate N^3LO QCD + NLO EW predictions; computes total and differential cross sections (e.g., top quark p_T and rapidity) at LHC energies.

Result: aN^3LO QCD + NLO EW predictions yield significant enhancements in cross sections and improved stability against renormalization/factorization scale variations for both inclusive and differential observables.

Conclusion: Higher-order soft-gluon corrections are essential for precise tt̄H predictions; the aN^3LO QCD + NLO EW framework substantially improves accuracy and reliability for LHC phenomenology and Higgs coupling extractions.

Abstract: We calculate higher-order perturbative corrections for $t{\bar t}H$ production. By adding second-order and third-order soft-gluon corrections to NLO QCD and NLO electroweak (EW) results, we present total and differential cross sections at LHC energies through approximate N$^3$LO (aN$^3$LO) in QCD and NLO in EW. Our aN$^3$LO QCD + NLO EW results provide significant enhancements and a reduced scale dependence for the cross section and the top-quark transverse-momentum and rapidity distributions.

</details>


### [16] [Cosmology & Astrophysics of the Dark Dimension with Bulk Majorana Neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15209)
*Luis A. Anchordoqui,Cumrun Vafa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a cosmological lower bound (~100 GeV) on the Majorana mass of sterile neutrinos in a dark extra dimension to avoid overproduction of KK modes; suggests lower-mass bulk neutrinos could explain the Fermi gamma-ray halo excess via Hawking radiation from long-lived 5D black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the cosmological neutrino catastrophe in extra-dimensional models—specifically, the overproduction of Kaluza-Klein (KK) neutrino towers in the early universe—and to provide a particle physics explanation for the Fermi-LAT gamma-ray halo excess.

Method: Analyzes thermal production and relic abundance of Kaluza-Klein excitations of sterile Majorana neutrinos propagating in a $B-L$-charged bulk of a dark extra dimension; incorporates constraints from Big Bang nucleosynthesis and cosmic microwave background; evaluates Hawking evaporation spectra of 5D black holes into bulk neutrinos and their subsequent decays to SM particles.

Result: Derives a lower bound of ~100 GeV on the zero-mode Majorana mass of bulk neutrinos to suppress KK tower production; shows that neutrinos near this bound can yield a gamma-ray signal consistent with the Fermi halo excess via 5D black hole evaporation and decay chain.

Conclusion: A sufficiently heavy bulk Majorana neutrino (~100 GeV or more) reconciles extra-dimensional neutrino models with cosmology, and its lighter end of the allowed mass range offers a testable explanation for anomalous astrophysical gamma rays.

Abstract: Recently, the possibility of having sterile Majorana neutrinos charged under $B-L$ gauge symmetry in the bulk of the dark dimension has been explored. We point out a cosmological restriction on the Majorana mass of bulk neutrinos leading to mass $\gtrsim 100~{\rm GeV}$. This avoids the over-production of Kaluza-Klein neutrino towers in the early universe thus resolving the cosmological neutrino catastrophe of extra dimension scenarios. If the mass of the bulk neutrino is at the lower end of this bound, it can potentially also explain the recently observed Fermi halo excess on gamma rays through the evaporation of long-lived 5d (i.e. dark) black holes through Hawking radiation to bulk neutrinos, which promptly decay into standard model particles.

</details>


### [17] [When hadrons collide: di-J/psi structure in the final state](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15248)
*Eric S. Swanson*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a non-exotic rescattering mechanism—rather than compact tetraquarks—to explain di-J/ψ spectral structures via $cc\bar c\bar c$ production followed by coupled-channel rescattering in ten charmonium-pair channels; achieves competitive fit (χ²/ndf = 56/50) driven by J/ψψ(nS) thresholds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the prevailing interpretation of di-J/ψ structures at the LHC as fully charmed tetraquarks by offering an alternative, non-exotic explanation rooted in conventional QCD dynamics—specifically, threshold-driven rescattering effects.

Method: Constructs the $2^{++}$ production amplitude for a compact $cc\bar c\bar c$ source, coupled to ten open charmonium-pair channels (e.g., J/ψψ(nS), χ_{cJ}χ_{cJ}); employs unitary coupled-channel formalism with only three free background parameters; fits to LHC di-J/ψ invariant mass spectrum.

Result: Achieves statistically competitive description of data (χ²/ndf = 56/50); reproduces prominent spectral features—including peaks and enhancements—as consequences of J/ψψ(nS) thresholds and threshold enhancements, without invoking exotic hadrons.

Conclusion: The observed di-J/ψ structures can be consistently explained by conventional rescattering dynamics rather than requiring new exotic hadronic states; thus, evidence for fully charmed tetraquarks from this channel remains inconclusive.

Abstract: The structures in the di-J/psi spectrum seen at the LHC are widely read as fully charmed tetraquarks. As an alternative scenario, the $2^{++}$ production amplitude for a compact $cc\bar c\bar c$ source rescattering through ten charmonium-pair channels is presented. The model is completely fixed except for three background parameters and provides a competitive description of the data, with chi-sq/ndf=56/50. Prominent features are driven by J/psi psi(nS) thresholds or threshold enhancement, with no need for exotic hadrons.

</details>


### [18] [A toy model for hadronic bonds in exotic hadrons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15436)
*Hua-Xing Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends a toy model for hadronic molecular configurations to unify three hadronic-bond mechanisms—covalent, creation, and annihilation bonds—to explain near-threshold exotic hadrons; it introduces an effective activation energy $E_{\rm{act}} \sim 17~\rm{MeV}$ for the creation bond (calibrated to $Z_c(3900)$), notes its numerical proximity to the $X_{17}$ mass, and predicts a $\sim 36~\rm{MeV}$ bound state in the $D^*\bar{D}^*B^*\bar{B}^*$ system, suggesting experimental searches in $4\mu$ and $4\mu2\pi$ channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a unified theoretical framework for near-threshold exotic hadrons interpreted as hadronic molecules, by generalizing a prior covalent-bond toy model to include creation and annihilation bonds—mechanisms previously proposed but not yet integrated into a single quantitative scheme.

Method: Extends a simple toy model for covalent bonding to incorporate creation and annihilation bonds via an effective Hamiltonian framework; introduces an activation energy $E_{\rm{act}}$ for coherent sea $q\bar{q}$ excitation in the creation bond; uses threshold counting, binding energy estimation, and subsystem decomposition (e.g., $Z_c(4020)$ and $Z_b(10650)$ as creation-bond subunits) within an isoscalar $D^*\bar{D}^*B^*\bar{B}^*$ configuration.

Result: The extended framework classifies covalent-bond states as typically subthreshold, creation-bond states as typically above threshold, and annihilation-bond states as tunable via mixing with compact states; $E_{\rm{act}} \sim 17~\rm{MeV}$ is extracted from $Z_c(3900)$; a $\sim 36~\rm{MeV}$ binding energy is predicted for the $D^*\bar{D}^*B^*\bar{B}^*$ tetrameson; numerical coincidence between $E_{\rm{act}}$ and $X_{17}$ mass ($\sim 17~\rm{MeV}$) hints at shared low-energy four-quark dynamics.

Conclusion: A unified hadronic-bond framework successfully organizes diverse exotic hadron phenomenology; the creation bond’s activation energy provides a quantitative handle on sea-quark dynamics; the predicted $D^*\bar{D}^*B^*\bar{B}^*$ bound state offers a concrete experimental target, and the $E_{\rm{act}}$–$X_{17}$ correspondence suggests deeper connections among light four-quark structures awaiting microscopic elucidation.

Abstract: Three hadronic-bond mechanisms were proposed in our previous studies to describe near-threshold exotic hadrons in terms of hadronic molecular configurations: the covalent bond arises from the sharing and exchange of valence light quarks between hadronic clusters, the creation bond originates from the activation of coherent sea $q\bar{q}$ degrees of freedom followed by the formation of effective covalent correlations, and the annihilation bond arises from valence $q\bar{q}$ annihilation and the associated mixing with a nearby compact state. A simple toy model was previously constructed only for the covalent bond, and in the present study we extend this model to incorporate the creation and annihilation bonds within a unified framework. The resulting framework organizes covalent-bond configurations as tending to lie below threshold and creation-bond configurations as tending to appear above threshold, while the annihilation bond can shift a pre-existing molecular configuration either downward or upward through mixing with a nearby compact state. An effective activation energy $E_{\rm{act}}$ is introduced for the coherent sea four-quark component involved in the creation bond, with the $Z_c(3900)$ providing the benchmark value $E_{\rm{act}}\sim17~{\rm{MeV}}$. Intriguingly, this value is numerically close to the reported mass of the $X_{17}$ particle, which we previously interpreted as a low-energy bare four-quark structure. This numerical proximity may therefore reflect common low-energy (four-quark) dynamics, although the microscopic connection remains to be established. For the isoscalar $D^*\bar{D}^*B^*\bar{B}^*$ configuration involving the $Z_c(4020)$ and $Z_b(10650)$ creation-bond subsystems linked by two additional covalent bonds, we estimate a binding energy of approximately $36~{\rm{MeV}}$ below its four-meson threshold and propose searches in the $4μ$ and $4\mu2π$ final states.

</details>


### [19] [Probing the chromo-electric dipole moment of top quark near the toponium production threshold at LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15493)
*Kai Ma,Tong Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the chromo-electric dipole moment (CEDM) of the top quark affects near-threshold toponium ($\eta_t$) production at the LHC, incorporating resonant and continuum $t\bar{t}$ amplitudes with CEDM-induced interference, and proposes lepton angular asymmetries to set exclusion limits on $d_t^g$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The observed excess near the $t\bar{t}$ threshold suggests a color-singlet $S$-wave toponium state ($\eta_t$); its non-relativistic dynamics make it highly sensitive to top-quark anomalous dipole moments—particularly the chromo-electric dipole moment (CEDM)—which violates CP symmetry and probes BSM physics.

Method: The authors model enhanced $\eta_t$ production via gluon fusion near threshold, include full interference between resonant ($\eta_t$) and continuum $t\bar{t}$ amplitudes modified by the CEDM operator at the $t\bar{t}g$ vertex, and construct optimized lepton angular asymmetries from $t\bar{t} \to \ell^+\nu b\, \ell^-\bar{\nu}\bar{b}$ decays to isolate CEDM effects.

Result: They derive projected 95% CL exclusion limits on the top CEDM parameter $d_t^g$, showing improved sensitivity over direct $t\bar{t}$ continuum analyses due to resonant enhancement and interference.

Conclusion: Toponium formation near threshold provides a uniquely sensitive probe of the top quark CEDM; leveraging resonant-continuum interference and lepton angular asymmetries enables significantly tighter constraints on $d_t^g$ at the LHC.

Abstract: The recent observation of an excess near the kinematic $t\bar{t}$ production threshold by the CMS and ATLAS collaborations suggests the existence of a color-singlet and $S$-wave quasi-bound state of top quark. Due to the non-relativistic nature of top and anti-top quarks in this threshold region, toponium production provides an ideal system to probe the anomalous dipole moments of the top quark. In this work, we investigate the impact of chromo-electric dipole moment (CEDM) in $t\bar{t}$-gluon vertex on toponium production at the LHC. We implement the enhanced near-threshold production of the spin-0 color-singlet toponium state $η_t$ via gluon fusion. The resonant toponium contribution is then combined with the continuum $t\bar{t}$ production amplitude, including the interference effects induced by the top CEDM. Furthermore, we construct a set of asymmetries based on the angular distributions of charged leptons in the leptonic decay channel of $t\bar{t}$. Finally, we derive the expected exclusion limit on the top quark CEDM parameter $d_t^g$ using these lepton angular asymmetries.

</details>


### [20] [Modular $S_4$ Inverse Seesaw at the LZ High-Recoil Frontier](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15557)
*Waqas Ahmed,Vasileios Basiouris,George K. Leontaris*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a modular-S4 model linking lepton flavor physics and inelastic dark matter, using LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) high-energy nuclear recoil data to constrain pseudo-Dirac dark fermions with endothermic scattering; it identifies viable parameter space near the kinematic ceiling (~1.85 TeV, ~372 keV splitting) and predicts one massless neutrino at leading order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain anomalous high-energy nuclear recoils in LZ by endothermic inelastic dark matter, while simultaneously unifying the explanation with lepton flavor structure via a common modular parameter τ in an S4 modular symmetry framework.

Method: Constructs a modular-S4 inverse seesaw model where charged leptons and neutrinos share a complex modulus τ with dark-sector pseudo-Dirac fermions; performs global χ² scans over lepton sector (2N_R+2S inverse seesaw) and dark sector (mass m_χ and splitting δ), constrained by LZ high-recoil data using a two-bin Poisson likelihood incorporating endothermic kinematics, Helm form factor, SHM velocities, and energy smearing.

Result: Finds 181,447 lepton-sector solutions with χ² < 30 across disconnected modular regions; identifies viable dark-sector points in 300 GeV ≤ m_χ ≤ 2 TeV and 300–400 keV ≤ δ; best-fit point at (m_χ, δ) ≈ (1.847 TeV, 371.5 keV); predicts one effectively massless light neutrino and TeV-scale quasi-Dirac neutral fermions with small non-unitarity.

Conclusion: The model establishes a direct, predictive connection between modular lepton flavor, TeV-scale inverse-seesaw neutrino mass generation, and high-recoil inelastic dark matter—offering a unified explanation testable in next-generation direct detection experiments.

Abstract: The extension of the LUX--ZEPLIN nuclear-recoil analysis to approximately $270\,\mathrm{keV}$ probes a qualitatively new dark-matter regime in which endothermic scattering can suppress conventional low-energy recoils and concentrate the surviving spectrum near the Galactic high-velocity tail. We construct a modular-$S_4$ realization in which a minimal $2N_R+2S$ inverse seesaw accounts for lepton flavor, while a separate pair of Standard-Model-singlet fermions forms the pseudo-Dirac state relevant for high-recoil xenon scattering. The sectors share a common complex modulus $τ$, so the modular forms selected by charged-lepton and neutrino data also enter the operators generating the dark Dirac mass and Majorana splitting. Our scan yields $181447$ lepton-sector solutions satisfying $χ^2_{\ell+ν}<30$, distributed among several disconnected modular regions, and predicts one effectively massless light neutrino at leading order. Restoring the physical scales allows TeV-scale heavy quasi-Dirac neutral fermions with small non-unitarity. We subsequently scan $2\times10^6$ dark-sector points only over flavor-accepted values of $τ$ and identify common-modulus solutions in the LZ-oriented region $300\,\mathrm{GeV}\le m_χ\le2\,\mathrm{TeV}$ and $300\,\mathrm{keV}\leδ\le400\,\mathrm{keV}$. An exploratory two-bin Poisson likelihood, including endothermic xenon kinematics, the Helm form factor, Standard-Halo-Model velocities, Gaussian energy smearing, and profiling over the signal normalization, gives a best grid point at $(m_χ,δ)\simeq(1.847\,\mathrm{TeV},371.5\,\mathrm{keV})$, close to the terrestrial inelastic kinematic ceiling. The construction provides a direct link between modular lepton flavor, TeV-scale inverse-seesaw dynamics, and high-recoil inelastic dark matter.

</details>


### [21] [Quantum circuit representation and incomplete Bell measurements in Compton scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15526)
*Wei Xie,Yu-Chen Guo,Shuang Li,Ji-Chong Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates massless tree-level Compton scattering as a two-qubit quantum operation on electron and photon helicities, revealing a Bell-basis structure with parity-separated dynamics; it introduces an unsharp incomplete Bell measurement via Kraus-normalized filtering, and shows that only a full Bell-basis readout achieves unit conditional teleportation fidelity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge high-energy scattering physics and quantum information by interpreting Compton scattering as a controllable quantum operation and exploring its utility for quantum tasks like teleportation.

Method: Represents Compton scattering in the helicity basis as a two-qubit map; decomposes it in the Bell basis; identifies parity sectors; constructs a quantum circuit with a nonunitary filter; applies Kraus normalization to interpret the filter as an unsharp incomplete Bell measurement; evaluates teleportation fidelity under partial vs. complete Bell measurements.

Result: The scattering map splits into two Bell-parity sectors; the normalized filter implements an unsharp incomplete Bell measurement distinguishing parity but not individual Bell states; this incomplete measurement fails to exceed the classical teleportation fidelity threshold (2/3), while full Bell-basis readout achieves unit conditional fidelity.

Conclusion: Compton scattering naturally encodes a structured quantum channel suitable for quantum information processing; however, maximal utility—e.g., deterministic teleportation—requires complete Bell-state resolution, highlighting a fundamental link between scattering observables and measurement completeness.

Abstract: We formulate massless tree-level Compton scattering as an effective two-qubit operation on electron and photon helicities. In the Bell basis, the scattering map separates into two parity sectors and admits a simple quantum circuit representation with one nonunitary filter. After Kraus normalization, this filter defines an unsharp incomplete Bell measurement that distinguishes the two Bell-parity sectors but not the relative Bell label within each sector. We further show that the incomplete measurement alone does not surpass the classical teleportation threshold, whereas a subsequent complete Bell-basis readout enables unit conditional teleportation fidelity.

</details>


### [22] [Dalitz plot binning optimization for the model-independent measurement of the CKM angle $β$ with $B^0 \to D π^+ π^-$, $D \to K_S^0 π^+ π^-$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15839)
*Anfeng Li,Wenbin Qian,Boan Shi,Ao Xu,Liming Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper optimizes the Dalitz plot binning for a model-independent measurement of the CKM angle β in B⁰ → Dπ⁺π⁻, D → Kₛ⁰π⁺π⁻ decays using a Fisher information-based quality factor Q² and simulated annealing, achieving ~10% improved statistical sensitivity over equal-phase-difference binning; robustness is validated against experimental effects including acceptance, efficiency, resolution, and background.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance statistical sensitivity to the CKM angle β in a model-independent time-dependent Dalitz analysis of B⁰ → Dπ⁺π⁻, D → Kₛ⁰π⁺π⁻, where binning choice critically affects precision.

Method: Optimization of Dalitz plot binning for B⁰ → Dπ⁺π⁻ using a Fisher information-based quality factor Q² combined with simulated annealing; validation via toy Monte Carlo studies; robustness tested against decay-time acceptance, Dalitz efficiency/resolution, and background.

Result: Optimized binning yields ~10% relative improvement in statistical sensitivity to β compared to equal-phase-difference binning; improvement confirmed robust under realistic experimental effects.

Conclusion: Fisher-information-driven binning optimization significantly enhances precision in model-independent β measurements, providing a principled, experimentally robust strategy for future analyses.

Abstract: A model-independent measurement of the CKM angle $β$ can be performed using a time-dependent analysis of $B^0 \to D π^+ π^-$, $D \to K_S^0 π^+ π^-$ decays, where the Dalitz plots of both the $B$ and $D$ decays are divided into bins. The choice of binning scheme for the $B^0 \to D π^+ π^-$ Dalitz plot directly impacts the statistical sensitivity to $β$. This paper presents an optimization of this binning using a Fisher information-based quality factor $Q^2$ and simulated annealing. The optimized binning improves the sensitivity to $β$ by approximately $10\%$ relative to the equal-phase-difference baseline, as validated by toy Monte Carlo studies without experimental effects. The robustness of this improvement against decay time acceptance, Dalitz plot efficiency, Dalitz plot resolution, and background contributions is also assessed.

</details>


### [23] [From Dirac Neutrino Mass to Dark Radiation: An $A_4$-Symmetric FIMP Dark Matter Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15618)
*Pritam Das,Anupam Deka,Debajyoti Dutta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified A4×Z2×U(1)L model extending the Standard Model to simultaneously generate small Dirac neutrino masses and explain dark matter as a Feebly Interacting Massive Particle (FIMP) via freeze-in production. It numerically fits neutrino data (NuFit 6.1), identifies viable Yukawa couplings, computes relic abundance from NL→χ+η decays, confirms non-thermal χ production (λ ∼ 10⁻⁵·⁵–10⁻⁷·⁵), and estimates ΔNeff ≈ 0.14 from light right-handed neutrinos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify the explanation of tiny Dirac neutrino masses and dark matter within a single symmetry-based extension of the Standard Model, avoiding fine-tuning while accommodating neutrino oscillation data and cosmological constraints.

Method: Constructs an A4×Z2×U(1)L symmetric model with vector-like fermions (N, χ), flavons (η, φ), and an inert Z2-odd scalar η; performs numerical fit to NuFit 6.1 data; solves Boltzmann equation for χ relic density; verifies freeze-in regime by comparing interaction rate to Hubble expansion; computes ΔNeff from thermal/non-thermal RH neutrino contributions.

Result: Identifies parameter region satisfying 3σ NuFit 6.1 constraints; reproduces ΩDMh² ≈ 0.12 for χ via freeze-in with λ ∼ 10⁻⁵·⁵–10⁻⁷·⁵; confirms χ never thermalizes; obtains ΔNeff ≈ 0.14 from light RH neutrinos.

Conclusion: The model consistently realizes Dirac neutrino masses and FIMP dark matter in a common symmetry framework, with testable implications for neutrino physics, dark radiation (ΔNeff), and collider/indirect detection signatures.

Abstract: We propose a unified $A_4\times Z_2\times U(1)_L$ framework for generating small Dirac neutrino masses and address dark matter through feeble interactions in an extended particle physics model. The model extends the Standard Model with a pair of heavy vector-like neutral $A_4$ fermions $N(N_{L,R})$, two additional flavon fields $η$ and $φ$, among which $η$ being a $Z_2$-odd inert scalar and a dark-sector vector-like fermion field $χ$. We numerically explore the Dirac-sector Yukawa couplings and identify the parameter region that is consistent with the {\tt NuFit 6.1} neutrino oscillation data at the $3σ$ level. The dark matter candidate $χ$, stabilized by the $Z_2$ symmetry, is produced non-thermally through the decay $N_L\rightarrowχ+η$. By solving the Boltzmann equation, we find that the observed relic abundance, $Ω_{\rm DM}h^2\simeq0.12$, can be reproduced for freeze-in couplings of order $λ\sim10^{-5.5}$--$10^{-7.5}$ over the considered dark matter mass range. For the chosen parameter space, we also verify the interaction rate remains below the Hubble expansion rate, confirming $χ$ never reaches thermal equilibrium. Thus, our results demonstrate that the model provides a consistent realization of Dirac neutrino masses and FIMP dark matter within a common symmetry-based setup. The presence of light Dirac RH neutrinos can contribute to the dark radiation and hence the effective degrees of freedom via thermal and non-thermal processes. We further estimate the contribution of the light right-handed neutrinos to \(ΔN_{\rm eff}\), finding a maximum characteristic value of \(ΔN_{\rm eff}\simeq0.14\) in the benchmark parameter region.

</details>


### [24] [Conserved-charge diffusion in hot and dense QCD matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16829)
*Dushmanta Sahu,Kangkan Goswami,Raghunath Sahoo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates coupled diffusion of baryon number, electric charge, and strangeness in hot, dense quark matter using a three-flavor NJL model with dynamical quark masses; it computes the full diffusion matrix (including off-diagonal terms) via a relaxation-time kinetic approach, reveals flavor-dependent transport behavior, identifies a crossing of baryon and strangeness mobilities at finite μ_B, and provides benchmarks for conserved-charge transport in baryon-rich QCD matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify coupled transport of conserved charges (baryon number, electric charge, strangeness) in hot and dense quark matter—particularly relevant for heavy-ion collisions and neutron star interiors—where off-diagonal (cross-coupling) diffusion coefficients are essential but poorly constrained.

Method: Three-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model with self-consistent constituent quark masses; relaxation-time approximation to kinetic theory; computation of the full 3×3 diffusion matrix (diagonal and off-diagonal components); derivation of generalized diffusivities and mobilities using charge susceptibilities.

Result: Off-diagonal diffusion coefficients show nontrivial T and μ_B dependence tied to flavor composition of charge carriers; baryon and strangeness mobilities cross at finite baryon chemical potential due to contrasting density dependence of their transport coefficients and susceptibilities.

Conclusion: The study establishes quantitative, model-based predictions for coupled conserved-charge diffusion in baryon-rich QCD matter, highlighting the necessity of including cross-couplings and providing benchmark results for hydrodynamic simulations and equation-of-state modeling.

Abstract: We study the coupled diffusion of baryon number, electric charge, and strangeness in hot and dense quark matter using a three-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model with self-consistently generated constituent quark masses. The complete diffusion matrix is evaluated within a relaxation-time kinetic approach, including its diagonal and off-diagonal components. We find that the off-diagonal transport coefficients exhibit distinct temperature and baryon-chemical-potential dependence associated with the flavor-dependent composition of the charge carriers. We further investigate generalized charge diffusivities and mobilities obtained from the corresponding susceptibilities. At finite baryon chemical potential, the baryon and strangeness mobility scales cross, reflecting the different density dependence of their transport coefficients and susceptibilities. The results provide model benchmarks for coupled conserved-charge transport in baryon-rich QCD matter.

</details>


### [25] [Axionic Casimir Torque](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15825)
*Soham Ghatak*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a twist to the Casimir effect by incorporating an axion gradient, breaking rotational symmetry and inducing a macroscopic mechanical torque on parallel plates proportional to b² sin(2ϑ).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how symmetry breaking—specifically via a spatial axion gradient—affects quantum vacuum forces beyond the standard isotropic Casimir attraction.

Method: Theoretical analysis of zero-point fluctuations in the presence of an axion field gradient, leveraging axion electrodynamics and boundary-condition-modified quantum field theory for parallel plates.

Result: A mechanical torque (twist) arises on the plates, scaling as b² sin(2ϑ), where b is the axion gradient vector and ϑ is its angle relative to the plate normal.

Conclusion: The axion-induced symmetry breaking converts the isotropic Casimir force into an anisotropic interaction with a controllable rotational component, enabling new probes of axion-like fields and topological photonics.

Abstract: In the canonical Casimir effect, the quantum vacuum is isotropic, and the minimization of vacuum energy drives only a linear attractive force between the plates. However, the introduction of an axion gradient breaks this rotational symmetry. It can be demonstrated that these asymmetric zero-point fluctuations exert a macroscopic mechanical twist on the plates proportional to $\vec{b}^2 \sin (2\vartheta)$ where $\vec{b}$ is the spatial axion gradient and $\vartheta$ is the angle between the axion gradient and the normal to the plates.

</details>


### [26] [Event Generation for Neutrino and Muon DIS at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16836)
*Paulina Hernandez-Sainz,Felix Kling,Jelle Koorn,Juan Rojo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates and compares Monte Carlo event generators (Sherpa, Herwig7, POWHEG) against analytical (YADISM) and neutrino-specific (GENIE, NuWro, GiBUU) tools for simulating neutrino- and muon-initiated deep-inelastic scattering (DIS) at the LHC—specifically for FASER and SND@LHC. It quantifies impacts of NLO QCD corrections, PDFs (including nuclear and strange), parton shower/hadronisation, and QED radiation on inclusive and fiducial observables, and demonstrates applications to FASER data, strange PDF constraints via dimuons, and semi-inclusive charged-hadron production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precise DIS physics with forward neutrinos/muons at the LHC (FASER/SND@LHC), robust and validated simulations are required—yet discrepancies among existing generators hinder reliable extraction of proton/nuclear structure, fragmentation, and high-energy neutrino interaction properties.

Method: Systematic comparison of NLO general-purpose MC generators (Sherpa, Herwig7, POWHEG) with analytical structure-function calculations (YADISM) and dedicated neutrino MCs (GENIE, NuWro, GiBUU); assessment of theoretical uncertainties from higher-order QCD, PDF choices (including nuclear and strange), parton shower/hadronisation models, and QED radiation; validation against FASER emulsion/electronic data and application to dimuon and semi-inclusive DIS observables.

Result: Modern MC generators agree well on inclusive and differential DIS cross sections when settings (PDFs, scales, αs) are harmonised; residual differences persist in hadronic final-state-sensitive observables (e.g., hadron multiplicity, pT spectra); QED radiation and nuclear PDFs significantly affect fiducial acceptance; strange PDF sensitivity is demonstrable in dimuon channels; predictions for semi-inclusive charged-hadron production show generator-dependent systematics.

Conclusion: Accurate DIS simulations for forward experiments require careful generator selection and tuning—particularly for hadronic observables—while inclusive measurements benefit from cross-generator consistency. The study establishes a benchmark for theory uncertainty control in the emerging TeV-neutrino DIS programme at the LHC.

Abstract: Neutrinos and muons produced in the forward direction of LHC collisions and detected by the FASER and SND@LHC experiments enable a comprehensive deep-inelastic scattering (DIS) programme, covering both neutral and charged currents, at TeV energies. This programme aims to provide stringent constraints on proton and nuclear structure as well as on hadron fragmentation, and to offer novel insight into neutrino interactions at the highest energies achieved in a laboratory. Realising this potential requires accurate and precise simulations of neutral- and charged-current DIS. We systematically compare the predictions of NLO general-purpose Monte Carlo (MC) event generators for neutrino- and muon-initiated DIS at the LHC (Sherpa, Herwig7, and POWHEG) against analytical structure-function calculations (YADISM) and dedicated neutrino simulation packages (GENIE, NuWro, GiBUU). Within this framework, we quantify the role of higher-order QCD corrections, (nuclear) parton distribution functions (PDFs), parton shower and hadronisation modelling, and QED radiation both for inclusive observables and for the FASER fiducial selections. We also present representative applications to the FASER neutrino programme: comparisons with its most recent data from the emulsion and electronic sub-detectors, a study of strange PDF sensitivity in the dimuon final state, and predictions for charged-hadron production in semi-inclusive DIS. Our analysis highlights the agreement of modern event generators for inclusive and differential DIS observables once common settings are adopted, while differences remain for observables sensitive to the hadronic final state.

</details>


### [27] [Testing Minimal $U(2)^3$ Right-Handed Flavor Breaking in a Singlet-Scalar Vector-Like Quark Portal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15911)
*Qazi Maaz Us Salam*

Main category: hep-ph
