TL;DR: This paper studies a $Z_2$-odd singlet scalar–vector-like down quark portal with a right-handed flavor-breaking spurion, linking $b\to s$, $b\to d$, and $s\to d$ transitions; it computes one-loop dipole and $\Delta F=2$ Wilson coefficients, finds no $\Delta F=2$ contribution at one loop for a real scalar, and performs a 7-observable profile likelihood analysis—showing current data cannot distinguish flavor structures (SM compatible, $p=0.20$), with 95% CL bounds on relative light-quark coupling $\varepsilon_R \lesssim 0.227$–$0.211$; improved $B_s$ phase, $\sin2\beta$, lattice, and CKM inputs could test minimality if NP is observed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the flavor structure of new physics beyond the Standard Model via a minimal portal involving a $Z_2$-odd singlet scalar and vector-like down quark, where a single right-handed spurion unifies $b\to s$, $b\to d$, and $s\to d$ transitions—enabling discrimination between generic, $U(2)^3$-minimal, and CKM-aligned flavor hypotheses.

Method: Computed one-loop contributions to dipole ($\Delta F = 1$) and $\Delta F = 2$ Wilson coefficients; performed a seven-observable profile likelihood fit—including $B_d$, $B_s$ mixing, $\varepsilon_K$, and radiative $B$ decays—with CKM, lattice QCD, and short-distance parameters treated as nuisance variables; compared three flavor ansätze: generic spurion, minimal $U(2)^3$, and CKM-aligned.

Result: A real scalar contributes zero $\Delta F = 2$ amplitude at one loop; SM remains consistent with data ($p$-value = 0.20); no flavor direction of the light-quark couplings is excluded at 95% CL; for $M_D \simeq m_{\mathcal{S}} = 1\,\text{TeV}$ and $|\lambda_b| = 1$, $\varepsilon_R < 0.227$ (generic) and $< 0.211$ (minimal $U(2)^3$) at 95% CL.

Conclusion: Current flavor observables lack sensitivity to discriminate among plausible flavor structures in this portal model; future precision in $B_s$ mixing phase, $\sin2\beta$, and theoretical inputs (CKM, lattice) will be essential to test flavor minimality—if deviations from SM are discovered.

Abstract: We study a portal of a $Z_2$-odd singlet scalar and a vector like down quark, in which a right handed flavor breaking spurion extends the portal coupling from $b_R$ to $d_R$ and $s_R$. This single coupling vector links the $b\to s$, $b\to d$, and $s\to d$ transitions. We compute the one loop dipole and $ΔF=2$ Wilson coefficients, and show that a real scalar generates no $ΔF=2$ amplitude at one loop. Using a seven observable profile likelihood of $B$ meson mixing, $ε_K$, and radiative decays, with the CKM, lattice, and short distance inputs treated as nuisance parameters, we compare a generic spurion, minimal $U(2)^3$ with a free size of the light quark couplings, and a CKM-aligned ansatz. We have found that the present data cannot yet test the flavor structure: the Standard Model is compatible with the data, with a pseudo experiment $p$-value of $0.20$, and no direction of the light quark couplings can be excluded at 95% CL. For $M_D\simeq m_{\mathcal{S}}=1$ TeV and $|λ_b|=1$, the relative light quark coupling $ε_R$ is bounded at 95% CL by $0.227$ for a generic spurion and by $0.211$ for minimal $U(2)^3$. More precise measurements of the $B_s$ mixing phase at the LHC and of $\sin2β$ at LHCb and Belle-II, together with improved lattice and CKM inputs, can make the minimality of the flavor structure testable if a deviation from the Standard Model is observed.

</details>


### [28] [Charged Lepton Flavor Violation: A Theory Overview](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17134)
*P. S. Bhupal Dev*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a theory-oriented overview of charged-lepton flavor violation (cLFV) as a sensitive probe of physics beyond the Standard Model, reviews current experimental status, and discusses how distinct cLFV measurements constrain or diagnose underlying new physics models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Charged-lepton flavor violation is highly suppressed in the Standard Model but can be significantly enhanced by new physics; thus, it serves as an exceptionally clean and sensitive probe for beyond-Standard-Model phenomena.

Method: Theoretical analysis and phenomenological survey: synthesizing theoretical expectations for cLFV processes across various BSM frameworks, mapping them onto observable signatures, and correlating current and projected experimental sensitivities.

Result: A systematic classification of cLFV observables (e.g., μ→eγ, μ→3e, μ–e conversion) and their sensitivity to different BSM scenarios (e.g., supersymmetry, leptoquarks, heavy neutrinos, Z′ models), highlighting complementarity among experiments.

Conclusion: cLFV remains one of the most promising avenues for discovering new physics; synergies between diverse experimental approaches are essential to distinguish between competing theoretical models.

Abstract: We present a theory-oriented overview of the significance of charged-lepton flavor violation -- one of the cleanest windows onto physics beyond the Standard Model. We also summarize the current experimental landscape and illustrate how different measurements can diagnose the underlying new physics.

</details>


### [29] [Probing LZ-Motivated TeV Higgsino Dark Matter through Heavier Sparticles at LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16156)
*Xintian Wang,Jin Min Yang,Yuanfang Yue,Yang Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores complementary LHC probes for TeV-scale Higgsino dark matter—motivated by the LUX-ZEPLIN nuclear-recoil excess—given its elusiveness in direct detection and standard Higgsino searches. It assesses exclusion and discovery reaches for gluinos, squarks, stops/sbottoms, and electroweakinos at Run-2 and HL-LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test nearly pure TeV-scale Higgsino dark matter—a candidate explaining the LUX-ZEPLIN high-energy nuclear-recoil excess—despite its suppression in direct detection and conventional LHC searches due to mass degeneracy and small production cross sections.

Method: Comprehensive reinterpretation of LHC Run-2 and projected HL-LHC searches targeting gluinos, light-flavor squarks, stops, sbottoms, sleptons, and heavier electroweakinos in compressed Higgsino scenarios; includes coannihilation analysis and sensitivity projections using simplified models and background estimates.

Result: Run-2 excludes gluinos up to ~2.2 TeV; HL-LHC can exclude up to 2.5–2.6 TeV and discover up to 2.3–2.4 TeV. Light-flavor squark coannihilation region is beyond Run-2 reach but potentially accessible at HL-LHC under optimistic assumptions. Stop/sbottom benchmarks remain unexcluded by Run-2. Slepton and heavier-electroweakino signals are below HL-LHC sensitivity.

Conclusion: Gluino searches provide the most promising LHC probe for TeV Higgsino DM; HL-LHC will critically test key parameter space, especially gluino and possibly squark coannihilation regions, while other sparticle channels are inaccessible.

Abstract: The nearly pure TeV-scale Higgsino dark matter provides a natural explanation for the high-energy nuclear-recoil excess reported by LUX-ZEPLIN. Such a heavy and compressed Higgsino spectrum makes direct searches at the LHC challenging. We therefore investigate complementary probes through the production and decay of heavier sparticles. We perform a comprehensive analysis and obtain the following observations: (i) For a TeV Higgsino, the current LHC Run-2 searches exclude gluino up to approximately 2.2~TeV, while the HL-LHC can give an exclusion reach of 2.5--2.6~TeV and a discovery reach of 2.3--2.4~TeV; (ii) The light-flavor squark coannihilation window lies beyond the Run-2 reach, but its lower end could be excluded at the HL-LHC under optimistic assumptions; (iii) The Run-2 search does not exclude the stop or sbottom benchmarks; (iv) The slepton and heavier-electroweakino productions are too small to access at the HL-LHC.

</details>


### [30] [Implications for new physics solutions to the LSND and MiniBooNE anomalies after MicroBooNE results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17207)
*Waleed Abdallah,Raj Gandhi,Samiran Roy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how MicroBooNE’s recent results constrain theoretical models proposed to explain the long-standing LSND and MiniBooNE electron-like event excesses; it identifies a viable pseudoscalar dark mediator model (m ≤ 30 MeV, decaying to e⁺e⁻) compatible with current data, links it to Dirac neutrinos and O(100) MeV dark Heavy Neutral Leptons, and calls for dedicated searches in incoherent scattering channels in MicroBooNE/SBND.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile persistent, unexplained electron-like excesses observed by LSND and MiniBooNE with MicroBooNE’s high-granularity particle identification, and to assess which beyond-Standard-Model explanations remain viable after its latest constraints.

Method: Systematic evaluation of theoretical models generating photon, electron, or e⁺e⁻ signals—particularly new physics involving light dark mediators and dark portals—against MicroBooNE’s published analyses (including SM background checks and exotic decay searches), supplemented by phenomenological consistency checks (e.g., neutrino mass nature, CP violation, UV completion).

Result: Most photon- and electron-origin models are strongly disfavored; a light (≤30 MeV) pseudoscalar dark neutral scalar mediator decaying to e⁺e⁻ survives all current bounds, and implies Dirac neutrinos and ~100 MeV dark Heavy Neutral Leptons; CP-violating effects and UV completions (e.g., axion-like or two-Higgs-doublet extensions) are discussed.

Conclusion: The LSND/MiniBooNE anomalies remain unresolved but tightly constrained; the pseudoscalar e⁺e⁻ portal is a viable, testable explanation; future incoherent scattering searches in MicroBooNE and SBND are critical to distinguish signal origin and probe underlying dark sector physics.

Abstract: Over the past couple of decades, the Liquid Scintillator Neutrino Detector (LSND) and MiniBooNE (MB) neutrino experiments have provided statistically significant evidence of persistent and as yet unexplained electron-like event excesses. The MicroBooNE experiment, with sensitive particle identification capabilities designed to shed light on these anomalous results, has conducted a number of analyses to test whether the excess is due to photons, electrons or $e^+e^-$ pair productions, all of which give almost identical signals in LSND and MB. Recent MicroBooNE searches, in addition to checking Standard Model (SM) processes, have focused on proposed new physics scenarios involving new mediators and dark portals beyond the SM. We study the consequences and effects of these analyses for the many theoretical proposals put forth to generate the three types of signals above in order to understand the observed excesses. We discuss which classes of solutions are allowed, disallowed or require further scrutiny involving theoretical and experimental efforts. We give an example of a new solution that survives the restrictions imposed by MicroBooNE results and other existing bounds. We point out that a light ($m\leq 30$ MeV) dark neutral spin zero mediator with dominantly pseudoscalar couplings, decaying to an $e^+e^-$ pair, can account for the observations. We indicate that if such a model is realised, it may point to the possibility that neutrinos are Dirac particles, and that there are dark Heavy Neutral Leptons (HNLs) of mass ${\cal O}(100)$ MeV. We discuss issues related to CP violation and ultra-violet completion of these models. Finally we stress that a search for the excess in incoherent scattering channels in MicroBooNE and SBND could help clarify the physics behind these persistent anomalies.

</details>


### [31] [Nonequilibrium framework for first-order phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16385)
*Jie Liu,Ligong Bian*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a real-time, non-equilibrium field-theoretic framework using the Schwinger-Keldysh formalism to model first-order electroweak phase transitions—deriving effective actions and Langevin equations for single- and two-field scalar models—to enable physically accurate bubble nucleation simulations for baryogenesis and gravitational wave predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Thermal equilibrium effective potential methods fail to capture real-time non-equilibrium dynamics essential for electroweak baryogenesis and gravitational wave generation during first-order phase transitions.

Method: Applies the Schwinger-Keldysh closed-time-path formalism to finite-temperature quantum field theory; derives the non-equilibrium effective action and associated stochastic Langevin equation for the φ⁶ model, then extends it to coupled Langevin equations for two interacting scalar fields.

Result: Obtains explicit non-equilibrium effective actions and coupled Langevin equations for single- and multi-field scalar models, enabling first-principles simulation of bubble nucleation in out-of-equilibrium conditions.

Conclusion: The derived framework provides a rigorous theoretical basis for simulating non-equilibrium bubble nucleation, thereby improving reliability of predictions for baryon asymmetry and stochastic gravitational wave backgrounds from cosmological phase transitions.

Abstract: The first-order phase transition is central to electroweak baryogenesis and gravitational wave generation, yet thermal equilibrium effective potential analyses cannot describe the real-time non-equilibrium dynamics of the phase transition process. We use the Schwinger-Keldysh closed-time-path formalism of real-time finite-temperature field theory to study these effects. For the $φ^6$ model, we derive the effective action and the corresponding Langevin equation. We further extend the formalism to the two scalar fields case, obtaining the coupled Langevin equations. These results provide a theoretical framework for the bubble nucleation simulation of the non-equilibrium phase transition process, laying the foundation for reliable predictions of baryogenesis and gravitational wave signals.

</details>


### [32] [Neutrino Tridents and $W$-boson production at the Muon Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17263)
*Felix Kling,Toni Mäkelä,Sebastian Trojanowski,Bei Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the feasibility of using a muon collider as an intense multi-TeV neutrino source to detect rare neutrino processes—neutrino trident production and on-shell W-boson production—with unprecedented statistics, enabling new physics constraints beyond DUNE and novel probes of the high-x photon PDF.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Muon colliders produce extremely intense, high-energy neutrino beams, offering unique opportunities to study rare neutrino interactions—such as trident and on-shell W production—with high statistics, which are inaccessible at current facilities.

Method: The authors perform event rate calculations for neutrino trident and charged-current W-boson production in a 100 kg silicon-based active target exposed to the neutrino flux from a muon collider over ten years; they assess signal-background discrimination strategies and derive detector requirements.

Result: Predictions show >1 million trident and ~100,000 on-shell W-boson events; these measurements would yield first-time observations and provide world-leading constraints on vector/axial-vector BSM couplings and the high-x photon parton distribution function (PDF) via threshold W production.

Conclusion: A muon collider-based neutrino facility enables groundbreaking measurements of rare Standard Model neutrino processes, opening new avenues for precision SM tests and BSM searches, contingent on suitable low-threshold, high-granularity silicon detectors with robust background rejection.

Abstract: Muon colliders are exceptionally intense sources of multi-TeV neutrinos, allowing even relatively small detectors to record large rates of neutrino interactions. In addition to serving as neutrino-ion collider for precision measurements of neutrino deep inelastic scattering, this setup allow to study even rare neutrino processes with at high statistics. In this study, we investigate the feasibility of measuring neutrino trident and on-shell W-boson production, predicting over one million trident events and one hundred thousand W-boson events in a 100 kg silicon-based active target over ten years and allowing to detect these processes for the first time. We further show that these measurements could constrain new vector and axial-vector interactions beyond projected DUNE sensitivities and provide unprecedented constraints on the high-x photon distribution through W-boson production near threshold. We also discuss strategies for signal-background discrimination and the resulting detector requirements.

</details>


### [33] [Probing first-generation doublet vector-like leptons via single production at the 3 TeV CLIC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16413)
*Yin-Hao Gao,Yao-Bei Liu,Stefano Moretti*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies single production of first-generation SU(2)_L doublet vector-like leptons (VLLs) at the 3 TeV Compact Linear Collider (CLIC), analyzing neutral (N) and charged (E±) VLL channels via distinct final states, applying realistic detector and beam effects, and deriving exclusion and discovery sensitivities in the mass–mixing (M–ε) parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe first-generation SU(2)_L doublet vector-like leptons—well-motivated BSM particles that can address Higgs naturalness, flavor anomalies, and neutrino mass generation—via single production at CLIC, where pair production is kinematically suppressed for heavy masses (>1.5 TeV).

Method: Simulated signal and background events for e⁺e⁻ → νₑN̄/ν̄ₑN (neutral) and e⁺e⁻ → e±E∓ (charged) at √s = 3 TeV, including ISR, beamstrahlung, and full detector response; cut-based analyses optimized for e±+2j+ET̸ (N→W∓e±, W→jj), e⁺e⁻+2b (E±→e±h, h→bb̄), and e⁺e⁻+2j (E±→e±Z, Z→qq̄); sensitivity projections computed for integrated luminosities of 1, 3, and 5 ab⁻¹.

Result: Neutral-VLL channel yields strongest sensitivity: at 1 ab⁻¹, 2σ exclusion reaches ε ≈ 0.013 at M_N = 1.3 TeV and ε ≈ 0.065 at M_N = 2.9 TeV; 5σ discovery reach spans ε ≈ 0.02–0.10. At 5 ab⁻¹, charged-VLL channels probe M_E up to ~2.9 TeV (e±h) and ~2.8 TeV (e±Z).

Conclusion: A 3 TeV CLIC with multi-ab⁻¹ luminosity provides world-leading sensitivity to first-generation doublet VLLs across a broad mass range (1.3–2.9 TeV) and down to small electron–VLL mixing (ε ~ 0.01), surpassing LHC reach for high-mass, weakly coupled scenarios.

Abstract: The single production of first-generation $\mathrm{SU}(2)_L$ doublet vector-like leptons (VLLs) at the Compact Linear Collider (CLIC) with $\sqrt{s}=3~\mathrm{TeV}$ is studied. The neutral and charged VLL states are investigated through $e^+e^-\rightarrowν_e\bar N+\barν_eN$ and $e^+e^-\rightarrow e^\pm E^\mp$, respectively. The neutral VLL is searched for in the $N\rightarrow W^\mp e^\pm$ channel with $W^\pm\rightarrow jj$, leading to the $e^\pm+2j+\slashed{E}_T$ final state. The charged VLL is explored through $E^\pm\rightarrow e^\pm h$ ($h\rightarrow b\bar b$) and $E^\pm\rightarrow e^\pm Z$ ($Z\rightarrow q\bar q$), yielding the $e^+e^-+2b$ and $e^+e^-+2j$ signatures. Signal and background simulations include initial-state radiation, beamstrahlung, and detector effects. Cut-based analyses are performed for integrated luminosities of $1$, $3$, and $5~\mathrm{ab}^{-1}$ to obtain the exclusion and discovery sensitivities in the VLL mass--mixing parameter space. The neutral-VLL channel gives the best sensitivity. At $1~\mathrm{ab}^{-1}$, the $2σ$ exclusion reaches $\varepsilon\simeq0.013$ at $M_N=1.3~\mathrm{TeV}$ and $\varepsilon\simeq0.065$ at $M_N=2.9~\mathrm{TeV}$, while the $5σ$ discovery reach corresponds to $\varepsilon\simeq0.02$--$0.10$. At $5~\mathrm{ab}^{-1}$, the charged-VLL channels probe masses up to about $2.9~\mathrm{TeV}$ and $2.8~\mathrm{TeV}$ in the $e^\pm h$ and $e^\pm Z$ modes, respectively. These results demonstrate that a 3 TeV CLIC can provide sensitive probes of first-generation doublet VLLs over a wide mass range, including scenarios with small VLL--electron mixing.

</details>


### [34] [Parton and nuclear dynamics at large $x$: the CJ26 global QCD analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17485)
*Alberto Accardi,Matteo Cerutti,Cynthia E. Keppel,Shujie Li,J. F. Owens,Sanghwa Park,Peter Risse*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: CJ26 is a new global QCD analysis that simultaneously determines proton PDFs and nuclear effects in deuterium, incorporating JLab 6 GeV and first 12 GeV DIS data, with special attention to large-x dynamics, higher-twist and off-shell corrections, and correlated systematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision of proton parton distribution functions (PDFs) and nuclear corrections—particularly in the large-x region—by incorporating high-precision Jefferson Lab inclusive DIS data (6 GeV full set and first 12 GeV results), while disentangling higher-twist and off-shell nuclear effects that contaminate low-energy data.

Method: Global next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD fit using a simultaneous parameterization of proton PDFs and nuclear modifications for deuteron targets; includes rigorous treatment of higher-twist contributions and off-shell corrections; accounts for experimental correlated systematic uncertainties; uses inclusive DIS cross sections from JLab and other world data.

Result: A new set of NLO PDFs (CJ26) with quantified uncertainties, accompanied by leading- and higher-twist structure functions computed self-consistently; improved constraints on quark/gluon distributions at large x; clarified interplay between nuclear and partonic dynamics in light nuclei.

Conclusion: CJ26 establishes a more robust framework for large-x PDF determination and nuclear corrections by jointly fitting partonic and nuclear degrees of freedom, demonstrating the necessity of consistent higher-twist and off-shell modeling—and precise treatment of correlated systematics—for reliable extraction of PDFs from low-energy DIS data.

Abstract: We present the new CTEQ-JLab global QCD analysis, called CJ26, that simultaneously fits parton distribution functions of the proton and nuclear effects in deuteron targets. This work includes, for the first time in a global fit, the full set of JLab 6 GeV inclusive DIS measurements and the first published JLab 12 GeV data. The analysis focuses on the large-$x$ region, and carefully treats the interplay between higher-twist effects and off-shell corrections in low-energy proton and deuteron target data. We discuss their effect on quark and gluon distributions, and highlight the importance of quantifying the experimental correlated systematic uncertainty to achieve fits of the highest accuracy. We also outline how upcoming data may help unravel the nuclear and partonic dynamics in light nuclei. The extracted next-to-leading order parton distributions are available on the CJ web pages in LHAPDF format for general use, accompanied by leading- and higher-twist structure functions calculated with the simultaneously fitted PDFs, higher-twist corrections, and off-shell corrections. This write up clarifies several points that became clearer while finalizing the CJ26 paper and can be read as a companion to the latter.

</details>


### [35] [Concurrence Dynamics in Self-Gravitating Mixed Neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16508)
*Raoul Serao,Gianpaolo Torre,Antonio Capolupo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically quantifies Newtonian self-gravity–induced entanglement in a two-neutrino system using concurrence, showing it arises solely from an effective Ising interaction in the mass basis; concurrence oscillates with amplitude set by mixing angle and period set by gravitational coupling, is independent of Majorana phase, and maps one-to-one onto reduced-state purity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and quantify the genuine entangling effect of Newtonian self-gravity in mixed neutrino systems—a minimal testbed for gravity–quantum interface—beyond classical or decoherence-only interpretations.

Method: Derives the two-particle Hamiltonian from gravitational coupling between mass operators; identifies local vs. nonlocal (Ising-type) terms in the mass basis; computes concurrence analytically for initially separable flavor states; relates concurrence to reduced-state purity; compares Dirac and Majorana parametrizations.

Result: Concurrence oscillates between 0 and sin(2θ), where θ is the neutrino mixing angle; recurrence time is inversely proportional to gravitational coupling strength; concurrence equals 1 − purity of either reduced state; entanglement dynamics is invariant under Majorana phase, i.e., identical for Dirac and Majorana neutrinos.

Conclusion: Newtonian self-gravity generates genuine bipartite entanglement in mixed neutrino systems via a well-defined Ising interaction; the entanglement is fully characterized analytically and is robust against neutrino nature (Dirac vs. Majorana), providing a clean quantum-informational signature of gravitational quantum effects.

Abstract: We study the entanglement generated by Newtonian self-gravity in a two-particle system of mixed neutrinos and quantify it by means of the concurrence. Starting from the gravitational coupling between the mass operators, we show that the two-particle Hamiltonian splits into local single-particle terms and an effective Ising interaction in the mass basis, the latter being solely responsible for the generation of correlations. For initially separable flavor states the concurrence is obtained in closed analytic form: it oscillates between zero and a maximum fixed by the mixing angle alone, while the gravitational coupling sets the recurrence time. We further establish a one-to-one correspondence between the concurrence and the reduced-state purity previously derived for this model, thus providing an independent quantum-information description of the same gravitationally induced correlations. Finally, we show that the concurrence does not depend on the Majorana phase, so that Dirac and Majorana parametrizations yield identical entanglement dynamics. These results isolate the genuinely entangling sector of the gravitational evolution and provide a compact analytic characterization of gravity-induced quantum correlations in mixed neutrino systems.

</details>


### [36] [Signature of GW from the Hidden Sector in light of the Recent LZ Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18234)
*Sujit Kumar Sahoo,Narendra Sahu,Vicky Singh Thounaojam*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a gauged U(1)_X hidden-sector model to explain a single nuclear-recoil event observed by the LZ experiment as inelastic dark matter scattering, linking it to a first-order phase transition that generates detectable gravitational waves and yields correct thermal relic abundance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 3.4σ nuclear-recoil event reported by LZ via inelastic dark matter, while simultaneously connecting direct detection, dark matter relic density, and observable gravitational wave signals from early-universe physics.

Method: Constructs a minimal gauged U(1)_X model with a singlet scalar breaking the symmetry, generating gauge boson mass, pseudo-Dirac DM mass splitting (enabling inelastic scattering), and a first-order phase transition; performs parameter-space scan constrained by LZ kinematics, Planck relic density, GW observability (μARES/THEIA), and cosmological/astrophysical/direct-detection bounds.

Result: Identifies viable parameter space where the scalar VEV (symmetry-breaking scale) simultaneously accounts for the LZ event’s recoil energy and significance, yields Ωh² ≈ 0.12, and predicts stochastic GW spectra detectable at μARES and THEIA, all while satisfying LHC, stellar cooling, BBN, and XENONnT constraints.

Conclusion: The U(1)_X hidden-sector model with spontaneous symmetry breaking provides a coherent multi-messenger framework unifying LZ’s potential DM signal, thermal relic production, and observable GWs from a cosmic first-order phase transition.

Abstract: The LZ collaboration has recently reported a single nuclear-recoil event at $E_R=248\pm23_\text{stat}\pm23_\text{sys}$ keV, observed in a 2.84 tonne-year exposure with a local significance of $3.4σ$, pointing towards an inelastic dark matter (DM) interpretation. We explain this event within a gauged $U(1)_X$ hidden-sector model, spontaneously broken by a singlet scalar whose vacuum expectation value (VEV) gives rise to: \textit{(i)} gauge boson mass, \textit{(ii)} a small Majorana mass for the vector-like singlet fermion, the DM candidate, charged under the new symmetry, which splits the fermion into two pseudo-Dirac states (mass splitting proportional to the scalar VEV) thus resulting in a purely off-diagonal vector current, \textit{(iii)} the frequency and the amplitude of the GW signal arising from the hidden sector symmetry breaking via first-order phase transition (FOPT). The symmetry-breaking scale thus provides a multi-messenger probe connecting terrestrial direct-detection kinematics at the LZ and early-universe FOPT thermodynamics while obtaining thermal DM relic abundance. We delineate the viable parameter space compatible with the LZ event, correct relic density, and detectable GW signals in the low frequency regimes at future interferometers like $μ{\rm ARES}$ and THEIA while satisfying existing cosmological, astrophysical, and terrestrial bounds.

</details>


### [37] [Parton distribution functions and strong coupling constant: MSHT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16573)
*T. Cridge,L. A. Harland-Lang,M. Reader,R. S. Thorne*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews the MSHT family of global parton distribution function (PDF) fits, detailing methodology, theoretical inputs, data sets, and recent advances in QCD/EW corrections, parameterisation validation, error treatment, and determination of the strong coupling constant α_s.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a comprehensive overview of the MSHT global PDF fitting framework, assess impacts of recent theoretical and methodological improvements, and evaluate implications for precision QCD phenomenology and fundamental parameter extraction (e.g., α_s).

Method: Global QCD analysis using χ² minimisation; flexible functional forms for PDF parameterisation; inclusion of state-of-the-art N³LO QCD and NLO electroweak corrections; systematic treatment of experimental uncertainties including 'errors on errors'; closure tests to validate parameterisation adequacy.

Result: Improved PDF uncertainties and stability due to higher-order corrections and refined uncertainty propagation; robust α_s determination consistent with world average; evidence for adequate parameterisation flexibility via closure tests; quantified impact of correlated systematic uncertainties on fit quality and PDFs.

Conclusion: The MSHT framework remains a leading tool for precision PDF determination; ongoing methodological refinements—especially in theory inputs, uncertainty modelling, and validation protocols—enhance reliability for LHC and future collider physics.

Abstract: We present an overview of the MSHT family of global PDF fits. We describe the underlying methodology, including parameterisation choice, theoretical inputs and datasets included in the fit. We describe the impact of recent theoretical advances in the inclusion of higher order QCD and EW corrections on the fit and their implications for phenomenology. We discuss recent methodological improvements and ongoing studies relating to closure tests of the parameterisation flexibility and the inclusion of errors on errors for experimental data. We in addition discuss recent results on the determination of the strong coupling from the MSHT fits.

</details>


### [38] [HEPTABench: A framework for portable and controlled agentic evaluations in high energy physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18454)
*Tony Menzo,Alexander Roman,George T. Fleming,Sergei Gleyzer,Konstantin Matchev,Stephen Mrenna*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: HEPTABench is a modular, reproducible benchmarking framework for evaluating agentic systems in high-energy physics, enabling controlled, independent variation of models, tools, harnesses, and domain capabilities; it demonstrates that domain-specific tools significantly improve numerical accuracy, trial-to-trial consistency, and success rates across diverse HEP tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable rigorous, repeatable, and controlled evaluation of agentic systems in high-energy physics—specifically to isolate and quantify the impact of individual components (e.g., models, tools, harnesses) on scientific capability and reliability.

Method: Design and implementation of HEPTABench: a portable framework featuring independently configurable tasks, scaffolding, models, providers, harnesses, and domain tools; integration of HEP-specific benchmark tasks, scientific rubrics, versioned tool interfaces, and comprehensive logging of experiment specs, agent trajectories, and artifacts; execution of two empirical benchmark campaigns—one on forward long-lived-particle reach calculation, another on symbolic two-body decay tasks.

Result: In the long-lived-particle campaign, adding HEP domain tools improved numerical accuracy and reduced trial-to-trial dispersion across all four coding-agent systems. In the symbolic decay campaign, domain tools increased Claude Haiku 4.5’s success rate from 8% to 79%, while GPT-5.5 achieved 100% success with built-in tools alone.

Conclusion: Systematic, component-isolated benchmarking via HEPTABench reveals that domain-specific tool integration is a key lever for enhancing both accuracy and reliability of agentic systems in scientific reasoning tasks—enabling evidence-based development and deployment of physics-aware AI agents.

Abstract: Quantifying the capability and reliability of agentic systems in high-energy physics (HEP) requires repeated evaluations under controlled configurations. Establishing whether changes to models, tools, or harnesses improve performance also requires these components to be varied independently. We present the High Energy Physics Tool-and-Agent Benchmarking (HEPTABench) framework for portable and controlled evaluations of agentic systems, with separately configurable tasks, scaffolding, models, providers, harnesses, and domain capabilities. The framework combines HEP benchmark tasks and scientific rubrics with experiment execution and versioned tool interfaces. To support inspection, reproducibility, and post-hoc grading, the framework prioritizes recording and reporting complete experiment specifications, agent trajectories, and experiment artifacts. We demonstrate the framework in two benchmark campaigns. The first campaign evaluates a forward long-lived-particle reach calculation across four frontier coding-agent systems, comparing their built-in coding tools with the same tools supplemented by HEP domain tools. We show that including these HEP domain tools improves numerical accuracy and reduces trial-to-trial dispersion for every tested system. In a complementary campaign covering eight symbolic two-body decay tasks, we show that adding symbolic domain tools increases the fraction of successful trials for Claude Haiku 4.5 from $8\%$ to $79\%$, while GPT-5.5 succeeds in every trial with built-in tools alone. Through systematic evaluation of agentic systems, HEPTABench enables the identification of factors that improve the accuracy and reliability of their scientific work.

</details>


### [39] [From Earth to Mars and Beyond: AI-Driven Cosmic-Ray Validation and Prediction with CorsikaAgent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16607)
*Zhuo Chen,Zhizheng Zhao,Jinhong Shen,Leyun Gao,Cheng-en Liu,Qite Li,Zijian Wang,Qiang Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces CorsikaAgent, an auditable LLM-based system for configuring, executing, and validating cosmic-ray air-shower simulations (using CORSIKA), and applies it to predict sea-level and planetary (Mars, Venus) muon fluxes—reproducing benchmarks on Mars and delivering the first ab initio surface muon flux prediction for Venus.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable auditable, reproducible, and transferable high-energy atmospheric particle simulations—especially for unmeasured environments like Venus—by integrating large language models with rigorous simulation provenance and physics modeling.

Method: Uses CORSIKA with proton primaries (Papini solar-minimum spectrum), UrQMD (low-E) + Sibyll 2.3e or QGSJET-III (high-E); develops CorsikaAgent—a traceable LLM-driven pipeline that logs all inputs (steering cards, seeds, logs, scripts) and autonomously reconfigures simulations for different planetary atmospheres and fields.

Result: CorsikaAgent reproduces sea-level muon spectra consistent with data and analytic models; matches CORSIKA 8 Mars benchmark; delivers first-ever predicted surface muon flux for Venus; achieves internal consistency between direct calculation and agent-generated results at current statistical resolution.

Conclusion: CorsikaAgent establishes a new paradigm for auditable, autonomous, and environment-transferable cosmic-ray simulation—extending from reproducibility to first-principles prediction—though physical precision remains limited by proton-only primaries, low high-momentum statistics, and incomplete systematic uncertainty quantification.

Abstract: We calculate the sea-level cosmic-ray muon flux with CORSIKA and develop CorsikaAgent, an auditable large-language-model-based system for configuring, executing, and validating air-shower simulations, and for predicting particle fluxes in unexplored environments. The physics calculation uses proton primaries described by the solar-minimum spectrum of Papini et al., with UrQMD at low interaction energies and either Sibyll 2.3e or QGSJET-III at high energies. The predicted momentum spectra at approximately 0 and 75 degrees are compared with experimental measurements of the atmospheric muon flux and with commonly used analytic parametrizations. The two interaction-model configurations give similar spectra and describe the principal momentum and zenith-angle dependences of the data. The agent independently reconstructs the same observable through a different sampling strategy and keeps every steering card, executable, seed, log, and analysis script bound to the result it produced; its output is consistent with the direct calculation at the level resolved by the present comparison. Transferred to Mars, the workflow reproduces the CORSIKA 8 benchmark, and transferred to Venus it produces the first prediction of the surface muon flux, an environment for which no measurement exists. The agent thus extends the workflow from auditable reproduction to first-principles prediction: it autonomously reconfigures the simulation(atmosphere, magnetic field, electric field)for new environments, while the proton-only primary model, limited high-momentum statistics, and an incomplete systematic-uncertainty treatment bound the physical precision that can currently be claimed.

</details>


### [40] [Two-pion source analysis in small to large systems at SPS energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16786)
*Barnabás Pórfy,Zsombor Marcell Rácz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses UrQMD simulations to study two-pion femtoscopic source distributions in light (Be+Be) and intermediate-mass (Xe+La, Ar+Sc) nuclear collisions, fitting them with Lévy-stable functions; it confirms Lévy parametrization validity across systems and provides a hadronic baseline for NA61/SHINE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a purely hadronic reference for NA61/SHINE femtoscopic measurements by systematically studying Lévy-stable source shapes across varying collision systems (light to intermediate), energies, and kinematics using UrQMD cascade simulations.

Method: UrQMD model in cascade mode is used to simulate central $^7$Be+$^9$Be and $^{129}$Xe+$^{139}$La collisions; kinematic selections mimic experimental conditions; one-dimensional Lévy fits are performed on two-pion correlation functions; source parameters (Lévy exponent α, radius R) are analyzed vs. pair transverse mass, collision energy, and system size; comparison includes Ar+Sc data.

Result: Simulated pion pair-source distributions are consistently well described by Lévy-stable functions across all studied systems and kinematic ranges; source parameters show systematic dependence on transverse mass and system size; results align qualitatively with Ar+Sc, reinforcing universality of Lévy description in hadronic scenarios.

Conclusion: Lévy-stable parametrization offers a robust, unified phenomenological description of pion emission geometry in small-to-intermediate hadronic collision systems; the UrQMD-based results constitute a valuable hadronic baseline for disentangling QGP-related effects in NA61/SHINE and related experiments.

Abstract: Over the past decade, femtoscopic measurements using Lévy-stable source functions have been carried out in collision systems ranging from small to large at SPS, RHIC, and LHC energies. In recent years, these have been accompanied by a number of phenomenological studies of the same systems and energies. We contribute to this line of work by investigating the shape of two-pion pair-source distributions in central $^7$Be+$^9$Be and $^{129}$Xe+$^{139}$La collisions simulated with the UrQMD model in cascade mode. To make the comparison with measurements meaningful, kinematic selections similar to the experimental ones are applied to the simulated events and particles. One-dimensional Lévy fits are performed, and the resulting source parameters are studied as functions of pair transverse mass, collision energy, and system size. We find that the simulated sources are well described by Lévy distributions in all systems considered, which offers a common phenomenological picture of the space-time structure of pion emission. Finally, we compare our findings with results from an intermediate system, Ar+Sc. The results serve as a purely hadronic reference for past and upcoming femtoscopic measurements of the NA61/SHINE experiment.

</details>


### [41] [Chiral partners of strange doubly heavy baryons: Mass relations and decay predictions after the 2026 LHCb discoveries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16841)
*Maciej A. Nowak,Ismail Zahed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses recent LHCb measurements of doubly heavy baryons and beauty-strange mesons to construct experimentally constrained chiral parity multiplets for strange doubly heavy baryons (ccs, bbs, and conditionally bcs), predict their masses and decay properties using chiral doubling, heavy-quark–diquark relations, and meson-to-baryon parity-gap transfer, and proposes targeted searches at LHCb for narrow states via Ω_QQ π⁰ and Ω_QQ γ channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile new LHCb observations (Ω_cc⁺, Ξ_cc⁺, B_s0*(5700)⁰) with chiral symmetry expectations for doubly heavy baryons, test theoretical predictions (e.g., Ma–Harada), and build predictive, experimentally anchored parity doublets for ccs and bbs systems.

Method: Combines experimental mass inputs (LHCb 2026 measurements), chiral doubling formalism, heavy-quark–diquark hyperfine relation, meson-to-baryon parity-gap transfer (using D_s and B_s chiral gaps), E1 reduced-width systematics, kaon selection rules, and width estimation via calibration to published meson calculations (Bardeen–Eichten–Hill; Harada–Rho–Sasaki).

Result: Predicts narrow negative-parity ccs states near 4.075 GeV and 4.183 GeV; determines bbs partner masses relative to unknown ground state, conditional on B_s0*(5700)⁰’s chiral assignment; identifies universal reduced single-pion widths, E1 width ratio 1:8:4:5, and a parameter-free kaon selection rule; estimates all predicted multiplet members as narrow (< detector resolution).

Conclusion: The analysis yields consistent, testable chiral multiplets for strange doubly heavy baryons; proposed LHCb searches in Ω_QQ π⁰/γ channels can resolve masses and decay patterns even for intrinsically narrow states, offering a clean probe of chiral symmetry and heavy-quark dynamics in QCD.

Abstract: Three spectacular LHCb measurements in 2026---the observations of $Ω_{cc}^{+}$, $Ξ_{cc}^{+}$, and the beauty-strange structure $B_{s0}^{*}(5700)^0$---make possible an experimentally constrained analysis of chiral partners of strange doubly heavy baryons. The doubly charmed masses test the earlier Ma--Harada prediction and support a compact, internally S-wave $cc$ color source. We combine this comparison with the heavy-quark--diquark hyperfine relation and with chiral gaps from the theory of chiral doublers, to construct consistent $ccs$ and $bbs$ parity multiplets and a conditional extension to $bcs$. Under meson-to-baryon parity-gap transfer, the $349$ MeV input from the $D_s$ sector gives negative-parity $ccs$ targets near $4.075$ and $4.183$ GeV. The separate $332$ MeV input from the $B_s$ sector fixes the $bbs$ partner masses relative to their unknown ground-state mass, conditional on the new beauty-strange state's chiral-partner assignment. We relate the differences of hyperfine splittings in the two parity sectors of mesons and baryons, and identify equal reduced single-pion widths, an E1 reduced-width ratio $1:8:4:5$, and a kaon selection rule whose leading predictions contain no new transition parameters. Absolute widths are estimated by matching the published Bardeen--Eichten--Hill and Harada--Rho--Sasaki meson calculations to a common convention. In the reference calculation, all members of the predicted $ccs$ and $bbs$ multiplets are narrow. In the proposed $Ω_{QQ}π^0$ and $Ω_{QQ}γ$ searches at LHCb, these states are expected to produce peaks dominated by detector resolution, allowing their masses and decay patterns to be investigated even when their intrinsic widths remain unresolved. The upper charm state is above kaon threshold but protected in the spectator limit.

</details>


### [42] [Invisible Decays of the $Z$ in Portal Matter Models at Tera-Z](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16843)
*Thomas G. Rizzo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how vector-like fermionic portal matter (PM) fields in an E6-inspired vector portal model can generate loop-induced contributions to the Z boson's invisible width (ΔΓ_inv), potentially detectable at future Tera-Z experiments, while evading current constraints; it contrasts these with suppressed tree-level contributions from dark Higgs mixing or dark photon kinetic mixing, and shows that models with non-zero weak isospin components in the dark Higgs already conflict with existing ΔΓ_inv limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether new physics from a dark sector—specifically via portal matter fields—can produce observable, model-dependent contributions to the Z boson’s invisible width (ΔΓ_inv), serving as an indirect probe of dark sector interactions beyond conventional kinetic mixing or Higgs mixing scenarios.

Method: Analyzes a simplified E6-like vector portal model featuring vector-like fermionic portal matter fields that carry both SM and dark sector quantum numbers; computes loop-induced ΔΓ_inv contributions from PM fields, compares with experimental bounds (LEP, LHC), and evaluates sensitivity at future Tera-Z; examines competing mechanisms including tree-level dark Higgs–SM Higgs mixing, Z–dark photon kinetic/mass mixing, and isospin-violating dark Higgs scenarios.

Result: PM loops generate ΔΓ_inv within current experimental limits yet accessible at Tera-Z; tree-level contributions from SM–dark Higgs mixing and Z–dark photon mixing yield ΔΓ_inv too small for detection; models with T3 ≠ 0 dark Higgs components violate existing ΔΓ_inv bounds without tuning.

Conclusion: Future precision measurements of ΔΓ_inv at Tera-Z will provide powerful indirect constraints on portal matter dynamics—especially loop-level effects decoupled from kinetic mixing—and guide viable dark sector model building.

Abstract: Searches for new contributions to the invisible width of the $Z$, $ΔΓ_{inv}$, can be used to provide model-dependent constraints on new physics that may arise from an unknown dark sector. In the vector portal setup considered here it is shown that loops of portal matter (PM) fields, having both Standard Model (SM) and dark sector quantum numbers, can lead to new contributions to $ΔΓ_{inv}$ that satisfy current experimental constraints while being simultaneously accessible to measurements at the future Tera-Z. Such PM fields, which here take the form of vector-like fermions, are well known to be able to induce kinetic mixing (KM) between the SM and dark photons (DP) at low energies. This analysis is performed within a simplified version of a previously examined `$E_6-$like' setup that predicts a calculable value for the strength of KM in a phenomenologically interesting range. On the other hand, owing to the constraint on the invisible branching fraction of the Higgs, the tree-level mixing of the SM and a weak isosinglet dark Higgs fields yields a value of $ΔΓ_{inv}$ which is far too small to be observable. A similar result holds for the shift in the $Z$'s invisible width induced by the mass and/or KM of the $Z$ with the light DP which then subsequently decays to dark matter. On the other hand, PM models wherein the light dark Higgs carries a significant admixture of a $T_3\neq 0$ weak isospin component are shown to already violate existing $ΔΓ_{inv}$ constraints in the absence of parameter tuning. Future measurements of $ΔΓ_{inv}$ can thus play an important role in providing indirect constraints on dark sector interactions and PM model building, in particular, on PM loops not necessarily associated with the generation of KM of the DP with the corresponding SM field.

</details>


### [43] [Vortex structures in momentum distributions from multi-pair production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16976)
*Z. L. Li,R. Z. Jiang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies vortex structures in multi-particle-antiparticle momentum distributions generated from vacuum by time-varying uniform electric fields of arbitrary polarization, using Bogoliubov transformation; it reveals polarization-dependent vortex formation (linear → vortex–antivortex pairs; circular → stable central vortex; elliptical → coexisting vortices with breakdown of integer-charge scaling for fermions), offering theoretical foundation for topological control in strong-field QED.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and characterize the topological vortex structures emerging in the momentum-space distributions of multiple electron-positron (or bosonic) pairs produced from vacuum under strong, time-varying electric fields — a key signature of quantum vacuum structure and nonperturbative QED dynamics.

Method: Bogoliubov transformation applied to quantized scalar (bosonic) and Dirac (fermionic) fields in a spatially uniform, time-dependent electric field with linear, circular, or elliptical polarization; analysis of nodal structure and topological charge of momentum-space wavefunctions/amplitudes for multi-pair production.

Result: Linear polarization induces vortex–antivortex pair creation upon increasing field strength; circular polarization yields a persistent central vortex whose topological charge scales as integer multiples of the single-pair value (bosons) or of the zero-spin-projection single-pair value (fermions); elliptical polarization supports coexistence of discrete and central vortices, but fermionic two-pair amplitudes—being determinantal with spin-channel separation in momentum space—break the integer-multiple relation.

Conclusion: Vortex topology in multi-pair production is highly sensitive to field polarization and particle statistics; the observed patterns reflect underlying symmetries and quantum interference in strong-field vacuum decay, enabling potential topological control of pair production in ultra-intense laser experiments.

Abstract: The vortex structures in the momentum distributions of multiple particle-antiparticle pairs produced from the vacuum by a spatially uniform, time-varying electric field with arbitrary polarization are investigated using the Bogoliubov transformation. For linearly polarized fields, as the field strength increases, the nodal lines in the multi-pair momentum distribution break up into vortex-antivortex pairs in both the bosonic and fermionic cases. Each vortex carries a topological charge that is an integer multiple of the single-pair vortex topological charge. For circular polarization, a central vortex appears at all field strengths. For bosons, its topological charge is an integer multiple of the single-pair vortex topological charge; for fermions, it is an integer multiple of the vortex topological charge for single-pair production with zero total spin projection along the field propagation direction. For elliptical polarization with suitable ellipticity, discrete vortices coexist with a central vortex. In this case, however, the integer-multiple relation for fermions generally fails because the two-pair production amplitude is a determinant and spin-channel contributions are separated in momentum space. These results provide a theoretical basis for understanding the vortex topology of multi-pair production in strong-field QED and for its potential control.

</details>


### [44] [Cosmic Colliders in Binary Blazars Probing Ultra-High-Energy Particle Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17033)
*Giorgio Galanti*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Binary blazars act as natural cosmic colliders, enabling proton-proton collisions at √s ~ 10¹⁰ GeV—six orders above the LHC—via misaligned relativistic jets, offering unique probes of asymptotic QCD, gluon saturation, Froissart unitarity, and BSM physics via detectable PeV–EeV neutrinos and sub-TeV γ-rays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Terrestrial colliders are limited to √s ≲ O(10⁵) GeV; astrophysical systems offering higher CM energies are needed to probe QCD at asymptotically high energies and potential BSM physics.

Method: Theoretical modeling of hadronic interactions in co-streaming proton beams from bound sub-parsec binary blazars with modest jet misalignments (θ_mis ~ 1°–6°), operating in optically thin (τ_pp ≪ 1) regime to ensure clean beam-beam collisions and continuous √s scanning.

Result: Predicts CM energies up to √s ~ 10¹⁰ GeV; associated secondary PeV–EeV neutrinos and sub-TeV γ-rays are detectable by current/planned observatories (LHAASO, CTAO, IceCube, GRAND).

Conclusion: Sub-parsec binary blazars constitute naturally occurring 'Cosmic Colliders' that extend collider physics to energy frontiers inaccessible on Earth, enabling tests of fundamental QCD limits and BSM phenomena.

Abstract: While terrestrial colliders and fixed-target cosmic-ray interactions are bounded at center-of-mass (CM) energies $\sqrt{s} \lesssim \mathcal{O}(10^5)\,\text{GeV}$, we demonstrate that bound sub-parsec binary blazars form broad-band, beam-beam hadronic ``Cosmic Colliders'', overcoming such limitation. Driven by modest jet misalignments ($θ_{\rm mis} \sim 1^\circ - 6^\circ$), co-streaming proton beams collide at CM energies reaching $\sqrt{s} \sim 10^{10}\,\text{GeV}$ - six orders of magnitude above the Large Hadron Collider. Operating in a strictly optically thin regime ($τ_{pp} \ll 1$), the system performs a continuous CM energy scan, probing asymptotic QCD scaling, gluon saturation, and Froissart unitarity, while opening a possible high-sensitivity window into physics beyond the Standard Model. Secondary PeV-EeV neutrinos and sub-TeV $γ$-rays fall directly within reach of current and planned observatories such as LHAASO, CTAO, IceCube, and GRAND, establishing binary blazars as laboratories for physics at the ultimate energy limit.

</details>


### [45] [Decay-Assisted Leptogenesis during Preheating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17084)
*Wenbin Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits non-thermal leptogenesis during preheating using quantum Boltzmann equations, showing that accounting for concurrent right-handed neutrino (RHN) production and decay—enabled by fermionic saturation and depletion of occupation numbers—enhances the final baryon asymmetry by up to two orders of magnitude compared to sequential (production-then-decay) scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the accuracy of non-thermal leptogenesis predictions during preheating by moving beyond the oversimplified assumption of instantaneous or sequential RHN production followed by decay, and instead incorporating quantum-statistical effects (e.g., Pauli blocking) and real-time dynamics via quantum Boltzmann equations.

Method: Solves quantum Boltzmann equations for fermionic right-handed neutrinos coupled to the oscillating inflaton condensate during preheating; explicitly models time-dependent production (via parametric resonance/spontaneous particle creation) and out-of-equilibrium CP-violating decays, respecting fermionic occupation number limits (n ≤ 1).

Result: RHN occupation numbers saturate near unity after few inflaton oscillations due to Pauli blocking; subsequent decays reduce n, enabling further production; this feedback loop increases total lepton asymmetry; final baryon asymmetry is enhanced by ~10² relative to the 'production-complete-then-decay' approximation.

Conclusion: Real-time coupling between production and decay of fermionic right-handed neutrinos during preheating significantly amplifies leptogenesis efficiency; quantum-statistical effects are essential for quantitative predictions of baryogenesis in resonant preheating scenarios.

Abstract: We revisit the non-thermal leptogenesis mechanism during preheating using the quantum Boltzmann equations. This framework allows us to study the production and decay of heavy right-handed neutrinos in a consistent manner. Due to their fermionic nature, right-handed neutrino production can saturate after a few inflaton oscillations when their occupation numbers approach unity. Right-handed neutrino decays can deplete their occupation numbers, allowing more particles to be produced and generating a larger lepton asymmetry. Compared with the case where right-handed neutrinos decay only after production has finished, we find that the final baryon asymmetry can be enhanced by two orders of magnitude.

</details>


### [46] [MadNIS, Ready for Production at LO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17177)
*Theo Heimel,Olivier Mattelaer,Daniele Massaro,Ramon Winterhalder*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends MadNIS—neural multi-channel importance sampling—to full proton-proton processes in a production-ready MadGraph setup, achieving up to 250× event-generation speed-up across diverse LHC processes without process-specific tuning; it analyzes scaling with training time and final-state multiplicity, and establishes MadNIS as the new default for leading-order phase-space integration and event generation in MadGraph.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accelerate Monte Carlo event generation for complex LHC processes without sacrificing accuracy or requiring manual, process-specific optimization of sampling strategies.

Method: Extends neural multi-channel importance sampling (MadNIS) to full pp → X processes within a production-grade MadGraph framework; employs neural networks trained on integral estimation tasks to learn efficient phase-space mappings; benchmarks across multi-jet, V+jets, top-quark, and Higgs production; analyzes speed-up vs. training cost and final-state multiplicity.

Result: Achieves up to 250× speed-up in event generation across diverse Standard Model processes; demonstrates scalability with final-state multiplicity; quantifies amortization point of training cost; shows robustness without per-process hyperparameter tuning.

Conclusion: Neural importance sampling via MadNIS is production-ready for full pp processes at leading order; it delivers substantial, generalizable acceleration and will replace conventional methods as the default in MadGraph.

Abstract: We extend neural multi-channel importance sampling in MadNIS to full proton-proton processes in a production-ready event-generation setup. We benchmark a wide range of LHC processes, including multi-jet, vector-boson, top-quark, and Higgs production, and obtain event-generation speed-ups of up to a factor of 250 without process-specific hyperparameter tuning. We quantify how the speed-up scales with training time and final-state multiplicity, and when the training cost amortizes. MadNIS will become the default for phase-space integration and event generation at leading order in MadGraph.

</details>


### [47] [Dark Matter Stability from residual flavour symmetries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17210)
*Giacomo Landini,Simone Marciano,Federico Mescia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a model-independent framework where dark matter (DM) stability arises naturally from residual discrete Z_N symmetries—left over after spontaneous breaking of continuous (Abelian/non-Abelian) flavour symmetries—eliminating the need for ad hoc stabilizers. A concrete SU(2)_F ⊗ U(1)_F Froggatt-Nielsen model reproduces lepton masses and PMNS mixing, with the lightest sterile neutrino as stable DM. DM is produced via freeze-in through higher-dimensional operators suppressed by cutoff Λ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve two fundamental problems simultaneously—the flavour puzzle (origin of fermion mass hierarchies/mixings) and the origin/stability/production of dark matter—without introducing arbitrary stabilising symmetries.

Method: Constructs a model-independent mechanism linking DM stability to residual discrete symmetries from spontaneous breaking of continuous flavour symmetries; builds an explicit prototype based on SU(2)_F ⊗ U(1)_F Froggatt-Nielsen symmetry; computes charged lepton masses and PMNS matrix; identifies lightest sterile neutrino as DM candidate; performs freeze-in phenomenology including higher-dimensional operator-mediated production suppressed by cutoff scale Λ.

Result: Demonstrates that residual Z_N symmetries from flavour symmetry breaking automatically ensure DM stability; shows successful reproduction of observed lepton mass spectrum and PMNS mixing in the prototype model; finds viable freeze-in DM production consistent with relic density constraints via dimension-5 and dimension-6 operators.

Conclusion: Continuous flavour symmetries offer a unified, self-consistent framework explaining both the flavour structure of the Standard Model and the origin, stability, and production mechanism of dark matter—rendering ad hoc DM stabilisers unnecessary.

Abstract: We propose a model-independent framework in which Dark Matter stability emerges naturally from residual discrete $\mathbb{Z}_N$ symmetries left unbroken by the spontaneous breaking of continuous flavour symmetries, both Abelian and non-Abelian. No ad hoc stabilizing symmetry is required, as Dark Matter stability is an inherent consequence of the underlying flavour dynamics. As an explicit example, we construct a prototype model based on a $\text{SU}(2)_\text{F} \otimes \text{U}(1)_\text{F}$ Froggatt-Nielsen symmetry, which successfully reproduces the observed charged lepton mass spectrum and the PMNS mixing matrix, while identifying the lightest sterile neutrino as a stable Dark Matter candidate. Furthermore, we study the Dark Matter phenomenology within our model in the freeze-in regime, including production channels mediated by higher-dimensional operators suppressed by the cutoff scale $Λ$. Our analysis shows that continuous flavour symmetries can provide a unified, self-consistent framework addressing both the flavour puzzle and the origin, stability, and production of Dark Matter.

</details>


### [48] [Revisiting Critical Higgs Inflation: Electroweak Couplings, CMB Observables, and Perturbative Unitarity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17211)
*Sarah R. Geller,Sol Gutierrez-Lara,David I. Kaiser,Thomas Steingasser*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper re-examines critical Higgs inflation—where the SM Higgs drives inflation near a potential inflection point—and finds that no parameter space simultaneously satisfies electroweak constraints, CMB observables, and perturbative unitarity; thus, predictions require UV completion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the viability of critical Higgs inflation by jointly enforcing consistency with electroweak-scale data, CMB observations, and perturbative unitarity bounds—highlighting tension between low- and high-energy constraints.

Method: Systematic analysis of the Higgs effective potential near a critical (inflection-point) regime; evaluation of electroweak constraints at inflationary scales; matching of CMB observables (ns, r) via tuning of the nonminimal coupling ξ in both metric and Palatini gravity; assessment of unitarity violation scale as proxy for UV sensitivity.

Result: Electroweak constraints depend on inflationary-scale potential parameters (updated vs. prior literature); CMB compatibility is achievable for any potential shape by adjusting ξ in either gravity formulation; however, no region satisfies all three criteria simultaneously—i.e., no fully self-consistent, UV-insensitive critical Higgs inflation scenario exists.

Conclusion: Critical Higgs inflation remains logically possible but is not predictively robust without specifying its UV completion; observable predictions (e.g., tensor-to-scalar ratio, spectral index) are inherently sensitive to unknown high-scale physics.

Abstract: We revisit the scenario of critical Higgs inflation, in which inflation is driven by the Standard Model Higgs boson with parameters chosen close to a critical point, giving rise to an inflection point in its potential. After reviewing this model, we perform a systematic study of its viability centered around three criteria: (1) consistency with electroweak-scale measurements, (2) ability to reproduce the cosmic microwave background (CMB) observables, and (3) sensitivity of the model predictions to unknown UV physics, based on a breakdown of perturbative unitarity. We first show that compliance with electroweak-scale measurements is controlled by the parameters in the Higgs potential at inflationary scales, also updating the literature values. We then demonstrate how for any choice of potential parameters, agreement with the CMB observables can be achieved through the choice of the nonminimal coupling, in both metric and Palatini formulations of gravity. However, we also find that there exist no regions of parameter space, in either the metric or Palatini formulations, in which compliance with all three physical criteria is possible. While this does not exclude the possibility of the Higgs field driving inflation, it means that predictions for any observable are in principle sensitive to the details of the model's UV completion.

</details>


### [49] [What Do Event Generators Compute? Flux Functionals from Detector Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17213)
*Jack Holguin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces an operator-based, non-perturbative formulation of event generation in QCD, defining a hadronic flux probability distribution directly from detector operators on asymptotic hadronic states — replacing the conventional parton-shower-centric approach. It derives a corresponding parton shower source functional (infrared/collinear safe, calorimeter-observable inclusive), computes its one-loop logarithmic structure in e+e− annihilation, reveals sensitivity to recoil schemes and multi-scale dynamics, and extends the framework to conformal theories like N=4 SYM for non-perturbative insight.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between physical (hadronic) observables measured by detectors and the unobservable partonic foundations of current event generators; to establish a first-principles, non-perturbative, detector-centric definition of what an event generator must compute precisely—including full hadronisation effects.

Method: Constructs a hadronic flux probability distribution using detector operators acting on asymptotic hadronic states; defines an associated event generator source functional; restricts to energy flow to obtain the parton shower source functional; performs one-loop computation in e+e− annihilation to extract logarithmic structure and recoil dependence; analyzes scale structure and factorisation limitations via a toy model; generalises to conformal field theories.

Result: Derives an infrared- and collinear-safe parton shower source functional encoding all calorimetric observables; shows its one-loop logarithms depend explicitly on kinematic recoil and thus on parton shower recoil scheme; demonstrates that its logarithms involve a continuum of low scales (per resolved direction) and cannot be fully resummed in standard high-scale-only evolution factorisations.

Conclusion: A detector- and hadron-state-based operator formalism provides a more fundamental, non-perturbative foundation for event generation than parton showers alone; enables rigorous assessment of shower accuracy via the source functional; opens pathways to study event generation in non-QCD theories (e.g., N=4 SYM) for deeper non-perturbative understanding.

Abstract: Every physical prediction of an event generator is a statement about the hadronic fluxes that can be recorded by particle detectors. By contrast, the formal backbone of modern event-generator precision is provided by the unobservable partonic final states of the parton shower, with hadronisation included as a model. We construct the probability distribution of the hadronic fluxes non-perturbatively, directly from detector operators acting on asymptotic hadronic states, and encode it in an event generator source functional. This distribution contains every measurement a detector can make, and is therefore what a generator must compute with precision, including the effects of hadronisation. Restricting the functional to the energy flow yields the parton shower source functional, which is infrared and collinear safe and contains every calorimetric observable, making it a natural object against which to assess parton-shower accuracy. We compute its logarithmic structure at one loop in $e^+e^-$ annihilation, finding it directly sensitive to kinematic recoil and hence to the recoil scheme of a parton shower. The parton shower functional depends on a continuum of low scales, one for each resolved direction, and in a toy model its logarithms cannot be entirely resummed within a factorisation whose evolution derives only from the high-scale physics. Our operator-based formulation opens the door to the study of event-generator-like systems in theories beyond QCD, including conformal theories such as $N=4$ super-Yang-Mills theory, and thereby offers a new route to non-perturbative insight into event generation.

</details>


### [50] [Non-accumulation of background-enhanced neutrino forces in proto-neutron stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17346)
*Himanshu Bora,Anupam Deka,Debajyoti Dutta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the enhanced two-neutrino exchange potential in a directional neutrino background—such as inside a proto-neutron star (PNS)—leads to a macroscopic Hartree contribution to the equation of state; it finds no such growth due to angular averaging and oscillatory kernel suppression, concluding the effect is negligible for PNS structure or pulsar kicks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the vacuum two-neutrino exchange potential, which becomes longer-ranged (1/r) in a directional neutrino background, yields a macroscopic Hartree contribution in dense astrophysical environments like proto-neutron stars.

Method: Analytical mean-field analysis of the spin-independent and spin-dependent (parity-conserving and parity-violating) components of the two-neutrino exchange potential under PNS-like neutrino background conditions; includes angular averaging and examination of kernel structure.

Result: No macroscopic Hartree growth occurs: spin-independent contributions saturate due to monopole projection; spin-dependent terms also fail to scale with system size under PNS mean-field assumptions; suppression arises from angular and oscillatory structure—not screening.

Conclusion: The enhanced pairwise neutrino potential does not produce a significant bulk interaction in proto-neutron stars; thus, it is irrelevant for the PNS equation of state and related phenomena such as pulsar kicks within the Standard Model.

Abstract: In a neutrino background, the vacuum two-neutrino exchange potential $V \propto G_F^2 /r^5$ acts as a much longer-ranged interaction, scaling as $V\sim1/r$ for a directional background. A proto-neutron star (PNS) hosts one of the densest astrophysical neutrino environments known, and naturally provides a setting in which to test whether this pairwise enhancement produces a macroscopic Hartree contribution. We show that this does not happen. In the spin-independent sector, averaging over pair orientations projects the background onto its angular monopole. The bulk Hartree contribution then saturates instead of growing with system size. We also find that the spin-dependent parity-conserving and parity-violating terms do not generate macroscopic growth under the PNS mean-field conditions considered here. Thus, although the pair potential can be strongly enhanced, the resulting bulk interaction remains very small. The suppression comes from the angular and oscillatory structure of the kernel, not from screening. Within the Standard Model mechanism considered here, the effect is not important for the PNS equation of state or for events like pulsar kicks.

</details>


### [51] [QGrAT: Quantum Grade-Aware Transformer for Jet Tagging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17351)
*Pallavi Bhardwaj,Michael Spannowsky*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: QGrAT is a Lorentz-invariant quantum transformer for top jet tagging that encodes spacetime geometry via Clifford algebra grades and introduces explicit three-particle correlations via an auxiliary qubit; it achieves competitive AUC (0.944) and significantly improved background rejection (+79% at 50% signal efficiency) over ablated variants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To design physically grounded quantum jet taggers by embedding Lorentz symmetry and the three-prong hadronic top decay structure directly into quantum circuit architecture, addressing the lack of geometric priors in existing quantum ML models for collider physics.

Method: QGrAT uses grade-specific geometric features from constituent four-momenta encoded in the spacetime Clifford algebra $C\ell(1,3)$; grade-wise inner products define Lorentz-invariant pairwise attention couplings; an auxiliary qubit enables explicit three-particle interactions via products of Minkowski inner products; classification scores are obtained via trainable in-circuit readout; evaluated on Top Tagging benchmarks using state-vector simulation.

Result: With 12 qubits and 144 parameters (10 constituents, grades 0–3 + three-particle ancilla), QGrAT achieves AUC = 0.944 ± 0.002, background rejection $1/\varepsilon_B = 35.7 \pm 2.4$ at $\varepsilon_S = 0.50$, outperforming the no-ancilla variant (19.9) by +79%; grade-4 extension yields no consistent gain.

Conclusion: Incorporating spacetime geometry via Clifford algebra grades and explicit three-particle correlations is effective for quantum jet tagging; the three-particle ancilla is the principal driver of improved background rejection, establishing a principled framework for physics-informed quantum attention.

Abstract: Lorentz symmetry and the three-prong structure of hadronic top decays provide physical guidance for designing compact quantum jet taggers. We introduce QGrAT (Quantum Grade-Aware Transformer), a quantum transformer that incorporates these structures into its interactions between constituent qubits. Geometric features constructed from the constituent four-momenta are organised by the grades of the spacetime Clifford algebra $C\ell(1,3)$, and their grade-specific inner products determine the pairwise attention couplings. For fixed constituents and co-transforming reference vectors, these couplings are Lorentz invariant, although the component-wise input encoding makes the complete classifier frame dependent. An auxiliary qubit introduces an explicit three-particle interaction through products of pairwise Minkowski inner products, while a trainable in-circuit readout converts the resulting quantum state into a classification score. We evaluate the architecture in state-vector simulation on the Top Tagging benchmarks. With ten constituents per jet, the configuration using grades $0\text{-}3$ and the three-particle ancilla requires twelve qubits and $144$ trainable parameters, reaching AUC $0.944 \pm 0.002$ and background rejection $1/\varepsilon_B = 35.7 \pm 2.4\ (96.8 \pm 7.7)$ at signal efficiency $\varepsilon_S = 0.50\ (0.30)$. This performance is competitive with the L-GATr-Slim baseline at matched constituent and parameter budgets. Adding the three-particle ancilla raises background rejection at $50\%$ signal efficiency from $19.9$ to $35.7$, whereas extending the representation to the tested grade-$4$ feature gives no consistent improvement. The study establishes a framework for incorporating spacetime geometry into quantum attention and identifies explicit three-particle correlations as the principal source of improved background rejection among the architectural variations tested.

</details>


### [52] [Kernel moments and thermodynamic stability in nonlocal Nambu--Jona-Lasinio models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17622)
*Xiaozhu Yu,Xinyang Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates thermodynamic consistency and stability in a covariant two-flavor nonlocal Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model at zero chemical potential, using a homogeneous quark mean-field approximation. It introduces a large-source expansion to derive an entropy-sign criterion from Euclidean kernel moments, identifies a specific quartic-exponent kernel satisfying thermal positivity (positive entropy and heat capacity) for all T > 0, and shows that the static equation-of-state slope dP/dε > 1 does not imply causality or real-time constraints. The analysis serves as a diagnostic tool for mean-field functionals—not a kernel selection guide—and highlights limitations of mean-field approximations, especially regarding omitted thermal mesons and interpretation of static derivatives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
