Motivation: To assess thermodynamic consistency (e.g., positivity of entropy and heat capacity) and stability in nonlocal NJL models beyond standard local or momentum-cutoff treatments; to clarify whether static thermodynamic derivatives (e.g., dP/dε) reliably signal physical constraints like causality or thermal stability in mean-field approximations.

Method: Covariant two-flavor nonlocal NJL model in homogeneous quark mean-field approximation at μ = 0; controlled large-source expansion of the thermodynamic potential; entropy-sign criterion derived directly from Euclidean kernel moments (avoiding pole analysis); light-source fitting to pion observables and subtracted chiral condensate; numerical refitting with varying condensate inputs; construction and certification of counterexamples within the adopted matching domain.

Result: Every admissible positive Gaussian-mixture kernel yields negative total entropy above some source mass at fixed T > 0; one quartic-exponent kernel achieves globally positive entropy and heat capacity for all T > 0, yet violates dP/dε ≤ 1 on a finite temperature interval; numerical refits show thermal positivity weakens with larger condensate input; a certified counterexample proves uniform thermal positivity fails over the full matching domain; omission of thermal mesons affects low-T slope tests; dP/dε is decoupled from real-time front velocity.

Conclusion: Thermodynamic positivity is kernel- and parameter-sensitive, not generic in nonlocal NJL mean-field frameworks; the static EOS derivative dP/dε does not govern causality or real-time dynamics; results serve as diagnostics for mean-field functional validity rather than phenomenological kernel preference; inclusion of thermal mesonic degrees of freedom remains essential for quantitative thermodynamics.

Abstract: We study thermodynamic consistency and stability in a covariant two-flavor nonlocal Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model at zero chemical potential in the homogeneous quark mean-field approximation. A controlled large-source expansion gives a sufficient entropy-sign criterion directly from Euclidean kernel moments, without locating propagator poles. At fixed couplings and kernel, every admissible positive Gaussian mixture develops negative total entropy at sufficiently large source mass for each fixed positive temperature. In a separate light-source fit to pion observables and a subtracted condensate, one quartic-exponent kernel has positive entropy and heat capacity for all $T>0$, while $dP/dε=s/C_V$ exceeds unity on a finite interval. This constructive example separates thermal positivity from the static EOS slope test. Numerical refits show that the enhancement weakens with the condensate input, and a certified counterexample excludes uniform thermal positivity over the adopted matching domain. The results provide diagnostics of the mean-field functional, rather than a preferred phenomenological kernel. Omitted thermal mesons can change the low-temperature slope test, and the static derivative does not determine a real-time front velocity.

</details>


### [53] [Octupole parton structure of spin-3/2 targets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17624)
*Brandon B. Le,Dustin Keller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs the complete leading-twist gluon transverse-momentum-dependent distribution (TMD) basis for spin-3/2 targets, identifying 14 octupole-polarization-sensitive TMDs; proposes a clean experimental probe using heavy-quark-pair leptoproduction with octupole-flipped targets; derives corresponding quark TMD responses in SIDIS and Drell-Yan; confirms time-reversal symmetry preservation under renormalization; and links the formalism to polarized ⁷Li via collinear limits and nuclear spin-orbital correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To access novel quark and gluon correlations—beyond vector polarization and quadrupole alignment—in spin-3/2 hadrons via octupole polarization, which encodes higher-order spin-orbit and multi-parton dynamics inaccessible in spin-1/2 or spin-1 systems.

Method: Systematic construction of the leading-twist gluon TMD operator basis for spin-3/2 targets using Lorentz and gauge invariance, spin-tensor decomposition, and Wilson-line/color structure classification; derivation of process-dependent factorization theorems and angular modulations for octupole isolation in heavy-quark-pair leptoproduction, SIDIS, and Drell-Yan; application of time-reversal symmetry and renormalization-group consistency; mapping to nuclear structure via collinear limits and spin-orbital coupling in ⁷Li.

Result: A complete 32-component gluon TMD basis for spin-3/2 targets, of which 14 are octupole-sensitive (for number, helicity, and linear polarization); unambiguous experimental strategy to isolate and resolve all 14 gluon octupole TMDs at leading power using beam-helicity reversal and joint angular projections; analogous quark octupole TMDs derived for SIDIS and Drell-Yan; verification that time-reversal relations hold under symmetry-preserving evolution; explicit connection to ⁷Li nuclear structure through collinear reduction and spin-orbital correlation interpretation.

Conclusion: Octupole polarization in spin-3/2 targets provides a unique, high-sensitivity probe of three-dimensional gluon structure and multi-parton correlations; the proposed framework enables first-principles extraction of 14 new gluon TMDs, extends TMD phenomenology to higher-spin nuclei, and bridges QCD TMD physics with nuclear structure via polarized light nuclei like ⁷Li.

Abstract: In spin-$3/2$ targets, octupole polarization can reveal quark and gluon correlations beyond those probed by vector polarization and quadrupole alignment. Here, we construct the complete leading-twist gluon transverse-momentum-dependent distribution basis, with 32 independent coefficients for each specified Wilson-line and color structure. Fourteen of these describe octupole dependence in gluon number, helicity, and linear polarization. In heavy-quark-pair leptoproduction at small, nonzero pair imbalance, comparing target states with opposite octupoles and identical lower moments isolates the octupole contribution. Beam-helicity reversal and joint angular projections in target orientation and pair-imbalance azimuth can then separate all fourteen gluon octupole responses at leading-power Born order. We also derive the incoming-target quark responses in semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering and Drell-Yan production. Time-reversal relations between distributions remain valid under symmetry-preserving renormalization and evolution, while predictions across processes require factorization formulas connecting those distributions to scattering observables. Finally, we connect this framework to polarized $^{7}$Li through its collinear limits and nuclear spin-orbital correlations.

</details>


### [54] [A mechanism of the power-counting breaking in chiral perturbation theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17721)
*Ming-Ze Ma,Shao-Zhou Jiang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the anomalous size of O(p^6) corrections to πK scattering lengths in chiral perturbation theory, revealing that reducible two-loop, sunset, and vertex diagrams violate power counting; partial numerical cancellation occurs but is insufficient, and lower symmetry plus large kaon mass (minor role) explain the breakdown—unlike better-behaved ππ scattering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin of the anomalously large O(p^6) correction to πK scattering lengths, which undermines the convergence of chiral perturbation theory expansion.

Method: Detailed diagrammatic analysis of πK scattering amplitude at O(p^6), including separation and evaluation of tree-level, one-loop, and two-loop contributions (especially reducible two-loop, sunset, and vertex integrals); comparison with ππ scattering to assess symmetry and power-counting behavior.

Result: All O(p^6) components—reducible two-loop, sunset, and vertex integrals—violate chiral power counting; sunset and vertex contributions largely cancel numerically but residual violation remains; ππ scattering exhibits smaller power-counting violation due to higher symmetry; large kaon mass contributes weakly.

Conclusion: The poor convergence in πK scattering stems primarily from low-symmetry, many-loop diagrams violating power counting; such diagrams must be carefully treated—not ignored—in future chiral analyses.

Abstract: Chiral perturbation theory is a power tool for studying the low-energy strong interactions. In the conventional chiral expansion, according to the power counting rule, the higher-order corrections for physical observables are expected to decrease order by order as the chiral dimension increases. However, most previous study of $πK$ scattering lengths by different references showed that the $\mathcal{O}(p^6)$ correction to the scattering lengths is anomalously large and even exceeds the $\mathcal{O}(p^4)$ one. It poses a serious challenge to the convergence of chiral expansion. In order to explore the underlying origin of this phenomenon, the contributions of two-loop diagrams, one-loop diagrams and tree diagrams for the $πK$ scattering amplitude are detailed analyzed. The reducible two-loop integrals, sunset integrals and vertex integrals from the $\mathcal{O}(p^6)$ order all violate the power counting rule. The sunset and vertex integrals can be largely canceled numerically, but not enough. Furthermore, $πK$ scattering is compared with $ππ$ scattering, which seems not violate the power counting rule. The analysis indicates that $ππ$ scattering contains a higher symmetry, although each $\mathcal{O}(p^6)$-order part also violates the power counting rule, but the violation is much smaller than the one from $πK$ scattering. Large kaon mass also makes a contribution to the power-counting breaking, but it is not a major origin. Hence, it needs to pay more attention to the many-loop low-symmetry diagrams in future work. These diagrams may give large contribution and cannot be ignored.

</details>


### [55] [Heavy flavour production in the presence of a weak magnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17787)
*Sudeep Saha,Sandeep Chatterjee,Ujjal Kumar Dey,Snigdh Ghosh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the magnetic-field-induced anisotropic corrections to the gluon-fusion heavy-quark pair production cross section at order (eB)², revealing cos 2θ and cos 4θ angular dependence and a threshold-peaked asymmetry scaling as (eB)²/m_Q⁴.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide theoretically robust, observable signatures of the strong, transient magnetic fields generated in the initial stage of relativistic heavy-ion collisions, via their imprint on heavy-quark production dynamics.

Method: Weak-field expansion (up to (eB)²) of the fermion propagator within Schwinger's proper-time formalism; rotational symmetry breaking is handled by explicit angular dependence (θ) between B and incoming gluons; external quark spinors are treated in vacuum due to m_Q² ≫ eB.

Result: The magnetic correction to σ(gg → QQ̄) is O((eB)²), anisotropic with cos 2θ and cos 4θ terms; cross section is maximal at θ = 0, π and minimal at θ = π/2; asymmetry peaks near threshold and scales as (eB)²/m_Q⁴.

Conclusion: The derived angular and mass dependence constitutes a direct, quantitative probe of early-time magnetic fields in heavy-ion collisions, potentially accessible through heavy-flavour observables.

Abstract: We calculate analytic expression for the cross section up to order $(eB)^2$ for the production of a heavy quark $(Q)$ of mass $m_Q$ through the gluon $(g)$ fusion process $g+g\to Q+\bar{Q}$ in the presence of a constant weak external magnetic field $(B)$ with strength $eB \ll m_Q^2$. This is done using the weak-field expansion of the fermion propagator calculated within Schwinger's proper-time formalism. Owing to the hierarchy of scales ($m_Q^2 \gg eB$), we neglect the effects of the magnetic field on the quark and antiquark spinors in the external lines. Since the magnetic field breaks rotational invariance, the cross section becomes anisotropic and depends on the relative orientation angle $(θ)$ between the magnetic field and the incoming gluons in the center-of-mass frame. The lowest-order magnetic correction to the vacuum cross section scales as $(eB)^2$ and is found to have $\cos 2θ$ and $\cos 4θ$ dependence, reflecting the breaking of rotational symmetry by the magnetic field. The cross section is maximized when the gluons propagate along the field direction ($θ= 0, π$) and minimized in the perpendicular configuration ($θ= π/2$). The resulting asymmetry peaks near the production threshold and falls with increasing center-of-mass energy, scaling as $(eB)^2/m_Q^4$ with the quark mass. These results provide direct, calculable signatures of the initial-stage magnetic field in relativistic heavy-ion collisions which can, in principle, be imprinted on heavy-flavour observables measured in the final state of heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [56] [One-loop contributing for $A \rightarrow Z Z γ$ in the Two-Higgs-Doublet Model with Vector-Like Quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17801)
*Dzung Tri Tran,Vo Quoc Phong,Khiem Hong Phan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop decay A → ZZγ in the Two-Higgs-Doublet Model (2HDM) and its extension with Vector-Like Quarks (VLQs), reporting branching ratios up to 10⁻⁵–10⁻⁴ and VLQ-induced rate modifications of ~5%, potentially observable at future high-luminosity muon colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics beyond the Standard Model via rare Higgs-like pseudoscalar decays, specifically A → ZZγ, which is highly suppressed in the SM but enhanced in extended Higgs sectors such as the 2HDM+VLQ.

Method: Analytic and numerical computation of one-loop Feynman diagrams contributing to A → ZZγ, including full electroweak gauge-invariant amplitude construction; evaluation of unpolarized and polarized partial decay widths and branching ratios; phenomenological scan over viable parameter space of 2HDM and 2HDM+VLQ.

Result: Branching ratios reach 10⁻⁵–10⁻⁴ for benchmark points consistent with theoretical and experimental constraints; VLQ contributions alter total decay rates by ~5%; polarization observables show sensitivity to VLQ couplings and mass scales.

Conclusion: The A → ZZγ decay provides a novel, clean probe of extended Higgs and fermion sectors; its observation—or improved limits—at future muon colliders would constrain 2HDM parameters and VLQ properties, complementing direct searches.

Abstract: In this paper, we present the first results for the one-loop decay $A \rightarrow ZZγ$ in the Two-Higgs-Doublet Model and its extension with Vector-Like Quarks. We calculate the one-loop partial decay widths and branching ratios for both unpolarized and polarized final-state $Z$ bosons. The effects of Vector-Like Quarks propagating in the loop are also examined in our phenomenological analysis. We find that the branching ratios can reach $10^{-5}-10^{-4}$ for several benchmark points within the viable parameter space of the considered models. The contributions from Vector-Like Quarks can modify the total rates by approximately $5\%$. With the high luminosities expected at future multi-TeV muon colliders, these effects may be experimentally accessible.

</details>


### [57] [Covariant approach to allowed beta decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18062)
*Sergio Cruz-Alzaga,Adam Falkowski*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Lorentz-covariant, on-shell effective field theory formalism for allowed nuclear beta decay, organizing nuclear matrix elements in a recoil-momentum expansion and expressing decay distributions via Lorentz-invariant form factors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a systematic, Lorentz-covariant framework for nuclear beta decay that unifies traditional nuclear physics parameterizations with nucleon- and quark-level EFTs, and enables consistent inclusion of recoil corrections, radiative effects, and forbidden transitions.

Method: Constructs a Lorentz-covariant on-shell formalism using little-group-covariant structures to encode nuclear spin/polarization; performs an effective field theory expansion in recoil momentum; derives the most general standard and exotic vector/axial-vector currents for allowed transitions up to first recoil order; obtains the full differential decay distribution in terms of Lorentz-invariant form factors.

Result: A complete, recoil-ordered decomposition of allowed beta-decay amplitudes into model-independent, Lorentz-invariant form factors; explicit mapping between these form factors and conventional nuclear physics parameters (e.g., Fermi, Gamow–Teller, recoil corrections) in the parent rest frame; compatibility with both nucleon-level and quark-level EFTs.

Conclusion: The formalism establishes a rigorous, unified language for beta decay that bridges nuclear, hadronic, and particle physics scales, facilitates precision tests of the Standard Model (e.g., scalar/tensor currents), and provides a foundation for controlled extensions to higher orders and more complex transitions.

Abstract: We propose a Lorentz-covariant on-shell formalism for allowed nuclear beta decay. Nuclear matrix elements are organized in a systematic effective field theory expansion in the recoil momentum, with the dependence on nuclear spin and polarization encoded in little-group-covariant structures. In this language, we construct the most general standard and exotic currents for allowed transitions through first recoil order. We then derive the differential decay distribution encoded in a set of Lorentz-invariant form factors. Specializing to the rest frame of the parent nucleus, we relate our parameters to those used in the traditional beta-decay literature and in nucleon- and quark-level effective field theories. The formalism provides a natural framework for extensions to higher recoil orders, radiative corrections, and forbidden transitions.

</details>


### [58] [Color Lorentz force on a transversely polarized quark in the nucleon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18063)
*Ziqi Zhang,Chandan Mondal,Siqi Xu,Xingbo Zhao,James P. Vary*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the x² moment of the twist-3 parton distribution e(x) — linked to transverse color Lorentz forces on quarks — using off-forward matrix elements, generalized form factors, and light-front wave functions from Basis Light-Front Quantization; their Fourier transforms yield an impact-parameter-space visualization of the Boer–Mulders force.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To access nonperturbative quark–gluon correlations via the genuine twist-3 unpolarized distribution e(x), whose x² moment encodes the average transverse color Lorentz force on a transversely polarized quark in an unpolarized nucleon — a key probe of final-state interactions underlying spin-momentum correlations like the Boer–Mulders effect.

Method: Derives the Lorentz-covariant decomposition of the off-forward matrix element of the twist-3 operator into generalized form factors; computes these form factors using light-front wave functions obtained by solving the light-front Hamiltonian eigenvalue problem within the Basis Light-Front Quantization (BLFQ) framework; performs Fourier transforms to obtain impact-parameter-space distributions.

Result: Obtains the generalized form factor ℱ₂, whose forward limit equals the twist-3 matrix element e₂; interprets ℱ₂ as the impact-parameter-space representation of the Boer–Mulders force, providing the first spatial visualization of the color Lorentz force driving the Boer–Mulders effect.

Conclusion: The study establishes a direct connection between the twist-3 distribution e(x) and spatially resolved transverse color forces in the nucleon, demonstrating that the Boer–Mulders effect originates from a well-defined, calculable final-state interaction visualized in impact-parameter space — a major step toward quantitative, ab initio understanding of higher-twist QCD dynamics.

Abstract: The $x^2$ moment of the genuine twist-3 component of the unpolarized scalar parton distribution function $e(x)$ is related to the average transverse color Lorentz force experienced by a transversely polarized quark in an unpolarized nucleon. This higher-twist quantity probes quark--gluon correlations and provides unique insight into the nonperturbative dynamics of quantum chromodynamics. For the first time, we study the corresponding off-forward matrix element and derive its Lorentz-covariant decomposition in terms of generalized form factors. We calculate these form factors from light-front wave functions obtained by solving the light-front Hamiltonian eigenvalue problem within the Basis Light-Front Quantization framework. Their Fourier transforms provide a spatial picture of the transverse color forces in impact-parameter space. In particular, $\mathscr{F}_2$, whose forward limit is directly related to the twist-3 matrix element $e_2$, can be interpreted as the impact-parameter-space representation of the Boer--Mulders force, providing an intuitive spatial picture of the final-state interaction mechanism underlying the Boer--Mulders effect.

</details>


### [59] [Cosmological Consequences of a Highly Decoupled Right-handed Neutrino](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18334)
*Stephen F. King,Rishav Roshan,Sudipta Show*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores cosmological implications of a decoupled right-handed neutrino (RHN) in a minimal $U(1)_{B-L}$ seesaw model, showing it can induce an early matter-dominated era that dilutes baryon asymmetry and alters leptogenesis dynamics; associated cosmic strings from $B-L$ breaking produce a detectable gravitational-wave signal with a characteristic spectral break tied to RHN and lightest neutrino masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the cosmological impact of a minimally coupled, long-lived right-handed neutrino—often neglected in seesaw models—particularly its role in generating an early matter-dominated era and influencing baryogenesis, leptogenesis, and gravitational-wave signatures.

Method: Analyzes thermal production of RHNs via $B-L$ gauge interactions in a minimal $U(1)_{B-L}$ extension of the Standard Model; computes evolution of energy density, baryon asymmetry dilution, charged-lepton Yukawa equilibration delays, and gravitational-wave spectra from cosmic strings formed during $B-L$ symmetry breaking, including effects of early matter domination.

Result: A decoupled RHN (either lightest $N_1$ or heaviest $N_3$) dominates the early Universe energy density before decay, causing an early matter-dominated era; this dilutes baryon asymmetry and—when $N_3$ is decoupled—delays lepton-flavor equilibration, enabling unflavoured leptogenesis by a lighter $N_1$; cosmic strings yield a GW background with a spectral break at frequencies detectable by SKA, THEIA, LISA, μARES, DECIGO, and ET.

Conclusion: A seemingly inert RHN in minimal type-I seesaw can have profound cosmological consequences: driving early matter domination, reshaping leptogenesis conditions, and producing observable gravitational-wave signatures—making it testable in next-generation GW observatories.

Abstract: In the minimal type-I seesaw, one right-handed neutrino (RHN) can decouple from neutrino mass generation. Such a state is usually neglected, yet its feeble couplings make it long-lived, and it can dominate the energy density of the Universe before decaying. We study this in a minimal $U(1)_{B-L}$ model, where the RHNs are produced through $B-L$ gauge interactions, for the cases of a decoupled lightest RHN $N_1$ and a decoupled heaviest RHN $N_3$. The resulting early matter-dominated era dilutes the baryon asymmetry, and for decoupled $N_3$ it also delays the equilibration of the charged-lepton Yukawa interactions, allowing a lighter $N_1$ to generate the asymmetry in the unflavoured regime. Breaking $U(1)_{B-L}$ forms cosmic strings whose gravitational-wave background acquires a spectral break from the early matter-dominated era, with a frequency set by the decoupled RHN mass and the lightest neutrino mass, within reach of future detectors such as SKA, THEIA, LISA, $μ$ARES, DECIGO and ET.

</details>


### [60] [Deviations to cobimaximal mixing in a 3+1 neutrino scenario](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18487)
*Diana C. Rivera-Agudelo,S. L. Tostado*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how adding a sterile neutrino breaks cobimaximal mixing and μ–τ reflection symmetry in a 3+1 neutrino model; it derives predictions for mixing parameters and CP phases under small symmetry breaking, and finds that normal ordering remains viable for neutrinoless double beta decay, while inverted ordering is excluded by current limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how cobimaximal mixing — compatible with current data — is broken by a light sterile neutrino in a 3+1 framework, and to assess implications for μ–τ reflection symmetry, CP violation, and neutrinoless double beta decay.

Method: Perturbative analysis around the symmetric (cobimaximal) limit; imposition of experimental constraints on active–sterile mixing; derivation of parameter dependencies for mixing angles, Dirac and Majorana CP phases; evaluation of effective Majorana mass |m_ββ| in both neutrino mass orderings.

Result: Small sterile-induced symmetry breaking yields testable deviations in θ₂₃ and δ_CP; Majorana phases are constrained such that inverted ordering is ruled out by current |m_ββ| limits, whereas normal ordering predicts |m_ββ| within reach of next-generation experiments.

Conclusion: Cobimaximal symmetry in 3+1 models is viable only under normal neutrino mass ordering; inverted ordering is excluded for small active–sterile mixing, reinforcing the importance of upcoming neutrinoless double beta decay searches in testing sterile neutrino extensions.

Abstract: Cobimaximal mixing remains a viable approximate pattern since the predicted atmospheric angle ($π/4$) and Dirac CP phase ($-π/2$) are compatible with current experimental data at the $3σ$ level. In this work, we investigate how an additional sterile neutrino state breaks the cobimaximal mixing and the underlying $μ-τ$ reflection symmetry in a $3+1$ neutrino scenario. Given the strong constraints already imposed on active-sterile mixings, we examine the resulting restrictions on the model parameter space, assuming a small symmetry breaking induced by the sterile state. We derive predictions for the relevant model parameters, active-sterile mixing angles, and Dirac CP-violating phases, by considering small deviations from the symmetric limit. We also analyze the allowed combinations of Majorana phases through their impact on the effective Majorana mass governing neutrinoless double beta decay. Current experimental limits on this process impose severe constraints on the inverted ordering realization of the $3+1$ scenario, excluding the small breaking regime within the parameter ranges considered. In contrast, the normal ordering case remains viable and predicts values of the effective Majorana mass that lie within the sensitivity reach of upcoming neutrinoless double beta decay experiments.

</details>


### [61] [Multiboson production at multi-TeV lepton colliders as complementary probes to rare Higgs decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18703)
*Dario Buttazzo,Keshav Chaudhary,Roberto Franceschini,Arman Korajac*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how multi-TeV lepton colliders can constrain effective operators influencing loop-induced Higgs decays (h→γγ, h→Zγ) via two- and three-boson final states, outperforming HL-LHC and FCC-ee projections—especially triboson channels whose energy-enhanced cross sections may rival FCC-hh sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide complementary constraints on effective field theory (EFT) operators affecting loop-induced Higgs decays (h→γγ, h→Zγ), beyond current and projected LHC and FCC-ee limits, leveraging the clean environment and high energy reach of lepton colliders.

Method: Analyzes two- and three-boson final-state processes (e.g., e⁺e⁻ → hγ, hZ, hγγ, hZγ, hZZ) at multi-TeV lepton colliders; performs EFT interpretation using SMEFT operators; compares projected sensitivities with rare Higgs decay measurements and electroweak precision observables.

Result: Multi-TeV lepton colliders significantly improve bounds on CP-even and CP-odd dimension-6 SMEFT operators (e.g., c_{W}, c_{B}, c_{WW}, c_{BB}, c_{W}) governing hγγ/hZγ couplings; triboson processes dominate sensitivity at high energies due to ∝ s scaling of NP cross sections, exceeding HL-LHC and FCC-ee projections and approaching FCC-hh reach for select operators.

Conclusion: Very high-energy lepton colliders offer uniquely powerful and complementary probes of Higgs EFT structure through multi-boson channels—particularly triboson final states—establishing them as essential tools for BSM physics exploration beyond the LHC era.

Abstract: We study the potential of very high-energy lepton colliders to probe effective operators affecting the loop-induced Higgs boson decays $h\to γγ$ and $h\to Zγ$, providing constraints complementary to Higgs coupling measurements at the Large Hadron Collider. In particular, we consider processes with two and three bosons in the final state and analyze their individual and combined impact on the effective field theory parameter space. We demonstrate that these processes at a multi-TeV lepton collider can improve the bounds from rare Higgs decays and electroweak precision observables, surpassing the projections for High-Luminosity LHC and FCC-ee. This is especially true for triboson processes, whose sensitivity is enhanced by the growth with energy of the new physics cross-sections, making them the dominant constraint at very high energies, potentially competitive with FCC-hh.

</details>


### [62] [Virtual photon bremsstrahlung in a magnetized quark-gluon-plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18749)
*Aritra Bandyopadhyay,Krzysztof Redlich,Chihiro Sasaki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the LPM formalism to compute dilepton production in a magnetized, hot QGP, showing that magnetic-field effects enter solely through equilibrium modifications—specifically Landau-level–induced changes to medium density and Debye screening—and yield a nonzero, calculable imprint on the dilepton production rate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and quantify the magnetic-field imprint on dilepton production in heavy-ion collisions, where strong transient B-fields coexist with a hot, dense QGP, and where conventional LPM suppression must be generalized to include magnetic catalysis and Landau quantization.

Method: Extends the Landau-Pomeranchuk-Migdal (LPM) formalism to incorporate a background magnetic field and Fermi-Dirac statistics; solves the Boltzmann equation in relaxation-time approximation to obtain off-equilibrium quark distribution; analytically demonstrates cancellation of the linear-in-E electric-field correction upon angular integration; computes equilibrium thermodynamic quantities (density, Debye mass) using Landau-level quantization in a magnetized QGP; evaluates the modified LPM-suppressed dilepton production rate for quark scatterers.

Result: The off-equilibrium correction linear in electric field vanishes upon full solid-angle integration and thus contributes zero to the dilepton production rate; the leading magnetic-field effect arises entirely from equilibrium modifications—enhanced density and reduced Debye screening mass due to Landau-level quantization—which alter the LPM suppression parameter; a nonzero, field-dependent DPR is obtained for the first time within the LPM framework.

Conclusion: Magnetic fields leave a quantifiable imprint on dilepton production in the QGP via equilibrium thermodynamic modifications encoded in the LPM suppression parameter—not via nonequilibrium transport corrections—establishing a novel probe of early-stage B-field strength in heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: We investigate dilepton production via virtual quark bremsstrahlung in a hot, magnetized quark-gluon plasma (QGP), extending the Landau-Pomeranchuk-Migdal (LPM) framework to account for a background magnetic field and for Fermi-Dirac quark statistics. An external electromagnetic field drives the quark distribution out of equilibrium, and this off-equilibrium correction, obtained from the Boltzmann equation in the relaxation-time approximation, is in principle explicit in the electric and magnetic field strengths. We argue that this correction, being linear in the electric field, vanishes identically upon integration over the full dilepton solid angle and therefore does not contribute to the dilepton production rate (DPR). The leading magnetic-field signature in the rate instead enters entirely through the equilibrium sector: the medium density and Debye screening mass, computed from the Landau-level structure of the magnetized QGP, modifying the LPM suppression parameter. Working in the regime relevant to the hydrodynamic phase of heavy-ion collisions, we present the LPM-induced DPR for quark scatterers and establish, for the first time, a nonzero magnetic-field imprint on this observable through the LPM mechanism.

</details>

### [63] [No-Go Theorem for Phantom Crossing in Scalar-Tensor Gravity with Non-Minimal Derivative Coupling and Soft Scale-Invariance Breaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14354)
*Reza Baghbani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that scale-invariant scalar-tensor theories with non-minimal derivative coupling (NMDC) cannot realize phantom-crossing dark energy (w < −1) preferred by recent DESI 2024 BAO data, due to fundamental theoretical obstructions: ghost instability, violation of swampland conjectures (de Sitter and distance), and their mutual reinforcement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the recent DESI 2024 BAO indication of dynamical dark energy crossing the phantom divide (w < −1) can be realized in theoretically consistent, scale-invariant scalar-tensor theories with non-minimal derivative coupling to the Einstein tensor.

Method: Employed the effective field theory of dark energy formalism to derive necessary ghost-free and gradient stability conditions for NMDC models; systematically tested compatibility with the swampland de Sitter and distance conjectures; proved three no-go theorems using analytical stability and quantum gravity consistency arguments.

Result: Three rigorous no-go theorems: (i) phantom crossing requires ξ_eff < 0, violating the ghost-free condition ξ_eff > 0; (ii) both swampland de Sitter and distance conjectures independently forbid phantom crossing; (iii) these constraints reinforce each other. Hence, scale-invariant NMDC models are ruled out as explanations for the DESI w < −1 preference.

Conclusion: Scale-invariant NMDC theories are theoretically incompatible with phantom-crossing dark energy, providing a robust exclusion for this class of models and guiding future dark energy model building toward frameworks that can satisfy both observational hints and fundamental consistency requirements.

Abstract: Recent baryon acoustic oscillation measurements from the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument (DESI 2024) reveal a preference for dynamical dark energy with an equation of state parameter crossing the phantom divide ($w < -1$) at low redshifts. We investigate whether this phantom crossing can be realized within scale-invariant scalar-tensor theories featuring non-minimal derivative coupling (NMDC) between the scalar field and the Einstein tensor. Employing the effective field theory of dark energy formalism, we systematically derive the ghost-free and gradient stability conditions, and examine their compatibility with the swampland conjectures from quantum gravity. We establish three rigorous no-go theorems demonstrating that: (i)~phantom crossing necessitates $ξ_{\text{eff}} < 0$, directly violating the no-ghost stability requirement $ξ_{\text{eff}} > 0$; (ii)~the swampland de Sitter and distance conjectures independently obstruct phantom crossing; and (iii)~these theoretical obstructions are mutually reinforcing. Our results show that scale-invariant NMDC models cannot account for the DESI preference for $w < -1$, providing robust theoretical guidance for dark energy model building beyond this class.

</details>


### [64] [Non-Stationary Wormholes and Matter Source Reconstruction in General Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14357)
*Geová Alencar,Tiago Crispim,Roberta Dárlla,Ricardo Landim*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs non-stationary wormholes in regular, singularity-free cosmological backgrounds using Simpson-Visser regularization and a homogeneous Cuscuton field; it shows that specific wormhole geometries (defect and Perry-Mann type) can be supported by canonical scalar or mimetic matter, but general separable non-stationary wormholes face strong constraints—especially ruling out smooth, reflection-symmetric, asymptotically flat solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile traversable wormholes with physically realistic, non-singular cosmological evolution—avoiding the initial singularity while maintaining consistent gravitational dynamics and matter sourcing.

Method: Uses Simpson-Visser-like temporal regularization of (n+1)D FLRW metric + homogeneous Cuscuton field to obtain finite, non-zero scale factor at t=0; constructs two explicit dynamical wormhole metrics (4D defect and topologically charged Perry-Mann); analyzes energy conditions and matter sourcing via Einstein equations; derives a universal scaling condition for separable non-stationary spacetimes; tests compatibility with canonical scalar fields and nonlinear electrodynamics.

Result: Defect wormhole supported by massless canonical scalar field; Perry-Mann type requires mimetic matter sector; Cuscuton governs cosmology in both; separable ansatz yields stringent scaling constraint eliminating functional freedom in static reconstruction; no smooth, reflection-symmetric, asymptotically flat regular wormhole satisfies all conditions.

Conclusion: Non-stationary wormholes can exist in regular cosmologies, but only for highly constrained geometries and matter sectors—generic constructions are ruled out by consistency requirements of smoothness, symmetry, and background regularity.

Abstract: We investigate non-stationary wormhole geometries in General Relativity embedded in evolving cosmological backgrounds. We employ a Simpson-Visser-like regularization of the temporal coordinate of the $(n+1)$-dimensional FLRW metric, together with a homogeneous Cuscuton field, to construct cosmological backgrounds in which the scale factor remains finite and non-zero at the would-be initial singularity. We then consider two specific dynamical wormhole geometries: the four-dimensional defect wormhole and the topologically charged Perry-Mann type wormhole. We show that the former can be supported by a canonical scalar field with vanishing potential, while the latter requires a mimetic matter sector to support its spatial structure, with the Cuscuton governing the cosmological evolution in both cases. We subsequently generalize the analysis to a separable non-stationary spacetime and derive a scaling condition that strongly constrains the admissible matter sources. In particular, for minimally coupled scalar and nonlinear electrodynamics fields, this condition removes the functional freedom available in static reconstruction schemes. We show that, within this framework, regular, asymptotically flat wormhole geometries cannot be obtained while simultaneously satisfying the required smoothness and reflection symmetry conditions. Our results illustrate both the possibility of constructing specific non-stationary wormholes in regular cosmological backgrounds and the restrictions that arise when attempting to generalize such constructions to arbitrary spatial geometries.

</details>


### [65] [Post-Newtonian secular dynamics of hierarchical triples II. Canonical derivation of the 1PN\,$\times$\,quadrupole cross terms and of the quadrupole-squared term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14505)
*Hideyoshi Arakida*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the secular Hamiltonian for hierarchical triple systems at 1PN order using a canonical Lie-Hori-Deprit transformation, resolves open relativistic cross terms from Paper I, confirms and extends EFT results for 1PN × quadrupole interactions, provides closed-form expressions for next-to-leading-order terms and the full quadrupole-squared secular Hamiltonian, clarifies gauge structure (e.g., $2\omega_2$ harmonic), establishes equivalence of EIH and ADM frameworks at 1PN, and quantifies eccentricity enhancement in LR-type triples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve unresolved relativistic cross terms (especially 1PN × multipole couplings) in the secular dynamics of hierarchical triples left open in Paper I, and to unify and clarify discrepancies between different relativistic formulations (ADM, EIH, EFT).

Method: Two-stage Lie-Hori-Deprit canonical transformation applied to the 1PN ADM three-body Hamiltonian; systematic bookkeeping in hierarchy parameter $\varepsilon$ and 1PN parameter $\delta$; use of Tremaine's fictitious time for gauge analysis; osculating-contact mapping between EIH and ADM orbital elements.

Result: Exact agreement with Kuntz–Serra–Trincherini’s EFT for electric/magnetic 1PN × quadrupole terms (magnetic term first independently derived); closed-form next-to-leading 1PN × quadrupole term depending on outer argument of pericenter $\omega_2$; complete quadrupole-squared secular Hamiltonian reproducing Will’s and Conway–Will’s equations; identification of $2\omega_2$ harmonic as removable gauge term; EIH–ADM equivalence at 1PN modulo element definitions; quantitative eccentricity enhancement ~2–3× larger than Paper I due to $\omega_2$-dependent NLO term.

Conclusion: The canonical secular Hamiltonian framework consistently unifies ADM, EIH, and EFT descriptions at 1PN; relativistic cross terms are fully characterized; gauge freedom is explicitly handled; and dynamical predictions (e.g., eccentricity evolution) are refined—yet LR’s accelerated mergers remain unexplained within this order.

Abstract: We derive the secular Hamiltonian of a hierarchical triple from the 1PN ADM three-body Hamiltonian by a two-stage Lie-Hori-Deprit transformation carried out entirely in canonical variables, and use it to settle the relativistic cross terms left open in Paper I. A bookkeeping rule enumerates all contributions to each order in the 1PN parameter $δ$ and the hierarchy parameter $\varepsilon$. The 1PN monopole interaction reduces to a renormalization of the inner mass by its binding energy, and the order $δ\varepsilon^{3/2}$, at which Lim and Rodriguez (LR) found a libration term, is empty. The electric and magnetic 1PN $\times$ quadrupole cross terms agree exactly with the effective-field-theory result of Kuntz, Serra, and Trincherini; for the magnetic term this is the first independent derivation. We give the next-to-leading order 1PN $\times$ quadrupole term in closed form; it depends on the outer argument of pericenter $ω_2$. At quadrupole-squared order we obtain the complete secular Hamiltonian, which generates the secular equations of Will and of Conway and Will; its $2ω_2$ harmonic is a gauge term, removable even on the commensurability between the ZLK period and the nodal precession, by a generator given in closed form via Tremaine's fictitious time. The EIH and ADM descriptions coincide at 1PN order; the formulations differ only in the definition of the elements, for which we give the osculating-contact map. In secular integrations of LR's triples, the eccentricity enhancement with the octupole is controlled by the $ω_2$-dependent part of the next-to-leading order term and is two to three times that of Paper I, while LR's accelerated mergers remain absent.

</details>


### [66] [Stability conditions for light rings in circular stationary axisymmetric spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14747)
*Jake O. Shipley*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a curvature-based stability criterion for light rings in 4D stationary axisymmetric spacetimes using Hamiltonian null geodesic formalism and Killing symmetries; stability is determined by the inequality Φ₀₀ > |Ψ₀|, equivalent to the strict null energy condition dominating tidal shear, and rules out stable light rings for real scalar fields but allows them for harmonically charged complex scalar fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous, geometrically transparent stability criterion for light rings—key structures in black hole shadows and strong-field gravity—beyond case-by-case analysis, leveraging symmetries and curvature scalars in an adapted null tetrad.

Method: Uses Hamiltonian formulation of null geodesics in stationary axisymmetric spacetimes with two commuting Killing vectors; constructs geodesic potential; computes transverse Hessian at light ring; reduces it via Killing symmetries and Newman–Penrose formalism to Ricci (Φ₀₀) and Weyl (Ψ₀) scalars; links eigenvalues to Sachs optical equations; recasts stability condition using Einstein equations and stress-energy tensor; applies to scalar field matter models.

Result: Derives exact stability condition μ± = Φ₀₀ ± |Ψ₀|; proves stable light rings require Φ₀₀ > |Ψ₀|, equivalently 4πTₐb pᵃpᵇ > |Ψ₀|; shows violation of strict null energy condition implies instability; confirms no stable light rings for minimally coupled real scalar fields (due to symmetry constraints), but permits them for complex scalar fields with harmonic Killing dependence.

Conclusion: Light ring stability is governed solely by local curvature scalars in adapted null tetrads, with a sharp algebraic condition expressible as an energy-condition dominance over tidal shear; this provides a universal, computationally efficient stability test applicable across matter sectors, and fundamentally constrains viable exotic compact object models involving scalar fields.

Abstract: We consider the stability of light rings in four-dimensional circular stationary axisymmetric spacetimes. Using a Hamiltonian formalism for null geodesics and the Killing symmetries of the spacetime, we show that the transverse Hessian of the geodesic potential, which controls the light ring's stability, reduces there to a pure curvature expression. Its eigenvalues are $μ_{\pm} = Φ_{0 0} \pm \lvert Ψ_{0} \rvert$, where $Φ_{0 0}$ is a Ricci scalar and $Ψ_{0}$ a Weyl scalar of a complex null tetrad adapted to the light ring. Through the Sachs optical equations, these quantities are responsible for focussing and shear of a null geodesic congruence. Stability requires both eigenvalues to be positive, so a light ring is stable if and only if $Φ_{0 0} > \lvert Ψ_{0} \rvert$ there. Assuming the Einstein field equations with generic stress--energy tensor $T_{a b}$, we recast this as $4 πT_{a b} p^{a} p^{b} > \lvert Ψ_{0} \rvert$, where $p^{a}$ is the light ring's tangent vector: for stability, the focussing by matter must dominate the local tidal shear. The trace of the Hessian alone provides an inexpensive test of stability, requiring no knowledge of the Weyl curvature, since a light ring at which the strict null energy condition is violated cannot be stable. Applying the test to specific matter models, we recover known results for vacuum and electrovacuum, with and without a cosmological constant. We also demonstrate that stable light rings are forbidden for a minimally coupled real scalar field with any self-interaction potential, since such a field must be invariant under both isometries. We show that a complex scalar field escapes this obstruction whenever it carries harmonic dependence on the Killing coordinates.

</details>


### [67] [Analytic Electromagnetic Effective Source for Geodesic Orbit in Schwarzschild Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14909)
*Xuchen Lu,Yungui Gong,Bokai Zhang,Chao Zhang,Wenting Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an analytic electromagnetic effective source for geodesic motion in Schwarzschild spacetime using angular completion and tetrad decomposition to express vector-harmonic puncture modes as finite scalar-mode sums; it derives parity-sector coupling coefficients, eliminates the delta-function singularity, and yields finite mode sources suitable for future 1+1D residual-field self-force computations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate modeling of small-body motion in curved spacetime requires self-force effects; higher-order self-force calculations need robust effective-source methods, particularly for electromagnetic fields in Schwarzschild geometry.

Method: Analytic construction of electromagnetic effective source via angular completion and tetrad decomposition of vector-harmonic puncture modes into finite sums of scalar spherical harmonics; derivation of coupling coefficients for both electric and magnetic (parity) sectors; solution of Maxwell mode equations with puncture subtraction to cancel the point-particle delta contribution.

Result: Finite, regular effective source terms for each multipole mode near the particle on eccentric geodesics in Schwarzschild spacetime; explicit expressions for coupling coefficients and mode-decomposed sources ready for use in 1+1D time-domain residual-field evolution.

Conclusion: The derived analytic effective sources eliminate distributional singularities and enable stable, high-accuracy numerical computation of the electromagnetic self-force in Schwarzschild spacetime, paving the way for second-order gravitational self-force studies.

Abstract: Self-force effects are essential for accurately describing the motion of small bodies in curved spacetime. The effective-source approach privides a promising route to higher-order self-force calculations. In this paper, we construct analytic electromagnetic effective source for geodesic motion in Schwarzschild spacetime. An angular completion and a tetrad decomposition express the vector-harmonic puncture modes as finite sums of scalar modes. We derive the coupling coefficients for both parity sectors and obtain the effective sources from the Maxwell mode equations. The point-particle delta contribution cancels, and representative eccentric-orbit results show finite mode sources near the particle. These expressions provide the source terms for future $1+1$-dimensional residual-field calculations.

</details>


### [68] [Quantum Energy Inequalities with a twist: rotating frames along stationary worldlines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14927)
*Christopher J. Fewster,Daniel Lomax Ford*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives closed-form Quantum Energy Inequality (QEI) bounds for massless scalar and electromagnetic fields along stationary worldlines in Minkowski spacetime—characterized by constant Frenet-Serret curvature invariants—and identifies optimal tetrads: Fermi-Walker for EM, but not for scalar fields, where the optimum is found analytically and numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quantum Energy Inequalities (QEIs) constrain negative energy densities of quantum fields, but general QEI bounds are hard to compute in closed form; this work aims to obtain tractable, closed-form QEI bounds for physically relevant stationary worldlines in Minkowski spacetime.

Method: The authors consider stationary worldlines solving the 4D Frenet-Serret equations with constant curvature invariants (including inertial, uniformly accelerated, and uniformly rotating cases); they derive closed-form QEI bounds for massless scalar and electromagnetic fields along such worldlines, varying over tetrads with constant angular velocity relative to the Frenet-Serret tetrad, then optimize the bound over angular velocity—analytically and numerically—to identify the tightest bound and corresponding optimal tetrad.

Result: Closed-form QEI bounds are obtained for both fields; the tightest electromagnetic bound occurs for the Fermi-Walker tetrad; for the scalar field, the Fermi-Walker tetrad is suboptimal—the optimal tetrad and bound are determined analytically in special cases and numerically in general; the results improve upon prior work by extending tetrad optimization beyond Frenet-Serret frames.

Conclusion: Tetrad choice critically affects QEI bound tightness; while Fermi-Walker transport yields the optimal bound for the electromagnetic field, it does not for the massless scalar field—highlighting field-dependent geometric sensitivity in QEIs and enabling more precise energy condition analysis for quantum fields on curved or non-inertial backgrounds.

Abstract: The energy densities of the quantised scalar and electromagnetic fields need not be positive, but their averages along timelike worldlines are constrained by Quantum Energy Inequalities (QEIs) that hold in flat and curved spacetimes for arbitrary Hadamard quantum field states. However the general QEI bound is not easy to compute in closed form. Here, we compute closed form expressions for the massless scalar and electromagnetic fields along stationary worldlines in Minkowski spacetime. These worldlines are solutions to the four-dimensional Frenet-Serret equations with constant curvature invariants and include inertial, uniformly linearly accelerated, and uniformly rotating worldlines as special cases. The QEI bound depends on a choice of tetrad along the worldline, and we compute it for any tetrad with constant angular velocity relative to the Frenet-Serret tetrad. Optimising over the angular velocity, we show that the tightest electromagnetic QEI bound occurs when the tetrad is invariant under Fermi-Walker tetrads. Surprisingly, the Fermi-Walker tetrad does not produce the tightest QEI bound for massless scalar fields, and we investigate the optimal bound both analytically and numerically. Our results and methodology significantly improve on those in [CJ Fewster & J Thompson, Class. Quantum Grav. 40 (2023) 175008], in which only Frenet-Serret tetrads were considered for massless scalar fields.

</details>


### [69] [Scalar wave dark matter imprints on binary black hole mergers: dependence on field mass and density](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15053)
*Yu-Peng Zhang,Shao-Wen Wei,Yu-Xiao Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how scalar-field dark matter environments affect binary black hole mergers via numerical-relativity simulations, revealing that scalar fields accelerate merger, modify gravitational-wave signals, alter horizon-mass ratios in unequal-mass systems, suppress GW energy/angular momentum emission, and significantly enhance gravitational recoil kicks—up to ~2064 km/s—depending on scalar mass and density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how dark matter environments—specifically complex scalar-field clouds—modify binary black hole dynamics and gravitational-wave signatures, beyond vacuum general relativity predictions.

Method: Numerical-relativity simulations of nonspinning binary black holes (mass ratios q = 1–4) immersed in complex scalar-field clouds; systematic variation of dimensionless scalar mass \hat{m}_Φ = m_Φ M₀ and initial amplitude Π₀ (controlling density); controlled initial data preserving scalar energy-density profile and binary geometry when varying \hat{m}_Φ at fixed Π₀.

Result: Scalar environments accelerate merger; unequal-mass binaries exhibit differential accretion altering horizon-mass ratio (strongly at high Π₀); GW energy and angular momentum are suppressed; lighter scalar fields radiate efficiently to large radii, heavier ones localize and enhance inward energy transport; scalar angular-momentum transport is nonmonotonic and can reverse direction near the binary; net linear momentum from scalar + GW radiation determines recoil enhancement/suppression; maximum kick = 2063.6 km/s (q=2, \hat{m}_Φ=0, Π₀=0.01), vs. 137.8 km/s in vacuum.

Conclusion: The scalar-field mass and density jointly govern binary asymmetry evolution, inspiral/merger dynamics, radiative losses (GW and scalar), and linear momentum balance—implying dark matter environments leave distinctive imprints on gravitational-wave observations.

Abstract: Dark matter environments can modify binary black hole mergers and imprint their gravitational-wave signals. We perform numerical-relativity simulations of nonspinning binary black holes immersed in complex scalar-field clouds, considering mass ratios $q=1,2,3,$ and $4$ and varying both the dimensionless scalar-field mass $\hat m_Φ=m_ΦM_0$ and initial amplitude $Π_0$, which controls the density. Our initial data keep the scalar energy-density profile and binary geometry unchanged when varying $\hat m_Φ$ at fixed $Π_0$, allowing the dynamical effect of the field mass to be isolated. We find that the scalar environment generally accelerates the merger and significantly modifies the gravitational-wave signal. For unequal-mass binaries, differential scalar-field accretion changes the horizon-mass ratio during inspiral, weakly at low density but substantially at high density, thereby increasing the binary asymmetry before merger. Dense scalar environments suppress the energy and angular momentum carried by gravitational waves. Lighter scalar fields propagate more efficiently to large radii, whereas heavier fields remain more localized and show stronger inward energy transport. Scalar angular-momentum transport is more complex, showing nonmonotonic behavior and even direction reversal near the binary. For unequal-mass systems, gravitational and scalar radiation both carry net linear momentum, and their relative orientation determines whether the recoil is enhanced or suppressed. The largest kick is $2063.6~{\rm km,s^{-1}}$ for $q=2$, $\hat m_Φ=0$, and $Π_0=0.0100$, compared with $137.8~{\rm km,s^{-1}}$ in vacuum. These results show that the scalar-field mass and density jointly control the binary asymmetry, dynamics, radiative channels, and momentum balance.

</details>


### [70] [Non-Commutativity in Geodesic Congruences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15105)
*Aarav Shah,Louis H. Kauffman*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a geometric framework for spacetime non-commutativity that preserves manifold smoothness, modifies the Raychaudhuri equation via a non-commutative expansion correction θ^NC, formulates non-commutative energy conditions (NCEC), generalizes singularity theorems, and derives cosmological implications—including a geometric origin for dark energy evolution in FLRW without new fields or modified gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometric, background-smooth realization of spacetime non-commutativity that avoids UV divergences or discrete structures, while retaining predictive power for gravitational dynamics—especially singularity formation and cosmic acceleration—without invoking exotic matter or modifying Einstein's equations.

Method: Applies Frobenius' theorem to generalize commutation relations between tangent and separation vectors of geodesic congruences; derives a modified Raychaudhuri equation with shifted expansion scalar \tilde{θ} = θ + θ^{NC}; defines timelike/null Non-Commutative Energy Conditions (NCEC); re-proves focusing lemmas, conjugate-point results, and singularity theorems under NCEC; applies framework to FLRW cosmology to obtain corrected Friedmann and continuity equations.

Result: A consistent non-commutative kinematic deformation yielding: (i) persistence of geodesic focusing and incompleteness even when SEC fails; (ii) generalized singularity theorems for both timelike and null congruences; (iii) FLRW cosmological corrections where θ^{NC} induces universal fluid dilution modification and a nonlinear, density-dependent dark energy contribution.

Conclusion: Spacetime non-commutativity can be realized geometrically on smooth manifolds, leading to testable gravitational consequences—most notably a purely geometric, non-dynamical origin for dark energy evolution—thereby offering an alternative to quintessence, modified gravity, or quantum-gravity-motivated effective fluids.

Abstract: We propose a geometric realization of spacetime non-commutativity where the spacetime manifold remains smooth while the commutation relation between tangent and separation vectors of geodesic congruences is generalized via Frobenius' theorem. This non-commutative kinematic deformation leads to a modified Raychaudhuri equation with a shifted expansion scalar $\tilde θ=θ+θ^{\rm NC}$. We introduce a timelike and null-like Non-Commutative Energy Condition (NCEC) under which focusing and geodesic incompleteness can, in some regimes, persist even when the standard strong energy condition is violated. Using this, we generalize the key focusing lemmas, conjugate-point propositions, and singularity theorems for both timelike geodesics orthogonal to a Cauchy surface and null geodesics orthogonal to a trapped surface. Applied to a FLRW spacetime, we argue that $θ^{\rm NC}$ modifies both the Friedmann acceleration equation and the continuity equations. The non-commutative corrections separate into a universal modification of the dilution of the standard cosmological fluids and another contribution to the dark energy sector that is non-linear in energy density. Crucially, this provides a purely geometric origin for the evolution of dark energy, without introducing exotic dark energy fields or modifying Einstein's field equations.

</details>


### [71] [Primordial Graviton Entanglement in Quasicrystalline Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15249)
*Jiro Soda*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes graviton entanglement in quasicrystalline inflation, showing how parity-even/odd interactions and the inflation-to-radiation transition generate momentum-dependent two-mode squeezing; it demonstrates that while full helicity-sector entanglement is preserved under homogeneous evolution, co-propagating pair entanglement can vanish, illustrated via a solvable four-mode model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum gravitational fluctuations—specifically graviton entanglement—are structured and modified during quasicrystalline inflation and the subsequent transition to radiation domination, with emphasis on parity-sensitive interactions and their impact on observable entanglement signatures.

Method: Analytical derivation of field-momentum covariances for graviton modes; classification of normal and anomalous covariance components under parity-even and parity-odd interactions; analysis of homogeneous, helicity-preserving Bogoliubov evolution; explicit construction and solution of a four-mode model to distinguish global vs. reduced-state entanglement dynamics.

Result: Parity-even interactions allow opposite-helicity graviton pairs with identical momenta to be entangled, while parity-odd contributions vanish for such configurations; post-inflation radiation transition induces additional two-mode squeezing; reciprocal blocks of the full momentum-space covariance remain nonzero under evolution, but reduced-state entanglement for co-propagating pairs can disappear despite unitary, helicity-sector-preserving evolution.

Conclusion: Graviton entanglement in quasicrystalline inflation exhibits a hierarchical structure: global entanglement between left- and right-helicity sectors is robust under homogeneous evolution, but local (pairwise) entanglement is fragile and can be erased—highlighting the necessity of sector-resolved versus mode-resolved diagnostics in primordial quantum gravity probes.

Abstract: We study the entanglement structure of gravitons in quasicrystalline inflation. Both parity-even and parity-odd interactions generate graviton pairs with nonzero total momentum. For opposite-helicity pairs with identical momenta, the parity-even contribution can be nonzero, whereas the parity-odd contribution vanishes. The transition from inflation to radiation domination produces additional conventional two-mode squeezing. We derive the resulting normal and anomalous covariances. The initially nonzero reciprocal blocks of the full field-momentum covariance remain nonzero under homogeneous evolution, although individual components can vanish. We show that this homogeneous, helicity-preserving evolution acts unitarily within each helicity sector and therefore preserves the entanglement between the complete right- and left-helicity sectors. Nevertheless, it can eliminate entanglement in the reduced state of a co-propagating pair. We illustrate this distinction using a solvable four-mode model.

</details>


### [72] [Boundary-induced corrections to the response of an Unruh-DeWitt detector outside a compact star with Robin surface conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15411)
*Nodirbek Anarboev,Javlonbek Khasanov,Javlon Rayimbaev,Bobomurat Ahmedov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a finite-time response framework for an Unruh–DeWitt detector radially accelerated outside a horizonless compact star, using Schwarzschild exterior geometry and Robin boundary conditions; it avoids the Wightman coincidence singularity via mode-amplitude representation and cleanly separates physical parameters (compactness, surface condition, acceleration, gap, duration).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend detector physics beyond static or infinite-time idealizations—specifically, to model realistic finite-time, radially moving probes near compact, horizonless stars where surface physics is encoded via Robin boundary conditions rather than Dirichlet/Neumann assumptions.

Method: Reduces the Klein–Gordon equation to a half-line Schrödinger problem; enforces positivity to select a stable Robin parameter branch; constructs delta-normalized continuum modes; defines a static ground state w.r.t. Schwarzschild time; derives a smooth-switching detector response in mode-amplitude form to bypass the Wightman coincidence singularity; parametrizes constant proper-acceleration trajectories via rapidity measured by static observers.

Result: A fully separated, analytically controlled formulation of the finite-time detector response, isolating contributions from stellar compactness, Robin surface condition, closest approach radius, detector energy gap, and interaction duration; includes well-defined limiting cases (flat space, Dirichlet, static, low-frequency, etc.) for numerical validation.

Conclusion: The framework enables rigorous, singularity-avoiding analysis of accelerating quantum detectors near horizonless compact objects, bridging idealized static models and realistic finite-duration experiments while preserving control over boundary-induced field effects.

Abstract: We formulate the finite-time response problem for a radially accelerated Unruh--DeWitt detector coupled to a real massless scalar field outside a static, spherically symmetric, horizonless compact star. The stellar exterior is described by the Schwarzschild geometry, while unresolved field--surface interactions are represented by a local Robin condition at the stellar radius. After reducing the Klein--Gordon equation to a half-line Schrödinger problem, we identify a sufficiently stable branch of the Robin family from positivity of the radial operator, construct delta-normalized continuum modes, and define the static ground state with respect to Schwarzschild time. We derive a mode-amplitude representation of the smoothly switched detector response that avoids a direct numerical treatment of the coincidence singularity of the Wightman distribution. We obtain a radially aligned trajectory of constant signed proper acceleration in terms of the rapidity measured by static observers. The resulting formulation separates the effects of compactness, surface condition, closest approach, detector gap, and interaction duration. We also state the flat-space, Dirichlet, static-detector, low-frequency, large-distance, and long-interaction limits required to validate a numerical implementation. The framework provides a controlled extension of detector studies with static reflective compact objects to finite-time moving probes.

</details>


### [73] [Finite-Time Bounds on Conformal Vacuum Energy Relaxation in Scalar-Tensor Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15636)
*Yousef Bisabr*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies finite-time vacuum energy relaxation in conformally coupled scalar-tensor cosmology, focusing on exponential potentials and constant conformal coupling; it identifies stability exchange between solutions, derives a conservative upper bound on vacuum suppression (e.g., D_Λ(60) < 47.7 for n > 2), and shows numerically that transient dynamics significantly exceed asymptotic rates (e.g., ~7.53 decades for f(R) ∝ R^2.14, with ~3.27 decades from transients).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how quickly vacuum energy can relax during cosmic evolution in scalar-tensor gravity, distinguishing transient suppression along physical trajectories from asymptotic rates—addressing fine-tuning concerns in dark energy and hierarchy problems.

Method: Analyzes an autonomous dynamical system for a canonical scalar field with exponential potential and constant conformal coupling; computes stability properties of fixed points (scalar-dominated vs. kinetic scaling); derives analytical finite-time bounds on vacuum energy suppression D_Λ(N) using phase-space constraints and initial conditions; validates with numerical integration of background evolution in f(R) = f₀Rⁿ models.

Result: For n > 2, the stable scalar-dominated solution yields asymptotic relaxation rate < 0.579 decades per e-fold; a rigorous finite-time bound gives D_Λ(60) < 47.7 for Ω_Λ,i = 0.99 and Ω_kin,i = 10⁻⁴; numerics show actual suppression is far smaller (~7.53 decades for n = 2.14), with ~3.27 decades attributable to transient excess over uniform asymptotic evolution.

Conclusion: Vacuum energy relaxation is bounded and strongly sensitive to initial conditions and transient dynamics—not captured by late-time attractors alone; while the model permits significant suppression, it falls short of extreme reduction needed for full hierarchy resolution, and viability remains conditional on homogeneity and initial state.

Abstract: We investigate finite time vacuum energy relaxation in conformally coupled scalar-tensor cosmology distinguishing the suppression accumulated along a physical trajectory from its asymptotic rate. For a canonical scalar with an exponential potential and constant conformal coupling, the autonomous system exhibits a stability exchange between scalar dominated and vacuum kinetic scaling solutions. In the power-law metric $f(R)=f_0R^n$ realization with $n>2$, the stable scalar dominated branch has a sustained relaxation rate below $0.579$ decades per Einstein frame e-fold. We additionally derive a finite time upper bound that accounts for depletion of the vacuum relative to the scalar potential. For the specified reference initial state $Ω_{Λ,i}=0.99$ and $Ω_{{\rm kin},i}=10^{-4}$, the bound excludes $60$ decades of suppression within $60$ e-folds throughout the $n>2$ family: $D_Λ(60)<47.7$. Numerical trajectories illustrate why this bound is conservative and why the transient contribution cannot be inferred from the final attractor alone. For the reference $f(R)\propto R^{2.14}$ solution, $D_Λ(60)\simeq7.53$ decades including a transient excess of about $3.27$ decades relative to uniform evolution at the asymptotic rate. The relaxed quantity is the dimensionless vacuum to gravitational scale hierarchy, its evolution also entails variation of the conformal frame Planck mass. The bounds are conditional on the homogeneous model and specified initial state, not a demonstration of complete cosmological viability.

</details>


### [74] [Probing black holes extremality bounds with non-extensive entropies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15970)
*Javier Cancino,Roberto Santos-Silva,Pablo Carlos López Vázquez,Aldo Martínez-Merino*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores quantum-gravity-induced corrections to Einstein's gravity by analyzing modifications to the black hole extremality mass bound using non-extensive entropies derived from the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study quantum-gravity-induced corrections to general relativity, particularly through entropy-based approaches that may encode such corrections.

Method: Investigates corrections to the black hole extremality mass bound using generalized (non-extensive) entropies defined as functions of the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy.

Result: Derives modified extremality conditions for black hole mass under non-extensive entropy frameworks, suggesting possible signatures of quantum-gravitational effects.

Conclusion: Non-extensive entropies provide a viable phenomenological tool for probing quantum-gravity corrections to classical black hole thermodynamics and extremality bounds.

Abstract: Due to the importance of studying quantum-gravity-induced corrections to Einstein's theory of gravity, it is of interest to consider non-extensive entropies whose potential applications include identifying such corrections. In this Letter, we investigate possible corrections to a universal relation associated with the extremality bound of the mass of a black hole by employing entropies defined as functions of the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy.

</details>


### [75] [Quasinormal modes of rotating black holes with ultralight scalar fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16054)
*Leif Lui,Adrian Ka-Wai Chung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes highly accurate leading-order shifts in quasinormal-mode frequencies of rapidly rotating black holes coupled to massive, nonminimally curved scalar fields, enabling new constraints on ultralight scalar dark matter using gravitational-wave data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable detection or exclusion of ultralight scalar fields—viable dark-matter candidates—via their imprints on black-hole quasinormal modes in gravitational-wave signals.

Method: Numerical computation of leading-order frequency shifts in quasinormal modes of Kerr black holes perturbed by massive scalar fields with nonminimal curvature coupling, achieving precision of 10⁻⁸–10⁻⁵ across spin range a/M ∈ [0, 0.9].

Result: Accurate frequency-shift values for scalar-field Compton wavelengths ≥ black-hole mass scale; errors ≤ O(10⁻³) near the scalar-mass threshold; first such high-precision results for rapidly rotating black holes with nonminimally coupled scalars.

Conclusion: The high-accuracy shifts provide a robust theoretical foundation for inferring properties of ultralight scalar particles from existing and future gravitational-wave observations of black-hole ringdowns.

Abstract: We report the first accurate computation of the leading-order shifts in the quasinormal-mode frequencies of rapidly rotating black holes surrounded by massive scalar fields with nonminimal curvature coupling. For fields with reduced Compton wavelengths no shorter than the black-hole mass scale, the numerical errors in the frequency shifts are typically $10^{-8} - 10^{-5}$ for dimensionless spins between 0 and 0.9, and at most $\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})$ next to the scalar-mass threshold. Our results open up the possibility of searching for ultralight scalar particles, prime dark-matter candidates, using existing gravitational-wave signals.

</details>


### [76] [Spiral galaxy spacetime in extended scalar-tensor-Gauss-Bonnet gravity coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16096)
*Pedro Cañate*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new static, spherically symmetric spacetime solution in extended scalar-tensor-Gauss-Bonnet gravity coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics (E-STGB-NLED), which successfully explains flat galactic rotation curves and gravitational lensing without dark matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a dark-matter-free explanation for flat rotation curves and gravitational lensing in spiral galaxies using modified gravity combined with a physically viable electromagnetic source.

Method: Constructs an exact static, spherically symmetric solution of E-STGB gravity sourced by a specific nonlinear electrodynamics (NLED) model; analyzes asymptotic behavior, energy condition satisfaction (null and weak), and astrophysical consistency (rotation curves, lensing).

Result: A novel black-hole–like or galaxy-centered spacetime solution emerges; it reproduces observed flat rotation curves and lensing; the NLED reduces to Maxwell theory in weak/strong field limits and satisfies energy conditions over astrophysically relevant parameter ranges.

Conclusion: E-STGB-NLED is a theoretically consistent and observationally viable alternative to dark matter for explaining galactic dynamics, circumventing the need for exotic matter while preserving standard electrodynamics in appropriate limits.

Abstract: In this work, using nonlinear electrodynamics (NLED) as a source in the field equations of extended scalar-tensor-Gauss-Bonnet (E-STGB) gravity, a novel type of static and spherically symmetric spacetime is found. We show that this novel type of spacetime can be used to explain the rotation curves and gravitational lensing of spiral galaxies. The NLED model used to derive our solution, in the weak and strong field limits, reduces to Maxwell electrodynamics. Furthermore, for a wide range of parameter values where our solution describes the gravitational field of a spiral galaxy, the NLED model satisfies the null and weak energy conditions. Hence, our results suggest that the extended scalar-tensor-Gauss-Bonnet gravity minimally coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics (E-STGB-NLED) provides a viable alternative to explain flat rotation curves of spiral galaxies without invoking dark matter.

</details>


### [77] [Charge-activated birefringence of an Einstein-tensor-coupled vector field on Reissner--Nordström black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16650)
*Mao-Yuan Wan,Chen Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how a neutral vector field, non-minimally coupled to the Einstein tensor, propagates on a charged (Reissner–Nordström) black hole background; the coupling activates only in the presence of electric charge, induces a common shift (not birefringence) in photon sphere and shadow radius at leading order, and yields second-order polarization splitting; absorption asymmetry, glory scattering, and shadow rim splitting probe potential differences; stability boundaries correspond to bound-state instabilities—with distinct regularity properties—and a pole in the even-parity potential at the positive-coupling boundary introduces non-unique continuation without extra physical conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
