Motivation: To understand curvature-induced electromagnetic responses beyond minimal coupling—specifically, how non-minimal coupling of a neutral vector field to the Einstein tensor manifests in strong-field gravity, requiring electric charge for activation and offering observational signatures distinct from standard QED or charge-only corrections.

Method: Analytical and semi-analytical study of the radial Teukolsky-like equations for both parities; symmetry analysis of electric/magnetic susceptibilities under coupling sign reversal; high-frequency (geometric optics) and full-wave (absorption, scattering, glory) calculations; Frobenius analysis near singular points; ray-tracing of double shadows; identification of stability windows via effective potential analysis.

Result: At first order in coupling, no birefringence—only common shift of photon sphere and shadow radius; polarization splitting appears only at second order; absorption parity asymmetry and shadow rim splitting quantify potential differences; backward scattering isolates helicity-reversing response (vanishing without coupling); two stability boundaries exist: negative coupling forms a regular potential well, positive coupling introduces a pole outside horizon with forced logarithmic branch and non-unique continuation.

Conclusion: The non-minimal Einstein-tensor coupling generates charge-dependent, non-birefringent shadow modifications at leading order, enabling degeneracy with pure charge variation; higher-order effects break this degeneracy and encode intrinsic coupling signatures; the positive-coupling boundary signals breakdown of potential regularity—not just instability—necessitating additional physical input for well-posed evolution.

Abstract: We study the propagation of a neutral vector test field non-minimally coupled to the Einstein tensor on a Reissner--Nordström black hole. The interaction vanishes on a vacuum background, so electric charge is required to activate the curvature-induced response. The parity potentials have one symmetry: reversing the sign of the coupling exchanges the electric and magnetic susceptibilities. Because of this symmetry, the two polarizations respond identically at first order in the coupling. The dominant propagation-level effect is therefore a common shift of the effective photon sphere and of the shadow radius, not a birefringent splitting. The variation of shadow size can mimic the charge variation of a black hole. When the coupling is weak, polarization splitting appears only in the second-order term, distinguishing this interaction from corrections involving only charge. We follow this ordering through full-frequency absorption and scattering, the semiclassical glory, and ray-traced double shadows. The absorption parity asymmetry and the shadow rim splitting probe the difference between the effective potentials. The polarization-summed backward scattering isolates the helicity-reversing response, which is zero without the coupling. At the positive-coupling boundary the splitting grows but stays finite. Beyond it the even-parity potential has a pole outside the horizon. In the high-multipole limit, the two boundaries of the stability window both correspond to the thresholds of bound-state instability, but their physical properties differ: the negatively coupled boundary forms a regular potential well, while the positively coupled boundary marks the breaking of regularity. A Frobenius analysis shows a forced logarithmic branch at that pole. Without an additional physical connection condition, the continuation across the pole is not unique.

</details>


### [78] [Optical Signatures of a Black Hole in Effective Quantum Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16721)
*Jonibek Khasanov,Xuwei Zhang,Mirzabek Alloqulov,Sanjar Shaymatov,Chen Zhou,Chengxun Yuan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates black hole (BH) observational signatures in effective quantum gravity (EQG), focusing on gravitational weak lensing and shadow properties in uniform and non-uniform plasma environments; it finds that BH charge and plasma parameters shrink the shadow radius, derives constraints on EQG parameters using EHT data, and shows opposing effects of uniform vs. non-uniform plasma on light deflection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum-gravitational corrections to black hole spacetimes through observable astrophysical signatures—specifically, how plasma environments modulate BH shadows and weak lensing in effective quantum gravity.

Method: Analytical and semi-numerical study of null geodesics in an EQG-inspired BH spacetime; comparison with Schwarzschild and Reissner–Nordström metrics; computation of shadow radius and weak lensing deflection angle in uniform and radially varying (non-uniform) plasma; fitting of model parameters to EHT observational constraints on M87* and Sgr A* shadow sizes.

Result: Shadow radius monotonically decreases with increasing BH charge and plasma frequency (both uniform and non-uniform); uniform plasma increases light deflection angle, whereas non-uniform plasma decreases it; EHT-compatible bounds are derived for EQG spacetime parameters (e.g., quantum correction scale and effective charge).

Conclusion: Plasma environment significantly affects EQG BH observables; combined shadow and weak lensing analyses provide complementary probes of quantum gravity effects, and current EHT data already constrain viable EQG parameter space.

Abstract: In this article, we present an investigation of observational signatures of a black hole (BH) in effective quantum gravity (EQG), particularly gravitational weak lensing and shadow analyses with the assumption that the BH is surrounded by uniform and non-uniform plasma. First, we review the spacetime by comparing it with well-known BH spacetimes such as Schwarzschild and Reissner--Nordström BHs. Subsequently, we explore the effect of the uniform and non-uniform plasma on the BH shadow. The results indicate that shadow radii decrease under the influence of the BH charge and both uniform and non-uniform plasma parameters. In addition, we obtain theoretical constraints on the spacetime parameters using observational data from the Event Horizon Telescope (EHT) collaboration. Finally, we study gravitational weak lensing around the BH in EQG surrounded by uniform and non-uniform plasma. It was found that increasing the uniform plasma frequency leads to a rise in the deflection angle and vice versa for the non-uniform plasma.

</details>


### [79] [Analytic Brill wave initial data with trapped surfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16773)
*Piotr Koc*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs the first explicit real-analytic, non-trivial, asymptotically flat vacuum initial data for axisymmetric Brill waves — a one-parameter family with closed-form conformal factor involving elementary functions and dilogarithm; it yields exact expressions for previously numerical quantities (e.g., ADM mass as digamma series), establishes a sharp existence threshold k_c ≈ 0.8048, proves trapped surface formation near criticality, and identifies apparent horizons without approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the long-standing limitation that finite-amplitude Brill wave initial data had only been obtained numerically since Brill's 1959 work, and to provide exact analytic insight into gravitational radiation in vacuum general relativity.

Method: Constructs an explicit one-parameter family of axisymmetric, time-symmetric, asymptotically flat vacuum initial data on ℝ³ using toroidal conformal symmetry (Beig–Husa), applies a Natanzon-type change of variable to reduce mode equations to Gauss hypergeometric form, derives Gamma-function-coefficient series for the conformal factor, employs variational analysis and ground-state identity for existence criterion, and rigorously analyzes trapped surfaces via rescaling and convergence arguments.

Result: Exact closed-form conformal factor (elementary + dilogarithm); exact ADM mass as absolutely convergent digamma series; sharp existence threshold k_c = 0.8048016772…; proof of outer trapped tori/spheres near k_c; identification of apparent horizon; numerical evidence (under equatorial symmetry) of minimal spheres forming a fold at k ≈ 0.7670.

Conclusion: The constructed family provides the first fully explicit, non-perturbative, real-analytic solution for Brill waves, enabling exact computation of global geometric and physical quantities, establishing rigorous critical behavior, and confirming black hole formation thresholds without relying on weak-field or thin-torus approximations.

Abstract: Since Brill's work of 1959, the conformal factor of finite-amplitude Brill waves, the axisymmetric and time-symmetric vacuum initial data on $\mathbb{R}^3$ that describe pure gravitational radiation, has been obtained only numerically, up to the most recent studies. Here an explicit one-parameter family of such data is constructed: the first explicit real-analytic, non-trivial, asymptotically flat vacuum initial data with trivial topology and a single end. The free metric function is a closed-form combination of elementary functions and a dilogarithm. The data have the toroidal conformal symmetry of Beig and Husa, and a change of variable of Natanzon type reduces every mode equation to the Gauss hypergeometric equation, so that the conformal factor is a series with explicit Gamma-function coefficients. The quantities that have so far required numerical work are thereby obtained exactly. A ground-state identity gives a sharp existence criterion: complete, asymptotically flat data exist precisely for $k<k_c=0.8048016772\ldots$, and the explicit series is identified with the unique variational solution. The ADM mass is an absolutely convergent digamma series, with $m/a=πk^2/10+O(k^3)$ for small amplitudes and an explicit residue at its critical divergence. Sufficiently strong waves contain trapped surfaces: it is proved that for $k$ close to $k_c$ the rescaled conformal factor converges to an explicit function and explicit tori and spheres become outer trapped, so that the data contain an apparent horizon. A numerical search, restricted to equatorially symmetric star-shaped surfaces, finds marginally trapped (minimal) spheres, which appear in a fold near $k=0.7670$. No thin-torus or weak-field approximation is used.

</details>


### [80] [Hamiltonian surface charges in General Parallel Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16799)
*Tomi Koivisto*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces General Parallel Relativity as a dynamically equivalent reformulation of Einstein gravity using parallel frames to define explicit boundary reference data; it derives quasi-local energy-momentum and horizon entropy as distinct Hamiltonian surface charges, validates them on Reissner-Nordström and dilatonic charged black holes, and confirms consistency with thermodynamic relations from the Euclidean boundary action.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometrically transparent, boundary-aware reformulation of general relativity where finite-region conserved quantities—including energy-momentum and horizon entropy—arise naturally as Hamiltonian surface charges tied to canonical frame directions and boost symmetries.

Method: Constructs General Parallel Relativity using a parallel frame to fix boundary reference data; identifies diffeomorphisms along frame directions and normal-plane boosts at horizons as generators of quasi-local energy-momentum and entropy, respectively; computes resulting Hamiltonian surface charges covariantly; applies them to Reissner-Nordström and dilatonic charged black hole solutions; compares with Misner-Sharp mass and Euclidean boundary action thermodynamics.

Result: Derives explicit, covariant expressions for quasi-local energy-momentum (including matter surface term) and horizon entropy as Hamiltonian surface charges; shows that for both black hole examples, the matter contribution and its Legendre transform extend the Misner-Sharp mass to the full Hamiltonian energy consistent with electromagnetic boundary conditions; verifies that horizon charges satisfy the expected first law of black hole thermodynamics.

Conclusion: General Parallel Relativity provides a unified, covariant framework in which energy-momentum and entropy emerge as distinct, physically interpretable Hamiltonian surface charges—demonstrating dynamical equivalence to Einstein gravity while enhancing quasi-local gravitational thermodynamics and boundary control.

Abstract: General Parallel Relativity is a dynamically equivalent formulation of Einstein gravity in which the parallel frame supplies explicit boundary reference data. In this formulation, finite-region energy-momentum and horizon entropy arise as distinct Hamiltonian surface charges. Diffeomorphisms along canonical frame directions define a quasi-local energy-momentum including a matter surface term, while normal-plane boosts at a smooth horizon give entropy. These covariant formulas are applied to the Reissner-Nordström black hole and to a dilatonic charged black hole. In both examples, the matter contribution and its Legendre transform complete the Misner-Sharp mass into the Hamiltonian energy appropriate to the chosen electromagnetic boundary datum. The resulting horizon charges satisfy the thermodynamic relation obtained independently from the Euclidean boundary action.

</details>


### [81] [Where is the conformal coupling in Palatini gravity?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16954)
*Alexandros Karam,Sotirios Karamitsos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates Weyl invariance and conformal coupling of a scalar field in torsion-free Palatini gravity with ordinary kinetic term; it shows that Weyl invariance cannot be restored by tuning curvature coupling, analyzes scalar propagation and effective metric limits, computes one-loop beta functions for curvature couplings under two prescriptions, and identifies infrared fixed-point behavior and gravitational coefficient breakdown.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify whether and how Weyl invariance can be realized in Palatini gravity with an ordinary scalar kinetic term — specifically, whether curvature coupling (ξ) can compensate for the Weyl non-invariance of the kinetic term, contrary to common assumptions based on metric theories.

Method: Analytic field-theoretic analysis in the torsion-free Palatini formalism: (i) evaluation of energy-momentum trace on scalar EOM to assess Weyl variation independence from ξ; (ii) elimination of the independent connection to map Palatini curvature coupling ξ to effective metric coupling ξ_eff; (iii) study of scalar propagation via Hessian analysis; (iv) one-loop renormalization group analysis of both metric-curvature and independent-curvature couplings under two prescriptions — fixed vs. dynamical connection response to scalar fluctuations.

Result: 1. No finite ξ restores Weyl invariance due to separate Weyl invariance of the Rφ² term and non-canceling Weyl variation of (∂φ)²; 2. Scalar remains dynamical even when reduced kinetic coefficient vanishes; 3. Weyl compensator behavior with scale-invariant potential emerges only in the rescaled infinite-ξ limit, linked to Palatini R² gravity in D=4; 4. Metric-curvature coupling acquires induced running from conformal shift, while independent-curvature coupling runs homogeneously; 5. Two prescriptions yield distinct beta functions but both allow IR approach to (ξ_eff, λ_eff) = (1/6, 0); 6. On the positive gravitational branch, gravitational coefficient vanishes at finite scale before reaching IR fixed point.

Conclusion: Weyl invariance is fundamentally obstructed in Palatini gravity with standard scalar kinetic term, irrespective of curvature coupling tuning; the theory’s quantum behavior depends critically on whether the connection is treated as fixed or dynamical under scalar fluctuations, yet both admit a universal IR conformal fixed point — albeit with a breakdown of unitarity/gravity on the positive branch prior to reaching it.

Abstract: We study the conformal coupling in the torsion-free Palatini formulation with an ordinary scalar kinetic term. Tuning the curvature coupling cannot restore Weyl invariance in a Palatini theory with an ordinary scalar kinetic term because, for an independent and inert connection, the scalar-curvature term is separately Weyl-invariant, and no finite curvature coupling cancels the Weyl variation of the kinetic term. This can be demonstrated by evaluating the energy-momentum trace on the scalar equation of motion and noting that it is explicitly independent of the Palatini coupling. Eliminating the connection, we construct a map between the curvature coupling and the effective coupling for the equivalent metric theory, and demonstrate that the scalar continues to propagate even if the reduced kinetic coefficient vanishes. We also show that it becomes a Weyl compensator with a scale-invariant potential only in the rescaled infinite-coupling limit, which in four dimensions is directly related to Palatini ${\cal R}^2$ gravity. We calculate the running of the couplings at one loop, and we find that the conformal shift in the metric-curvature coupling's beta function generates a metric-curvature coupling on a fixed metric-affine background, whereas the independent-curvature coupling runs homogeneously. Furthermore, we follow an alternate prescription in which the connection is allowed to respond to the scalar fluctuations, which results in a different scalar Hessian and therefore different beta functions. Nonetheless, we find that both prescriptions admit the formal infrared limit $(ξ_{eff},λ_{eff})\to(1/6,0)$ through different limits of the original couplings in the case of positive perturbative quartics. Finally, we note that trajectories starting on the positive gravitational branch encounter a vanishing gravitational coefficient at a finite scale before this endpoint.

</details>


### [82] [Large mass-ratio ($3< q \leq 10$) inspiral-merger-ringdown model for eccentric binary black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16958)
*Pratul Manna,Tamal RoyChowdhury,Chandra Kant Mishra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: EccHybIMR22 is an analytical eccentric IMR waveform model for the (2,2) mode, built by smoothly joining an eccentric inspiral with a quasi-circular merger-ringdown, calibrated to 91 eccentric NR-informed hybrids; it achieves <3% mismatch for ~65% of held-out hybrids and ~80% of SXS simulations at moderate eccentricities (e₂₀Hz ≲ 0.3) and mass ratios up to 10.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a fast, accurate, fully analytical gravitational waveform model for eccentric binary black hole coalescences—particularly for large mass ratios (q > 3)—where existing NR simulations are sparse and low-frequency coverage (M_low = 20 M⊙ at 20 Hz) is inaccessible with NR alone.

Method: Constructs EccHybIMR22 by smoothly attaching an analytic eccentric inspiral (PN-based) to a quasi-circular merger-ringdown ansatz; trained on 91 eccentric hybrids (PN+NR), using ~30 as training targets; hybrid construction leverages expanded SXS NR catalog; model validated via mismatch against held-out hybrids and 175 SXS NR waveforms.

Result: Recovers 65% of held-out hybrids with mismatch < ~3% at M = 40 M⊙ and e₂₀Hz ≲ 0.33 (q ≤ 3) or ≲ 0.12 (q > 3); achieves ~80% recovery with similar mismatches against 175 SXS simulations for 0 ≲ e^NR₂₀Hz ≲ 0.3.

Conclusion: EccHybIMR22 is a robust, computationally efficient, and observationally relevant eccentric IMR model—especially for high-q systems—enabling improved parameter estimation and detection of eccentric BBHs in current and future GW observatories.

Abstract: We present $\texttt{EccHybIMR22}$, a fully analytical $\textbf{Ecc}$entric-$\textbf{Hyb}$rid-$\textbf{I}$nspiral-$\textbf{M}$erger-$\textbf{R}$ingdown model for the quadrupole ($\ell$=$2$, $|m|$=$2$ or simply the $\textbf{22}$) mode. The model is obtained by smoothly attaching an eccentric-inspiral to a quasi-circular-merger-ringdown prescription and is trained on eccentric numerical relativity (NR) simulations with mass ratios $q\leq 10$. In particular, we focus on obtaining a model that is sensitive to large mass ratios ($q>3$). Leveraging the recent expansion of SXS catalog of NR simulations, we construct a set of 91 hybrids (obtained by combining waveforms from post-Newtonian (PN) theory with NR simulations) with a target low total mass limit ($M_{\rm low}$) of $20M_{\odot}$ at an averaged 22 mode frequency of 20Hz, inaccessible with existing NR simulations alone. Employing a little less than a third of these hybrids as targets, we construct $\texttt{EccHybIMR22}$ and recover $65\%$ of the hybrids from the remaining set with mismatches smaller than $\sim3\%$ for a total mass of $40M_\odot$ (chosen arbitrarily) and orbital eccentricity $e_{\rm 20Hz}\lesssim0.33$ ($e_{\rm 20Hz}\lesssim 0.12$) for mass ratios $q\leq3$ ($q>3$). Furthermore, when tested against 175 SXS simulations, the model is able to recover nearly $80\%$ of the simulations with similar mismatches for systems with $0\lesssim e^{\rm NR}_{\rm 20Hz}\lesssim 0.3$.

</details>


### [83] [Quasibound states of charged Dirac fields around Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17034)
*Nodirbek Anarboev,Javlonbek Khasanov,Javlon Rayimbaev,Bobomurat Ahmedov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasibound states of a massive charged Dirac field on the Einstein–Euler–Heisenberg (EEH) black hole — a QED-corrected extension of Reissner–Nordström spacetime — and finds that EEH corrections induce small, angular-momentum-dependent shifts in real frequencies and damping rates, with no instability onset; Coulomb screening enhances wave leakage, while metric corrections govern the sign and magnitude of ℓ-dependent effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess stability and spectral structure of charged Dirac fields on QED-corrected black holes (EEH), specifically whether vacuum-polarization effects from Euler–Heisenberg theory can trigger superradiant instabilities or modify hydrogenic quasibound spectra relative to Reissner–Nordström.

Method: Analytic derivation of first-order EEH background geometry and electrostatic potential; separation of Dirac equation; proof of absence of real-frequency quasibound states via an exact identity; two-sided numerical shooting with Muller root finder for complex quasinormal frequencies; WKB analysis of inner potential barrier; ab initio time-domain evolutions with Richardson-extrapolated matrix-pencil spectral extraction.

Result: No real-frequency quasibound states exist (hence no instability); trapping condition and leading hydrogenic spectrum remain identical to RN; real frequencies shift linearly in Euler–Heisenberg coupling a, suppressed by ℓ; damping rates show branch-dependent changes: ℓ=0 ground state lifetime increases slightly, while higher-ℓ states (e.g., (nr,j,ℓ,λ)=(0,3/2,1,−2)) decay up to 80% faster near extremality; Coulomb screening universally enhances leakage, metric correction controls ℓ-dependent sign/magnitude.

Conclusion: The EEH correction does not destabilize the Dirac field but induces quantifiable, ℓ-selective modifications to quasibound lifetimes and frequencies; the absence of fine-structure splitting and robust exclusion of zero-damping modes confirm structural stability of the RN hydrogenic paradigm under leading QED vacuum polarization.

Abstract: We study quasibound states of a massive charged Dirac field on the Einstein--Euler--Heisenberg (EEH) black hole, the extension of Reissner--Nordström (RN) by the leading weak-field vacuum-polarization term of QED. We derive the background to first order in the Euler--Heisenberg coupling $a$. We show that it admits a nondegenerate horizon at $Q=M$ and an extremal charge $Q_{\rm ext}=M[1+a/(40M^2)+\mathcal{O}(a^2)]>M$, with horizon data that depend on $\sqrt a$ near extremality. From the separated radial equations, we derive an identity that excludes real-frequency quasibound states on any background of this class, so damped branches cannot become unstable. Since the EEH corrections first enter at $\mathcal{O}(r^{-5})$ in the electrostatic potential, the trapping condition $Mμ^2-qQω_R>0$ and the hydrogenic spectrum coincide with RN, and no fine-structure-order splitting arises. Two-sided shooting with a Muller root finder shows that the real frequencies shift only by near-zone amounts that are linear in $a$ and strongly suppressed with angular momentum. The damping rates respond in a branch-dependent way. At $Q/M=0.99$ the $\ell=0$ ground state becomes slightly longer-lived, while the $(n_r,j,\ell,λ)=(0,\tfrac32,1,-2)$ state decays up to $80\%$ faster for $a/M^2\le1$, and near the trapping threshold the damping rate increases by up to $60\%$. Switching on the metric and potential corrections separately, together with a WKB analysis of the inner barrier, identifies the mechanisms: Coulomb screening always increases leakage, while the metric correction controls the sign and size of the $\ell$-dependent effect. Characteristic time-domain evolutions with Richardson-extrapolated matrix-pencil extraction reproduce the frequency-domain shifts.

</details>


### [84] [Bartnik's splitting conjecture and the cosmological wall: a Cauchy Busemann function approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17092)
*E. Minguzzi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates Bartnik's conjecture on timelike geodesic completeness and splitting of cosmological spacetimes under the timelike convergence condition. It introduces invariant geometric objects—the cosmological wall, horizons, and developments—defined via Cauchy Busemann functions; establishes regularity and dynamical properties of these objects; proves that intersection of developments implies existence of a timelike line and hence splitting; and obtains a localized rigidity version of Hawking’s singularity theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the global causal structure of potential counterexamples to Bartnik's conjecture—i.e., timelike geodesically complete cosmological spacetimes satisfying the timelike convergence condition that do not split—and to identify intrinsic, Cauchy-surface-independent geometric structures characterizing such spacetimes.

Method: Uses Lorentzian geometric analysis: defines cosmological developments and wall via Cauchy Busemann functions $b^\pm$ of a compact Cauchy hypersurface; employs a 'product trick' embedding into $M \times \mathbb{R}$ to interpret $b^+$ as a horizon graph function; applies regularity theory for horizons (continuity, local Lipschitz/semi-concavity); analyzes calibrated curves (timelike rays); combines causality, convexity, and mean curvature conditions to derive rigidity and splitting results.

Result: Any counterexample to Bartnik's conjecture must contain a non-empty closed causally convex cosmological wall separating disjoint future/past developments bounded by $C^0$ null horizons containing lightlike lines; $b^+$ is continuous, locally Lipschitz and semi-concave on its domain, with calibrated curves being timelike rays; if developments intersect (e.g., when $b^+$ is $C^1$), a timelike line exists and splitting follows; a localized Hawking-type rigidity holds: non-positive future mean curvature on a compact Cauchy hypersurface forces splitting; if the wall has empty interior, it coincides with both horizons and is ruled by lightlike lines, and is smooth totally geodesic under null completeness.

Conclusion: The paper establishes that any counterexample to Bartnik's conjecture must exhibit highly constrained, physically meaningful global causal structure—centered on invariant objects defined via Busemann functions—and provides new rigidity criteria (including a localized version of Hawking's theorem) that narrow the space of possible counterexamples, reinforcing the plausibility of the conjecture.

Abstract: Bartnik's conjecture states that a timelike geodesically complete cosmological spacetime satisfying the timelike convergence condition splits as a static product. We show that any counterexample exhibits a peculiar global causal structure: two disjoint open regions, the future and the past cosmological developments, separated by a non-empty closed causally convex set: the cosmological wall. The developments are bounded by $C^0$ null hypersurfaces, the cosmological horizons, and a component of each horizon contains a lightlike line. These objects are natural and hence carry physical significance, as they do not depend on the choice of Cauchy hypersurface. They can be defined through the Cauchy Busemann functions $b^\pm$ of a compact Cauchy hypersurface $Σ$, the developments being their finiteness domains. Using a product trick we show that $b^+$ is the graph function of a Cauchy horizon in $M\times \mathbb{R}$, and so inherits the regularity and dynamical properties of horizons: $b^+$ is continuous, locally Lipschitz and locally semi-concave on its finiteness domain, and its calibrated curves are timelike rays. We prove that if the two cosmological developments intersect, which happens whenever $b^+$ is $C^1$ on its finiteness domain, then the spacetime contains a timelike line and hence splits, a result which recovers several positive results of the literature. We also obtain a localized rigidity version of Hawking's singularity theorem: if some compact Cauchy hypersurface has non-positive mean curvature on the future development, the spacetime splits. Hence, in a counterexample, every compact Cauchy hypersurface expands somewhere in the future development and contracts somewhere in the past one. If the wall has empty interior, it coincides with both horizons and is ruled by lightlike lines, and under null completeness its components are smooth totally geodesic null hypersurfaces.

</details>


### [85] [Vanishing Love Numbers of Kerr-Newman Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17179)
*Sumanta Chakraborty,Takuya Katagiri,Paolo Pani,Jun-Xi Shi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analytically computes the static tidal (Love) numbers for the general Kerr–Newman black hole in Einstein–Maxwell theory and proves they all vanish—including gravito-electromagnetic couplings—while the dissipative (tidal heating) response due to frame-dragging remains nonzero.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the open question of tidal response for charged, rotating black holes, where gravitational and electromagnetic perturbations are intrinsically coupled, and to test the 'no-hair' and universality properties of black hole tidal deformability.

Method: Leverages a recent breakthrough in the separability of gravitational and electromagnetic perturbations around Kerr–Newman black holes; performs analytic computation of static linear response using metric and Maxwell field perturbations in the low-frequency, adiabatic limit; constructs and evaluates the full conservative Love number matrix (including mixed gravito-electromagnetic components).

Result: All static conservative Love numbers—including diagonal (gravitational and electromagnetic) and off-diagonal (mixed) entries—vanish identically for arbitrary mass, charge, and spin; the static dissipative (imaginary-part) response, associated with tidal heating via frame-dragging, is nonzero.

Conclusion: The vanishing of all static Love numbers confirms that Kerr–Newman black holes, like their uncharged or nonrotating counterparts, exhibit no static conservative tidal deformability—reinforcing their status as universal, structureless objects in general relativity coupled to electromagnetism; nonzero dissipative response reflects irreversible energy transfer due to rotation.

Abstract: Tidal Love numbers characterize the conservative response of compact objects to external perturbations and provide a powerful probe of their nature. While the static Love numbers of Schwarzschild, Kerr, and Reissner--Nordström black holes remarkably vanish, the tidal response of a generic Kerr--Newman black hole has remained unexplored due to the intrinsic coupling between gravitational and electromagnetic perturbations. Exploiting a recent breakthrough in the separability of these perturbations, we analytically determine the static tidal response of the most general stationary black hole in four-dimensional Einstein--Maxwell theory. For arbitrary multipole order, charge, and spin, we show that the entire matrix of conservative Love numbers vanishes, including the mixed gravito-electromagnetic couplings. We also compute the nonvanishing static dissipative response induced by frame dragging.

</details>


### [86] [Null-Energy Bounds on Photon-Orbit Instability in Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17273)
*Sarandas A.,C. Fairoos,C. L. Ahmed Rizwan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a local upper bound on the coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent for unstable circular null geodesics in static, spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat Einstein–Gauss–Bonnet gravity (n ≥ 5), showing how matter density and Gauss–Bonnet coupling affect orbital instability, with geometric interpretations via optical curvature and null convergence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Einstein-gravity optical bound on null-orbit instability to higher-dimensional Einstein–Gauss–Bonnet gravity and identify sharp matter conditions under which the bound remains valid despite higher-curvature corrections.

Method: Analytical derivation using null geodesic equations, field equations, spherical mass relation, and geometric optics tools—including optical Gaussian curvature and tangential null convergence—within the regular Einstein branch of Gauss–Bonnet gravity; employs energy conditions (tangential null energy condition) and Misner–Sharp mass formalism.

Result: A local upper bound on the coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent is obtained, retaining the matter-density contribution from field equations; departure from the Einstein value (n−3) is quantified by a density–mass contrast term; vacuum saturates the bound, charged Maxwell fields induce a strict gap; a density-free corollary follows from an alternative matter condition.

Conclusion: The Einstein optical bound admits a robust generalization to Gauss–Bonnet gravity under physically mild assumptions; the bound’s saturation and gap structure encode gravitational dynamics beyond GR, with implications for strong-deflection lensing and eikonal damping of test fields.

Abstract: We derive a local upper bound on the coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent of unstable circular null orbits in static, spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat Einstein--Gauss--Bonnet gravity in $n\geq5$ dimensions with nonnegative Gauss--Bonnet coupling. On the regular Einstein branch, the tangential null energy condition suffices; no additional density-pressure inequality is required for the principal result. The bound retains the local matter-density contribution dictated by the field equations. Using the spherical mass relation, we express the departure of its dimensionless coefficient from the Einstein value $n-3$ in terms of the difference between the local density and the enclosed generalized Misner--Sharp mass per areal volume. This gives a sufficient density-contrast criterion for the Einstein optical bound to remain valid at nonzero coupling. Optical Gaussian curvature and tangential null convergence provide geometrical interpretations that distinguish radial tidal instability from the focusing of a null congruence. Vacuum solutions saturate the bound, while a Maxwell field with nonzero charge produces a strictly positive gap. The smooth Einstein limit and explicit vacuum and charged solutions provide consistency checks. A separate matter condition yields a density-free corollary. We also discuss consequences for strong-deflection lensing and test-field eikonal damping within their respective regimes of validity.

</details>


### [87] [Einstein Telescope: the triangle in a network and the impact of the relative orientation of detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17446)
*Giacomo Bruno,Antonin Oswald,Federico De Lillo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the scientific performance of different geometrical configurations for the Einstein Telescope (ET), comparing a triangular layout, two tilted L-shaped detectors, and a novel hybrid (triangle + distant L-shaped) configuration—both standalone and in networks with Cosmic Explorer or LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA. The hybrid configuration outperforms others in compact binary coalescence and stochastic background detection standalone; network integration reduces configuration-dependent differences. Correlated noise effects on stochastic background are mitigable with additional detectors, and optimal tilt between dual L-shaped ET detectors is ~30°.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the optimal geometrical configuration for the Einstein Telescope (ET) — a next-generation gravitational-wave observatory — by quantifying how design choices impact key science cases: compact binary coalescence (CBC) localization/parameter estimation and stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) detection sensitivity.

Method: Quantitative comparison of ET configurations (triangular, dual-L-shaped at ~45°, and novel triangle+L hybrid) using Fisher matrix analysis and Bayesian inference frameworks for CBCs, and cross-correlation sensitivity curves for SGWB. Performance assessed both standalone and in networks with Cosmic Explorer (US) or LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA. Impact of correlated noise and relative detector orientation (tilt angle) is systematically studied.

Result: The triangle+L hybrid ET configuration yields significantly superior standalone performance in both CBC (e.g., sky localization, mass/spin estimation) and SGWB (higher signal-to-noise ratio, lower confusion noise) compared to the two baseline configurations. Network integration (e.g., with Cosmic Explorer) reduces performance gaps among configurations. Correlated noise in co-located detectors degrades SGWB sensitivity but is mitigated by adding ≥3 independent baselines. For dual-L ET, ~30° tilt optimizes network-wide angular response and minimizes orientation-dependent performance loss.

Conclusion: The currently unconsidered triangle+L hybrid ET configuration is scientifically superior standalone and warrants serious reconsideration. While network context diminishes configuration sensitivity, optimal design remains critical for maximizing standalone capability and robustness against noise systematics; a ~30° tilt is recommended for dual-L ET if that configuration is retained.

Abstract: The geometrical configuration of the Einstein Telescope (ET) gravitational-wave observatory is one the most important design choices to be made for this future scientific facility. Two baseline configurations are currently being considered, a single triangle or 2 distant L-shaped detectors tilted by about $45^{\circ}$. In this document we present a study of the scientific performance of the ET baseline configurations in both stand-alone mode and in the presence of either an L-shaped Cosmic Explorer detector with 40-km long arms located in the United States or the existing LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA network. We also include in the study an alternative ET configuration, which is currently not being considered and consists of a triangle plus a distant L-shaped detector. We find that in stand-alone mode this alternative configuration performs significantly better than the other two in both the compact binary coalescence and stochastic gravitational wave background scientific cases. The differences in performance between the ET configurations are reduced in the presence of the considered, additional non-ET detectors. We also show that the impact of possible correlated noise in co-located detectors on the stochastic gravitational-wave background performance can be addressed with the addition of extra detectors. Finally, we study the impact on the ET scientific performance of the relative orientation of the future detectors in the network. We find that, while the configurations that include a triangular ET detector are basically insensitive to this orientation, a balanced compromise for the ET configuration consisting of two L-shaped detectors could be represented by a tilt angle between the two detectors of about $30^{\circ}$.

</details>


### [88] [Mathematical framework for simulation of non-perturbative cavity optomechanics with plate-like dielectrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17475)
*Andrew Laeuger,Nancy Aggarwal*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the vacuum two-photon formalism to model finite-thickness planar dielectrics in cavity optomechanics, self-consistently incorporating Fresnel reflection, internal propagation, optical forces, and mechanical back-action to derive carrier/sideband transfer functions for detector response and noise analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing cavity optomechanical models fail to self-consistently determine the spatially resolved cavity field, optical force, mechanical equilibrium, and back-action when a finite-thickness dielectric fully intercepts and restructures the intracavity optical mode.

Method: Extends the vacuum two-photon formalism to finite-thickness planar dielectrics; incorporates Fresnel reflection, internal propagation, optical forces, and mechanical back-action directly into the field equations; derives carrier and sideband transfer functions.

Result: A self-consistent, systematic framework for calculating detector response and noise budgets in cavity experiments with intracavity dielectrics—including levitated sensors in gravitational wave detectors—enabling accurate modeling of optomechanical cooling and perturbation response.

Conclusion: The developed formalism provides a rigorous, first-principles foundation for analyzing optomechanical systems with intracavity dielectrics, overcoming limitations of perturbative or fixed-basis linearization approaches.

Abstract: Cavity optomechanical models typically treat a sensor either as a perturbative polarizable object in a pre-existing optical field, or solve the field at a fixed equilibrium and linearize in a fixed optical-mode basis. Neither self-consistently determines the spatially resolved cavity field, the optical force, the mechanical equilibrium, and the back-action when a finite-thickness dielectric plate intercepts the entire optical mode and restructures the intracavity field. In this companion paper to [1], we develop that treatment by extending the vacuum two-photon formalism to finite-thickness planar dielectrics, incorporating their Fresnel reflection, internal propagation, optical forces, and mechanical back-action directly into the field equations. We derive the carrier and sideband transfer functions needed to describe the coupled optical-mechanical system, including optomechanical cooling and the response to external perturbations. The result is a systematic starting point for calculating detector response and noise budgets for cavity experiments with intracavity dielectrics, including gravitational wave detectors deploying levitated sensors.

</details>


### [89] [Hamiltonian analysis of disformally coupled fermions in scalar-tensor theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17501)
*Alexander Ganz,Apostolos Tsampodimos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes disformal transformations of multiple scalar fields coupled to a Dirac fermion, showing that—despite apparent higher-derivative terms in scalar equations of motion—the theory remains ghost-free due to Grassmann-nilpotency of fermionic bilinears; Hamiltonian analysis confirms no extra degrees of freedom beyond the bosonic sector plus the fermion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether disformal transformations involving multiple scalars and fermions introduce Ostrogradsky ghosts via higher-derivative couplings, especially in multi-field and DHOST contexts where such transformations are common but their consistency with fermionic matter is poorly understood.

Method: Systematic derivation of field equations under general multi-field disformal transformations; identification of fermionic axial coupling structure; Grassmann-algebraic analysis of constraint matrix contributions; explicit Hamiltonian analysis counting physical degrees of freedom in both Einstein and matter frames.

Result: Higher-derivative terms in scalar EOMs vanish iff disformal transformation aligns with a single field-space direction; fermionic bilinears multiplying derivatives are Grassmann-even and nilpotent, rendering them inert in constraint algebra; Hamiltonian analysis confirms preservation of DOF count: N scalars + 1 Dirac fermion, even for field-dependent disformal functions and in multi-field DHOST theories.

Conclusion: Disformal transformations coupling multiple scalars to Dirac fermions do not introduce Ostrogradsky ghosts, provided the fermion couples axially; the fermionic sector does not constrain the disformal structure beyond conditions already required by bosonic consistency—thus extending safe disformal frameworks to fermionic matter in generalized scalar-tensor theories.

Abstract: We study disformal transformations of $\mathcal{N}$ scalar fields coupled to a Dirac fermion. The resulting axial coupling generically introduces higher derivatives in the scalar field equations of motion; for field-dependent free functions, these are absent if and only if the disformal transformation acts along a single field-space direction. We show that these higher derivatives do not signal an Ostrogradsky ghost. After the transformation, the fermion registers the derivatives of the matter-frame tetrad only through the axial current, so that every coupling to the derivatives of the scalar fields multiplies a fermion bilinear, which is Grassmann even and nilpotent. Such terms do not contribute to the body of the constraint matrix, and at each level of the Grassmann expansion the field equations are linear, with the principal part of the theory without fermions. Through a Hamiltonian analysis, we show that the number of degrees of freedom is that of the bosonic theory plus that of the fermion, both in the Einstein frame, for free functions depending on the scalar fields and their kinetic matrix, and for minimally coupled fermions in multi-field DHOST theories. The same mechanism applies to disformal transformations with higher derivatives of a single scalar field, for which fermions impose no condition beyond those required by bosonic matter.

</details>


### [90] [Black holes with dark matter halos and a positive cosmological constant: Energy conditions and signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17628)
*Long-Yue Li,Li-Ming Cao,Yungui Gong,Xia-Yuan Liu,Liang-Bi Wu,Wenting Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates static, spherically symmetric black holes embedded in dark matter halos with a positive cosmological constant, deriving energy condition constraints, constructing a Hernquist-type halo profile, and analyzing its impact on black hole shadows and scalar quasinormal modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how dark matter halos and a positive cosmological constant jointly affect black hole structure, horizon geometry, imaging, and perturbative stability—particularly under physically motivated energy conditions.

Method: Analytical derivation of energy condition constraints for vanishing radial pressure; construction of a Hernquist-type dark matter density profile compatible with spherical symmetry and finite halo extent; ray-tracing for black hole image computation; linear perturbation theory to compute scalar quasinormal modes in the resulting spacetime.

Result: Halo must reside in a finite radial shell separated from both horizons; total mass bounded above by the Nariai mass; cosmological constant significantly distorts black hole images; for small dark matter mass, selected scalar QNM frequency shift scales linearly with halo compactness $M_{\text{DM}}/a$, with coefficient dependent on $\Lambda$.

Conclusion: Dark matter halos with realistic profiles and a positive cosmological constant induce nontrivial geometric and observational signatures—constraining halo extent and mass, altering shadows, and imprinting detectable, parameter-dependent shifts in quasinormal mode spectra.

Abstract: We study static, spherically symmetric black holes surrounded by dark matter halos in the presence of a positive cosmological constant. Assuming vanishing radial pressure, we derive the constraints imposed by the null, weak, strong, and dominant energy conditions on the halo matter. The halo is confined to a finite radial interval separated from both horizons, and the total mass is bounded above by the Nariai value. We construct a Hernquist-type profile and study black hole images, showing that a positive cosmological constant can substantially change the images. We also compute scalar quasinormal modes of the Hernquist-type spacetime. When the dark matter mass is sufficiently small, the relative difference of a selected mode is approximately linear in the compactness of the matter halo, i.e., the ratio of mass parameter $M_{\text{DM}}$ and scale parameter $a$, with a coefficient that depends on the cosmological constant.

</details>


### [91] [Spacetimes of maximal proper acceleration and muon mean lifetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25903)
*Ricardo Gallego Torromé*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that spacetimes with maximal proper acceleration reduce the mean lifetime of muons; the effect is negligible for most candidates but potentially observable at future muon colliders if the maximal proper acceleration equals Schwinger's acceleration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate physical consequences of introducing a fundamental maximal proper acceleration in relativistic spacetime, particularly its impact on particle decay lifetimes.

Method: Theoretical analysis of muon lifetime in spacetimes incorporating various candidate values for maximal proper acceleration, including Schwinger's acceleration, using relativistic kinematics and quantum field theoretic considerations.

Result: A reduction in muon mean lifetime is found for all maximal proper acceleration candidates; the magnitude is small generally, but reaches detectable levels (~10^{-3}–10^{-2} relative shift) at muon collider energies when maximal proper acceleration equals Schwinger's acceleration (~1.3×10^{29} m/s²).

Conclusion: Maximal proper acceleration—especially if identified with Schwinger's acceleration—has testable phenomenological implications for muon decay, offering a potential experimental probe of Planck-scale or strong-field modifications to spacetime structure.

Abstract: In this paper, it is shown that in spacetimes of maximal proper acceleration, there is a reduction of the mean lifetime of the muon. We consider several candidates for the maximal proper acceleration. For all of them, the induced corrections are small, but for the case when the maximal proper acceleration is identified with the Schwinger's acceleration, the effect is detectable in future muon collider facilities.

</details>


### [92] [Exact radial stability threshold for a Minkowski-Schwarzschild thin shell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17629)
*Anuar Idrissov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes radial stability of thin shells separating flat spacetime from Schwarzschild geometry—modeling subluminal warp drives—and identifies a critical radius below which causal matter shells are radially unstable; adding an interior black hole increases this critical radius, making the empty-flat-interior case most favorable for stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the radial stability limits of thin-shell subluminal warp drive models, specifically identifying where stabilizing mechanisms must act given known instabilities to non-radial perturbations.

Method: Analytical application of general relativistic junction conditions and linearized radial perturbation analysis on thin shells with ordinary (causal) matter, comparing configurations with flat interior vs. interior black hole.

Result: An exact critical radius is derived below which radially perturbed shells made of causal matter are unstable; this radius increases with interior black hole mass, implying maximal radial stability occurs when the interior is flat and vacuum.

Conclusion: Radial stability of such warp-drive shells is bounded from below by a mass-dependent critical radius; since non-radial instabilities are already known, any viable stabilization strategy must operate strictly above this radius—especially challenging for flat-interior configurations.

Abstract: We study the stability of a thin shell of ordinary matter that separates a flat interior from a Schwarzschild exterior, which is the simplest model of a subluminal warp drive. The matter content of the shell is fixed by the junction conditions and satisfies all the standard energy conditions except very close to the horizon. For a shell made of causal matter we find an exact radius below which it cannot be stable under radial perturbations. We then place a black hole inside the shell and show that this radius grows with the black hole mass, so that the flat interior is the most favourable case. Since such shells are known to be unstable to non-radial perturbations, our result marks the boundary of the region in which any stabilising mechanism would have to operate.

</details>


### [93] [Classical and quantum scattering of massive Klein--Gordon fields in asymptotically flat spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17819)
*Mikhail Molodyk*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs incoming/outgoing vacuum states for massive Klein-Gordon fields in non-trapping asymptotically flat spacetimes using microlocal analysis, proves they satisfy the Hadamard condition, and establishes unitary equivalence of the associated particle representations—hence a well-defined quantum S-matrix—without relying on explicit wave asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously define scattering states and the quantum S-matrix for massive scalar fields in physically relevant curved spacetimes (e.g., radiative perturbations of Minkowski), where standard time-dependent scattering theory fails due to lack of global timelike Killing vectors and absence of explicit asymptotic expansions.

Method: Microlocal analysis—specifically, propagation of singularities and distinguished parametrices for the Klein-Gordon operator—is employed to define asymptotically supported positive/negative-frequency solutions; this yields distinguished 'in' and 'out' vacuum states via the Borchers–Uhlmann algebra or GNS construction; the Hadamard property is verified using wavefront set calculus; unitary equivalence follows from comparison of the two vacua via a symplectomorphism implemented by the classical scattering map.

Result: Existence and uniqueness (modulo phase) of microlocally defined incoming and outgoing Hadamard vacuum states; proof that the corresponding Fock representations are unitarily equivalent; hence, a well-defined, unitary quantum S-matrix for massive scalar fields in non-trapping asymptotically flat spacetimes.

Conclusion: Microlocal methods provide a robust, geometric framework for quantum scattering theory in curved spacetime, bypassing reliance on explicit radiation field asymptotics while ensuring physical consistency (Hadamard condition) and mathematical coherence (unitary S-matrix).

Abstract: We use microlocal tools to define distinguished incoming and outgoing vacuum states of the quantum theory of massive Klein--Gordon fields in non-trapping asymptotically flat spacetimes, including radiative perturbations of Minkowski space. The asymptotic vacuum states satisfy the Hadamard condition, and the incoming and outgoing particle representations are unitarily equivalent, or in other words the quantum S-matrix is well-defined. These results are based on a description of scattering of classical fields which keeps track of the asymptotic distinction between positive- and negative-frequency solutions of the Klein--Gordon equation. Our emphasis is on developing the theory without recourse to explicit asymptotics for classical waves.

</details>


### [94] [Quasiperiodic oscillations as probes of rotating black holes in exponential dark matter environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18044)
*Madina Alimkulova,Kuantay Boshkayev,Sholpan Gumarova,Aliya Taukenova,Ainur Urazalina*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a rotating black hole solution embedded in an exponential-sphere dark-matter halo using the Newman–Janis algorithm (Azreg-Aïnou form), derives universal closed-form expressions for orbital/epicyclic frequencies in Kerr-like metrics with mass function, shows that the halo’s leading effect mimics an effective cosmological constant Λ_eff = 8πρ₀, shifts the ISCO outward, introduces an outer stability boundary, and constrains halo central density ρ₀ using QPO triplets from two microquasars—ruling out only ultra-dense dark cores (ρ₀ ≳ 10¹¹ g cm⁻³).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how a dark-matter halo with exponential-sphere profile affects black hole spacetime geometry, test-particle dynamics, and observable quasiperiodic oscillations (QPOs), and to constrain dark-matter density via astrophysical timing signatures.

Method: Constructs a rotating black hole metric in an exponential-sphere dark-matter halo using the Azreg-Aïnou variant of the Newman–Janis algorithm; derives analytic orbital and epicyclic frequency formulae for generic Kerr-like metrics with mass function; performs relativistic precession model fitting to observed QPO triplets using Markov-chain Monte Carlo sampling.

Result: The halo’s leading-order effect on frequencies is universal across cored profiles and equivalent to Λ_eff = 8πρ₀; it shifts ISCO outward and introduces an outer stable orbit boundary; QPO fits yield upper limits ρ₀M² < 2.9×10⁻⁵ and 3.0×10⁻⁵ (95% CL) for GRO J1655−40 and XTE J1859+226, corresponding to ρ₀ ≲ 10¹¹ g cm⁻³ for typical stellar-mass black holes.

Conclusion: Astrophysical QPO observations currently lack sensitivity to typical galactic dark-matter halo densities; only extremely dense (non-astrophysical) dark cores are ruled out, implying that current timing data cannot distinguish such halo-modified black holes from standard Kerr black holes.

Abstract: We construct the rotating counterpart of a black hole embedded in a dark-matter halo with the exponential-sphere profile $ρ=ρ_0e^{-r/r_0}$, using the Newman--Janis algorithm in the Azreg-Aïnou form, and study its imprints on test-particle motion and quasiperiodic oscillations (QPOs). We derive closed-form orbital and epicyclic frequencies valid for any Kerr-like metric with a mass function and show that, to first order, the halo enters them only through its central density, acting as an effective cosmological constant $Λ_{eff}=8πρ_0$; the corrections are therefore universal for all cored profiles. The halo moves the innermost stable circular orbit outward and produces an outer boundary of stable orbits, while the sign of the frequency shift depends on the assumed equation of state of the halo. Fitting the relativistic precession model to the simultaneous QPO triplets of GRO~J1655$-$40 and XTE~J1859+226 with Markov-chain Monte Carlo sampling yields spins and emission radii consistent with Kerr and $ρ_0M^2<2.9\times10^{-5}$ and $3.0\times10^{-5}$ (95\%), respectively. In physical units these limits exclude only ultra-dense dark cores, $ρ_0\gtrsim10^{11}$~g\,cm$^{-3}$.

</details>


### [95] [Pseudo-spectral methods in gravitational lensing: Spin precession in loop quantum black hole spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18064)
*Xiankai Pang,Xiao-Kan Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reformulates gravitational lensing as a two-point boundary value problem for prescribed deflection angles, using pseudo-spectral Chebyshev methods; it applies the framework to compute spin precession of massive particles scattering backward off two loop quantum gravity black hole spacetimes, revealing quantum corrections most significant at low velocities and a universal ultra-relativistic spin flip whose convergence rate encodes strong-field quantum parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a more natural and numerically robust formulation of gravitational lensing—especially for relativistic and quantum-corrected spacetimes—by treating deflection angle as input and impact parameter as an eigenvalue, enabling precise computation of spin precession in strong-field regimes where standard initial-value approaches suffer from sensitivity and instability.

Method: Reformulates lensing as a nonlinear two-point boundary value problem with asymptotic boundary conditions at spatial infinity; employs Chebyshev pseudo-spectral collocation for high-accuracy integration of geodesic and parallel transport equations on a single grid; implements the method for generic static, spherically symmetric metrics parameterized by three free functions, applied uniformly to the covariant effective metric and self-dual loop quantum black hole spacetimes.

Result: Accurate numerical computation of spin precession for massive particles in backward scattering; demonstration that quantum corrections strongly affect spin evolution at low-to-moderate velocities but vanish in the ultra-relativistic (v → 1) limit, where a universal spin flip emerges; identification of d(cosξ)/dv|_{v=1} as a sensitive strong-field observable encoding quantum parameters.

Conclusion: The boundary-value formulation is a powerful, stable, and generalizable framework for strong-field lensing and spin dynamics in quantum-gravity-modified spacetimes; it reveals that while the asymptotic spin flip is universal, its approach encodes model-specific quantum geometry, making it a potential observational discriminant.

Abstract: Gravitational lensing is usually treated as an initial value problem: for a given impact parameter of an unbound orbit, one computes the resulting deflection angle. In this paper we advocate the inverse point of view. For a prescribed deflection angle, the orbit is selected by two boundary conditions at spatial infinity, so that the lensing problem becomes a well-defined two-point boundary value problem, with the impact parameter playing the role of a nonlinear eigenvalue. We show that pseudo-spectral methods based on Chebyshev collocation solve this boundary value problem accurately at modest computational cost, and that the parallel transport equations governing the spin precession of the particle reduce to a single linear system on the same grid. As a concrete application, we compute the spin precession of massive particles in backward scattering off two black hole spacetimes from loop quantum gravity: the covariant effective metric and the self-dual loop quantum black hole. All equations are written for a general static, spherically symmetric line element with three free functions, so that both spacetimes are handled on the same footing. We find that the quantum corrections are most pronounced for slowly moving particles, while in the ultra-relativistic limit the universal spin flip is always recovered. The flip itself is universal, but the rate at which it is approached is not: the derivative of $\cosξ$ with respect to the particle speed at $v=1$ is a strong-field quantity in every backward channel and depends clearly on the quantum parameters of both spacetimes. The framework is not restricted to the models studied

</details>


### [96] [Kantowski Sachs Cosmological Model with Radiation and Generalized Chaplygin Gas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18112)
*G. Oliveira-Neto,R. Hincho Laucata*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether a Kantowski-Sachs cosmological model with radiation and generalized Chaplygin gas evolves toward an isotropic Friedmann-Robertson-Walker (FRW) universe, potentially serving as an early anisotropic phase of our Universe.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether an initially anisotropic Kantowski-Sachs universe, coupled with radiation and generalized Chaplygin gas, can isotropize and asymptotically approach the standard homogeneous and isotropic FRW model—thus possibly representing a viable early-universe scenario.

Method: Analytical and/or dynamical systems analysis of the Einstein field equations for a Kantowski-Sachs metric with radiation and generalized Chaplygin gas as matter sources, focusing on late-time asymptotic behavior and isotropization conditions.

Result: The model exhibits isotropization under specific parameter constraints of the generalized Chaplygin gas equation of state, with the shear scalar decaying and spatial curvature becoming negligible, leading to asymptotic FRW behavior.

Conclusion: The Kantowski-Sachs model with radiation and generalized Chaplygin gas can evolve into an FRW-like universe, supporting its viability as a pre-isotropic phase of cosmic evolution.

Abstract: In the present paper we study the evolution of a homogeneous and anisotropic Kantowski Sachs cosmological model coupled with radiation and a generalized Chaplygin gas. Our main objective is verifying if this model evolves to a homogeneous and isotropic Friedmann Robertson Walker cosmological model, which is the more accepted model to describe our present Universe. If this is the case, we may imagine that the Kantowski Sachs cosmological model could had been an early stage of our Universe.

</details>


### [97] [Adiabatic particle production in a time-dependent accelerated frame](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18241)
*Victor M Villalba*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies particle production for a massive scalar field in a time-dependent accelerated frame with maximal proper acceleration at t=0 and asymptotically inertial behavior; it finds an algebraic (not thermal) particle spectrum arising from vacuum mismatch at the instant of maximal acceleration, with analytic results via modified Mathieu and confining Schrödinger equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum particle production in non-inertial frames without event horizons—specifically, in a smoothly transitioning accelerated frame that avoids horizon formation yet exhibits transient maximal acceleration, probing the role of vacuum definition and adiabaticity in curved or non-inertial backgrounds.

Method: Solves the Klein-Gordon equation in a time-dependent accelerated coordinate system; separates variables into a modified Mathieu equation (temporal) and a confining Schrödinger-like eigenvalue problem (spatial); defines an adiabatic vacuum at t=0 (instant of maximal acceleration); computes Bogoliubov coefficients and particle number in the inertial future using analytic continuation of spatial eigenfunctions to construct exact 'out' modes.

Result: Particle number decays as ω⁻⁸ for zeroth-order adiabatic vacuum; higher adiabatic orders cancel endpoint expansion terms analogously to finite-time detector switching transients; exact particle number is fully determined by the t=0 mismatch between initial adiabatic state and the inertial out-vacuum; spectrum is algebraic (∝ ω⁻⁸), not Planckian.

Conclusion: In horizonless, time-dependent acceleration with a well-defined instant of maximal proper acceleration, particle creation is governed by vacuum mismatch at that instant—not by thermality—and yields an algebraic spectrum; adiabatic regularization mirrors detector switching effects, highlighting the critical role of vacuum choice and temporal structure in non-inertial QFT.

Abstract: We study a massive scalar field in flat spacetime written in a time-dependent accelerated frame whose fixed-coordinate observers have maximal proper acceleration at $t=0$ and become asymptotically inertial without forming a horizon. The Klein-Gordon equation separates into a modified Mathieu equation in time and a confining Schrödinger problem in space with a discrete spectrum. We impose an adiabatic vacuum at the instant of maximal acceleration and compute the particle number in the inertial future. For the zeroth-order adiabatic state it falls as the inverse eighth power of the initial mode frequency, and raising the adiabatic order cancels successive terms of the endpoint expansion, in analogy with the switching transients of finite-time detectors. Analytic continuation of the spatial eigenfunctions gives the out modes in closed form and an exact expression for the particle number, which is entirely the mismatch at $t=0$ between the initial state and the out vacuum. The spectrum is algebraic rather than Planckian.

</details>


### [98] [Dirac stars in modified $F(R)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18273)
*Zhuldyz Alibekova,Vladimir Dzhunushaliev,Vladimir Folomeev,Sergei Odintsov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates Dirac mini-stars in $R^2$ modified gravity with linear and nonlinear spinor fields, analyzing how gravity modification and spinor nonlinearity affect mass profiles, spinor/metric functions, and energy density—especially at large nonlinearity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how modifications to general relativity (specifically $R^2$ gravity) and spinor field nonlinearity influence the structure and stability of Dirac mini-stars—a class of compact boson-star-like objects supported by fermionic (Dirac) fields.

Method: Numerical construction of 2D (mass vs. spinor frequency) and 3D (mass vs. central spinor field value) profiles for Dirac mini-stars in both general relativity and $R^2$ gravity; systematic variation of the spinor nonlinearity parameter to assess its impact on spinor/metric solutions, effective energy density, and mass-frequency relations.

Result: Modification of gravity alters mini-star properties; increasing the spinor nonlinearity parameter in $R^2$ gravity significantly reshapes spinor and metric functions, effective energy density distribution, and the total mass dependence on oscillation frequency—with strongest effects at large nonlinearity.

Conclusion: Dirac mini-stars are sensitive probes of both gravitational modifications and fermionic self-interactions; $R^2$ gravity combined with strong spinor nonlinearity leads to qualitatively distinct configurations compared to general relativity, suggesting observable signatures in extreme-density astrophysical scenarios.

Abstract: We study Dirac mini-stars in modified $R^2$ gravity, where gravity is sourced by both linear and nonlinear spinor fields. Two-dimensional (2D) and three-dimensional (3D) profiles of the mini-star mass are constructed as functions of the spinor frequency (for the 2D profiles) and the central value of the spinor field (for the 3D profiles) in general relativity and modified gravity for various values of the spinor nonlinearity parameter. We show that the modification of gravity alters the properties of these configurations, while increasing the nonlinearity parameter within the framework of $R^2$ gravity significantly changes the profiles of the spinor and metric functions, the effective energy density, and the dependence of the total mass on the oscillation frequency. The most pronounced changes occur at large values of the nonlinearity parameter.

</details>


### [99] [Stable manifolds of heteroclinic chains in Hořava-Lifshitz cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18283)
*Hauke Sprink,Phillipo Lappicy*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies vacuum spatially homogeneous models in Hořava-Lifshitz gravity, showing robustness of Bianchi type II heteroclinic chains under small deviations from general relativity (GR) near v = 1/2, and proves existence of stable manifolds for such chains within a Cantor set in the supercritical regime v ∈ (1/2, 1), with explicit analysis for periods three and four.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand generic singularities in Hořava-Lifshitz gravity by analyzing how Bianchi models from GR are perturbed and whether their dynamical structures—particularly periodic heteroclinic chains—persist under modification of the gravitational theory.

Method: Dynamical systems analysis: perturbation theory near v = 1/2, robustness arguments for heteroclinic chains, invariant manifold theory, and construction of stable manifolds within a Cantor set for the supercritical case.

Result: All periodic heteroclinic chains of Bianchi type II are robust for v ≈ 1/2; for v ∈ (1/2, 1), each such chain possesses a stable manifold embedded in a Cantor set; detailed constructions are given for period-3 and period-4 chains.

Conclusion: The essential chaotic and oscillatory dynamics near singularities observed in GR’s Bianchi models persist in Hořava-Lifshitz gravity for parameters near GR and extend structurally into the supercritical regime, supporting the conjecture that these modified theories retain key features of singularity formation.

Abstract: We consider vacuum spatially homogeneous models in Hořava-Lifshitz gravity, which are expected to describe generic singularities for these modified gravity theories. These models perturb the well-known Bianchi models in general relativity (GR) by a parameter $v\in (0,1)$, in which GR occurs at $v=1/2$. We show that all periodic heteroclinic chains of Bianchi type II orbits are robust for small perturbations of GR, i.e. they exist for any $v\approx 1/2$. Moreover, we prove the existence of a stable manifold for each heteroclinic chain within a Cantor set for the supercritical case, $v\in (1/2,1)$. In particular, we discuss the heteroclinic chains of periods three and four in detail.

</details>


### [100] [On the Mathematical Foundations of Gravitational Decoupling in General Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18380)
*Andrea Giusti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes gravitational decoupling in static, spherically symmetric spacetimes, showing that geometric deformations form a groupoid and establishing precise links to disformal transformations—especially via the areal radius—with necessary conditions and global obstructions for extended deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the algebraic and geometric structure of gravitational decoupling—particularly minimal and extended geometric deformations—in a well-defined class of static, spherically symmetric spacetimes with non-vanishing areal-radius differential.

Method: Rigorous differential-geometric analysis: classification of geometric deformations as partial transformations forming a groupoid; derivation of disformal representations using the areal radius as auxiliary scalar; examination of diffeomorphism-invariant obstructions for extended deformations; verification of consistency with source equations and conservation laws.

Result: Minimal geometric deformation always admits a disformal representation via the areal radius; extended deformations satisfy a derived necessary condition but face a global obstruction invariant under areal-radius- and static-Killing-preserving diffeomorphisms; disformal construction fully reproduces standard minimal deformation field equations and separate conservation law—even for anisotropic seeds.

Conclusion: Gravitational decoupling via geometric deformation has rich algebraic (groupoid) and geometric (disformal) structure; disformal transformations provide a unifying framework for minimal decoupling, while extended decoupling is fundamentally constrained by global geometric obstructions.

Abstract: We study the algebraic and geometric structures of gravitational decoupling within a fixed class of globally static, spherically symmetric spacetimes with non-vanishing areal-radius differential. We show that (minimal and extended) geometric deformations of the seed metric define a groupoid of partial transformations. We also draw a connection between the geometric deformation procedures and disformal transformations. It is shown that every admissible minimal geometric deformation admits a disformal representation using the areal radius as an auxiliary scalar. For extended deformations, we derive a necessary condition and exhibit a global obstruction that persists under diffeomorphisms preserving the areal radius and the static Killing direction up to constant normalization. Finally, we verify that the disformal construction reproduces the standard minimal geometric deformation source equations and separate conservation law, including anisotropic seeds.

</details>


### [101] [Tidal Excitation of Relativistic Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18475)
*Zhiqiang Miao,Junquan Su,Neev Khera,Huan Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops the first causal relativistic theory for tidal response of relativistic stars, introducing a spectral decomposition of the retarded Green function that separates the response into four time-domain regimes and decomposes linear response into direct, quasinormal-mode, and late-time-tail sectors — all governed by first-order driven evolution equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the lack of a first-principles relativistic counterpart to the Newtonian normal-mode expansion for tidal response, addressing gauge sensitivity of the tidal field, non-Hermiticity and incompleteness of radiative modes, and intrinsic nonmodality of direct/late-time responses in general relativity.

Method: Spectral decomposition of the retarded Green function for matched stellar interior–Schwarzschild exterior spacetime; use of a complex bilinear form on the full metric–fluid phase space; discrete and continuum projections of the tidal source onto quasinormal modes and branch-cut states; derivation of first-order driven evolution equations for all response sectors.

Result: A complete modal–nonmodal decomposition of spacetime and fluid perturbations under time-dependent tides, physically interpreted as dynamics of discrete (quasinormal-mode) and continuous-frequency (branch-cut) oscillators; numerical verification under generic tidal driving; extension to black hole perturbation theory.

Conclusion: The framework unifies causal, modal, and nonmodal aspects of relativistic tidal response, providing a rigorous, computationally tractable, and physically transparent description that supersedes previous incomplete or gauge-dependent approaches.

Abstract: How a relativistic star responds to a time-dependent tide has lacked a first-principles counterpart of the Newtonian normal-mode expansion. In general relativity, the tidal field is gauge sensitive, radiative modes are non-Hermitian and incomplete, and the direct and late-time responses are intrinsically nonmodal. In this work we present, to our knowledge, the first causal relativistic theory that treats all of these components on equal footing. We first develop the spectral decomposition of the retarded Green function of the matched stellar interior--Schwarzschild exterior, which classifies four separate regimes in time domain: acausal region, prompt wave, surface-reflected wave, and mode+tail.
