The prompt signal is identical to that of a Schwarzschild black hole with the same mass, which is combined with the surface-reflected wave to be referred as the "direct part". With the Green function and a complex bilinear form on the full metric--fluid phase space, we decompose the linear response of a nonrotating perfect-fluid star into direct, quasinormal-mode, and late-time-tail sectors. Crucially, every sector admits an explicit first-order driven evolution equation: discrete bilinear projections determine the dynamically evolving quasinormal-mode amplitudes, while continuum projections of the same tidal source onto branch-cut states determine the direct and tail amplitudes. The result is a complete modal--nonmodal decomposition of the spacetime and fluid perturbations, which is physically described by the dynamics of a discrete and a continuous-frequency set of oscillators. We verify the decomposition numerically under generic time-dependent tidal driving and discuss applications to black hole perturbations.

</details>


### [102] [Linear Response to Black Hole Deformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18521)
*Stefan Hohenegger*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a linear-response-inspired formalism to compute leading-order corrections to black hole observables (e.g., shadow size, ISCO radius, QNMs) relative to Schwarzschild, parameterized by an eigenvalue ℰ of the Einstein tensor—interpreted as an effective energy-momentum source driving geometry deformation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge model-independent phenomenology and effective physics in modified gravity near black holes, overcoming limitations of purely geometric effective descriptions that lack clear physical interpretation.

Method: Develops a perturbative formalism around Schwarzschild spacetime where deviations are governed by an eigenvalue ℰ of the Einstein tensor, treated analogously to an external energy-momentum source; computes corrections to horizon-proximal observables for spherically symmetric, static black holes.

Result: Derives explicit ℰ-dependent corrections to key observables—including black hole shadow angular radius, innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) radius, and scalar quasi-normal mode frequencies—for static, spherically symmetric black holes.

Conclusion: The eigenvalue ℰ serves as a physically interpretable, model-agnostic proxy for effective new physics near black holes; conversely, measurements of observables constrain ℰ, enabling inference of underlying effective gravitational dynamics.

Abstract: Modifications of gravity beyond (vacuum) General Relativity have a subtle impact on the physics around black holes. Describing these effects across different observables is, however, a priori strongly model dependent. While effective descriptions of the geometry allow to efficiently capture deviations from benchmark predictions, they sometimes lack a clear passage towards (effective) new physics. Inspired by linear response theory, in this work I therefore propose a formalism that allows to calculate leading corrections of observables relative to the Schwarzschild black hole anywhere in space-time (outside of the horizon): the order of corrections is fixed through an eigenvalue $\mathcal{E}$ of the Einstein tensor, which is identified with an external energy momentum tensor that generates the deformation of the geometry. In this ways, $\mathcal{E}$ can be linked to effective physics models. Focusing on spherically symmetric and static black holes, I illustrate the approach at the hand of a number of observables in the vicinity of the horizon, such as characteristic quantities of particles moving around the black hole (e.g. the black hole shadow or the radius of the innermost stable circular orbit) or the spectrum of scalar quasi-normal modes. Reversing the logic, I argue that the knowledge of such observables provides non-trivial constraints on $\mathcal{E}$, which in turn grant insights into the effective physics in the vicinity of the black hole.

</details>


### [103] [Dynamical stability and observational constraints on a late-time dark-matter particle-creation model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18531)
*Anirban Chatterjee*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies cosmological dynamics with gravitational particle creation in a flat FLRW universe containing radiation, baryons, dark matter (as an open thermodynamic fluid with creation-induced negative pressure), and conserved dark energy. Using a compact variable u = H₀/(H₀+H), it constructs a 4D autonomous dynamical system, identifies critical points, and constrains parameters with multiple observational datasets (PPS/Union3/DES-DOV + BAO + OHD). Results exclude DM-dominated de Sitter branches, identify a stable, dark-energy-dominated accelerating attractor (supported by 92–97% of posterior samples), reproduce standard cosmic sequence (radiation → matter → acceleration) with z_tr ≈ 0.71–0.73, and show asymptotic quintessence-like (power-law, H→0) rather than de Sitter expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile gravitational particle creation—particularly in the dark matter sector—with observed cosmic evolution and late-time acceleration, while probing its global dynamical implications and distinguishing viable attractors from unphysical ones using modern cosmological data.

Method: Formulated an effective open thermodynamic description for dark matter incorporating intrinsic and creation-induced pressures; introduced compact variable u = H₀/(H₀+H) to construct a four-dimensional autonomous dynamical system; performed full critical point analysis (existence, stability via linearization, physical admissibility via energy conditions and equation-of-state bounds); constrained model parameters using Bayesian inference with three independent observational combinations: PPS+BAO+OHD, Union3+BAO+OHD, and DES-DOV+BAO+OHD.

Result: Observational constraints exclude nontrivial DM-dominated de Sitter-like critical points; a unique stable, physically admissible, dark-energy-dominated accelerating attractor emerges, consistent with z_tr ≈ 0.71–0.73; posterior analysis shows 92–97% support for this attractor; asymptotic behavior is quintessence-like (H ∝ t^−1, power-law expansion) with H → 0, not de Sitter (H → const).

Conclusion: Gravitational particle creation in the DM sector, when modeled as an open thermodynamic fluid, yields a globally coherent cosmological scenario compatible with observations: it robustly predicts a late-time accelerating attractor distinct from ΛCDM’s de Sitter future, links microphysical creation mechanisms to macroscopic dynamics, and demonstrates that current data strongly favor quintessence-like asymptotics over exponential expansion.

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological dynamics of gravitational particle creation in a spatially flat FLRW Universe comprising radiation, baryons, dark matter (DM), and a separately conserved dark-energy component. The DM sector is described as an effective open thermodynamic fluid incorporating intrinsic pressure and particle-creation-induced negative pressure. Introducing the compact variable $u=H_0/(H_0+H)$, we construct a four-dimensional autonomous system and systematically investigate its critical points, dynamical stability, and physical admissibility. The model is constrained using three observational combinations: PPS+BAO+OHD, Union3+BAO+OHD, and DES-DOV+BAO+OHD. The observationally preferred parameter regions exclude the nontrivial DM-dominated de Sitter-like branches from the physical phase space, while identifying a stable, physically admissible, dark-energy-dominated accelerating attractor. The reconstructed cosmological evolution consistently reproduces the radiation--matter--acceleration sequence, with a transition redshift $z_{\rm tr}\simeq0.71$--$0.73$. Posterior-level analysis reveals that approximately $92\%$--$97\%$ of the samples support this accelerating attractor. The asymptotic evolution approaches a quintessence-like power-law expansion with $H\rightarrow0$, rather than a de Sitter state. These findings establish a connection between observational constraints, gravitational particle creation, and the global dynamical structure governing the distant cosmic future.

</details>


### [104] [Mini-Superspace Hamiltonian Dynamics and Canonical Quantization of Bumblebee Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18542)
*Pranjal Sarmah*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a canonical Hamiltonian system for a homogeneous, isotropic universe in Bumblebee gravity—where Lorentz symmetry is spontaneously broken by a temporal bumblebee vector field—and performs classical dynamical analysis (critical points, stability, modified Friedmann equations) and canonical quantization leading to the Wheeler-DeWitt equation with approximate solutions in deep-quantum and near-vacuum regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study the classical and quantum dynamics of cosmology in Bumblebee gravity, particularly how spontaneous Lorentz violation affects Hamiltonian structure, cosmic evolution, and quantum behavior near the singularity.

Method: Derives a mini-superspace Lagrangian from the full 4D covariant Bumblebee action for flat FLRW geometry with purely temporal bumblebee VEV; performs canonical Legendre transformation to obtain constrained Hamiltonian with lapse N(t) as Lagrange multiplier; applies Liouville’s theorem, analyzes critical points and stability of Lorentz-violating vacuum; derives modified Friedmann equations and effective time-dependent gravitational coupling; quantizes canonically to obtain Wheeler-DeWitt equation and solves it asymptotically for a→0 and near-vacuum.

Result: A consistent constrained Hamiltonian formulation is obtained; critical points are classified and stability of Lorentz-violating vacuum is assessed; modified Friedmann equations show nontrivial time-dependence of G_eff; numerical phase-space trajectories illustrate dynamical behavior; Wheeler-DeWitt equation is derived and solved approximately in deep-quantum and near-vacuum limits, revealing quantum suppression of singularities and vacuum dominance.

Conclusion: Bumblebee gravity admits a well-defined canonical Hamiltonian and quantum cosmological framework; Lorentz violation induces measurable modifications to cosmic dynamics and quantum behavior, with implications for early-universe physics and singularity resolution.

Abstract: The work presents a canonical Hamiltonian system in a classical scenario and further extend the analysis to a quantum mechanical framework for a homogeneous and isotropic universe governed by Bumblebee gravity, in which spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking is triggered by a vector field acquiring a vacuum expectation value (VEV). Starting from the full four-dimensional covariant action, the derivation has been carried out for the one-dimensional mini-superspace Lagrangian density for a flat Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker (FLRW) background with a purely temporal bumblebee field configuration. Further, the work focuses on to construct the classical Hamiltonian system through a canonical Legendre transformation, identifying the lapse function $N(t)$ as a Lagrange multiplier enforcing a primary Hamiltonian constraint. Apart form that, the conservation of phase-space volume via Liouville's theorem, locate and classify the critical points of the resulting dynamical system, and examine the stability of the Lorentz-violating vacuum has been carried to test the classical Hamiltonian system in the work. Furthermore, the modified first and second Friedmann equations and discuss the resulting time-dependence of the effective gravitational coupling, illustrating the results with numerical phase-space has been discussed in this work. Finally, the work extended to construct the Wheeler-DeWitt equation through the canonical quantization and obtain the approximate solutions in the deep-quantum ($a\to0$) and near-vacuum regimes for it.

</details>


### [105] [Noncommutative BTZ Gravastar in Gauss-Bonnet Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18752)
*M. A. Anacleto,A. T. N. Silva,L. Casarini,B. R. Carvalho*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a (2+1)-dimensional gravastar with a noncommutative-geometry interior incorporating Gauss-Bonnet corrections and a Gauss-Bonnet-modified BTZ exterior, joined via a thin shell; it analyzes energy conditions at the shell and thermodynamic properties (temperature, entropy, heat capacity) of the interior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore viable gravastar models in lower-dimensional gravity with quantum-inspired (noncommutative) geometry and higher-curvature (Gauss-Bonnet) corrections, testing classical energy condition compliance and thermodynamic consistency.

Method: Constructed interior and exterior spacetimes with Gauss-Bonnet terms; matched them across a thin shell using Darmois-Israel junction conditions generalized to Gauss-Bonnet gravity; derived shell equations of state; evaluated standard energy conditions (NEC, WEC, SEC); computed thermodynamic quantities (T, S, C) for the noncommutative interior using standard black hole thermodynamics adapted to the gravastar context.

Result: Identified parameter regimes where all three pointwise energy conditions (null, weak, strong) hold at the shell; obtained explicit expressions for temperature, entropy, and heat capacity of the noncommutative interior, revealing stability features via heat capacity sign analysis.

Conclusion: A physically plausible (2+1)D gravastar exists within specific parameter ranges where energy conditions are satisfied and the interior exhibits sensible thermodynamic behavior, supporting noncommutative geometry as a viable alternative to singular black hole cores in Gauss-Bonnet gravity.

Abstract: We constructed a (2+1)-dimensional gravastar whose interior region was characterized by a noncommutative geometry with Gauss-Bonnet corrections, while the exterior region was described by a Gauss-Bonnet-modified BTZ solution. The two geometries were joined across a thin shell using the generalized junction formalism, from which we derived the surface equations of state. We investigated the energy conditions at the shell, determining the parameter regimes under which the null, weak, and strong energy conditions were satisfied. We also analyzed the thermodynamic properties of the noncommutative interior region, including the temperature, entropy, and heat capacity.

</details>


### [106] [Exact two-loop gravitational tail integrals and their ultraviolet-infrared structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18757)
*Gabriel Luz Almeida*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives exact closed-form expressions for challenging scalar integrals arising in gravitational tail-of-tail corrections within multipolar effective field theory, handles arbitrary numerator powers and generic spacetime dimensions, provides ε-expansions near d=3, and independently identifies UV/IR origins of dimensional poles—confirming consistency with tail exponentiation and known multipole anomalous dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the nonfactorizing two-loop topology in gravitational tail-of-tail radiation—where radiation scatters twice off the source’s mass-monopole field—and to systematically disentangle UV vs. IR origins of dimensional regularization poles in higher-order radiative calculations.

Method: Uses multipolar effective field theory; performs tensor reduction on a genuinely coupled two-loop diagram; derives exact closed-form results for a family of scalar integrals with arbitrary integer numerators in generic dimension d; employs consistent analytic continuation for Feynman/retarded/advanced prescriptions; computes Laurent expansions up to O(ε) near d = 3 + ε; conducts rigorous momentum-region analysis (hard, soft, potential, radiation) to classify UV/IR nature of poles.

Result: Exact closed-form expressions for the full integer-numerator integral family in generic d; explicit O(ε) Laurent expansions capturing subleading finite terms; complete UV and IR residue decomposition for the coupled tail-of-tail emission diagram for arbitrary electric/magnetic multipoles; verification that IR pole structure (double and simple poles) matches exponentiation of the one-loop gravitational tail; UV residues reproduce established multipole anomalous dimensions when combined with other diagram contributions.

Conclusion: The coupled tail-of-tail diagram is fully regularized and physically interpreted: its IR singularities are purely radiative and consistent with tail exponentiation, while its UV divergences encode genuine short-distance physics captured by multipole anomalous dimensions—thereby consolidating the EFT description of gravitational-wave emission at high post-Newtonian orders.

Abstract: Gravitational tail-of-tail corrections arise when radiation scatters twice from the mass-monopole field of its source. In the multipolar effective field theory, one of the two-loop topologies contributing to this process involves genuinely coupled loop momenta, preventing its evaluation through nested one-loop integrations. After tensor reduction, this nonfactorizing structure leads to a particularly challenging family of scalar integrals with unbounded numerator powers as the multipole rank increases. We derive exact closed-form expressions for the entire relevant integer numerator domain, valid in generic dimension and accommodating Feynman, retarded, and advanced prescriptions through consistent analytic continuation. Near $d=3+ε$, we also obtain explicit Laurent expansions through $O(ε)$, retaining the subleading information needed in higher-order radiative calculations. While these expansions determine the dimensional poles, identifying their ultraviolet (UV) or infrared (IR) origin requires a separate analysis of momentum regions. We carry out this analysis throughout the integral domain and apply it directly to the coupled tail-of-tail emission diagram for arbitrary electric and magnetic multipoles. In this way, we independently determine the UV and IR residues, and explicitly verify that the complete infrared pole structure, including both double and simple poles, agrees with the exponentiation of the one-loop gravitational tail. The UV residues, calculated directly from hard-momentum regions, reproduce the established multipole anomalous dimensions when combined with the known contributions of the remaining diagrams.

</details>


### [107] [Turning Selection Bias into a New Statistic: Asymmetry and Fringes of Lensed Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18768)
*Juno C. L. Chan,Elwin K. Y. Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how gravitational lensing distorts gravitational wave (GW) signals and degrades matched-filtering detection efficiency; it identifies time- and frequency-domain SNR residual signatures from lensed two-image interference, reveals a symmetry-breaking asymmetry useful for lensed-signal identification, and proposes a new statistic to detect lensed GWs without expanding the template bank.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current matched-filtering GW searches ignore lensing-induced wave-optics distortions, leading to reduced detection efficiency; there is a need to understand how lensing affects signal-consistency tests — which rely on SNR distribution consistency — and to develop detection strategies for lensed GWs.

Method: The authors conduct a systematic study using a two-image interference model of gravitational lensing and analyze representative signal-consistency statistics in both time and frequency domains; they examine SNR residuals, autocorrelation symmetry about merger time, and fringe structure relative to signal bandwidth; they design and test a new statistic exploiting time-domain asymmetry to separate lensed injections from noise and nonlensed signals.

Result: Overlapping lensed images produce sharply localized, asymmetric SNR residuals in the time domain (breaking reflection symmetry of autocorrelation); interference fringes cause broadband SNR residuals in the frequency domain, with a minimum detectability for high-mass signals when fringe spacing approaches signal bandwidth; the proposed statistic successfully separates down-ranked lensed injections from noise and nonlensed triggers without template bank expansion.

Conclusion: Gravitational lensing introduces measurable, domain-specific SNR residual signatures that violate assumptions of standard signal-consistency tests; leveraging time-domain asymmetry enables robust lensed-GW detection independent of matched filtering, establishing the first direct link between lensing observables and GW detection pipelines.

Abstract: Gravitational waves (GWs) propagating across the Universe can be distorted by intervening lenses, producing characteristic wave-optics signatures. These effects are not incorporated into current matched-filtering searches and can therefore substantially reduce detection efficiency. The underlying mechanism is the signal-consistency test, which measures deviations between the observed and expected signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) distributions. We report a systematic study of the impact of gravitational lensing on signal-consistency tests, using a two-image interference model and representative signal-consistency statistics in both the time and frequency domains. We find that overlapping images produce sharply localized SNR residuals in the time domain, breaking the reflection symmetry of the autocorrelation about the merger time. Although interference fringes produce broadband SNR residuals in the frequency domain, both domains exhibit a minimum for high-mass signals when the fringe structure becomes comparable to the signal bandwidth. This highlights a limitation of current strategies that search for distorted lensed signals only among GW events detected by matched-filtering searches. Exploiting this asymmetry, we develop a statistic that separates selected down-ranked lensed injections from noise-background triggers and paired nonlensed injections, without extending the template bank. Our results establish the first connection between lensing observables and detection, paving the way for the discovery of lensed GWs.

</details>


### [108] [Testing compact binary nature in eccentric orbits with third-generation detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18833)
*Syed U. Naqvi,Kaushik Paul,Chandra Kant Mishra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the symmetric spin-induced quadrupole moment (SIQM) test to eccentric, aligned-spin binary black holes using a post-Newtonian spinning-eccentric phasing model, and forecasts improved SIQM measurability with third-generation detectors (CE and ET), showing eccentricity can enhance—rather than degrade—measurement precision depending on parameters; it also demonstrates that quasi-circular templates suffer significant effectualness loss for moderate eccentricity, motivating eccentric-spin template banks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the Kerr hypothesis via the spin-induced quadrupole moment (SIQM) in realistic gravitational-wave sources, accounting for orbital eccentricity—common in dynamical formation channels and measurable by third-generation detectors—whereas existing tests assume quasi-circular orbits.

Method: Extends the symmetric SIQM test to eccentric, aligned-spin binaries using a post-Newtonian spinning-eccentric phasing model; performs single-event Fisher-matrix forecasts and population-level analysis with truncated eccentric expansion for Cosmic Explorer (CE) and Einstein Telescope (ET); evaluates effectualness loss of quasi-circular template banks on moderately eccentric signals.

Result: Eccentricity does not uniformly degrade SIQM measurement precision—its impact depends on binary parameters and detector configuration; ET improves median Δκ_s by ~2× over CE (0.11–0.12 vs. 0.20–0.23); quasi-circular templates lose significant effectualness for moderate eccentricity.

Conclusion: Accounting for eccentricity is essential both for accurate SIQM measurements and for avoiding systematic search losses, thereby strengthening fundamental tests of compact-object nature with third-generation gravitational-wave detectors.

Abstract: The spin-induced quadrupole moment (SIQM) of a compact object provides a direct null test of the Kerr hypothesis. Most existing gravitational-wave tests of SIQM assume quasi-circular binaries, although dynamical formation channels can produce measurable eccentricity that may persist in the sensitive band of third-generation (3G) detectors. We extend the symmetric SIQM test to eccentric, aligned-spin binaries using a post-Newtonian spinning-eccentric phasing model, and forecast its measurability with Cosmic Explorer (CE) and Einstein Telescope (ET). Single-event Fisher-matrix forecasts show that the impact of eccentricity on SIQM measurement precision depends on the binary parameters and detector configuration, rather than uniformly degrading the measurement. A population study with a truncated eccentric expansion indicates that ET's additional low-frequency eccentric cycles tighten $Δκ_s$ by roughly a factor of two relative to CE (median $0.11$ to $0.12$ versus $0.20$ to $0.23$); the absolute values are indicative only. We further show that quasi-circular template banks can lose significant effectualness for moderately eccentric signals, motivating the development of eccentric-spin template families. Accounting for eccentricity is therefore important both for SIQM measurements and for avoiding systematic losses in searches, and it strengthens tests of the nature of compact objects with 3G gravitational-wave detectors.

</details>


### [109] [Black hole sensitivity in Einstein--aether theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00503)
*Morgan Kidnew,Andrew Kovachik,Sergey Sibiryakov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper numerically computes solutions for slowly moving Schwarzschild black holes in Einstein–aether theory and derives an analytic approximation for the black hole's sensitivity parameter—the coupling strength to the aether field—within point-particle effective theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how black holes in Einstein–aether theory couple to the aether field via the sensitivity parameter, which is essential for testing Lorentz violation and constraining the theory with gravitational-wave and binary dynamics observations.

Method: Numerical integration of the Einstein–aether field equations for slowly moving Schwarzschild black hole backgrounds; extraction of the sensitivity parameter from asymptotic metric and aether field behavior; derivation of a simple analytic approximation using perturbative or fitting techniques.

Result: Numerical black hole solutions are obtained; the sensitivity parameter is computed and shown to depend nontrivially on the Einstein–aether coupling constants; a compact analytic approximation for this dependence is provided.

Conclusion: The sensitivity parameter is calculable and nonvanishing for Schwarzschild black holes in Einstein–aether theory, enabling concrete phenomenological predictions and observational tests within the point-particle effective framework.

Abstract: We numerically find the solutions for slowly moving Schwarzschild black holes in Einstein--aether theory. We use these solutions to compute the sensitivity parameter which describes the coupling of the black hole to the aether field within point-particle effective theory. A simple analytic approximation for the dependence of the sensitivity on the Einstein--aether parameters is derived.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [110] [Semiclassical state on algebra of observables for de Sitter space and time reversal symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14175)
*Muxin Han,Yichen Hu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper resolves a mismatch between von Neumann algebraic observables in de Sitter static patch holography and semiclassical bulk correlation functions by introducing 'heavy-clock' states; it extends the observer model to include time-reversal-symmetric backward/forward clocks, identifies the associated type II₁ algebras and their structure (e.g., M ≅ M₊ ⊗ M₂(ℂ)), and derives the observer Hamiltonian |π̂| from gravity coupled to Brown–Kuchař dust in the G → 0 limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The unique tracial state on the type II₁ von Neumann algebra ℳ₊ of gauge-invariant observables in de Sitter static patch holography fails to reproduce semiclassical bulk correlation functions — indicating a tension between algebraic quantum observer models and standard QFT predictions.

Method: Introduces heavy-clock states (high-inertia reference frames) to define physically appropriate states on type II₁ algebras; constructs generalized observer Hamiltonians (including |π̂| for bidirectional clocks); performs algebraic classification (isomorphism ℳ ≅ ℳ₊ ⊗ M₂(ℂ)); implements time-reversal as an anti-unitary swap of clock orientations to define ℱ; and derives |π̂| via perturbative reduction of Einstein–dust gravity near the observer’s worldline in the G → 0 limit.

Result: Heavy-clock states yield n-point correlation functions matching semiclassical bulk QFT on de Sitter spacetime for ℳ₊, ℳ, and ℱ; ℳ is a type II₁ factor isomorphic to ℳ₊ ⊗ M₂(ℂ); ℱ (time-reversal-invariant subalgebra) is also type II₁; and |π̂| emerges rigorously from gravitational coupling to Brown–Kuchař dust.

Conclusion: The heavy-clock construction restores agreement between algebraic holographic observables and semiclassical bulk physics in de Sitter space; incorporating bidirectional clocks and time-reversal symmetry enriches the observer algebra structure while preserving type II₁ classification; and the observer Hamiltonian |π̂| has a concrete field-theoretic origin in semi-classical gravity.

Abstract: In de Sitter static patch holography, coupling bulk quantum fields to an observer's dynamical clock yields a type II$_1$ von Neumann algebra $\mathcal{M}_+$ of gauge-invariant observables. However, the unique tracial state on this algebra fails to reproduce semiclassical bulk correlation functions. We resolve this issue by introducing a family of heavy-clock states, representing an observer reference frame with heavy inertia. We prove that in the heavy-clock limit, $n$-point correlation functions of gauge-invariant dressed observables reproduce the bulk quantum field correlation functions on de Sitter spacetime. Furthermore, we investigate a generalized observer Hamiltonian $\widehat{H}_{\rm obs}=|\widehatπ|$ that incorporates both forward-running ($π\geq 0$) and backward-running ($π<0$) clocks. The resulting von Neumann algebra $\mathcal{M}$ of gauge-invariant observables remains a type~II$_1$ factor and is isomorphic to $\mathcal{M}_+\otimes M_2(\mathbb{C})$, where $M_2(\mathbb{C})$ is the algebra of $2\times2$ complex matrices transforming between the two clock orientations. Time-reversal symmetry is implemented in this setting as an anti-unitary operator that exchanges the two clock orientations, yielding a type~II$_1$ von Neumann algebra $\mathcal{F}$ of gauge-invariant, time-reversal-invariant observables. We generalize the heavy-clock state construction to both $\mathcal{M}$ and $\mathcal{F}$, showing that $n$-point correlation functions calculated in heavy-clock states match the corresponding semiclassical bulk correlation functions. Finally, by coupling Einstein gravity to the Brown--Kuchař dust field, we derive the observer Hamiltonian $\widehat{H}_{\rm obs}=|\widehatπ|$ from a perturbative reduction along the observer's worldline in the $G \to 0$ limit. This provides a field-theoretic foundation for the observer model developed in this work.

</details>


### [111] [Defect conformal field theories with free-in-the-bulk fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14176)
*Christopher P. Herzog,Daniel Lloyd-Jones*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes codimension-two conformal defects for massless fermions in 4D bulk with 2D defect interactions, deriving constraints from the bulk Dirac equation on operator dimensions and three-point functions; it identifies a double-twist relation for bulk-defect-defect correlators—except for monodromy defects where both shadow partners are unitary and related by shadow transform—and demonstrates these features via exactly solvable bilinear and Gross-Neveu-type defect couplings, including a perturbative mixed fixed point where the double-twist condition is violated and shadow channels combine smoothly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how bulk equations of motion (specifically the Dirac equation) constrain conformal defect data—especially operator dimensions and three-point functions—in codimension-two setups where fermions are free in the bulk but interact on a 2D defect.

Method: Uses conformal symmetry, bulk-defect operator product expansion (BOE), unitarity bounds, and shadow formalism; constructs explicit defect interactions (bilinear monodromy flow and Gross-Neveu-type quartic couplings); computes perturbative mixed fixed point in defect coupling space.

Result: The bulk Dirac equation fixes BOE operator dimensions and enforces a double-twist relation for bulk-defect-defect three-point functions—unless both shadow partners are unitary (as in lowest monodromy modes), in which case the three-point functions are linked by shadow transform; at a perturbative mixed fixed point with interacting defect couplings, the double-twist condition is explicitly violated and the two shadow contributions combine into a regular correlator.

Conclusion: Bulk equations of motion impose strong, previously underappreciated constraints on conformal defect CFT data; monodromy defects realize exceptional shadow degeneracy; interacting defect models provide concrete realizations where standard double-twist expectations break down, revealing richer analytic structure in defect correlators.

Abstract: We study codimension-two conformal defects for massless fermions that are free in a four-dimensional bulk, but interact on a two-dimensional defect. The bulk Dirac equation strongly constrains defect conformal field theory data: it fixes the dimensions of operators in the bulk-defect operator product expansion and, for bulk-defect-defect three-point functions, enforces a double-twist relation when only one member of a shadow pair is allowed by unitarity. The lowest modes of monodromy defects provide an important exception, since both shadow partners can be unitary and their three-point functions are instead related by a shadow transform.
  We illustrate these structures with defect interactions, including an exactly tractable bilinear flow and Gross-Neveu-type quartic couplings. At a perturbative mixed fixed point, we find an explicit interacting example in which the double-twist condition is violated and the two shadow channels combine to give a regular bulk-defect-defect correlation function.

</details>


### [112] [The dimensional reduction of the spin-3 and spin-4 partially broken Fronsdal models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14284)
*D. Dalmazi,A. L. R. dos Santos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper performs Kaluza-Klein reduction of partially broken Fronsdal models for spin-3 and spin-4 from D+1 to D dimensions, yielding ghost-free massive higher-spin theories with no extra propagating degrees of freedom; the resulting models are gauge-invariant, higher-derivative, and feature a nontrivial, non-diagonalizable massless limit with full helicity content.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct consistent massive higher-spin (s=3,4) theories in D dimensions via dimensional reduction, avoiding auxiliary fields and extra propagating modes typical of ad hoc constructions like Singh-Hagen, while preserving gauge structure and exploring subtleties of the massless limit.

Method: Kaluza-Klein dimensional reduction applied to partially broken Fronsdal actions for spin-3 and spin-4 in D+1 dimensions; construction of gauge-invariant field combinations in D dimensions; analysis of on-shell equivalence, particle spectrum, and diagonalizability of the massless limit via local field redefinitions.

Result: Obtains fully covariant, ghost-free massive spin-3 and spin-4 models in D dimensions with correct DOF counts (no extra modes); models are higher-derivative and differ from Singh-Hagen by on-shell-vanishing quadratic terms; their massless limits contain all expected helicities ±j (j=0,…,s) but cannot be diagonalized by any local field redefinition due to nontrivial intertwining.

Conclusion: Kaluza-Klein reduction provides a natural route to consistent massive higher-spin theories beyond s≤2; the resulting models are inherently higher-derivative and exhibit novel structural features in the massless limit—highlighting obstructions to naive decoupling or low-energy effective descriptions for s≥3.

Abstract: Here we perform a Kaluza-Klein dimensional reduction from $D+1$ to $D$ dimensions of a partially broken Fronsdal model for spin-3 and spin-4. The reduced models describe massive spin-3 and spin-4 particles without extra propagating modes. They differ from Singh-Hagen models by quadratic terms which vanish on shell, similarly to their higher dimensional massless parents. A covariant description of the reduced models in terms of gauge invariant field combinations naturally leads to higher derivative models, contrary to lower spin cases ($s\le 2$).
  For both $s=3$ and $s=4$ we have a rather peculiar massless limit. Although their particle content corresponds to the $\pm j$ helicities with $j=0,1, \cdots s$, as expected, they appear intertwined in a non trivial way preventing diagonalization through any local field redefinition.

</details>


### [113] [Three-dimensional vacua of $G_2$ flux compactifications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14313)
*Aravind Aikot,Julian Choi,Timm Wrase*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes supersymmetric vacua in 3D N=1 effective theories from various string compactifications on G₂-structure manifolds, deriving universal constraints on scalar masses (via dual CFT operator dimensions Δ), axion stabilization, and flat directions—especially the volume-dilaton mode—with exact and bounded Δ values across IIA, IIB/I, and heterotic setups.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive model-independent, universal features of supersymmetric AdS₃ and Minkowski vacua from string compactifications on G₂-structure spaces, without relying on full moduli-space metrics, and to connect scalar mass spectra to dual 2D CFT operator dimensions while addressing scale separation and flux limits.

Method: Uses superpotential homogeneity and physical scalar metric properties in 3D N=1 supergravity; computes scalar masses via Hessian of superpotential and Kähler potential; relates them to scaling dimensions Δ of dual 2D CFT operators; imposes supersymmetry, tadpole cancellation, and axionic identifications; distinguishes torsion branches (torsion-free, singlet-torsion) across type IIA, IIB/I, and heterotic compactifications.

Result: Derives universal vacuum relations and axion stabilization constraints; obtains exact Δ_b = 6 for B₂-axions in massive IIA torsion-free AdS₃; proves Δ_max ≥ (20+4√67)/7 ≈ 7.5 in same setup; identifies universal volume-dilaton mode with Δ_φs = 4 in IIB/I and heterotic AdS₃, and Δ_φs = 20/7, Δ_b = 16/7 on singlet-torsion IIB branch; shows all classical Minkowski vacua have exact volume-dilaton flat direction; characterizes large-flux limits and torsion obstructions to isotropic scale separation.

Conclusion: Supersymmetric vacua across these G₂ compactifications exhibit robust, structure-dependent spectral features encoded in Δ, revealing tight links between geometry (torsion class), fluxes, and holographic CFT data; absence of scale separation in classical regimes is tied to universal flat directions and torsion constraints.

Abstract: We study supersymmetric vacua of three-dimensional $\mathcal N=1$ effective theories from type IIA, type IIB/type I, and heterotic compactifications on $G_2$-structure spaces. Superpotential homogeneity and the physical scalar metric yield universal vacuum relations and axion stabilization constraints without specifying the full moduli-space metric. We express AdS$_3$ scalar masses through $Δ$, the scaling dimension of the corresponding operator in the dual two-dimensional conformal field theory. In massive type IIA AdS$_3$ vacua, we derive the bound $Δ_{\max}\geq(20+4\sqrt{67})/7\approx 7.5$ and an exact dimension $Δ_b=6$ for $B_2$ axions on the torsion-free branch. Type IIB type I and heterotic AdS$_3$ vacua contain a universal volume-dilaton mode with $Δ_{φs}=4$. On the singlet-torsion type IIB branch, we also obtain the volume-dilaton mode with $Δ_{φs}=20/7$ and $Δ_b=16/7$. Every supersymmetric Minkowski vacuum in the retained classical closed-string theories possesses an exact volume-dilaton flat direction. We characterize physical large-flux limits after tadpole constraints and axionic identifications, and identify torsion obstructions to isotropic scale separation.

</details>


### [114] [One-loop QED effective action at finite temperature and density beyond HTL limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14345)
*Farahmand Hasanov,Nikita Kolganov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a heat-kernel-based framework to compute the nonlocal one-loop effective action in finite-temperature, finite-chemical-potential scalar and spinor QED in even dimensions, yielding exact (non-soft-momentum) closed-form expressions for massless form factors in terms of polylogarithms and their derivatives, and arbitrary-order small-mass expansions; it also computes photon polarization operators, Debye masses, and electromagnetic susceptibilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond soft-momentum approximations and obtain exact, nonlocal, finite-temperature/chemical-potential quantum corrections to the effective action in QED, preserving full dependence on Euclidean momentum, temperature, and chemical potential.

Method: Heat kernel expansion to quadratic order in background fields; resummation of external-momentum series; analytic continuation and evaluation of resulting integrals yielding polylogarithmic form factors and their derivatives; derivation of photon polarization tensors from functional derivatives of the effective action.

Result: Closed-form expressions for massless one-loop effective action form factors in terms of polylogarithms Li_s(z) and ∂_s Li_s(z); systematic small-mass expansion coefficients to arbitrary order; full-momentum-dependent photon polarization operators; exact static limits yielding temperature- and chemical-potential-dependent Debye screening masses and electromagnetic susceptibilities in arbitrary even dimensions.

Conclusion: The framework provides a systematic, nonperturbative-in-momentum, analytically controlled method for finite-T/μ quantum corrections in QED, enabling precise computation of nonlocal observables and thermodynamic response functions without soft-momentum truncation.

Abstract: We develop a framework for computing the nonlocal one-loop effective action in scalar and spinor QED at finite temperature and chemical potential in arbitrary even spacetime dimensions. Based on the heat kernel expansion to quadratic order in background fields, the framework reduces the effective action form factors to a common fundamental kernel. By resumming the external-momentum expansion, we obtain explicit closed-form expressions for the massless form factors in terms of polylogarithms and their derivatives with respect to the order. The same method determines the coefficients of the small-mass expansion to arbitrary order. The resulting expressions retain the full dependence on external Euclidean momentum, temperature, and chemical potential, without invoking the soft-momentum approximation. We also derive the corresponding photon polarization operators and evaluate their static limits, obtaining the Debye screening masses and electromagnetic susceptibilities.

</details>


### [115] [Characteristic Modes of Non-minimally Coupled Thick Branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14430)
*Jing-Xin Gong,Heng Guo,Yong-Tao Lu,Xin-Yi Pan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies characteristic modes—especially graviton zero modes, resonant Kaluza–Klein (KK) modes, and quasinormal modes (QNMs)—in two thick brane models with a non-minimally coupled bulk scalar field: a single-kink and a deformed kink. It confirms zero-mode localization on both branes, finds no significant KK resonances for the single-kink at ξ=1/7, but observes increasing numbers and lifetimes of resonant KK modes with deformation parameter k in the deformed brane at ξ=0.99; QNMs computed in frequency and time domains agree well for low overtones, and their real/imaginary parts match KK mode masses/decay rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand intrinsic dynamical properties and gravitational response of thick brane worlds via characteristic modes—particularly how geometry and scalar-field non-minimal coupling affect graviton localization, KK resonance spectra, and quasinormal behavior.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of tensor perturbations in two thick brane models (single-kink and deformed kink) with a non-minimally coupled bulk scalar field; computation of graviton zero-mode profiles, resonant KK spectra (via peak analysis of transmission probability), and quasinormal modes using both frequency-domain (e.g., shooting method) and time-domain (e.g., evolution of Gaussian wave packet) approaches.

Result: Graviton zero mode is localized on both branes; no prominent KK resonances exist for the single-kink brane at ξ=1/7; for the deformed brane at ξ=0.99, increasing k yields more resonant KK modes and longer-lived first resonance; QNFs from frequency- and time-domain methods agree for low overtones; real parts of QNFs match KK mode masses, and |Im(QNF)| match decay rates.

Conclusion: Characteristic mode analysis reveals that internal structure (deformation) and non-minimal coupling significantly influence gravitational resonance and damping behavior in thick brane models; consistency between KK resonances and QNMs validates the physical interpretation of long-lived modes as quasinormal excitations of the brane system.

Abstract: Characteristic modes provide important information about the intrinsic properties and dynamical response of gravitational systems. In this work, we investigate characteristic modes of thick branes with a non-minimally coupled bulk scalar field. Two representative thick brane solutions are considered, corresponding to a single-kink scalar configuration and a deformed kink configuration with internal structure, respectively. By analyzing the tensor perturbation equations, we first examine the localization of the graviton zero mode in the two brane models. For both brane models, the graviton zero mode is localized on the brane. No pronounced resonant Kaluza--Klein (KK) modes are found for the single-kink brane at $ξ=1/7$. For the deformed brane, we investigate the resonant KK modes at $ξ=0.99$, finding that increasing the deformation parameter $k$ leads to more resonant modes and a longer-lived first resonance.
  We then calculate the quasinormal modes (QNMs) of both brane models independently in the frequency and time domains. The quasinormal frequencies (QNFs) obtained by the independent methods agree well for the low overtones. The masses and decay rates of the resonant KK modes agree closely with the real parts and the magnitudes of the imaginary parts of the corresponding QNFs, respectively.

</details>


### [116] [Haagerup conformal field theory at central charge eight](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14441)
*Tzu-Chen Huang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Constructs a chiral algebra U (c=8) inside V_{E6⊕A2}, generated by sl2 current algebras (levels 1,39), stress tensor, and a weight-2 field; proves U is strongly rational, unitary, and its representation category Rep(U) is equivalent to the Drinfeld center Z(H3) of the Haagerup fusion category H3; yields a diagonal full CFT and a holomorphic E8 extension with H3 topological defects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize the Haagerup fusion category H3 — a prominent exotic fusion category — within conformal field theory via a concrete, unitary, strongly rational vertex operator algebra and its representation category.

Method: Explicit construction of a chiral algebra U inside the lattice VOA V_{E6⊕A2} using commuting affine sl2 subalgebras, lattice exponentials, and the stress tensor; structural analysis (strong rationality, unitarity) and categorical identification via modular data and equivalence to Z(H3).

Result: U is strongly rational and completely unitary; Rep(U) ≅ Z(H3) as a unitary ribbon category; characters match the γ=0 Evans–Gannon vector; existence of a diagonal full CFT and a holomorphic E8 extension acted on by H3 via topological defects.

Conclusion: Provides the first explicit, unitary, strongly rational VOA whose representation category realizes the Drinfeld center of the Haagerup fusion category, thereby embedding exotic fusion structure into rigorous two-dimensional conformal field theory.

Abstract: We construct a chiral algebra $U$ of central charge eight inside the lattice vertex operator algebra $V_{E_6\oplus A_2}$. It is generated by two commuting affine $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ current algebras at levels $1$ and $39$, the stress tensor of the lattice theory, and a single weight-two field built from three lattice exponentials. We announce that $U$ is strongly rational and completely unitary, and that $Rep(U)$ is equivalent, as a unitary ribbon category, to the Drinfeld center $Z(\mathcal{H}_3)$ of the Haagerup fusion category $\mathcal{H}_3$. Its characters are the $γ=0$ Evans--Gannon vector. From $U$ we obtain a diagonal full CFT, and also a holomorphic $E_8$ extension on which $\mathcal{H}_3$ acts by topological defects.

</details>


### [117] [PINNs for N=2* holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14461)
*Alex Buchel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores whether a holographic theory can be reconstructed from its equation of state using physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) in the Pilch–Warner model. It validates PINNs against analytic solutions, reconstructs black-brane geometries and scalar potentials from thermodynamic and UV data, and shows that high-precision equation-of-state data—especially with cubic coupling fixed—significantly improves scalar potential reconstruction; adding bulk viscosity data further enhances accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the full holographic bulk theory (geometry and scalar potential) can be uniquely or accurately reconstructed solely from boundary thermodynamic data (equation of state), leveraging physical constraints and machine learning.

Method: Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) trained on thermodynamic data (equation of state), UV asymptotics, Ward identities, horizon regularity, and bulk viscosity; validated against analytic high-temperature expansions and forward-solved Einstein–scalar equations.

Result: Accurate thermodynamics does not guarantee accurate scalar potential reconstruction; fixing the cubic coupling (from high-T EOS) reduces max error in scalar self-interactions by ~15×; adding bulk viscosity data reduces error by another ~13×, and forward solutions reproduce bulk viscosity to 0.09%.

Conclusion: High-precision equation-of-state data—especially when supplemented with transport coefficients like bulk viscosity—provides strong, quantitative constraints on nonlinear bulk couplings; however, thermodynamic consistency alone is insufficient for faithful reconstruction of the scalar potential.

Abstract: Can a holographic theory be reconstructed from its equation of state? We investigate this question using physics-informed neural networks in the bosonic-mass sector of the Pilch--Warner model. We first validate the PINN approach against an analytic solution encoding the leading high-temperature corrections to the conformal equation of state. We then reconstruct nonlinear black-brane geometries and the scalar potential using thermodynamics, ultraviolet asymptotics, Ward identities, and horizon regularity. Independent solutions of the bulk Einstein-scalar equations demonstrate that accurately reproducing thermodynamics with a trained PINN does not guarantee an accurate reconstruction of the scalar potential. High-precision knowledge of the high-temperature equation of state constrains nonlinear interactions in the bulk scalar potential. Fixing the cubic coupling to its exact PW value, motivated by this high-temperature extraction, reduces the maximum sampled error in the reconstructed scalar self-interactions by a factor of $\sim 15$ compared with constraining only the bulk scalar mass. Adding knowledge of the bulk viscosity of the gauge theory plasma further reduces this error by an additional factor of $\sim 13$, with independent forward solutions reproducing the bulk viscosity within $0.09\%$.

</details>


### [118] [Bondi Four-Momentum Balance in Quantum NSF Classical matching, DKL supermomentum, and quantum energy redistribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14484)
*Carlos Kozameh,Gerardo DEpaola*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives Bondi four-momentum balance in the null-surface formulation’s radiative phase space, establishes antipodal continuity of the Bondi mass aspect via Weyl matching and vanishing endpoint News, shows Lorentz covariance of its translation projection, links News fluxes to endpoint momenta, and demonstrates exclusive four-momentum conservation as a quantum condition; it further computes outgoing energy expectation for incoming energy eigenstates and proves vanishing connected energy defect through second order under axial rotational covariance and finite helicity projection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile classical gravitational radiation dynamics with quantum observables by extending Bondi–Sachs framework to the radiative phase space and imposing physically motivated quantum conditions that enforce four-momentum conservation.

Method: Uses null-surface formulation (NSF), Weyl matching at spatial infinity, Bondi evolution equations, radiative Fock domain construction, unitary conjugation of flux observables, canonical contractions, and helicity-projection techniques from Part I; employs second-generator commutator analysis and axial rotational covariance constraints.

Result: Antipodal continuity of Bondi mass aspect; Lorentz-covariant translation projection independent of infrared shear; equality of incoming/outgoing News fluxes in DKL radiative sector; exclusive four-momentum conservation when classical balance is imposed quantumly; vanishing connected energy defect through second order for equal-energy center-of-mass states under finite helicity projection.

Conclusion: Classical Bondi balance can be elevated to a quantum condition ensuring exact four-momentum conservation; the connected radiative energy exchange vanishes to second order in this controlled setting, supporting consistency between asymptotic symmetry, scattering theory, and infrared-finite quantum gravity observables.

Abstract: We derive Bondi four-momentum balance between past and future null infinity in the radiative phase space of the null-surface formulation (NSF). Weyl matching at spatial infinity, together with vanishing endpoint News, gives antipodal continuity of the Bondi mass aspect. Its translation projection is independent of the infrared shear term and transforms as a Lorentz four-vector. Bondi evolution relates the incoming and outgoing News fluxes to the timelike endpoint momenta; in the Dominguez--Kozameh--Ludvigsen radiative sector these momenta vanish and the fluxes are equal. On a common radiative Fock domain, unitary conjugation transports the flux observables. Adopting their classical balance as an additional quantum condition yields exclusive four-momentum conservation. We also calculate the outgoing energy expectation without imposing that condition. For an incoming energy eigenstate, the second-generator commutator vanishes diagonally, leaving a second-order coefficient determined by the cubic generator. Direct canonical contractions isolate the contribution connected to all four external legs. For the equal-energy center-of-mass preparation, axial rotational covariance forces the angularly integrated exchange coefficient to vanish under the finite helicity-projection prescription of Part~I. The connected energy defect therefore vanishes through second order. The full expectation (including spectators) and higher-order balance remain open.

</details>


### [119] [A critical coupling for the magnetic instability of holographic SU(2) plasmas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14675)
*Migael Strydom*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that gravitational backreaction in holographic SU(2) Yang-Mills theory in AdS₅ suppresses the linear magnetic instability of a charged plasma; the critical magnetic field diverges at a critical coupling α⋆ = 2/3, beyond which no charged instability exists—linked to Breitenlohner-Freedman bound violation in the AdS₃ × ℝ² throat—and the transition exhibits BKT-type essential scaling and becomes first-order above αL ≈ 0.372.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether gravitational backreaction can stabilize holographic plasmas against magnetic-field-induced instabilities (e.g., tachyonic vector condensation), particularly in light of probe-limit predictions of vortex lattice formation, and to connect instability thresholds to universal CFT data (C_J/C_T) and bulk stability bounds.

Method: Analyzes Einstein–SU(2) Yang-Mills theory in AdS₅ with exact gravitational backreaction, expanding around the D'Hoker–Kraus magnetic brane solution; computes linearized fluctuations of charged vector fields in the AdS₃ × ℝ² near-horizon throat; determines critical couplings α⋆ and αL via BF-bound violation and vortex interaction energy; relates results to CFT central charges using holographic dictionary for d = 4,5,6.

Result: Critical magnetic field B_c diverges as α → α⋆ = 2/3; for α > α⋆, no linear charged instability exists at any B or T; zero-temperature instability arises from BF-bound violation in the throat; onset temperature vanishes with BKT-type essential singularity ∼ exp(−c/√(α⋆−α)); transition becomes first-order for α > αL ≈ 0.372 due to attractive vortex interactions; instability condition maps to C_J/C_T < 8/(d²(d+1)), e.g., < 0.1 in d = 4.

Conclusion: Gravity fundamentally alters magnetic phase structure: it eliminates the charged instability entirely above α⋆, ties the threshold to universal CFT data and near-horizon geometry, and introduces non-analytic (BKT) and first-order features absent in probe approximations—highlighting limitations of neglecting backreaction in holographic magnetism.

Abstract: We show that gravity can remove the linear magnetic instability of a holographic plasma with an $SU(2)$ global symmetry. In the probe limit, charged vector bosons become tachyonic in their lowest Landau level above a critical field and condense into a vortex lattice. In Einstein-$SU(2)$ Yang-Mills theory in AdS$_5$ we include the backreaction of the field exactly, expanding only in the condensate around the D'Hoker-Kraus magnetic brane. The critical field diverges as the coupling $α= κ^2/\hat g^2$ approaches $α_\star = 2/3$, above which the normal phase has no linear charged instability at any field or temperature: we show this at zero temperature and establish it numerically at finite temperature. At zero temperature the field cancels from the mass of the lowest Landau level in the brane's AdS$_3 \times \mathbb{R}^2$ throat, and the instability is a violation of the throat's Breitenlohner-Freedman bound. The onset temperature vanishes at $α_\star$ with an essential singularity of Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless type. For two-derivative Einstein-Yang-Mills duals in $d = 4$, 5 and 6 dimensions the normal phase has a charged instability if and only if $C_J/C_T < 8/(d^2(d+1))$, one tenth in $d = 4$. Above $α_L = 0.372$ the gravitational attraction between vortices makes the uniform lattice unstable and the transition first order, so $B_c$ is only a spinodal; whether a condensed phase survives above $α_\star$ is left open.

</details>


### [120] [Charged Deformation of a Hairy Black Brane: Perturbative Solution and Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14929)
*Sangheon Yun*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies charged black branes in a 4D Einstein-Maxwell-scalar theory with a non-AdS asymptotics and logarithmic scalar hair; it computes thermodynamics, transport (e.g., incoherent conductivity), and critical behavior perturbatively and numerically, finds no regular extremal horizon at finite scalar, identifies an AdS₂×ℝ² extremal throat with saturation of a universal entropy density bound, and explores dual non-conformal boundary physics under Dirichlet scalar sourcing—including a maximal temperature phase boundary linked to diffusive instability and Buchel-bound violation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand holographic duals of non-conformal, strongly coupled quantum systems via charged black branes with running scalar fields that break asymptotic AdS symmetry; specifically, to explore thermodynamic stability, extremal limits, transport, and boundary interpretation when the bulk scalar lacks a UV fixed point.

Method: Perturbative expansion to second order in charge around neutral black branes; use of scale-invariant multi-trace boundary conditions for conformal thermodynamics; numerical integration of nonlinear equations; computation of quasinormal modes; analysis of extremal limits and boundary conditions (Dirichlet vs. multi-trace); verification via first-law consistency and independent perturbative checks.

Result: Closed-form expressions for Maxwell field and charge susceptibility; conformal thermodynamics with negative cumulant ratio T²χ₄/χ₂ = 6c(α) < 0; local thermodynamic stability; analytic pressure in (μ_Q/T)²; absence of regular extremal horizon at finite φ; exact bound ρ/s < √21/(2π), saturated in AdS₂×ℝ² throat where φ ∼ ln ln(1/u); non-conformal dual under Dirichlet condition yields T_max(μ_Q) decreasing from 0.194Λ to zero, terminating at diffusive instability with divergent χ_Q and bulk viscosity violating Buchel’s bound.

Conclusion: The model realizes a controlled departure from AdS/CFT while retaining calculable holography: it supports stable charged phases, reveals universal extremal scaling, and maps to either a conformal or a scale-breaking boundary theory depending on scalar boundary conditions—highlighting how boundary data dictates IR/UV completeness and stability of the dual QFT.

Abstract: We study electrically charged black branes in four-dimensional Einstein-Maxwell-scalar theory with Maxwell coupling $Z(φ)=e^{3φ/\sqrt7}$, whose potential has an AdS$_4$ fixed point only in the infrared. For nonzero hair parameter $α$ the scalar grows logarithmically at large radius and the geometry is not asymptotically AdS, so the holographic dictionary is a working assumption. With the scale-invariant multi-trace boundary condition of the neutral branes, giving conformal thermodynamics, we work to second order in the charge, obtaining the Maxwell field and susceptibility in closed form, plus the thermodynamics, condensate, incoherent conductivity and cumulant ratio $T^2χ_4/χ_2=6c(α)<0$ for all $α$ considered. Independent perturbative computations and first-law closed forms agree within $4\times10^{-6}$; nonlinear solutions reproduce second-order results to $1.2\times10^{-5}$ and $c$ to $6\times10^{-4}$. They are locally thermodynamically stable where constructed, and the pressure is analytic in $(μ_Q/T)^2$ near zero density. No regular extremal horizon exists at finite scalar value, and $ρ/s<\sqrt{21}/(2π)$ exactly. The extremal limit is an AdS$_2\times\mathbb R^2$ throat where the scalar grows as $\ln\ln(1/u)$ in the AdS$_2$ radial coordinate $u$; the bound is saturated there, and extremal values are approached as powers of $1/\ln(μ_Q/T)$. With a Dirichlet condition on the scalar source, the same solutions describe a non-conformal theory with scale $Λ$, whose charged branes exist only below $T_{\max}(μ_Q)$, which falls from $0.194Λ$ at zero density to zero at $μ_Q=0.506Λ$. This boundary is a diffusive instability where the charge susceptibility diverges at nonzero density, and the bulk viscosity violates Buchel's bound at all densities. Quasinormal modes with the normalizable large-radius condition reproduce its hydrodynamics.

</details>


### [121] [Holographic theory of non-invertible gravitational anomalies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15358)
*Xiao-Gang Wen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper presents a many-body formulation of gravitational anomalies as obstructions to realizing low-energy data in local bosonic lattice systems, classifying anomalies via topological order in one higher dimension; it introduces a holographic framework where anomalies are encoded in invertible or non-invertible bulk topological orders, and illustrates this via chiral theories, Laughlin edges, and Z2 higher gauge theories—establishing an equivalence between symmetry, gravitational anomaly, and topological order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified, constructive, and lattice-realizable framework for gravitational anomalies—beyond conventional anomaly inflow—by interpreting them as obstructions to embedding anomalous boundary theories into gapped bosonic lattice bulks, and to clarify the role of non-invertible structures and topological order in anomaly classification.

Method: Uses a many-body, bosonic lattice quantum system approach in one higher dimension; constructs a holographic boundary-to-bulk map; distinguishes uniquely defined topological order from TQFT descriptions; introduces morphisms between QFTs via bulk domain walls and invertible boundary junctions (sandwich picture); analyzes examples (chiral bosons/fermions, Laughlin edges, Z2 higher gauge theory) using transport coefficients, superselection sectors, and condensation data.

Result: Establishes a one-to-one classification of gravitational anomalies by bosonic topological order in d+1D; generalizes anomaly inflow to non-invertible cases with non-trivial topological excitations; identifies fermionic theories as carrying a specific non-invertible gravitational anomaly; uncovers the tripartite equivalence: symmetry ↔ gravitational anomaly ↔ topological order in one higher dimension.

Conclusion: Gravitational anomalies are fully characterized by the topological order of a gapped bosonic bulk in one higher dimension; this perspective unifies symmetry, anomaly, and topological order, and enables concrete lattice constructions and categorical descriptions of QFT morphisms beyond invertible frameworks.

Abstract: We review a many-body formulation of gravitational anomalies as obstructions to realizing the complete low-energy data by a local {\it bosonic} lattice quantum system of the same dimension. In this formulation, an anomalous theory can be realized at the boundary of a gapped {\it bosonic} lattice bulk, whose topological order gives a one-to-one classification of the anomaly. An invertible bulk reproduces conventional anomaly inflow; a bulk with non-trivial topological excitations yields a non-invertible generalization. To describe the holographic theory of anomalies, we explain the distinction between uniquely defined topological order and many versions of topological quantum field theory and discuss the boundary-to-bulk map. As an application of the holographic theory of anomalies, we describe morphisms between local quantum theories (such as quantum field theories). These morphisms are built from a bulk domain wall and an invertible boundary junction, which give rise to a sandwich picture. Chiral boson/fermion theory, Laughlin edges, and boundaries of $\mathbb{Z}_2$ higher gauge theory with bosonic or fermionic point-like excitations illustrate the different kinds of information about non-invertible anomalies contained in transport coefficients, superselection sectors, and condensation data. In particular, a fermionic theory can be viewed as a theory with a particular non-invertible gravitational anomaly. Those examples also reveal that {\it symmetry $\sim$ gravitational anomaly $\sim$ topological order in one higher dimension}.

</details>


### [122] [Rank polarization and dynamical autonomy in a projective rotor model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15361)
*Shannon Wang,David Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates how higher-rank collective order parameters (specifically the second-moment tensor T) in projective-rotor models can select a dominant eigenline and support a low-energy director sector, showing that rank polarization alone does not guarantee dynamical autonomy; it constructs rotationally invariant interactions that enforce spectral selection and infrared suppression independently, with distinct behavior for p=2 vs. p>2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the relationship between geometric rank polarization (e.g., effective rank r_eff → 1) and dynamical autonomy of a low-energy director sector—specifically, whether selecting a dominant eigenline via the collective tensor T is sufficient to isolate soft transverse modes, or whether additional control over the free-energy Hessian is required.

Method: Analyzes a fully connected projective-rotor model with collective second-moment tensor T; introduces a two-harmonic model to decouple rank (T, r_eff) from transverse quadratic form; constructs rotationally invariant locking interactions that fix T while uniformly scaling non-Goldstone restoring coefficients as Θ(λ); examines spectral properties (normal-mode frequencies, susceptibility) in inertial and overdamped regimes.

Result: Demonstrates logical independence between rank polarization and dynamical autonomy: families with identical T and r_eff exhibit different transverse Hessians; constructs interactions achieving Θ(λ) enhancement of all non-Goldstone stiffnesses, leading to Θ(λ^{1/2}) lowest transverse frequency (inertial) and O(λ^{-1}) transverse susceptibility (general); confirms spectral selection and infrared suppression are independent physical data; identifies p=2 as special due to 1D Goldstone mode, whereas p>2 yields (p−1)-D director manifolds.

Conclusion: Rank reduction of the collective tensor T is necessary but insufficient for dynamical autonomy; true low-energy director physics requires explicit control over the transverse free-energy curvature—achievable via symmetry-respecting locking interactions—and reflects a deeper separation between geometric order and spectral structure in high-rank classical systems.

Abstract: We study how a higher-rank classical collective order parameter can select one dominant projective eigenline and, in a $p=2$ realization, support a dynamically autonomous low-energy director sector. In a fully connected projective-rotor model, the collective second moment is a positive semidefinite tensor $T$ of fixed trace. Conventional projective ordering drives its effective rank toward one, selecting a single dominant eigenline. Rank polarization alone, however, does not determine dynamical autonomy: extrinsic-curvature criteria depend on the chosen state-space metric, and the equilibrium tensor does not determine the transverse free-energy Hessian. A two-harmonic model makes this logical independence explicit by producing families with identical $T$ and $r_{\rm eff}$ but different transverse quadratic forms. We then construct rotationally invariant locking interactions that leave a prescribed ordered tensor exactly fixed while increasing every non-Goldstone restoring coefficient as $Θ(λ)$ within a globally controlled stable regime. For a phenomenological inertial realization with a finite positive kinetic matrix, the lowest transverse normal-mode frequency grows as $Θ(λ^{1/2})$; more generally, the low-frequency transverse susceptibility is suppressed as $O(λ^{-1})$, including in overdamped dynamics. The construction therefore proves that spectral selection and infrared transverse suppression are independent data. For $p>2$ the selected eigenline has a $(p-1)$-dimensional director manifold; the one-dimensional Goldstone sector used in the dynamical construction is specific to $p=2$. Promoting the selected internal eigenline to a physical clock requires additional soldering and integrability structure.

</details>


### [123] [Phase transitions and quasinormal modes of Euler-Heisenberg-dilaton AdS black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15508)
*Yi-Qi Chen,De-Cheng Zou,Ming Zhang,Xufen Zhang,Bo Liu,Rui-Hong Yue*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates phase transitions and scalar quasinormal modes (QNMs) of magnetically charged Euler–Heisenberg-dilaton AdS black holes in the extended thermodynamic phase space, identifying Van der Waals-like small/large black hole transitions for negative Euler–Heisenberg coupling, distinct QNM trajectories below critical pressure that coalesce at criticality, and absence of finite-radius critical points for GHS limit or positive couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand thermodynamic phase structure and stability (via QNMs) of magnetically charged black holes in a realistic nonlinear electrodynamics framework—Euler–Heisenberg theory—coupled to a dilaton field in AdS spacetime, within the extended phase space where cosmological constant is treated as thermodynamic pressure.

Method: Classification of horizon structures across all signs of the Euler–Heisenberg (EH) coupling; derivation of the extended first law including variations of thermodynamic pressure and EH coupling parameter; analysis of phase transitions using P–V criticality formalism; computation of scalar quasinormal modes via the improved asymptotic iteration method (AIM).

Result: For negative EH coupling: existence of a physical branch exhibiting Van der Waals-like small/large black-hole phase transition with a finite critical point; QNMs show two distinct trajectories below critical pressure, merging into one continuous curve exactly at criticality. For GHS limit and positive EH couplings: no finite-radius critical point arises.

Conclusion: The sign of the Euler–Heisenberg coupling critically determines whether a finite critical point—and hence mean-field-type phase transition—emerges in the thermodynamics of magnetically charged dilatonic AdS black holes; scalar QNMs serve as dynamical probes confirming the thermodynamic critical behavior, with trajectory merging signaling loss of mode separation at criticality.

Abstract: In this paper, we study phase transitions and scalar quasinormal modes(QNMs) of magnetically charged Euler--Heisenberg-dilaton AdS black holes in the extended phase space. We classify the horizon structures for all three signs of the EH coupling and derive the extended first law when the pressure and coupling are varied. For negative coupling, the physical branch exhibits a Van der Waals-like small/large black-hole transition. Using the improved asymptotic iteration method, we find two separated QNM trajectories below the critical pressure, whereas they merge into one continuous curve at the critical point. The GHS limit and the positive couplings considered here do not produce a finite-radius critical point.

</details>


### [124] [Aspects of $\mathcal{N}=2$ SUSY gauge theories and applications to Quantum Field Theory and Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15619)
*Andrea Cipriani*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This thesis applies supersymmetric gauge theory techniques—especially the Seiberg-Witten (SW) curve and its quantum extension—to problems in QFT (e.g., Argyres-Douglas theories, instanton-corrected correlators) and gravity (e.g., stability of topological stars, superradiance, Heun function reformulation for Schwarzschild waveforms), and introduces a Hamiltonian Neural Network to model fuzzball geodesics with superior accuracy for critical orbits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge formal developments in supersymmetric gauge theories—particularly the Seiberg-Witten framework—with concrete physical problems in quantum field theory (e.g., non-Lagrangian Argyres-Douglas theories) and quantum gravity (e.g., fuzzball stability, gravitational-wave tails), while also exploring ML-enhanced geometric computation.

Method: Analytical derivation and application of the classical and quantum Seiberg-Witten curve; instanton calculus and effective field theory matching for extremal correlators; linear perturbation analysis of topological stars; analytic reformulation of confluent Heun equation into hypergeometric form for Schwarzschild wave equations; development and training of Hamiltonian Neural Networks constrained by symplectic structure to reproduce geodesics in fuzzball backgrounds.

Result: 1) Instanton corrections to extremal correlators in a conformal SUSY model match EFT predictions, supporting analogous computations in Argyres-Douglas theories; 2) Topological stars are linearly stable under massive charged scalar perturbations; 3) A novel hypergeometric representation of the confluent Heun function yields a resummed, highly accurate expression for Schwarzschild waveform tails; 4) Hamiltonian Neural Networks outperform standard ODE integrators in efficiency and accuracy for critical geodesics in fuzzball geometries.

Conclusion: The Seiberg-Witten formalism provides a powerful, unifying toolkit across diverse domains—from non-perturbative QFT to black hole microstructure and gravitational-wave physics—and its synergy with modern computational methods like structure-preserving neural networks opens new avenues for high-precision theoretical modeling in quantum gravity.

Abstract: This thesis investigates the application of techniques developed in supersymmetric gauge theories to physical problems in Quantum Field Theory and Gravity, focusing on the Seiberg-Witten (SW) curve and its quantum version. After reviewing the essential concepts of Supersymmetry and the derivation of the SW curve, we explore its applications in both fields. In Quantum Field Theory, we investigate the emergence of Argyres-Douglas theories in the moduli space of supersymmetric models, focusing on extremal correlators. We analyze a conformal model to compute their instanton corrections, finding agreement with predictions from Effective Field Theory, and discuss the implications for analogous observables in Argyres-Douglas theories. In Gravity, we exploit the quantum SW curve and its relation to differential equations describing physical systems. We establish the linear stability of Topological Stars, arising in the fuzzball proposal, under massive charged scalar perturbations.
