We also investigate charge superradiance in configurations with a horizon and derive a novel hypergeometric representation of the confluent Heun function, relevant to gravitational-wave propagation in the Schwarzschild background. This result yields a new resummed expression for waveform tail contributions, in excellent agreement with numerical data. Finally, beyond the SW formalism, we develop a Machine Learning algorithm based on Hamiltonian Neural Networks to reproduce the geodesics of a neutral particle in a fuzzball geometry. For critical trajectories, our method outperforms standard numerical integrators in both efficiency and accuracy.

</details>


### [125] [New topological phase transitions in $κ$-deformed $φ^4$ lattice models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15644)
*Adith A,A. Bhagwat*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies a topological phase transition in the momentum-space structure of the κ-deformed φ⁴ lattice model, linking it to the noncommutative parameter α via E ∼ 1/α⁴, using both numerical and A₃ singularity-based analytical methods; it further predicts a nonperturbative, experimentally testable signature—the nonzero commutative limit of the degenerate-momentum-state population.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover observable, nonperturbative signatures of noncommutative spacetime through topological features in momentum space of a deformed quantum field theory.

Method: Combined numerical analysis of energy-dependent momentum-space topology and analytical treatment via singularity theory—specifically mapping constant-energy surfaces to the A₃ catastrophe—to characterize the topological phase transition in the κ-deformed φ⁴ lattice model under mean-field approximation.

Result: The topological phase transition occurs at energy E ∼ 1/α⁴; the constant-energy surface is classified as an A₃ singularity at the transition point; the fraction of particles occupying degenerate momentum states remains finite in the commutative (α → 0) limit—a nonperturbative effect.

Conclusion: The momentum-space topology of κ-deformed field theories hosts robust, nonperturbative signatures of noncommutativity, offering a concrete pathway for experimental detection of quantum spacetime structure.

Abstract: This paper explores topological phase transitions in the momentum space of the $κ$-deformed $φ^4$ lattice model within the mean-field approximation. We show that the double-well structure of the momentum space gives rise to degenerate momentum states at high energies and that this change is marked by a phase transition. We investigate this transition using both numerical and analytical methods, with the analytical approach based primarily on singularity theory. Our numerical analysis shows that the energy at which the topological phase transition occurs is related to the noncommutative parameter by the simple power law $E \sim 1/α^{4}$. By mapping the constant-energy-level surface to the $A_{3}$ singularity, we obtain the same result analytically. We also present results unique to noncommutative systems, showing that the fraction of particles in degenerate momentum states has a nonzero commutative limit. As a nonperturbative effect, this result provides a testable prediction for the existence of noncommutative spacetime.

</details>


### [126] [Higher Dressing and Shift-Invariance in 4D Topological BF Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15737)
*J. François,L. Ravera*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies the higher-form Dressing Field Method to 4D BF theory with cosmological term, constructing a shift-invariant dressed field B̃ = B − λ⁻¹F that renders the dynamics algebraic (B̃ = 0), thereby manifestly eliminating local propagating degrees of freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To make explicit the absence of local propagating degrees of freedom—a hallmark of topological field theories—in 4D BF theory with cosmological term, by exactly reducing its finite non-Abelian shift symmetry.

Method: Introduces a higher dressing field (a 1-form) that compensates the non-Abelian shift symmetry; uses it to construct the composite, shift-invariant dressed field B̃ = B − λ⁻¹F; reformulates the theory entirely in terms of B̃.

Result: The equations of motion reduce algebraically to B̃ = 0, confirming no local propagating degrees of freedom; this constitutes the first explicit realization of the higher-form Dressing Field Method.

Conclusion: The higher-form Dressing Field Method successfully exposes the topological nature of the theory at the level of fields and dynamics, offering a geometric, first-principles reduction of gauge redundancy beyond standard BRST or Stueckelberg approaches.

Abstract: A characteristic feature of topological field theories is the absence of local propagating degrees of freedom. In four-dimensional BF theory with cosmological term, we make this feature manifest by exactly reducing the finite non-Abelian shift symmetry. We do so by identifying a higher dressing field, a 1-form compensating the shift, allowing to build from first principles the composite shift-invariant field $\boldsymbol{B}=B-λ^{-1}F$. In terms of this dressed field, the dynamics becomes algebraic and sets $\boldsymbol{B}=0$, directly exposing the absence of local propagating degrees of freedom. This provides the first explicit instance of the higher-form Dressing Field Method.

</details>


### [127] [Area metric gravity à la Plebanski: connection, curvature and dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15838)
*Bianca Dittrich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs non-linear area metric dynamics from modified Plebanski actions, develops geometric tools (e.g., area-to-length metric extraction, canonical connection on two-forms), reformulates area metric gravity as Poincaré gauge theory, and derives an effective length-metric action featuring a graviton plus a massive spin-2 field with Barbero–Immirzi–driven parity violation—enabling cosmological applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the favorable propagator behavior (absence of extra poles) observed in linearized area metric gravity—derived from modified Plebanski theory—persists in the full non-linear regime, and to establish a rigorous, non-perturbative geometric foundation for area metric gravity.

Method: Develops intrinsic geometric tools for area metrics: (i) a canonical algorithm to extract a compatible length metric; (ii) a uniquely determined torsion-free connection on Λ²T*M; (iii) associated curvature and invariants. Derives non-linear area metric actions from both first- and second-order modified Plebanski formulations, and recasts them as Poincaré gauge theories. Computes effective length-metric action via background expansion and analyzes matter coupling and cosmological backgrounds.

Result: Non-linear area metric dynamics are successfully constructed; the geometric framework is fully non-perturbative; area metric gravity is shown to be equivalent to a Poincaré gauge theory; the effective length-metric action on flat background yields Einstein gravity plus a massive spin-2 field with parity-violating couplings controlled by the Barbero–Immirzi parameter; cosmological extensions and matter coupling are formulated.

Conclusion: The favorable linearized spectral property (no extra propagator poles) has a robust non-linear origin rooted in the geometric structure of area metrics and their derivation from Plebanski theory; the resulting framework provides a consistent, potentially phenomenologically viable extension of general relativity with built-in quantum-gravity-motivated features.

Abstract: Area metrics arise as effective description at intermediate scales in a number of approaches to quantum gravity, including string theory, holography and loop quantum gravity. Recently, it has been shown that area metric actions can be derived from the modified Plebanski framework on the linearized level. Surprisingly these actions avoid extra poles in the propagator for the effective length dynamics, and hence constitute interesting deformations of linearized Einstein gravity. To be able to study whether this property persist for the full theory, we construct here non-linear area metric dynamics from modified Plebanski actions. We provide the geometric tools for area metrics that do not rely on perturbation theory and allow a systematic construction and study of general non-linear area metric actions: an algorithm that extracts a length metric from an area metric and a connection on two-forms that is uniquely determined by the area metric, together with its curvature and curvature invariants. We derive area metric actions from modified Plebanski actions in first and second order versions. We also find that area metric actions can be reformulated as Poincare gauge theories. We determine the effective length metric action, which on a flat background describes a graviton and a massive spin-two field with parity-violating couplings set by the Barbero--Immirzi parameter. Additionally we discuss cosmological backgrounds and the role of the matter coupling, which together open the way to cosmological applications.

</details>


### [128] [From Lyapunov Growth to Weak Quantum Chaos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15880)
*Sašo Grozdanov,Tomaž Prosen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes spatial proliferation of quantum chaos via local OTOCs, proving tighter Lieb-Robinson bounds (t^d) for summed commutator and dOTOC in Gibbs states with exponential clustering, and demonstrates late-time power-law growth as signature of weak quantum chaos in Brownian cluster and SYK chain models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum chaos spreads spatially beyond local saturation—specifically, how the region where commutators are large expands in extended quantum systems, and to refine bounds on extensive out-of-time-order correlators reflecting this spread.

Method: Analytical derivation using Lieb–Robinson bounds and properties of Gibbs states with uniform exponential clustering; exact disorder symmetry analysis in solvable models (Brownian coupled-cluster and finite-temperature SYK chain) to relate dOTOC D(t) to summed local commutator \bar C(t).

Result: Proves \bar C(t) \lesssim t^d and—crucially—D(t) \lesssim t^d in Gibbs states with exponential clustering, improving prior t^{3d} bound; shows identical late-time t^d growth for both \bar C(t) and D(t) in the two models due to disorder symmetry, confirming weak quantum chaos manifests as persistent power-law expansion of the saturated chaos region.

Conclusion: The t^d scaling of both \bar C(t) and D(t) reflects universal spatial proliferation of operator growth in chaotic quantum many-body systems at finite temperature; this power-law regime after local scrambling is the operational signature of weak quantum chaos, distinct from exponential (Lyapunov) growth.

Abstract: Local out-of-time-order correlators (OTOCs) measure the growth of initially small commutators. For bounded local operators, this growth ends when the local commutator becomes of order one, but in an extended system the region over which it is large can continue to expand. We study this spatial proliferation using three related observables: the local squared commutator $C_r(t)$, its spatial sum $\bar C(t) = \sum_r C_r(t)$, and the symmetrically regularised connected dOTOC $D(t)$ of two extensive observables. We show that Lieb--Robinson locality gives $\bar C(t) \lesssim t^d$. We then prove that, in a Gibbs state with uniform exponential clustering, the full dOTOC obeys the same bound, $D(t) \lesssim t^d$, improving our earlier suboptimal $t^{3d}$ bound. We illustrate these results in two quantum many-body systems: a Brownian coupled-cluster model and a finite-temperature SYK chain. In both cases, an exact disorder symmetry relates $D(t)$ to $\bar C(t)$ and allows us to follow the same extensive observable from the pre-scrambling Lyapunov regime into the post-scrambling growth associated with an expanding saturated region. This late-time power-law growth is the concrete realisation of the weak quantum chaos introduced in our earlier work.

</details>


### [129] [Microscopic entropy of regular BTZ black holes in 3d Lovelock gravity at all orders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15917)
*Hikmet Özşahin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides a microscopic derivation of black hole entropy in 3D Lovelock gravity with infinite regularized corrections, using mini-superspace formalism and the Cardy formula—without relying on central charges—and extends the result to rotating (boosted) black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive black hole entropy microscopically in 3D Lovelock gravity—including recently found regular BTZ-like solutions—with arbitrary coupling constants, bypassing reliance on central charges in the Cardy formula.

Method: Uses mini-superspace reduction to obtain closed-form expressions for the bulk action and boundary term via the generating function of Lovelock couplings; identifies the ground state as a double-Wick-rotated soliton; computes soliton mass and applies the Cardy formula; extends to stationary (rotating) configurations via boost, computing mass and angular momentum.

Result: Reproduces semi-classical black hole entropy from the Cardy formula without central charges for arbitrary Lovelock couplings in both static and rotating cases; confirms consistency of thermodynamics for regularized 3D Lovelock black holes.

Conclusion: The entropy of 3D Lovelock black holes—including regular BTZ variants—is fully captured by the Cardy formula at the microscopic level, even without central charges, provided the correct ground state (soliton) and mini-superspace quantization are used—valid for both static and rotating configurations.

Abstract: We give a microscopic derivation of the semi-classical entropy of the black holes of 3d Lovelock gravity with an infinite tower of regularized Lovelock corrections, which include the regular BTZ black holes found recently. Working in the mini-superspace formalism, we show that the reduced action and its boundary term can be obtained in closed form at all orders in terms of the generating function of the couplings. Assuming the ground state is described by the soliton obtained from the static black hole by a double Wick rotation, we compute the soliton mass and reproduce the semi-classical entropy from the Cardy formula without central charges for arbitrary couplings. We then extend the mini-superspace to stationary configurations, compute the mass and the angular momentum of the rotating black holes obtained by a boost, and verify the Cardy formula in the presence of rotation.

</details>


### [130] [Random wave chaos on momentum-fractionated microstate geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15918)
*Vladan Djukić,Mihailo Čubrović*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates chaos in 3-charge D1-D5 supergravity solutions (superstrata), comparing momentum-fractionated vs. single-mode backgrounds using null geodesics and wave dynamics; fractionated cases exhibit strong, random-wave-like chaos, while single-mode ones are more separable—suggesting a link between momentum fractionation and quantum chaoticity of BPS microstates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how momentum fractionation—a feature distinguishing certain BPS microstate geometries—affects classical and wave chaos, and whether this reflects differences in quantum chaotic properties between 'monotone' and 'fortuitous' BPS states.

Method: Analyzes null geodesic motion and scalar wave propagation on smooth, horizonless superstratum geometries; quantifies chaos via phase-space structure (e.g., chaotic sea size) and statistical wavefunction properties (Berry random wave hypothesis); compares fractionated vs. non-fractionated (single-mode) superstrata.

Result: Momentum-fractionated superstrata exhibit uniformly strong chaos—large chaotic seas and excellent agreement with Berry random wave statistics—for both geodesics and waves; single-mode superstrata show weaker chaos and greater near-separability; the distinction persists despite both being smooth, horizonless, and BPS.

Conclusion: Momentum fractionation correlates with enhanced classical and wave chaos, potentially signaling deeper quantum chaotic distinctions among BPS microstates; the probes remain physically meaningful for microstate geometries because they resolve structure absent in the coarse-grained black hole limit.

Abstract: We study the role of momentum fractionation in the development of chaos on smooth 3-charge supergravity solutions of D1-D5 theory, specifically momentum-fractionated superstrata. We probe the chaos through the dynamics of null geodesics and waves. On fractionated backgrounds, both geodesics and waves show uniformly strong chaos (large chaotic sea and near-perfect agreement with the Berry random wave hypothesis). In contrast, geodesics and waves on single-mode (non-fractionated) superstrata are closer to separability (although still chaotic). We discuss a possibility that such dichotomy can be related to the expectation that monotone BPS states are less chaotic than fortuitous ones. Finally, we explain why our chaos probes are meaningful for microstate geometries even though they become trivial in the black hole limit.

</details>


### [131] [Symmetric Dual Breather Modes as a Dynamic Origin of Tiny Particle Masses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15990)
*Yoritaka Iwata,Yasuhiro Takei*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces an operator-theoretic framework for spontaneous symmetry breaking (SSB) in scalar field theory, showing that static SSB is only a small-amplitude limit; beyond a critical amplitude, dynamic barrier overflow leads to vanishing order parameter, curvature cancellation, emergent sub-eV mass scales, Majorana preference, and chiral symmetry reduction yielding the Weyl equation—linking nonperturbative dynamics to neutrino mass generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover the nonperturbative dynamical pathways underlying spontaneous symmetry breaking—beyond static vacuum selection—and explain the origin of light neutrino masses and chiral structure without fine-tuning.

Method: Develops an operator-theoretic framework based on spectral branching; identifies a 'symmetric dual breather mode' as an effective invariant manifold of the nonlinear Klein-Gordon equation; employs time-dependent finite-dimensional phase-space representation to analyze geometric and spectral evolution across amplitude regimes, especially near the heteroclinic boundary.

Result: Reveals that static SSB is a limiting case (A ≪ φ_v1); above threshold (A > φ_v1), dynamic barrier overflow yields ⟨φ⟩ = 0 and ⟨V''(φ)⟩_t → 0; logarithmic period divergence governed by universal scaling function ℱ(y) generates sub-eV effective masses; selects Majorana-like states; induces asymmetric phase-space contraction leading to decoupling of right-handed mode and emergence of left-handed Weyl dynamics.

Conclusion: Spontaneous symmetry breaking has a rich dynamical origin: heteroclinic wave dynamics—not just vacuum degeneracy—govern mass suppression, chirality, and neutrino-scale physics, providing a unified nonperturbative mechanism for active neutrino masses.

Abstract: Spontaneous symmetry breaking (SSB) is conventionally understood through static vacuum selection in low-energy approximations. However, the nonperturbative dynamical pathways connecting symmetric configurations to asymmetric states remain an open question. In this paper, we propose an operator-theoretic framework that characterizes $Z_{2}$ symmetry breaking in scalar field theory through spectral branching. Specifically, we identify an effective invariant manifold of the nonlinear Klein-Gordon equation termed the "symmetric dual breather mode," whose phase-space geometry is traced using a $t$-dependent finite-dimensional representation. Our analysis demonstrates that static SSB represents merely a small-amplitude limit ($A \ll φ_{v1}$). As the amplitude increases into the heteroclinic regime ($A > φ_{v1}$), the field dynamically overflows the central barrier, yielding a vanishing time-averaged order parameter ($\langle φ\rangle = 0$) and driving a geometric curvature cancellation ($\langle V''(φ) \rangle_{t} \to 0$). Governed by a universal scaling function $\mathfrak{F}(y)$, the logarithmic period divergence near the heteroclinic boundary naturally generates sub-eV effective mass scales without fine-tuning bare parameters. Furthermore, this dynamic barrier overflow naturally favors Majorana-like self-conjugate states over Dirac-type states. Additionally, nonlinear phase interference along the heteroclinic channel induces asymmetric phase-space contraction, decoupling the right-handed mode and dynamically giving rise to the left-handed Weyl equation. These results bridge nonperturbative wave dynamics, topological mass suppression, chiral selection, and the dynamic origin of active neutrino masses.

</details>


### [132] [Relativistic Covariance from Hamiltonian Hydrodynamics in the Chiral Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16095)
*ZiHao Liu,Mei Huang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives covariant nondissipative chiral hydrodynamics from an equal-time Hamiltonian framework, reveals its Lie–Poisson structure coupling fluid dynamics and chiral symmetry, solves Lorentz boost constraints for a parity-invariant quadratic Hamiltonian, and establishes a causality condition: subluminal pion propagation in the rest frame is necessary and sufficient for finite coefficients at all sublight velocities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate chiral hydrodynamics in a manifestly covariant, symmetry-respecting, and thermodynamically consistent way—starting from first principles (Hamiltonian structure) rather than phenomenological ansätze—and to clarify the role of Lorentz invariance and causality in chiral effective theories.

Method: Equal-time Hamiltonian formulation; derivation of covariant equations via Poisson brackets; identification of Lie–Poisson structure encoding both fluid advection and local chiral SU(2)_L × SU(2)_R transformations; explicit solution of Lorentz boost constraints for a quadratic parity-invariant Hamiltonian in the absence of external backgrounds; analysis of velocity dependence and causality via coefficient finiteness conditions.

Result: The chiral hydrodynamic equations admit a Lie–Poisson structure unifying fluid motion and chiral symmetry; the nonlinear boost constraints reduce to linear transformation laws of a symmetric tensor; finiteness of all Hamiltonian coefficients at sublight velocities is guaranteed iff the rest-frame pion speed ≤ c; the formulation explicitly encodes symmetries and supports future dissipative extensions compatible with thermodynamics.

Conclusion: A fully covariant, symmetry-transparent, and causal formulation of nondissipative chiral hydrodynamics is achieved through Hamiltonian reduction and Lie–Poisson geometry; the derived causality bound (subluminal pion speed in the rest frame) is both necessary and sufficient for physical consistency across all fluid frames, providing a rigorous foundation for extending to dissipative regimes.

Abstract: We derive the covariant equations of nondissipative chiral hydrodynamics from their equal-time Hamiltonian formulation and identify the Lie--Poisson structure implicit in the chiral brackets. This structure combines fluid motion with local chiral transformations. For a parity-invariant Hamiltonian quadratic in chiral charge densities and pion gradients, without external backgrounds, we derive and solve the constraints imposed by Lorentz boosts. Their coupled nonlinear sector is equivalent to the linear boost transformation laws of a symmetric tensor, which determine the velocity dependence from rest-frame data. For finite, positive rest-frame coefficients, finite ordinary-fluid energy density and thermodynamic derivatives, and a momentum--velocity relation that can be solved in either direction, the chiral Hamiltonian coefficients remain finite at every fluid speed below light speed if and only if the rest-frame pion propagation speed does not exceed the speed of light. This formulation makes symmetry constraints explicit and provides a starting point for future thermodynamically consistent dissipative extensions.

</details>


### [133] [Retarded Green Function For Bopp--Podolsky Electrodynamics in Four-Dimensional De Sitter Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16239)
*Wei Fan,Xiao Yan Chew*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes classical Bopp-Podolsky electrodynamics on de Sitter spacetime, deriving the transverse retarded Green function via invariant bitensor decomposition and hypergeometric reduction; it shows cancellation of leading short-distance singularities, emergence of a logarithmic singularity and softened wavefront, and identifies the massive sector as sole source of interior electromagnetic tail; it also obtains an exact horizon-regular static potential, recovers flat-space limit, formulates generalized Gauss law, and enforces global charge neutrality via antipodal configuration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-derivative (Bopp-Podolsky) electrodynamics behaves in curved, maximally symmetric cosmological spacetimes—specifically de Sitter—where quantum vacuum ambiguities and infrared issues arise, while maintaining full classical covariance and gauge invariance without invoking quantum prescriptions (e.g., Lee-Wick contour or preferred vacuum).

Method: Covariant bitensor decomposition of vector Green functions on de Sitter spacetime; factorization of the fourth-order BP field operator for conserved sources into Maxwell and Proca-like (massive-vector) operators; reduction of propagator equations to a hypergeometric master equation; distributional analysis of solutions including short-distance and light-cone behavior; explicit construction of horizon-regular static solutions and antipodal charge configurations satisfying global charge neutrality on compact spatial slices.

Result: The BP transverse retarded Green function is expressed analytically via hypergeometric functions; leading UV singularities cancel between Maxwell and massive sectors, leaving a logarithmic singularity and smoothed wavefront; the gauge-invariant field-strength kernel features a null-cone delta and a timelike tail sourced purely by the massive sector; an exact static potential regular at cosmological horizons is obtained, with Coulomb divergence canceled and flat-space BP limit recovered; generalized Gauss law is formulated; global charge neutrality is enforced via smooth antipodal initial data.

Conclusion: Classical Bopp-Podolsky electrodynamics on de Sitter spacetime is well-defined, covariant, and infrared-finite: its causal structure and static response are governed by interplay between massless and massive modes, with the massive sector exclusively generating long-range timelike tails and enabling horizon-regular potentials; the framework avoids quantum ambiguities and naturally accommodates global topological constraints like vanishing total charge.

Abstract: We study classical Bopp--Podolsky (BP) electrodynamics on four-dimensional de Sitter spacetime, focusing on its transverse retarded Green function and gauge-invariant electromagnetic response. Because de Sitter spacetime is maximally symmetric, its vector Green functions admit a covariant expansion in a finite basis of invariant bitensors, with scalar coefficients depending only on the invariant separation. For conserved sources, the fourth-order field operator factorizes into Maxwell and massive-vector operators, yielding the BP retarded kernel as the difference of their transverse Green functions. Using the invariant bitensor decomposition, we reduce the propagator equations to a hypergeometric master equation and determine the distributional structure of its solution. The common leading short-distance singularity of the Maxwell and massive-vector sectors cancels, leaving a logarithmic master singularity and a softened vector-potential wavefront. After one curl, the gauge-invariant field-strength kernel contains a null-cone delta distribution and a regular timelike tail. The smooth Maxwell master tail is annihilated by the curl, so the interior electromagnetic tail is generated entirely by the massive sector. We also derive an exact horizon-regular static potential, demonstrate the cancellation of the Coulomb divergence, recover the flat-space BP potential, and formulate the generalized Gauss law. On global de Sitter spacetime, the compactness of the spatial sections requires the total charge to vanish; we implement this constraint using an antipodal charge configuration with smooth factorized initial data. The analysis is classical and does not assume a quantum Lee--Wick contour or a preferred de Sitter vacuum.

</details>


### [134] [A first order nonabelian antisymmetric tensor model with hidden higher symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16250)
*Peter D. Jarvis,Jean Thierry-Mieg*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a classical nonabelian complex self-dual antisymmetric tensor model with gauged internal symmetry, using a first-order formulation and Dirac/internal matrix expansions; auxiliary fields become curvatures on-shell, and hidden internal symmetries—linked to $SU(2)	imes U(1)$, $SU(3)$, or $SU(2/1)$—are identified.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend self-dual tensor field theories to nonabelian, complex, and gauged settings while uncovering novel hidden internal symmetries beyond standard gauge invariance.

Method: First-order classical formulation; component expansion over Dirac algebra and internal matrix basis; Lorentz/gauge invariance enforced via traces and bilinear forms; auxiliary fields eliminated via algebraic equations of motion to yield curvatures; analysis of symmetry enhancement under internal matrix actions (Lie/graded Lie brackets).

Result: A consistent classical model of massless complex self-dual antisymmetric tensors coupled to Yang-Mills fields is constructed; auxiliary fields are shown to become field strengths on-shell; hidden internal symmetry is established, realized via additional internal matrices acting as $SU(3)$ generators or $SU(2/1)$ supergenerators.

Conclusion: The model provides a framework for nonabelian self-dual tensor dynamics with extended internal symmetry, suggesting potential relevance for higher-form gauge theories, supersymmetric extensions, and geometric formulations of chiral p-forms in diverse dimensions.

Abstract: We present a nonabelian complex self-dual antisymmetric tensor model, with gauged internal symmetry, comprising a complex multiplet of massless self-dual antisymmetric tensor fields and its conjugate (interacting with massless Yang-Mills gauge fields). We build the model at the classical level, via a first order formulation, assigning local fields to a component expansion over the Dirac algebra and a basis of internal matrices, with Lorentz and gauge invariance ensured by tracing, and applying an appropriate bilinear form. The full expansion requires auxiliary place-holder fields, which are not potentials, but become curvatures (field strengths) after elimination via their algebraic equations of motion. The component expansion over the Dirac algebra is closed under multiplication by additional internal matrices, which are not gauge group generators, but mix internal components, and link auxiliary curvatures with potentials. The model is shown to be invariant under such transformations, and thus possesses a type of hidden internal symmetry. We analyze the specific case of gauge group $SU(2)\! \times \! U(1)$, and as higher symmetry candidates, we consider both the Lie group $SU(3)$ and the Lie superalgebra $SU(2/1)$ (with the additional matrices acting via Lie brackets, or graded Lie brackets, respectively).

</details>


### [135] [Regular-Zero Drinfeld-Sokolov Charges in Doubled Four-Dimensional Chern-Simons Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16271)
*Wentao Zeng,Yifan He,Xian Wu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs classical local conserved charges via finite-defect reduction of doubled 4D Chern–Simons theory, proving their pairwise Poisson commutativity using Evans-compatible DS hierarchy tensors and residue analysis at simple zeros; it establishes a conditional existence result for an Abelian Poisson subalgebra in a constrained reduced phase space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and prove integrability (Poisson commutativity) of local conserved charges arising from finite-defect reductions of doubled 4D Chern–Simons theory—a framework linking integrable PDEs, affine Gaudin models, and boundary Hamiltonian structures.

Method: Finite-defect reduction at simple zeros of the twist function; residue evaluation of Gaudin currents; use of Evans-compatible invariant tensors from the principal Drinfeld–Sokolov hierarchy; case-wise analysis (same-zero, distinct-zero, mixed sectors); decomposition of the reduced Hamiltonian under Stedman’s boundary assumptions; symplectic reduction to physical quotient space.

Result: Proof of pairwise Poisson commutativity of constructed local circle-integral charges; termwise decomposition of the reduced Hamiltonian into finite-pole, infinity, finite-zero, and residual first-class constraint contributions; conservation of charges; identification of three distinct phase spaces (reduced, extended with constraint, physical quotient); conditional existence of a classical local Abelian Poisson subalgebra.

Conclusion: A rigorous, geometric-algebraic construction of commuting local charges is achieved under specific analytic (simple zero) and boundary (Stedman-type, trivial monodromy, phase-space-independent weights) assumptions; functional independence and Liouville completeness remain open.

Abstract: We study classical local conserved charges obtained after finite-defect reduction of doubled four-dimensional Chern--Simons theory. At a finite simple zero, the inhomogeneous derivative term in the affine gauge transformation induced by the twist function vanishes, so the residue evaluations of the two Gaudin currents transform by the ordinary adjoint action. For each simple ideal, we use the Evans-compatible invariant tensors of the principal Drinfeld--Sokolov hierarchy as external algebraic input, construct local circle integrals, and treat the same-zero, distinct-zero, and mixed sectors separately, proving pairwise Poisson commutativity. Under the explicit assumptions of Stedman's simple-pole/simple-zero boundary Hamiltonian, the periodic trivial-monodromy sector, and boundary weights independent of phase-space variables, the reduced Hamiltonian decomposes term by term into finite-pole, infinity, finite-zero, and residual first-class-constraint contributions, which yields conservation of the constructed charges. We distinguish the finite-defect reduced space, the extended space carrying the residual constraint, and the physical quotient. The result is a conditional existence statement for a classical local Abelian Poisson subalgebra; we make no claim of functional independence, maximality, or Liouville completeness. Extensions to higher-order zeros and nontrivial monodromy sectors are outside the scope of this work.

</details>


### [136] [A No-Go Theorem for Nonspherical Bounces with a Single Negative Mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16688)
*Guangshang Chen,Jianpeng Dai,Yang Xiao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper proves that nonspherical saddle points in false vacuum decay must have at least three negative fluctuation modes—ruling out nonspherical bounces with only one negative mode, thereby reinforcing spherical symmetry as a spectral necessity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the single negative mode requirement alone enforces spherical symmetry in false vacuum decay, independent of action minimization.

Method: Rigorous spectral analysis of localized nonspherical saddles with zero initial velocity for a single real scalar field in flat spacetime; application of symmetry constraints (e.g., reflection symmetries) and illustration via a sextic scalar model.

Result: Every localized nonspherical saddle with zero initial velocity has ≥3 negative fluctuation modes; reflection symmetries further increase the lower bound on the number of negative modes.

Conclusion: Nonspherical bounce candidates with only one negative mode are spectrally forbidden; spherical symmetry is not just action-minimizing but also spectrally mandated by the single-negative-mode criterion.

Abstract: False vacuum decay is conventionally described by a spherically symmetric bounce with a single negative fluctuation mode. This raises a fundamental question: can the single negative mode requirement itself enforce spherical symmetry, independently of action minimization? For a single real scalar field with a smooth potential in flat spacetime, we rigorously prove that every localized nonspherical saddle with zero initial velocity has at least three negative modes. Additional reflection symmetries further tighten these bounds, as illustrated in a sextic scalar model. Our findings exclude nonspherical bounce candidates with a single negative mode, providing a complementary spectral foundation for the spherical bounce description of false vacuum decay.

</details>


### [137] [Yang-Baxter deformed sigma models on $\mathrm{SU}(N)/\mathrm{SO}(N)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17362)
*Danila Alexandrov,Alexey Bychkov,Ivan Kalinichenko,Alexey Litvinov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes Yang-Baxter deformations of 2D sigma models on SU(N)/SO(N) symmetric spaces, identifying their UV fixed point as an SO(N,C)/SO(N,R) non-compact WZW model plus N−1 compact free bosons, and identifies leading corrections via screening current perturbations; the construction is generalized to maximal-rank symmetric spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the ultraviolet behavior of Yang--Baxter deformed sigma models on compact symmetric spaces, particularly whether they flow to well-defined conformal field theories in the UV and how those relate to known non-compact WZW models.

Method: Analytic study of integrable deformations using Yang--Baxter equations; identification of UV fixed points via symmetry analysis and current algebra; computation of leading irrelevant perturbations using screening currents associated with the non-compact WZW model.

Result: The UV fixed point of the deformed SU(N)/SO(N) sigma model is established as the SO(N,C)/SO(N,R) non-compact WZW model tensored with N−1 compact free bosons; the leading correction is explicitly identified with a screening-current-generated perturbation; the result extends to all symmetric spaces of maximal rank.

Conclusion: Yang--Baxter deformations of sigma models on maximal-rank compact symmetric spaces exhibit controlled UV behavior governed by non-compact WZW models, providing a new class of integrable, non-perturbatively defined CFTs with potential applications in AdS/CFT and quantum gravity.

Abstract: We study Yang--Baxter deformations of two-dimensional sigma models on compact symmetric spaces $\mathrm{SU}(N)/\mathrm{SO}(N)$, with particular emphasis on their ultraviolet behavior. We claim that the ultraviolet fixed point is described by the $\mathrm{SO}(N,\mathbb C)/\mathrm{SO}(N,\mathbb R)$ non-compact WZW model together with $N-1$ compact free bosons. We determine the leading correction to this fixed point and identify it with the perturbation generated by the corresponding screening currents. We also extend our construction to the broader class of symmetric spaces of maximal rank.

</details>


### [138] [About P-adic Numbers and Professor Igor Volovich on the Occasion of his 80th Anniversary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16708)
*Paul H Frampton*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits foundational work from 1987 by Volovich and later collaborations with Okada on p-adic string theory and its associated non-local field theory, outlining Feynman rules and noting their physical applications; it also includes personal recollections of academic visits to Moscow (1989–91).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To revisit and contextualize the historical development of p-adic string theory and its non-local field-theoretic formulation, emphasizing its conceptual significance and physical relevance.

Method: Conceptual and historical review; reconstruction and exposition of Feynman rules for p-adic strings and associated non-local field theory.

Result: Reaffirmation of the consistency and applicability of p-adic string theory’s Feynman rules and its non-local field-theoretic framework across diverse physical contexts.

Conclusion: The p-adic string formalism remains a valid and insightful approach to non-local quantum field theory, with enduring theoretical value and interdisciplinary impact.

Abstract: We recall a 1987 paper written by Volovich and our subsequent work with Okada which established the Feyman rules for the p-adic string and the related non-local field theory which found diverse physical applications. We add some memories of visits to Moscow during 1989-91.

</details>


### [139] [Beyond the circle: current algebras, geometry and higher gauge structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17781)
*R. Campoamor-Stursberg,A. Marrani,M. Rausch de Traubenberg*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes current algebras from circle-based to manifold-based settings, systematically separating coefficient algebra, harmonic analysis, and geometry; it characterizes central extensions via differential forms modulo exact ones, constructs explicit cocycles from divergence-free vector fields, derives stabilizer conditions for compatible derivations, and compares analytic frameworks across compact (e.g., S³), noncompact (SL(2,ℝ)), and soft geometric contexts; it unifies recent harmonic constructions with cohomological and loop-geometric approaches, links current algebras to higher brackets and Lie algebras of order F, and identifies binary + symmetric F-ary constraints on their central extensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize current algebras beyond the circle—incorporating arbitrary manifolds, diverse geometries (compact, noncompact, soft), and higher-algebraic structures—while clarifying the interplay among coefficient algebras, harmonic analysis, cohomology, and dynamics.

Method: Cohomological analysis of current Lie algebras using de Rham theory (central extensions ↔ H¹dR or H²dR mod exact forms), explicit construction of 2-cocycles from divergence-free vector fields, derivation classification via stabilizer equations, comparison of harmonic (Campoamor-Stursberg et al.) vs. cohomological (Coquereaux et al.) vs. geometric (Castellani) frameworks, and extension to Lie algebras of order F using coupled binary and symmetric F-ary cohomological conditions.

Result: 1) Central extensions of manifold-based current algebras are classified by differentials modulo exact forms; 2) Divergence-free vector fields yield explicit, geometrically natural 2-cocycles; 3) Compatible derivations satisfy a stabilizer equation; 4) Compact examples (S³) and noncompact cases (SL(2,ℝ), its symmetric space) are analytically characterized; 5) Transported multiplication is identified as key in soft geometries; 6) A coefficient-algebra formalism bridges currents, higher brackets, and dynamical consistency; 7) For Lie algebras of order F, central extensions obey coupled binary and symmetric F-ary cocycle conditions—with concrete examples and obstructions identified.

Conclusion: The geometric, analytic, and algebraic structures underlying current algebras are deeply interconnected: central extensions are governed by manifold topology and geometry (via de Rham cohomology), derivations reflect symmetry stabilization, and generalizations to higher-order Lie algebras impose strict coupled cohomological constraints—providing a unified framework that reconciles harmonic, cohomological, and loop-geometric perspectives while exposing necessary conditions for physical realizability.

Abstract: Replacing the circle by a manifold leads to current Lie algebras and their central and derivation extensions. We separate the coefficient algebra, harmonic analysis and geometric data. Differentials modulo exact forms describe central extensions; divergence-free vector fields give explicit cocycles, whose compatible derivations satisfy a stabilizer equation. Compact examples include the three-sphere and its current--Virasoro extension. We also explain the analytic qualifications for SL(2,R) and its symmetric space and the role of transported multiplication in soft geometries. The recent harmonic constructions of Campoamor-Stursberg, Marrani and Rausch de Traubenberg are compared with the cohomological work of Coquereaux, Frappat, Ragoucy and Sorba and Castellani's loop geometry and infinite-dimensional free differential algebras. A coefficient-algebra construction connects currents to higher brackets and clarifies the additional requirements of a dynamical theory. For Lie algebras of order F, central extensions obey coupled binary and symmetric F-ary conditions, with explicit examples and obstructions.

</details>


### [140] [TheoPhysEval: Assessing AI agents at autonomous theoretical physics problem solving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16733)
*A. Ramesh Chandra,Aaghaz Mahajan,Ainesh Sanyal,Mianqi Wang,Shubhadeep Chakraborty,Sudip Chakraborty,Shailesh Chandrasekharan,Rama Govindarajan,Akshit Goyal,Jonathan J. Heckman,Yasir Iqbal,Divya Jaganathan,Saumav Kapoor,Andreas Karch,Pushkar Khandare,Sandeep Krishna,Anupam Kundu,Alok Laddha,Tongyan Lin,Swarnendu Maity,Nilmani Mathur,Shani Nadir Meynet,Alessandro Mininno,Abhijeet Mishra,Vidur Niranjan,Abhishodh Prakash,Pratik Rath,Durbar Roy,Debargha Sarkar,Srikanth Sastry,Gary Shiu,Aninda Sinha,Thomas Van Riet,Praneeth Netrapalli,Suvrat Raju*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a benchmark of 50 unpublished theoretical physics problems and evaluates AI agents—especially GPT-6-Astra in Codex—on autonomous, tool-augmented solving; it achieves 98% accuracy at ~$3/problem, with evidence of substantial literature memorization and strong computational reasoning, yet no agent-led breakthroughs observed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously assess the current capability of off-the-shelf AI agents to perform expert-level, autonomous theoretical physics problem solving—without human hints—and to understand the gap between high accuracy on known-style problems and absence of genuine research breakthroughs.

Method: Curated 50 unpublished, research-level theoretical physics problems with verifiable answers; evaluated multiple AI agents (primarily GPT-6-Astra in Codex) operating autonomously with realistic tool access (literature search, symbolic/numerical computation); ablated tool availability (e.g., disabling web search) to probe reliance on memorization vs. retrieval.

Result: GPT-6-Astra + Codex achieved 98% answer accuracy at <$3/problem; minimal accuracy drop (~2%) when literature search was disabled, indicating strong internalized knowledge; agents consistently performed multi-step derivations, symbolic manipulations, and numerical validations correctly.

Conclusion: State-of-the-art AI agents possess robust, near-expert competence in executing concrete theoretical physics calculations—but this does not yet translate into original conceptual advances or breakthroughs, likely due to limitations in hypothesis generation, cross-domain synthesis, or deep physical intuition beyond pattern-matching and computation.

Abstract: We present a suite of 50 unpublished research-level theoretical physics problems with verifiable answers. Without AI assistance, a typical problem in our collection could be solved by a world expert in about a day or less but would take considerably longer for experts in adjacent specialties. We test the ability of off-the-shelf AI agents to tackle these problems autonomously, without human hints. When given access to tools available in a realistic setting, including the ability to search the literature and write scripts for numerical and symbolic work, the best-performing agent, GPT-6-Astra harnessed in Codex, obtains the final answers with a near-perfect accuracy of 98% at an average cost under USD 3 per problem. On average, agents show only small changes in accuracy when denied web search and access to background research papers, suggesting significant memorization of the literature. These results suggest that state-of-the-art agents can accurately perform concrete calculations required across theoretical physics research. We comment on the broader implications of these findings and discuss possible explanations for the current lack of agent-led breakthroughs in theoretical physics despite their outstanding problem-solving abilities.

</details>


### [141] [Supersymmetric Observables from Surface Defect Anomalies in 4d and 6d SCFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18086)
*Zi-Xiao Huang,Ma-Ke Yuan,Yang Zhou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes supersymmetric Casimir energy and the universal logarithmic term of supersymmetric Rényi entropy for half-BPS surface defects in 4D and 6D SCFTs using equivariant integration of defect anomaly polynomials; results are expressed in terms of defect ’t Hooft anomalies, with checks from chiral algebras, 5D localization, holography, and chiral fermion models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute universal observables—supersymmetric Casimir energy and the logarithmic term of supersymmetric Rényi entropy—for half-BPS surface defects in 4D and 6D SCFTs, leveraging anomaly-based methods and cross-validating across multiple frameworks.

Method: Equivariant integration of defect anomaly polynomials, extended to supersymmetric Rényi backgrounds; supplemented by chiral algebra techniques, five-dimensional localization, holographic probe brane analysis (large N), and modeling via localized chiral fermions.

Result: Closed-form expressions for Casimir energy and log Rényi entropy in terms of defect 't Hooft anomalies: for 4D N=4 SYM Gukov–Witten defects, dependence is solely on R-symmetry anomaly; for 6D N=(1,0) N=(0,4) defects, gravitational anomaly contributions appear; Wilson surface results in 6D N=(2,0) theories are recovered.

Conclusion: Defect 't Hooft anomalies fully control these universal supersymmetric observables; the anomaly polynomial framework unifies diverse defect types and dimensions, and is robustly verified across independent computational methods.

Abstract: We study the contributions of half-BPS surface defects to supersymmetric Casimir energy and the universal logarithmic term of supersymmetric Rényi entropy in four- and six-dimensional SCFTs. Using equivariant integration of defect anomaly polynomials and its extension to supersymmetric Rényi backgrounds, we obtain closed expressions for these observables in terms of defect 't Hooft anomalies. For Gukov--Witten defects preserving transverse rotations in four-dimensional $\mathcal{N} = 4$ super Yang--Mills theories, both observables depend on a single R-symmetry anomaly. For $\mathcal{N} = (0, 4)$ defects in six-dimensional $\mathcal{N} = (1, 0)$ theories, additional terms arise from the gravitational anomaly. We provide complementary checks using chiral algebras, five-dimensional localization, and holographic probe branes at large $N$, while localized chiral fermions reproduce the gravitational-anomaly contributions. The framework also recovers Wilson-surface results in six-dimensional $\mathcal{N} = (2, 0)$ theories.

</details>


### [142] [Out-of-time-order hydrodynamics and scrambling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16888)
*Marta Bucca,Akash Jain,Márk Mezei,Alexey Milekhin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends hydrodynamic EFT to a four-fold Schwinger-Keldysh contour to study OTOCs, introduces local SL(2,R) symmetry as a scrambling mechanism, derives the EFT from the low-temperature SYK lattice, computes energy-current OTOC saturating the chaos bound, and demonstrates hydrodynamics’ universality beyond time-ordered correlators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how hydrodynamics — traditionally predictive for time-ordered correlators — governs quantum scrambling, as probed by out-of-time-order correlators (OTOCs), especially in maximally chaotic systems like the SYK model.

Method: Constructs an all-derivative-order Schwinger-Keldysh EFT on a four-fold time contour; incorporates local SL(2,R) symmetry as a scrambling mechanism; derives the EFT microscopically from the low-temperature limit of the SYK lattice; computes energy-current OTOCs using this framework.

Result: The energy-current OTOC exhibits exponential growth saturating the MSS chaos bound; matches shockwave (scramblon) effective description; all transport coefficients are fixed from SYK lattice microscopics.

Conclusion: Hydrodynamic EFT — augmented with local SL(2,R) symmetry and extended to multi-contour formalism — universally describes scrambling via OTOCs, extending hydrodynamics’ predictive power beyond conventional correlators to quantum information observables.

Abstract: Hydrodynamics as an effective field theory (EFT) has been immensely successful for predicting the behaviour of time-ordered correlation functions in the long-time, large-distance regime. In this paper, we analyse the predictions of hydrodynamics for scrambling as probed by out-of-time order correlators (OTOCs). To do so, we consider an all-derivative order generalisation of the Schwinger-Keldysh EFT of hydrodynamics extended to a four-fold time contour required to capture OTOCs. A crucial ingredient of the construction is a local SL(2,R) symmetry, which has previously been suggested as a mechanism for scrambling. We also derive this exact EFT from the microscopic formulation of the maximally chaotic low-temperature limit of the SYK lattice, thereby determining all transport coefficients for this model. As a novel application, we compute the OTOC of energy currents, find that its exponential growth saturates the chaos bound, and match it to expectations based on a shockwave (or scramblon) effective description. Our work shows that the universality of hydrodynamics degrees of freedom extends beyond traditional time-ordered correlators to more complex observables such as OTOCs.

</details>


### [143] [Metric and dilaton information from high-overtone quasinormal modes of analytic Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16973)
*Yu Shi,Chikun Ding,Yuebing Zhou,Changqing Liu,Weike Deng,Sheng Long*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates how high-overtone quasinormal mode spectra of analytic Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton black holes encode interior geometry and matter properties—particularly via dilaton coupling and endpoint behavior of spacelike/timelike connections—and validates key predictions with exterior numerical spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe otherwise inaccessible black-hole interior geometry and supporting matter using high-overtone quasinormal modes, leveraging analytic black-hole solutions where interior structure is explicitly tied to spectral features.

Method: Analytic study of quasinormal mode spectra in Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton black holes; introduction of a neutral spectator field coupled to the dilaton; analysis of wave propagation across real- and complex-endpoint spacelike connections; examination of mass-label dependence, spectral convergence near causal thresholds, and local scaling behavior; comparison with exterior numerical spectra.

Result: High-overtone spectra encode metric exponents and signed dilaton slope via separable momentum response and mass-label dependence for real-endpoint spacelike connections; near the causal threshold, complex endpoints induce universal fractional corrections while preserving label dependence in regular propagation; the mechanism persists at criticality (no inner horizon) and extends locally into timelike interiors; exterior numerics confirm reconstruction feasibility and leading complex-endpoint responses.

Conclusion: Black-hole interior geometry and matter content are spectrally accessible through high-overtone quasinormal modes—not only in principle but via concrete, testable signatures rooted in wave connection, local scaling, and probe coupling—offering a pathway toward interior reconstruction from exterior observations.

Abstract: Black-hole interiors are hidden from exterior observers, yet their geometry can enter the high-overtone spectrum of linear perturbations. We study how this spectrum encodes metric and supporting-matter information in analytic Einstein--Maxwell--dilaton black holes. Coupling a neutral spectator to the dilaton at fixed UV dimension provides a matter-sensitive channel alongside the momentum response. For real-endpoint spacelike connections, the momentum response and mass-label dependence separate the metric exponents from the signed dilaton slope. Near the causal threshold, a complex endpoint controls the spectral family: the mass profiles approach a common value, producing a shared fractional correction, while regular propagation retains label dependence. This mechanism is present at criticality without an inner horizon and continues locally into timelike interiors. Exterior numerical spectra support the reconstruction and the leading complex-endpoint responses. Local scaling and probe coupling set the candidate responses, while wave connection determines their imprint on the exterior spectrum.

</details>


### [144] [Modular Flow and Holographic Timelike Entanglement Entropy in AdS and Flat Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17165)
*Mehdi Hakami Shalamzari,Zahra Paygozar,Mahdi Kord Zangeneh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs explicit modular flows for both spacelike and timelike intervals in AdS₃/CFT₂ and flat₃/CCFT₂, linking them to extremal curves and providing the first bulk realization of timelike modular flow underlying holographic timelike entanglement entropy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an explicit bulk realization of the modular flow underlying timelike extremal curves—previously lacking—for holographic timelike entanglement entropy.

Method: Derives boundary modular flows from symmetry generators in AdS₃/CFT₂ and flat₃/CCFT₂, then systematically extends them into the bulk; identifies fixed points of flows to recover extremal (spacelike/timelike) curves and computes associated entanglement entropies via integrated line elements.

Result: First explicit construction of bulk modular flow for timelike intervals yielding timelike extremal curves and holographic timelike entanglement entropy in both AdS₃ and flat space; shows flat-space results emerge as a limit of AdS₃ ones.

Conclusion: Modular flow can be systematically constructed from boundary symmetries and extended into the bulk to geometrically realize both spacelike and timelike holographic entanglement entropy, unifying and generalizing the extremal curve prescription beyond spacelike cases.

Abstract: Timelike entanglement entropy provides a useful probe of holographic theoris, yet an explicit bulk realization of the modular flow underlying its timelike extremal curve construction has so far been lacking. We construct modular flows associated with spacelike and timelike intervals in AdS$_{3}$/CFT$_{2}$ and extend this framework to flat$_{3}$/CCFT$_{2}$. Beginning with the boundary symmetry generators, we explicitly derive the boundary modular flows and extend them into the bulk, providing a systematic procedure that relates bulk modular flows to the extremal curves and holographic (timelike) entanglement entropy.
  For spacelike intervals, the resulting bulk modular flows determine the extremal curves, whose integrated line elements reproduce the standard holographic entanglement entropy. We then generalize this approach to timelike intervals, explicitly constructing the modular flows whose fixed points reproduce the extremal spacelike and timelike curves which gives the holographic timelike entanglement entropy. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first explicit construction of the modular flow associated with a timelike extremal curve for a timelike interval.
  The same procedure is then carried over to flat$_{3}$/CCFT$_{2}$, which give rise to the holographic (timelike) entanglement entropy. Finally, we show that the flat space results obtained in this work can be recovered from their AdS$_3$ counterparts in the flat space limit.

</details>


### [145] [On the analytic continuation and asymptotic behaviour of the two-point function in massless $φ^4$-theory in Euclidean de Sitter space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17215)
*Martin Beneke,Andrea F. Sanfilippo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the leading-logarithmic terms of the two-point function for a massless, minimally-coupled scalar field with λφ⁴ interaction in Euclidean de Sitter space up to O(λ²), identifies agreement with stochastic formalism upon analytic continuation, and proves Borel summability of the stochastic series, supporting use of Euclidean methods for late-time Lorentzian correlation functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in defining interacting quantum fields in de Sitter space and to establish a rigorous connection between Euclidean and stochastic approaches for computing late-time correlation functions of massless scalars.

Method: Perturbative computation in Euclidean de Sitter space using dimensional regularization and invariant distance expansion; analytic continuation to Lorentzian signature; asymptotic analysis and Borel transform techniques to study large-order behavior and summability of the stochastic series.

Result: Leading-logarithmic contributions to the two-point function at O(λ²) (N⁵LO in √λ) are derived; exact agreement is shown between Euclidean analytic continuation and stochastic formalism results; the stochastic series is proven Borel summable with explicitly determined large-order growth.

Conclusion: The Euclidean path integral approach, combined with Borel summability, provides a well-defined, predictive framework for late-time correlation functions in Lorentzian de Sitter space, validating its use over ad hoc stochastic or IR-resummed prescriptions.

Abstract: For the two-point function of the massless, minimally-coupled, real scalar field in Euclidean de Sitter space with $λφ^4$-interaction, we compute the leading-logarithmic terms in the invariant distance of the two-point function to $O(λ^2)$, which counts as the fifth subleading order (N$^5$LO) in the $\sqrtλ$-expansion. We show that the analytic continuation to Lorentzian signature coincides with the expansion of the analogous quantity computed in the stochastic formalism. We determine the large-order behaviour of the stochastic series expansion and prove its Borel summability, which suggests that the Euclidean theory can be used to extract the late-time behaviour of massless correlation functions in Lorentzian de Sitter space.

</details>


### [146] [New analytic Janus and $S^4$ solutions for $\mathcal{N}=2^*$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17238)
*Fridrik Freyr Gautason*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a three-parameter family of analytic supergravity solutions dual to N=2* gauge theory on S^4 and U(1)^2-invariant Janus interfaces in N=4 SYM, using a 5D consistent truncation of type IIB on S^5; it identifies a hidden integral of motion enabling analytic treatment, maps parameters to field theory sources/vevs, and discusses holographic Lagrangian one-point functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct explicit, regular, analytic supergravity duals for strongly coupled field theory configurations—specifically the N=2* theory on S^4 and symmetric Janus interfaces in N=4 SYM—with controlled deformations (mass terms) and to enable precise holographic dictionary mapping.

Method: Uses a five-dimensional N=2 gauged supergravity model with six scalars from a consistent truncation of type IIB supergravity on S^5; solves the BPS equations analytically by exploiting a hidden integral of motion in the curved domain wall ansatz; imposes regularity and boundary conditions to constrain parameter space; performs holographic renormalization for one-point function computation.

Result: Derives a fully analytic, three-parameter family of regular domain wall solutions; explicitly relates each parameter to field theory sources (e.g., mass deformation, marginal couplings) and vevs; determines the physically allowed parameter region via regularity constraints; computes the holographic one-point function of the Lagrangian operator for Janus interfaces.

Conclusion: The analytic solutions provide a complete holographic description of N=2* on S^4 and symmetric Janus setups in N=4 SYM, confirming consistency of the truncation and offering a calculable framework for testing AdS/CFT beyond maximally symmetric backgrounds.

Abstract: We construct a three-parameter family of regular analytic supergravity solutions which are dual to (a) the $\mathcal{N}=2^*$ gauge theory on the four-sphere, and (b) $\mathrm{U}(1)^2$-invariant Janus interfaces in $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM with or without the $\mathcal{N}=2^*$ mass turned on. To this end we employ a five-dimensional supergravity model coupled to six scalars which arises as a certain consistent truncation of type IIB supergravity on $S^5$. The curved domain wall solutions feature a hidden integral of motion which allows for their analytic treatment. We relate the three parameters of the solution to field theory sources and vevs and show how regularity of the solutions restricts their range. We briefly discuss the holographic evaluation of the one-point function of the field theory Lagrangian for our family of Janus solutions.

</details>


### [147] [Holographic Pseudo Entropy from the Rindler Method in AdS and Flat Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17239)
*Mehdi Hakami Shalamzari,Zahra Paygozar,Mahdi Kord Zangeneh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes holographic timelike entanglement entropy (TEE) for a timelike interval in AdS₃/CFT₂ and flat₃/CCFT₂ using the Rindler method; it identifies the geometric origin of the imaginary part of TEE as a timelike geodesic connecting black hole horizons (in AdS) or cosmological horizon and timelike infinity (in flat space), yielding universal imaginary contributions (iπc/6 in AdS, equal real/imaginary parts on the CCFT₂ plane).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the geometric origin of the imaginary part of timelike entanglement entropy (TEE) — previously attributed to a timelike geodesic in the bulk but unidentified in the Rindler picture — and extend the analysis to flat holography where analogous structures are less understood.

Method: Applies the Rindler transformation to map a timelike interval to a rotating BTZ black hole background (in AdS₃) and to flat-space cosmological geometry (in flat₃); identifies extremal curves (spacelike and timelike) on horizons and computes their lengths via geodesic analysis; evaluates holographic TEE using the length-area prescription adapted to pseudo-entropy and timelike intervals.

Result: In AdS₃/CFT₂: the imaginary part iπc/6 of TEE arises solely from a timelike geodesic of fixed length iπℓ connecting inner and outer horizons of the rotating BTZ black hole; in flat₃/CCFT₂: the timelike extremal curve stretches between cosmological horizon and timelike infinity, and on the CCFT₂ plane, real and imaginary parts of TEE are equal — unlike on the cylinder.

Conclusion: The Rindler method successfully identifies the bulk geometric dual of the imaginary part of timelike entanglement entropy: a horizon-bridging timelike geodesic in AdS and a horizon-to-infinity geodesic in flat space; this provides a unified geometric interpretation of TEE’s pseudo-entropy structure across holographic settings.

Abstract: Via the Rindler method, we study holographic timelike entanglement entropy (TEE) for a general timelike interval in AdS$_3$/CFT$_2$ and flat$_3$/CCFT$_2$. The TEE is the pseudo entropy of a timelike interval. In the bulk, the imaginary part of the TEE comes from a timelike geodesic, but in the Rindler picture this geodesic has not been identified. After a Rindler transformation to a rotating BTZ black hole, the two spacelike extremal curves lie on the inner and outer horizons, and exchanging $l_u\leftrightarrow \ell l_φ$ takes one to the other. We find the geometric meaning of the timelike extremal curve: a geodesic stretched between the two horizons. Its length, $iπ\ell$, is independent of the interval, and it alone produces the imaginary part $iπc/6$. We follow the same procedure in flat space holography, where the spacelike curve sits on the cosmological horizon and the timelike curve lies between the cosmological horizon and timelike infinity. Evaluating the holographic TEE for a timelike interval in CCFT$_2$ on the plane, we find that, in contrast with the cylinder, the real and imaginary parts are equal.

</details>


### [148] [Love at First Loop: Quantum Deformability of Near-Extremal Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17259)
*Pablo A. Cano,Marina David,Roberto Emparan,Stefano Trezzi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that static Love numbers of near-extremal Reissner–Nordström black holes become nonzero for ℓ ≥ 1 due to quantum effects in the strongly fluctuating near-horizon Schwarzian mode; ℓ = 0 remains zero by scalar shift symmetry. The response is defined via the retarded Green’s function, with a renormalized conservative part (logarithmic frequency dependence fixed by near-horizon physics) and a finite, scheme-independent dissipative part exhibiting a non-analytic threshold and t⁻³⁄² late-time tail.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum gravitational effects in the near-horizon region of near-extremal black holes modify the classical vanishing of static Love numbers — a hallmark of black hole 'no-hair' behavior — particularly when the Schwarzian effective theory dominates.

Method: Analyzes the scalar field response using the near-horizon AdS₂ × S² geometry of near-extremal RN black holes; computes the full-frequency-dependent retarded Green's function at leading order in r₊ω; separates conservative (real part, requiring renormalization) and dissipative (imaginary part, finite) contributions; identifies virtual transitions among quantum throat states as the origin of nonzero ℓ ≥ 1 Love numbers.

Result: Static Love numbers vanish for ℓ = 0 (protected by shift symmetry) but are nonzero for ℓ ≥ 1 due to rotational energy splitting of intermediate quantum states; the conservative part of the response contains a universal logarithmic divergence fixed by near-horizon physics; the dissipative part is finite, vanishes below the rotational gap, and exhibits non-analyticity and a t⁻³⁄² tail at threshold.

Conclusion: The classical no-Love-number theorem breaks down quantum mechanically in the near-horizon regime of near-extremal black holes; tidal response must be defined via the Green's function rather than geometric notions; the resulting polarizability encodes discrete quantum structure of the throat, with observable signatures in dissipation and late-time decay.

Abstract: The static Love numbers of black holes vanish in classical, four-dimensional, asymptotically flat General Relativity. We study how this changes for the response of near-extremal Reissner--Nordström black holes to a minimally coupled massless scalar, at energies where the Schwarzian mode of the near-horizon throat fluctuates strongly. The black hole then responds as a quantum system with a spectrum of states. Its static response is a polarizability, given by virtual transitions between them. For $\ell=0$ the static Love number vanishes, by the shift symmetry of the scalar, but for multipoles $\ell\geq1$ the static Love numbers are nonzero: the finite rotational energy of the spin-$\ell$ intermediate states spoils the cancellation responsible for the classical result. Since the throat no longer has a classical geometry from which to read off a tidal response, we define it through the retarded Green's function, and compute its full frequency dependence at leading order in $r_+ω$. Its conservative part requires renormalization: the near-horizon theory fixes the coefficient of its logarithm, and with it a dependence on the energy that no local term can remove, while its finite local terms are matching data of the four-dimensional theory. The dissipative part is finite and scheme independent. For $\ell\geq1$, it vanishes at low frequencies when the energy is below the rotational gap, while at the gap threshold it becomes non-analytic in frequency and produces a late-time $t^{-3/2}$ tail.

</details>


### [149] [Gauge-Invariant Brout--Englert--Higgs Mechanism without Spontaneous Breaking of Local Gauge Symmetry: A Quantum-Reference-Frame Formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17264)
*Kei-Ichi Kondo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the BEH mechanism in a gauge-invariant, relational framework by treating the Higgs field’s gauge orientation as dynamical internal reference data—yielding a relational gauge connection and mass generation without invoking spontaneous symmetry breaking of local gauge redundancy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To eliminate reliance on unphysical local gauge redundancy and spontaneous symmetry breaking in the standard BEH mechanism, and instead ground mass generation in gauge-invariant relational structures tied to the Higgs field as a quantum reference frame.

Method: Constructs a gauge-invariant formulation by defining a dynamical Higgs frame (complete or partial), deriving a relational connection relative to it, analyzing the induced quadratic mass form on gauge-orbit directions, and interpreting physical masses via renormalized gauge-invariant composite correlators or dressed operators in residual-charged sectors; applies the framework to electroweak theory with explicit treatment of $SU(2)_L$ relationalization and residual $U(1)_{\rm em}$ Gauss law.

Result: Reproduces standard tree-level vector boson masses on regular patches; distinguishes local mass generation from global symmetry breaking and topology; identifies electromagnetic dressing requirements for $W^\pm$ and charged fermions, while permitting local gauge-invariant interpolators for neutral channels; unifies conceptual frameworks of Higgs dressing, Fröhlich–Marchetti–Strocchi (FMS) composites, and internal quantum reference frames.

Conclusion: The BEH mechanism can be fully understood as relational gauge-invariant dynamics anchored to the Higgs field as an internal reference frame—rendering spontaneous local symmetry breaking obsolete as a physical concept, and clarifying the role of dressing, compositeness, and residual constraints in mass generation.

Abstract: The Brout--Englert--Higgs (BEH) mechanism can be formulated in gauge-invariant relational terms without assigning physical spontaneous breaking to a local gauge redundancy. We treat the gauge orientation of the Higgs field as dynamical internal reference data and describe the gauge connection relative to this Higgs frame. On a regular patch where the frame exists, the Higgs kinetic term induces a positive-semidefinite mass-squared quadratic form on the gauge-orbit directions and reproduces the standard tree-level vector masses. A complete Higgs frame yields a gauge-invariant relational connection, whereas a partial frame is defined only up to a stabilizer group $H$ and produces Higgs stiffness only along the coset directions $G/H$. In quantum field theory the normalized frame and relational connection are nonpolynomial composite variables; physical masses are therefore characterized through suitably renormalized gauge-invariant composite correlators, or through fully dressed operators in residual-charged sectors. Applying this construction to the electroweak theory, we show that the Higgs frame relationalizes the $SU(2)_L$ redundancy while leaving a residual electromagnetic $U(1)_{\rm em}$ Gauss law: $W^\pm$ and charged fermionic modes require electromagnetic dressing, whereas neutral channels admit local gauge-invariant interpolators. This formulation connects Higgs dressings, FMS composites, and internal quantum-reference-frame language while keeping local mass generation distinct from global symmetry realization and topological obstructions.

</details>


### [150] [Type I-II/II-I vortices from dimension-six self-interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17437)
*A. Alonso-Izquierdo,A. González-Parra,K. Slawinska,A. Wereszczynski,T. Winyard*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a dimension-six self-interaction to the Abelian-Higgs/Ginzburg-Landau model, revealing novel multi-vortex regimes—type I-II (short-range attraction, long-range repulsion) and type II-I (opposite)—beyond standard type I/II and BPS limits; it identifies stable axially symmetric vortices in I-II and clustered/Abrikosov-lattice-like configurations in II-I.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how higher-order self-interactions modify vortex interactions and stability beyond the standard classification in superconductivity and topological defect physics.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of the modified Abelian-Higgs/Ginzburg-Landau model with a dimension-six scalar self-interaction term; classification of vortex interaction potentials and identification of multi-vortex solution structures via energy minimization and symmetry considerations.

Result: Discovery of two new vortex interaction types—I-II and II-I—with non-monotonic inter-vortex forces; characterization of stable axially symmetric multi-vortices in I-II and clustered or lattice-like configurations in II-I.

Conclusion: The dimension-six extension enriches the vortex taxonomy and phase structure, implying broader applicability in condensed matter systems and cosmological defect dynamics where higher-derivative or higher-power interactions are relevant.

Abstract: We show that adding a dimension-six self-interaction to the Abelian-Higgs or Ginzburg Landau model results in a remarkable and rich structure of multi-vortex solutions. Besides the standard type I (attractive) and type II (repulsive) regimes, separated by the Bogomol'nyi-Prasad-Sommerfeld limit (BPS), we identify two new possibilities: type I-II vortices, which attract at short distances and repel at long distances; and type II-I vortices, with the opposite behaviour. Specifically, within the type II-I regime, multi-vortex solutions form chunks of the Abrikosov lattice or clusters.
