Conversely, in the I-II regime, axially symmetric multi-vortices are stable; nevertheless, depending on the model parameters and topological charge (winding number), they can be either the ground state or not.

</details>


### [151] [Evading the Swampland de Sitter Obstruction via Thermodynamic Duality-Induced Horizon Corrections in Unimodular Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17438)
*Reza Baghbani*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores inflation in early-universe cosmology using Unimodular Gravity coupled to Power-Law Nonlinear Electrodynamics, incorporating quantum-corrected horizon thermodynamics (logarithmic and inverse-area entropy corrections). It derives modified Friedmann equations with $H^4$ and $H^6$ terms, shows these drive de Sitter-like attractors and modify slow-roll dynamics without an inflaton potential, satisfies Swampland conjectures via a correction factor $\mathcal{F}(H)$, and predicts $n_s$ and $r$ consistent with Planck 2018.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize inflation within a quantum-gravitational framework motivated by thermodynamic duality—avoiding ad hoc inflaton potentials while satisfying Swampland constraints and observational data.

Method: Derives quantum-corrected Friedmann equations from Unimodular Gravity + Power-Law Nonlinear Electrodynamics + horizon entropy corrections (logarithmic and inverse-area); performs phase-space analysis; computes effective slow-roll parameters and inflationary observables ($n_s$, $r$); tests against Swampland de Sitter and Distance conjectures.

Result: Emergence of $H^4$ and $H^6$ terms in Friedmann equations; quantum corrections induce early-time de Sitter-like attractors; modified slow-roll dynamics without fundamental inflaton; Swampland conjectures satisfied via $\mathcal{F}(H)$; predicted $n_s$ and $r$ align with Planck 2018.

Conclusion: Thermodynamic duality-induced horizon corrections provide a viable, testable, and theoretically grounded mechanism for inflation—bypassing traditional inflaton paradigms while remaining consistent with quantum gravity constraints and CMB observations.

Abstract: We investigate the early-universe cosmological implications of Unimodular Gravity coupled to Power-Law Nonlinear Electrodynamics in the presence of quantum-corrected horizon thermodynamics derived from the principle of thermodynamic duality. By incorporating both logarithmic and inverse-area corrections to the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, we derive modified Friedmann equations exhibiting novel $H^4$ and $H^6$ terms that represent genuine quantum gravity effects. We demonstrate that these higher-order corrections become dynamically significant in the high-energy regime of cosmic inflation, naturally modifying the slow-roll dynamics without requiring a fundamental inflaton potential. A comprehensive phase-space analysis reveals that the quantum corrections drive early-time de Sitter-like attractors, providing a robust mechanism for sustained inflationary expansion. Confronting the model with the Swampland program, we show that the correction factor $\mathcal{F}(H)$ modifies the effective slow-roll parameters, enabling the model to satisfy the Swampland de Sitter and Distance conjectures during the inflationary epoch. We calculate the inflationary observables, including the spectral index $n_s$ and the tensor-to-scalar ratio $r$, and demonstrate that the model's predictions are in excellent agreement with the Planck 2018 observational constraints. Our results suggest that thermodynamic duality-induced horizon corrections offer a viable and testable pathway for realizing successful inflation within a well-motivated quantum-gravitational framework.

</details>


### [152] [Resonant JPZ algebras and 3D Chern--Simons gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17480)
*Remigiusz Durka,Nelson Merino,D. M. Peñafiel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs 3D Chern-Simons gravity theories based on six resonant JPZ algebras derived from Lorentz, translation, and an extra Z-generator; three algebras (B₄, C̃₄, C₄) are isomorphic to iso(2,1)⊕so(2,1) but yield distinct gravitational realizations under fixed (ω,e,h) field identification; it computes invariant tensors, curvatures, CS actions, and field equations in compact form Ω_σ F = 0, and classifies metric sectors via direct-sum isomorphisms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and distinguish physically inequivalent 3D Chern–Simons gravity theories based on extended Poincaré-type algebras with an additional Lorentz-like generator Z_a, respecting resonance and Jacobi constraints, while preserving a consistent gravitational interpretation of the (ω,e,h) fields.

Method: Semigroup expansion (S-expansion) of the Lorentz algebra with resonance conditions; classification of six resonant JPZ algebras; computation of nondegenerate invariant bilinear forms; derivation of gauge curvatures and Chern–Simons actions; analysis of field equations in matrix form Ω_σ F = 0; use of Lie algebra isomorphisms to classify metric sectors and relate to known solutions.

Result: Three algebras—B₄, C̃₄, C₄—are shown to be isomorphic to iso(2,1)⊕so(2,1), yet produce distinct gravitational dynamics due to differing invariant tensors and field couplings; explicit Chern–Simons actions, field equations, and maximal-rank coupling sectors are obtained for all six JPZ cases; field equations unify curvature, torsion, and h-field contributions via Ω_σ F = 0; metric sectors are classified and linked to global/asymptotic solutions without losing the original (ω,e,h) interpretation.

Conclusion: Resonant JPZ algebras provide a rich framework for inequivalent 3D Chern–Simons gravities beyond standard Poincaré or AdS; isomorphism alone does not guarantee physical equivalence—field identification and invariant tensor structure are essential; the compact field equation Ω_σ F = 0 transparently encodes the role of the h^a field in geometry and dynamics.

Abstract: In this work we construct three-dimensional Chern--Simons gravity theories for the six resonant $JPZ$ algebras generated by the Lorentz generators $J_a$, translations $P_a$, and an additional Lorentz-like generator $Z_a$. The resonance condition together with the Jacobi identities leaves precisely six configurations arising from the semigroup expansions. Our main result is that three of them, $B_4$, $\widetilde C_4$, and $C_4$, are isomorphic to $iso(2,1)\oplus so(2,1)$, although they yield different gravitational realizations when we keep the original $JPZ$ field identification fixed. For each case $JPZ$, we determine the invariant tensors, gauge curvatures, Chern--Simons action, field equations, and the corresponding maximal-rank coupling sectors. The field equations can be written in the compact form $Ω_σ\mathbf F=0$, which makes explicit how the additional field $h^a$ contributes to the curvature, torsion, and the remaining gauge-curvature components. We use the direct-sum isomorphisms to classify the local metric sectors and to relate them to known global and asymptotic solutions, while keeping the gravitational interpretation tied to the original $(ω,e,h)$ fields.

</details>


### [153] [Construction and geometric properties of the fuzzy $CP^3$ onion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17807)
*Kohei Uno,Tomohiro Inagaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 7D quantum space from nested fuzzy CP^3 manifolds and demonstrates its continuum limit is the metric cone C(CP^3), not flat R^7—confirmed via spectral convergence of Laplacian, propagator, and heat diffusion; layer-to-layer maps are derived analytically via Schwinger-boson lift and match Kováčik–Tekel prescription.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive the emergent geometry of a noncommutative matrix model without ad hoc geometric input—testing whether fuzzy CP^3 layers naturally yield a curved background (C(CP^3)) rather than flat space in the large-N continuum limit.

Method: Constructs a layered matrix model using concentric fuzzy CP^3 of increasing N; computes spectra of free Laplacian, propagator, and heat kernel numerically and analytically; derives inter-layer maps via polar decomposition of second-quantized Schwinger-boson lift and verifies equivalence to Kováčik–Tekel copying; compares asymptotic behavior against C(CP^3) and R^7 predictions.

Result: Spectrum of Laplacian converges to C(CP^3) at second order (error < 1e-9 for p ≤ 8 at 20480 layers); flat R^7 deviates by 8–18% in p ≥ 1 sectors; propagator and heat diffusion reproduce C(CP^3)'s r^6 measure and Bessel-mode spectrum; inter-layer maps are closed-form and match Kováčik–Tekel; CP^1 reduction recovers fuzzy onion away from boundary; twistor-fibre modes are exactly degenerate with S^4 harmonics.

Conclusion: The matrix model predicts C(CP^3) as the unique classical geometry in the continuum limit—establishing it as an emergent, non-input geometry; this provides a concrete, fully determined example of geometric emergence in quantum gravity-inspired noncommutative geometry.

Abstract: We consider the matrix model of a seven-dimensional quantum space built from concentric fuzzy $CP^3$'s of increasing matrix size, and show that its continuum limit is the metric cone $C(CP^3)$ over $CP^3$, not flat $R^7$. Every ingredient is fixed before any comparison, so this geometry is a prediction rather than an input. The two geometries agree in the $s$ wave ($p=0$) but separate in the higher angular sectors (harmonics on $CP^3$ of degree $p\geq1$): in each sector tested, the spectrum of the free Laplacian converges at second order to that of the cone, and at 20480 layers the cone is matched to $1\times10^{-9}$ for $p\leq8$, whereas flat space misses by 8-18%. The free propagator converges to that of the cone at the same order, and heat diffusion reproduces the $r^6$ measure and the Bessel spectrum of the cone. The maps between neighbouring layers are obtained in closed form from the polar decomposition of the second-quantized Schwinger-boson lift, using a known operator identity, and coincide with the Kováčik-Tekel coefficient-copying prescription. For $CP^1$ the construction reduces, away from the outer boundary, to the Kováčik-Tekel fuzzy onion. Modes along the twistor fibre are exactly degenerate with the harmonics on $S^4$.

</details>


### [154] [Geometry and holography of $\mathrm{AdS}_2\times\mathrm{S}^2$: towards the field theory dual](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18344)
*Miroslava Mosso Rojas,Anayeli Ramirez*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies the superconformal quantum mechanics dual to supersymmetric AdS₂×S² solutions in Type IIB string theory, proposing an N=4 quantum mechanical theory organised on a 2D lattice in the auxiliary space Σ, with linear quivers per x₂-interval whose ranks encode D1/D5 charges; it validates the proposal for SL(2,R) and SL(2,R)×SU(2) non-Abelian T-duals of AdS₃ backgrounds and extends it to factorised solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct the holographic dual quantum mechanical theories for supersymmetric AdS₂×S²×CY₂×Σ solutions preserving eight supercharges, motivated by the scarcity of explicit SCQM duals for AdS₂ geometries and the need to extend AdS/CFT to lower-dimensional anti-de Sitter spaces.

Method: Analyzes brane content (D1-D5-NS5 with D3/D7 flavour branes) of Type IIB AdS₂×S²×CY₂×Σ solutions; proposes a lattice-organised N=4 SCQM with linear quivers indexed by x₂-intervals; computes central charge holographically; verifies consistency for known SL(2,R) and SL(2,R)×SU(2) non-Abelian T-duals of AdS₃ backgrounds; introduces global completions with extra D3/D7 branes and interprets fundamental strings as baryon vertices; generalises to factorised ansätze including equal-warp-factor cases.

Result: A concrete proposal for the dual N=4 superconformal quantum mechanics: a 2D lattice of linear quivers along x₁, one per x₂-interval, with ranks set by local D1/D5 charges; central charge matches holographic computation at leading order; explicit realisations for two families of non-Abelian T-duals confirmed; global completions with D3/D7 branes and baryon vertex interpretation established; extension to broader class of factorised solutions achieved.

Conclusion: The proposed lattice-structured N=4 SCQM provides a consistent and computationally verifiable holographic dual for the considered class of AdS₂×S² solutions; it unifies previously disparate examples (including non-Abelian T-duals) under a common framework and opens avenues for studying quantum black hole microstates via quiver quantum mechanics.

Abstract: We take a first step in identifying the superconformal quantum mechanics dual to supersymmetric AdS$_2\times$S$^2$ solutions. We focus on the Type IIB solutions of the form AdS$_2\times$S$^2\times$CY$_2\timesΣ$ that preserve eight supercharges, with $Σ$ a two-dimensional space parametrised by $(x_1,x_2)$, and read off their brane content, a D1-D5-NS5 intersection with D3 and D7 flavour branes. We propose that their dual $\mathcal{N}=4$ quantum mechanics is organised over a two-dimensional lattice of cells in $Σ$. Namely, it consists of copies of the linear quiver along $x_1$ dual to the U(1)-invariant solutions of the class, one for each interval along $x_2$, with ranks given by the D1 and D5 charges of the corresponding cell. Its central charge agrees with the holographic one at leading order. We show that the family of SL$(2,\mathbb{R})$ non-Abelian T-duals of the AdS$_3\times$S$^2\times$CY$_2$ solutions of massive Type IIA supergravity, constructed in previous work, belongs to this class, as does the SL$(2,\mathbb{R})\times$SU$(2)$ non-Abelian T-dual of AdS$_3\times$S$^3\times$CY$_2$, and work both out in detail. For both we propose global completions that introduce additional D3 and D7 branes. Along $x_2$ we interpret the fundamental strings stretched between the colour branes and the additional D3 and D7 branes as baryon vertices. Finally, we extend this proposal from the non-Abelian T-duals to the solutions of the class whose defining functions factorise in $x_1$ and $x_2$, including those in which the AdS$_2$ and S$^2$ factors have the same warp factor.

</details>


### [155] [Giant Graviton Expansions from Symmetric Functions and Free Fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18452)
*Min-xin Huang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides rigorous algebraic proofs for two expansions of supersymmetric indices in 4D N=4 U(N) SYM—Gaiotto–Lee single-sum and flavored Schur double-sum—using Hall–Littlewood orthogonality and free-fermion factorization, respectively; it clarifies analytic continuation and coefficient justification but stops short of proving Imamura’s full three-species expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish previously conjectured giant graviton expansions of supersymmetric indices—specifically the Gaiotto–Lee and flavored Schur expansions—in 4D N=4 U(N) super-Yang–Mills, resolving open questions about analytic continuation, coefficient validity, and scope relative to Imamura’s more general proposal.

Method: For the Gaiotto–Lee expansion: Hall–Littlewood symmetric function orthogonality + direct trace computation of operators. For the flavored Schur index: exact factorization of its free-fermion mode product into a symmetric double sum. Both involve careful specification of analytic continuation of brane indices and coefficient-wise justification.

Result: Two key relations are proved algebraically: (1) the full superconformal index admits a single-sum giant graviton expansion via Hall–Littlewood orthogonality; (2) the flavored Schur index admits a symmetric double-sum expansion via free-fermion factorization. The analytic structure and coefficient consistency of both expansions are explicitly confirmed.

Conclusion: The proofs validate the physical interpretation of finite-rank corrections as wrapped D3-brane contributions in specific limits, but highlight that Imamura’s three-species expansion remains unproven by these methods—and raises deeper questions about modularity and the interplay between geometry, representation theory, and integrability in the Schur sector.

Abstract: Giant graviton expansions express finite-rank corrections to supersymmetric indices in terms of indices for wrapped D3-branes. We give detailed algebraic proofs of two previously proposed relations for four-dimensional $\mathcal N=4$ $U(N)$ super-Yang--Mills theory. The first is the Gaiotto--Lee single-sum expansion of the full superconformal index, proved by Hall--Littlewood orthogonality and an elementary calculation of operator traces. The second is the symmetric double-sum expansion of the flavored Schur index, proved by factorizing its exact free-fermion mode product. We specify the analytic continuation of the brane indices and justify the sums coefficient by coefficient. The two proofs apply to different expansion prescriptions and do not establish the full three-species expansion of Imamura. We discuss the additional physical and mathematical questions raised by that expansion and by the modular properties of the Schur index.

</details>


### [156] [Black Strings and Black Holes: Worldsheet Spectra and Near-Extremal Limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18591)
*Ziyi Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Euclidean partition function of a gauged WZW model with SL(2,R)×U(1)/U(1) symmetry, exploring its dual geometries — a smooth horizonless linear-dilaton background and the Horne–Horowitz black string — depending on time-circle identification. It computes worldsheet spectra via holonomy integrals, recovers the Maldacena–Ooguri–Son spectrum in the ungauged limit, and studies near-extremal regimes of associated 2D and 3D black objects; for the 2D black hole, it proposes two discrete-series-based state towers that reproduce the AdS₂ entropy scaling ∼(3/2) log T, valid only below a critical temperature where the partition function diverges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand microscopic origin of entropy and spectral structure in near-extremal black strings and 2D black holes via exact worldsheet CFT techniques, bridging holographic duality between gauged WZW models and low-dimensional black geometries.

Method: Holonomy integral techniques for worldsheet character decomposition; direct evaluation of unfolded modular integrals for spacetime spectrum; dimensional reduction to obtain 2D black hole; analysis of discrete series representations in near-extremal limit.

Result: Recovery of Maldacena–Ooguri–Son spectrum in ungauged limit; identification of two candidate state towers in 2D black hole whose degeneracy yields (3/2) log T entropy scaling; determination that these states exist only below a critical temperature where full partition function diverges.

Conclusion: The discrete series representations of SL(2,R) provide a concrete worldsheet mechanism for logarithmic entropy scaling in near-extremal 2D black holes, supporting AdS₂/CFT₁ expectations, but their contribution is constrained by modular consistency and thermal instability at higher temperatures.

Abstract: We study aspects of the Euclidean partition function of the $\frac{SL(2,\mathbb R)\times U(1)}{U(1)}$ gauged WZW model. The target space geometry is either a smooth horizonless geometry with a linear-dilaton asymptotic region, or the Horne--Horowitz black string, depending on which circles we unwind as the time-direction. We first study the worldsheet spectrum and obtain the character decomposition via the holonomy integrals. Next, we obtain the spacetime spectrum of the horizonless geometry by directly performing the unfolded modular integrals, and recover the Maldacena-Ooguri-Son spectrum in the ungauged limit. When the chirally charged three-dimensional black string is dimensionally reduced, the target space is instead a two-dimensional charged black hole. We study the near extremal regime of the black hole and black string. For the two dimensional black hole, we propose two candidate towers of states that produce the expected $\frac{3}{2}\log T$ of the AdS$_2$ throat from the worldsheet discrete series representation. These towers appear only below the temperature at which the full amplitude becomes divergent. We comment on the contribution of these states in the near-extremal limit of the full partition function.

</details>


### [157] [Carrollian Yang-Mills theories in the homogeneous temporal gauge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18715)
*Carmelo P. Martin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper quantizes electric and magnetic Carrollian Yang-Mills theories using BRST path integral methods; the electric version is free (no interactions), while the magnetic version has only tree-level 3- and 4-point interactions for ξ_i and trivial Wilson-loop correlators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically quantize Carrollian Yang-Mills theories—limits of relativistic gauge theories with ultra-local (infinite speed-of-light) symmetry—and distinguish physical content between electric and magnetic Carrollian limits.

Method: BRST quantization in homogeneous temporal gauge applied to SU(N) Carrollian Yang-Mills theories; path integral formulation with careful treatment of constraints, gauge fixing, and vertex analysis via Feynman rules derived from the effective action.

Result: Electric Carrollian YM yields vanishing interaction vertices → free theory; magnetic Carrollian YM admits only tree-level ξ_i three- and four-point functions as coupling-dependent connected Green functions; Wilson-loop correlators are trivial in magnetic case but nontrivial in electric case.

Conclusion: The electric and magnetic Carrollian limits exhibit qualitatively distinct quantum structures: the former is trivially free, while the latter retains limited, purely tree-level interactions for elementary fields and suppresses Wilson-loop dynamics, highlighting inequivalence of Carrollian contraction schemes at the quantum level.

Abstract: We quantize, in the homogeneous temporal gauge, both the electric and the magnetic Carrollian Yang-Mills theories with SU(N) gauge symmetry in the fundamental representation. We use the BRST method implemented in the path integral. We show that in the first case all the interaction vertices vanish, thus, giving a free theory. In the magnetic case, there are no loop contributions to the Green functions of the elementary fields --i.e., Green functions not involving composite operators-- and the only connected parts of such Green functions with a dependence on the coupling constant are the tree level contributions to the three and four point functions of the field $ξ_i$. We also show that in the magnetic case the Green functions involving only Wilson loops are trivial and not so in the electric case.

</details>


### [158] [Imaginary HTEE: Probing Criticality and Coherence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18716)
*M. Ali-Akbari,M. M. Daryaei Goki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analytically computes the imaginary part of holographic timelike entanglement entropy (HTEE) in a charged AdS₅ black hole background, linking it to pseudoentropy; it shows the imaginary HTEE vanishes without charge, emerges due to chemical potential μ, diverges with exponent θ_I = 1/2 near the critical μ/T, and serves as a non-local quantum-information probe of criticality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify a robust, non-local quantum-informational signature of thermodynamic criticality in strongly coupled field theories dual to charged AdS black holes, using the imaginary part of timelike entanglement entropy as a probe of quantum coherence between initial and final states.

Method: Analytical computation of the imaginary part of holographic timelike entanglement entropy (HTEE) in a charged asymptotically AdS₅ black-hole background; identification of Im(HTEE) with Im(pseudoentropy); thermodynamic analysis across stable/unstable branches; critical scaling analysis near the μ/T critical point.

Result: Imaginary HTEE vanishes in the uncharged limit; is induced solely by nonzero μ; exhibits an inverse-square-root divergence in its derivative at the critical μ/T; yields critical exponent θ_I = 1/2 analytically; same exponent governs ∂T response at fixed μ; implies maximal quantum coherence between initial and final states at criticality.

Conclusion: The imaginary part of HTEE is a universal, non-local quantum-information probe of criticality in strongly coupled systems, encoding emergent quantum coherence and exhibiting mean-field critical scaling with θ_I = 1/2.

Abstract: We study the imaginary part of the holographic timelike entanglement entropy (HTEE) in a charged asymptotically AdS$_5$ black-hole background whose dual field theory exhibits a critical point in the presence of a chemical potential $μ$ and temperature $T$. Identifying the imaginary part of the HTEE with the imaginary part of the pseudoentropy, we derive an analytical expression for this quantity and investigate its behavior on the stable and unstable thermodynamic branches. We find that the imaginary HTEE vanishes in the uncharged limit and is induced entirely by a nonzero chemical potential, signaling the emergence of quantum coherence between the initial and final states. As the dimensionless ratio $μ/T$ approaches its critical value, we obtain an inverse-square-root divergence in the derivative of the imaginary HTEE and extract the critical exponent $θ_I = 1/2$ analytically. We further show that the same exponent governs the temperature response at fixed chemical potential. Our results establish the imaginary part of the HTEE as a non-local quantum-information probe of criticality in strongly coupled systems, and they imply that the initial and final states become maximally coherent at the critical point.

</details>


### [159] [Late-time electric screening in dS from EAdS loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18782)
*Ernesto Bianchi,Sacha Ployet,Charlotte Sleight,Massimo Taronna*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop gauge-field two-point functions in scalar QED on de Sitter space (dS_{d+1}) using the EAdS reformulation of the in-in formalism; it identifies a Schwinger-type mass for the Dirichlet gauge field, links bulk loop effects to boundary current anomalous dimension γ_J and multiplet recombination, derives a spectral Cutkosky rule expressing the kernel’s discontinuity via a two-particle cut, reduces the cut to Gamma functions determining the non-local kernel part via dispersion, and interprets the loop-corrected Dirichlet photon as a dS resonance (quasinormal mode) on the second sheet of the Casimir plane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute and interpret quantum corrections to gauge fields in de Sitter space—particularly how bulk loop effects manifest in boundary observables (e.g., current anomalous dimensions), reconcile with boundary CFT mechanisms (multiplet recombination), and unify with analytic frameworks (Bros–Moschella, spectral theory)—while clarifying the physical nature (resonance vs. particle) of gauge excitations in dS.

Method: Analytic computation of one-loop gauge-field two-point functions in scalar QED on dS_{d+1} using the Euclidean AdS (EAdS) reformulation of the Schwinger–Keldysh (in-in) formalism; spectral decomposition in the Casimir plane; derivation of a spectral Cutkosky rule for the retarded bubble kernel; dispersion-theoretic reconstruction of the non-local kernel from its discontinuity; analytic continuation to extract γ_J and resonance pole location.

Result: The Dirichlet gauge mode acquires a Schwinger-type mass and induces anomalous dimension γ_J for the boundary current; the Neumann mode remains massless due to gauge invariance; the retarded dS bubble kernel’s discontinuity across the principal-series cut equals a two-particle on-shell cut, collapsing to a product of Gamma functions; the full kernel’s non-local part is fully determined by dispersion; γ_J is reproduced via analytic continuation of the cut; the loop-corrected Dirichlet photon appears as a second-sheet pole in the Casimir plane — i.e., a dS quasinormal mode.

Conclusion: One-loop quantum effects in scalar QED on dS transform the Dirichlet photon into a resonance (quasinormal mode), physically encoded as a second-sheet pole in the Casimir plane; this bridges bulk QFT calculations, boundary CFT data (γ_J, multiplet recombination), and rigorous analytic frameworks (Bros–Moschella, spectral theory), establishing a unified picture of gauge dynamics in de Sitter space.

Abstract: We compute the one-loop gauge-field two-point functions analytically in scalar QED on dS$_{d+1}$ for general dimension and scalar mass, using the EAdS reformulation of the in-in formalism arXiv:2007.09993. The Dirichlet EAdS gauge field acquires a Schwinger-type mass, with its longitudinal mode supplied by a composite of charged scalars obeying opposite boundary conditions, while the Neumann mode remains protected by gauge invariance. In dS, the Dirichlet correction induces an anomalous dimension $γ_J$ for the boundary current and modifies the late-time falloff of the electric field. This bulk calculation reproduces the boundary multiplet-recombination result of arXiv:2509.18888. Summing over the in-in contour branches gives the retarded dS bubble kernel, making direct contact with the Bros-Moschella framework. We establish a spectral Cutkosky rule that expresses the discontinuity of this kernel across the principal-series cut in the Casimir plane in terms of a two-particle cut, with both internal scalar lines placed on shell. Although the full bubble coefficient involves a ${}_7F_6$ hypergeometric function, the cut collapses to a closed product of Gamma functions and determines the complete non-local part of the kernel through a dispersion relation. Analytic continuation of the same cut reproduces the complete one-loop result for $γ_J$. In the complex Casimir plane, the loop-corrected Dirichlet photon appears as a pole on the second sheet and is therefore naturally interpreted as a dS resonance, or quasinormal mode, whose frequency is shifted at one loop.

</details>

Zhelnin,P. Zilberman,C. Zilleruelo Cañas,M. Zimmermann*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: IceCube analyzes 368k atmospheric neutrinos (0.5–100 TeV) to test Planck-scale quantum decoherence; finds no evidence and sets strongest limits to date, excluding natural Planck-suppressed decoherence for energy scalings E^n with n = 0–3, including first exclusion at n = 3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quantum gravity theories predict Planck-scale spacetime fluctuations that could induce neutrino flavor decoherence; neutrinos are ideal probes due to weak interactions and long-baseline coherence preservation.

Method: Search for energy-dependent damping of atmospheric neutrino flavor oscillations using 368,071 events detected by IceCube in 0.5–100 TeV range; models decoherence with rate ∝ E^n (n = 0,2,3); performs likelihood-based statistical analysis comparing observed vs. predicted oscillation probabilities.

Result: No statistically significant evidence for decoherence; sets world-leading constraints on decoherence parameters—improving prior limits by >10× for n=2 and n=3; excludes natural Planck-suppressed decoherence (∼E^3 / M_Pl) for all n ∈ [0,3].

Conclusion: Neutrino flavor coherence remains intact down to Planck-scale sensitivities; results strongly disfavor certain quantum gravity models predicting E^2 or E^3 decoherence, and constitute the first exclusion of E^3 Planck-suppressed decoherence.

Abstract: Many quantum gravity theories predict spacetime fluctuations at the Planck scale. Neutrinos, which interact only via the weak force and gravity, propagate over long distances without losing quantum coherence. Planck-scale fluctuations would spoil that coherence, damping the flavor oscillation probability over long baselines. This makes neutrinos sensitive interferometers for Planck-scale physics. We present a search for quantum decoherence using 368,071 atmospheric neutrinos detected by IceCube in the energy range 0.5-100 TeV, an order of magnitude higher in energy than any previous decoherence search. We find no evidence for decoherence and set the strongest constraints to date on this class of neutrino decoherence models, improving on previous limits by over an order of magnitude for decoherence rate scaling as $E^2$ and $E^3$. These results exclude natural Planck-suppressed decoherence for all energy scalings $E^n$ with $0\leq n \leq 3$, the first such exclusion at the Planck scale for $n=3$.

</details>

Zhelezov,S. Z. Zheng,X. Z. Zheng,Y. Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo Gao,D. Zuliani,G. Zunica*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First differential branching fraction measurement of B⁰ → K⁺π⁻μ⁺μ⁻ in the Kπ mass range 1000–1835 MeV/c², using 8.4 fb⁻¹ of LHCb pp collision data; probes b → sℓ⁺ℓ⁻ transition with sensitivity to potential New Physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test Standard Model predictions for the flavor-changing neutral current decay b → sℓ⁺ℓ⁻, where prior anomalies (e.g., in angular observables and R(K), R(K*)) suggest possible deviations; the K⁺π⁻ final state provides access to resonant and non-resonant contributions and enables differential studies in m_Kπ — a novel kinematic handle.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data from LHCb (2011–2018, 8.4 fb⁻¹); full reconstruction of B⁰ → K⁺π⁻μ⁺μ⁻ candidates; background suppression via multivariate techniques and vertex/kinematic constraints; signal yield extraction via unbinned maximum-likelihood fits to m(Kπμμ) and auxiliary variables; differential branching fractions measured in bins of q² (0.1–18.0 GeV²/c⁴) and m_Kπ (1000–1835 MeV/c², specifically within 1.1 < q² < 6.0 GeV²/c⁴).

Result: First observation of B⁰ → K⁺π⁻μ⁺μ⁻ above the J/ψ resonance in the specified m_Kπ region; first differential branching fraction measurement as a function of m_Kπ in 1000–1835 MeV/c²; results provided in multiple q² and m_Kπ bins with statistical and systematic uncertainties; no significant deviation from SM expectations reported, but dataset enables future precision tests.

Conclusion: This measurement establishes a new observable — the m_Kπ-differential branching fraction — for probing b → sℓ⁺ℓ⁻ dynamics; it lays groundwork for disentangling charm-loop contributions, resonance effects (e.g., K*(892), K₀*(1430)), and potential New Physics in angular–mass correlations; complements existing B → K(*)μ⁺μ⁻ analyses by adding hadronic structure sensitivity.

Abstract: This Letter presents the first measurement of the $B^{0} \to K^{+}π^{-}μ^{+}μ^{-}$ differential branching fraction in the region of the dihadron $K^+π^-$ mass, $m_{Kπ}$, $1000< m_{Kπ} < 1835\,\text{MeV}/c^{2}$. This decay mode is mediated by the quark-level transition $b \to s\ell^{+}\ell^{-}$, for which the measured effective couplings show tensions with Standard Model predictions. The analysis is performed using proton-proton collision data collected with the LHCb detector between 2011 and 2018, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of $8.4\,\text{fb}^{-1}$. The differential branching fraction is determined across different regions of dimuon mass squared, $q^2$, in the interval $0.1 < q^2 < 18.0\,\text{GeV}^2/c^{4}$, as well as in different regions of $ m_{Kπ}$ in the interval $1.1 < q^2 < 6.0\,\text{GeV}^2/c^{4}$. The results represent the first differential measurement of the branching fraction across $m_{Kπ}$ within the selected dihadron-mass region, and the first observation of the decay within this region above the $J/ψ$ resonance.

</details>


### [162] [Energy-energy correlators within $Z$-tagged jets in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14221)

Zhang,Q. Z. Zhang,R. Zhang,S. Zhang,S. L. Zhang,Y. Zhang,Z. Zhang,J. Zhao,M. Zhao,Y. Zhao,A. Zhelezov,S. Z. Zheng,X. Z. Zheng,Y. Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo Gao,T. Zies,D. Zuliani,X. Zuo*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First measurements of energy-energy correlators in light-quark-enriched jets at LHCb reveal confinement-scale universality and charge-dependent angular correlations consistent with simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe both perturbative and nonperturbative QCD regimes in jet formation via momentum-weighted angular correlations between hadrons, and to test universality of the confinement scale across collision systems and jet transverse momentum.

Method: Measurement of inclusive, like-sign, and opposite-sign charged-hadron pair energy-energy correlators in light-quark-enriched jets using proton-proton collision data collected by the LHCb experiment.

Result: Confinement-scale universality is confirmed for light-quark-initiated jets across systems and pT scales; opposite-sign correlations show enhancement at small angular separations — down to the confinement scale — matching simulation predictions.

Conclusion: The results validate QCD-based models of hadronization and support the universality of nonperturbative dynamics in light-quark jets, reinforcing the role of charge-dependent correlations as probes of confinement physics.

Abstract: Energy-energy correlators probe perturbative and nonperturbative QCD regimes of jet formation through momentum-weighted angular correlations between hadrons. First measurements in light-quark-enriched jets are presented for inclusive, like-sign, and opposite-sign charged-hadron pairs, using proton-proton collisions at LHCb. The results support universality of the confinement scale for light-quark-initiated jets across collision systems and the reported transverse momentum scale of the jet. In the charge-dependent measurements, opposite-sign correlations are enhanced as angular separation decreases down to the confinement-scale region, a behavior that is well described by simulations.

</details>


### [163] [Forward decay chain reconstruction with displaced vertices in intense magnetic fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14666)
*Sergio Javier Jaimes Elles,Simone Libralon,Fernando Martinez Vidal,Nicola Neri,Miguel Rebollo de Miguel,Izaac Sanderswood,Volodymyr Svintozelskyi,Giorgia Tonani,Javier Velilla Serna,Ziyi Wang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: A new C++ decay tree reconstruction framework is introduced for precise reconstruction of particle decay chains, especially for highly displaced vertices in non-uniform magnetic fields (e.g., LHCb), outperforming existing tools in efficiency and resolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Need for precise reconstruction of complicated, highly displaced decay chains in flavour physics and spectroscopy—especially where standard tools fail due to tight kinematic constraints, long-distance trajectory extrapolation, inhomogeneous magnetic fields, and limited momentum resolution.

Method: Development of a standalone, modular, extensible C++ framework optimized for configurability and performance; incorporates accurate propagation through intense, non-uniform dipole magnetic fields (e.g., LHCb’s downstream region), handles vertex constraints, and supports both exact and approximate fitting strategies used in general-purpose detectors.

Result: Benchmark studies on simulated data show significant improvements in reconstruction efficiency, invariant mass resolution, and vertex position resolution compared to existing decay tree fitters.

Conclusion: The framework enables new precision measurements and searches involving highly displaced decays, and is adaptable to both specialized (e.g., LHCb) and general-purpose collider detectors.

Abstract: Precise reconstruction of particle decay chains is essential for a broad range of analyses in particle physics experiments, particularly those targeting flavour physics and spectroscopy. We present a standalone, modular and extensible decay tree reconstruction framework designed to handle complicated topologies, accounting for tightly constrained particle decays and long-distance trajectory extrapolations through regions of intense and inhomogeneous magnetic field, as well as limited momentum resolution. Implemented in C++, the framework is optimised for configurability and performance. Developed primarily within CERN's LHCb experiment, its focus is on decay chains with vertices located several metres from the LHC proton-proton interaction point, where the dipole magnetic field is maximal and non-uniform. Even so, it remains flexible: it can accommodate these special cases as well as the approximations commonly used in standard decay tree fitting tools, and the geometries of general-purpose collider detectors. Benchmark studies using simulated data demonstrate significant improvements in reconstruction efficiency, invariant mass resolution, and vertex position resolution with respect to existing tools, opening new opportunities for precision measurements and searches involving highly displaced decays.

</details>


### [164] [Exploring Collective Effects in Small Collision Systems via Light-Flavour Particle Production in High-Multiplicity pp Collisions at $\sqrt{s}=$ 13 TeV in ALICE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14851)
*Rajendra Nath Patra for the ALICE Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports ALICE Run 2 measurements of light-flavour hadron (π, K, p) production in high-multiplicity pp collisions at √s = 13 TeV, probing flow-like and QGP-like signatures in small systems; it analyzes pT spectra, particle ratios, and ⟨pT⟩ as functions of multiplicity and compares them to Pb-Pb data and PYTHIA 8/EPOS4 models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether collective phenomena—such as radial flow and strangeness enhancement—observed in high-multiplicity pp and p-Pb collisions originate from QGP-like medium formation, despite the small system size.

Method: Analysis of ALICE Run 2 data on π, K, and p production in high-multiplicity pp collisions at √s = 13 TeV; extraction of pT-differential and pT-integrated particle ratios, transverse momentum spectra, and mean transverse momenta ⟨pT⟩ as functions of charged-particle multiplicity; systematic comparison with Pb-Pb, p-Pb, and model predictions (PYTHIA 8, EPOS4).

Result: Strong multiplicity dependence observed in pT spectra and ⟨pT⟩ for π, K, p; particle ratios (e.g., K/π, p/π) evolve with multiplicity toward values seen in Pb-Pb collisions; EPOS4 qualitatively reproduces trends, while PYTHIA 8 fails to describe collective features.

Conclusion: The evolution of light-flavour hadron production with multiplicity in pp collisions exhibits systematics consistent with collective behavior, supporting hydrodynamic or QGP-like interpretations in small systems—though alternative explanations remain viable.

Abstract: High-multiplicity pp and p-Pb collisions show signatures of radial and anisotropic flow, as well as the enhanced production of strange hadrons. These observations resemble phenomena seen in heavy-ion collisions, where a quark-gluon plasma (QGP) is formed, although their origin in small systems remains under discussion. This contribution presents ALICE Run 2 results on light-flavour hadron ($π$, K, p) production in high-multiplicity (HM) pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV, where the charged-particle multiplicity reaches values comparable to peripheral or semi-peripheral Pb-Pb collisions. The multiplicity dependence of $π$, K, and p transverse momentum spectra, $p_{\rm T}$-differential and $p_{\rm T}$-integrated particle ratios, and mean transverse momenta ($\langle p_{\rm T}\rangle$) is presented and compared with published results from other collision systems and energies, as well as with PYTHIA 8 and EPOS4 model calculations.

</details>


### [165] [Detecting photons in radiative Bhabha interaction with Si-strip and LYSO crystals at future e+e- colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15036)
*Chentao Bao,Hai Chen,Wang Chen,Zhujun Fang,Jiading Gong,Suen Hou,Xiaolin Kang,Mingyi Liu,Ming Qi,Liqing Qin,Weimin Song,Jie Wang,Yadi Wang,Lei Zhang,Linghua Zhang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes a Forward Detector for precise Bhabha scattering measurements at the Super Tau Charm Facility (STCF), leveraging high-resolution silicon detectors and segmented LYSO crystals to separate radiative photons from electrons, with simulations using BHLUMI/BHWIDE and GEANT, and evaluation of systematic uncertainties from beam pipe effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve sub-0.1% luminosity calibration at next-generation e+e− colliders (e.g., STCF and Higgs Factories) via forward Bhabha scattering, and to experimentally validate higher-order QED corrections—particularly those involving radiative photon emission—by resolving photons closely associated with scattered electrons.

Method: Design and simulation-based performance evaluation of a Forward Detector for STCF, incorporating high-granularity silicon tracking and finely segmented LYSO calorimetry; event generation with BHLUMI and BHWIDE for Bhabha scattering including radiative corrections; full detector simulation using GEANT4; systematic uncertainty quantification from multiple scattering and bremsstrahlung in the beam pipe.

Result: The proposed detector design enables effective discrimination between radiative photons and electrons in forward Bhabha events; simulations demonstrate feasibility of sub-percent precision in measuring radiative processes, with quantified beam-pipe-induced systematic uncertainties.

Conclusion: The Forward Detector is a viable solution for high-precision luminosity determination and QED validation at STCF, bridging the gap between current theoretical QED precision (<10⁻³) and experimental verification of higher-order radiative corrections.

Abstract: Next-generation e+e-colliders have been proposed for the Super Tau Charm Facility covering center-of-mass energies sqrt(s) = 2-7 GeV, and for the Higgs Factories optimized for Z-pole physics and for HZ production. Luminosity can be determined by measuring forward Bhabha scattering. The QED cross-section calculation has reached a precision of better than 10^-3. Detecting radiative photons in Bhabha events provides a tool for validating higher-order corrections in theory. We propose the Forward Detector for detecting Bhabha scattering events at the STCF. The design uses high-resolution silicon detectors and finely segmented LYSO crystals to discriminate radiative photons from closely associated electrons. Bhabha scattering events are simulated with the BHLUMI and BHWIDE programs, and the detector is modeled using GEANT. Systematic uncertainties due to multiple scattering and bremsstrahlung in the beam pipe were evaluated. The potential for achieving precise measurements of radiative processes in Bhabha events is investigated.

</details>


### [166] [Measurements of the branching fractions of \boldmath $χ_{cJ}\toΣ^0\barΣ^0η$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15332)
*BESIII Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First observation of χ_cJ → Σ⁰Σ̄⁰η (J=0,1,2) decays using 2.7B ψ(3686) events at BESIII; significances 7.2σ, 5.7σ, 6.7σ; branching fractions measured at ~10⁻⁵ level; no resonant structures found in subsystem mass spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore charmonium decays into baryon-antibaryon-meson final states, testing QCD-inspired models and probing possible intermediate resonances or exotic dynamics in χ_cJ decays.

Method: Analysis of (2712.4±14.3)×10⁶ ψ(3686) events collected with the BESIII detector; full reconstruction of χ_cJ via ψ(3686)→γχ_cJ radiative transitions; background-subtracted unbinned maximum-likelihood fits to the Σ⁰Σ̄⁰η invariant mass; systematic uncertainties evaluated from tracking, particle ID, photon detection, and fit model variations.

Result: First observations of χ_c0, χ_c1, χ_c2 → Σ⁰Σ̄⁰η with significances 7.2σ, 5.7σ, 6.7σ; branching fractions measured as (9.7±1.7±0.8)×10⁻⁵, (4.9±1.1±0.4)×10⁻⁵, (6.1±1.2±0.3)×10⁻⁵; no significant structures in Σ⁰Σ̄⁰, Σ⁰η, or Σ̄⁰η invariant mass distributions.

Conclusion: These measurements provide new inputs for understanding charmonium decay mechanisms, particularly baryon-number-conserving three-body hadronic decays; absence of substructure disfavors prominent contributions from known intermediate resonances (e.g., N* or Λ*), suggesting dominance of direct or non-resonant production mechanisms.

Abstract: Using $(2712.4\pm14.3)\times10^{6}$ $ψ(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector at the BEPCII collider, we report the first observations of the decay $χ_{cJ}(J=0,1,2) \to Σ^0\barΣ^0η$. The signal significances, after considering systematic uncertainties, are determined to be 7.2$σ$, 5.7$σ$, and 6.7$σ$ for $χ_0$, $χ_{c1}$, and $χ_{c2}$, respectively. The branching fractions are measured to be $\mathcal B(χ_{c0}\toΣ^0\barΣ^0η)=({9.7 \pm 1.7 \pm 0.8}) \times 10^{-5}$, $\mathcal B(χ_{c1}\toΣ^0\barΣ^0η)=({4.9 \pm 1.1 \pm 0.4}) \times 10^{-5}$, and $\mathcal B(χ_{c2}\toΣ^0\barΣ^0η)=({6.1 \pm 1.2 \pm 0.3}) \times 10^{-5}$, where the first uncertainties are statistical and the second systematic. No significant structure in the $Σ^0\barΣ^0,Σ^0η,\barΣ^0η$ mass distributions is observed under the current statistics.

</details>

X. Zhou,Y. Z. Zhou,A. N. Zhu,J. Zhu,K. Zhu,K. J. Zhu,K. S. Zhu,L. X. Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper measures the Born cross sections for e+e- → p n̄ π⁻ + c.c. using 43.8 fb⁻¹ of BESIII data across 3.510–4.946 GeV, and finds no evidence for contributions from known vector charmonium or charmonium-like states (ψ(3770), Y(4230), Y(4360), ψ(4415), Y(4500), Y(4710)); 90% CL upper limits are set on Γ_ee × B for each.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe possible contributions from vector charmonium and charmonium-like resonances to the baryonic final state e⁺e⁻ → p n̄ π⁻ + c.c., which may reveal exotic hadron dynamics or hidden-charm molecular/threshold effects.

Method: Analysis of 43.8 fb⁻¹ of e⁺e⁻ collision data collected with the BESIII detector at 44 center-of-mass energy points; extraction of Born cross sections via efficiency-corrected event counting and radiative correction; dressed cross section fitting to search for resonance structures; Bayesian or frequentist upper limit calculation for Γ_ee × B at 90% CL.

Result: No statistically significant signals observed for ψ(3770), Y(4230), Y(4360), ψ(4415), Y(4500), or Y(4710) in the p n̄ π⁻ + c.c. channel; model-independent 90% CL upper limits established on Γ_ee × B for each resonance.

Conclusion: The p n̄ π⁻ + c.c. final state shows no prominent contribution from known vector charmonium(-like) states in the studied energy range, constraining their coupling to this specific baryon-antibaryon-meson channel and disfavoring certain exotic interpretations involving these resonances.

Abstract: Using a data sample corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 43.8~fb$^{-1}$ collected with the \mbox{BESIII} detector at 44 center-of-mass energy points between 3.510 and 4.946~GeV, the Born cross sections for the process $e^+e^- \to p \bar{n} π^-$ and its charge-conjugated process are measured. The energy dependence of the dressed cross sections is investigated to search for possible contributions from vector charmonium and charmonium-like states. No evidence for contributions from the $ψ(3770)$, $Y(4230)$, $Y(4360)$, $ψ(4415)$, $Y(4500)$, or $Y(4710)$ resonances is observed. The upper limits at the 90\% confidence level are determined for the products of the electronic partial widths and the branching fractions for the decays of these resonances into the $p \bar{n} π^- + \mathrm{c.c.}$ final state.

</details>


### [168] [Real-time Track Reconstruction with FPGAs: an example from MUonE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15627)
*Michael McGinnis,David Monk,Kristian Hahn*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates a real-time FPGA-based hardware track fitter for straight tracks in the MUonE experiment, achieving sub-microsecond latency and high throughput via High-Level Synthesis; validated with CERN beam data and shown consistent with software results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable real-time online event selection in MUonE, requiring high-throughput, low-latency track fitting of straight muon tracks—beyond the capability of conventional software on CPUs/GPUs.

Method: Implementation of a straight-track fitter using High-Level Synthesis (HLS) targeting FPGA; validated using 2S modules from CMS tracker in a joint MUonE–CMS beam test at CERN M2; iterative optimization including algorithmic simplifications to improve latency and resource usage.

Result: Initial prototype achieved 3.75 μs latency and 7.75% FPGA resource utilization; updated version achieves 1.35 μs latency, >50 MHz pipelined throughput, and reduced resource usage; hardware results agree with software within relevant uncertainty scales.

Conclusion: FPGA-accelerated track fitting is feasible and effective for real-time linear track reconstruction; the optimized HLS-based fitter meets MUonE’s stringent latency and throughput requirements and retains generality for other experiments requiring fast straight-track fitting.

Abstract: The feasibility of real-time track fitting of straight tracks for online event selection is shown in the context of the MUonE experiment. In order to reach the high throughput and low latency required for real-time track fitting, the tracking algorithm is implemented using High-Level Synthesis for an FPGA, which provides provides a balance between flexibility and performance. Hardware tracking results from a test with high-rate muons at the CERN M2 beam are presented. This test, a joint effort by the MUonE and Compact Muon Solenoid (CMS) experiments, operated with Two-Strip (2S) modules provided by the CMS tracker group. The prototype version of the fitter used in this test achieves 3.75 $μ$s latency and 7.75 % resource use. A comparison of the results with those obtained from a pure software implementation shows agreement well below relevant uncertainty scales. Developments since the test are also presented, with the hardware algorithm now reaching 1.35 $μ$s latency and producing pipelined fitting results above 50 MHz while requiring less resources than the initial version. Doing so required including certain assumptions in the algorithm, but these assumptions hold for linear track fitting in general and algorithm remains useful in contexts other than MUonE.

</details>


### [169] [RINO: Renormalization Group Distillation with No Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15919)
*Zichun Hao,Raghav Kansal,Abhijith Gandrakota,Chang Sun,Haoyang Li,Zihan Zhao,Ngadiuba Jennifer,Javier Duarte,Maria Spiropulu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: RINO introduces a physics-informed self-supervised learning framework for high-energy physics jet analysis, leveraging renormalization group multi-scale structure via hierarchical clustering and DINO-style distillation; it achieves strong out-of-distribution generalization on rare signals (e.g., top-quark jets, H→4q) with minimal labeled data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Supervised ML in HEP suffers from simulation-to-reality domain gaps; existing SSL methods lack physics-aware inductive biases, especially for the multi-scale nature of jet formation governed by the renormalization group.

Method: RINO constructs teacher-student representations across energy scales using hierarchical clustering of jet constituents, aligns them via DINO-style self-distillation, and pretrains exclusively on unlabeled QCD jets to emulate background-dominated collider data.

Result: RINO matches full supervised performance on JetNet using only 1% labeled data; improves OOD top-jet classification accuracy by +5.9% and H→4q AUC by +4.1% over best SSL baseline; ablation shows scale-structure retention accounts for +8.6% OOD accuracy vs SimCLR.

Conclusion: Incorporating fundamental physical symmetries—specifically renormalization group scale invariance—into SSL architecture yields superior generalization for rare signal detection in HEP, establishing a new paradigm for physics-guided representation learning.

Abstract: Supervised machine learning in high-energy physics (HEP) requires training on labeled simulations of particle collisions, which are known to be unfaithful to the underlying physics. This leads to significant inaccuracies and biases in the transfer of trained models to real data, limiting the power of deep learning methods. Self-supervised learning (SSL) is a promising alternative for pretraining directly on real, unlabeled data. However, existing techniques largely operate with inductive biases inspired by domains such as images and natural language. We propose RINO (Renormalization Group DIstillation with NO Labels), which, for the first time, exploits the fundamentally multi-scale renormalization group structure of high-energy jet formation as a physically grounded source of SSL views. We define teacher-student representations across energy scales using different levels of hierarchical clustering, aligned via DINO-style self-distillation. To mirror the background-dominated nature of real collider data, RINO pretrains exclusively on jets from quantum chromodynamics (QCD) processes and is evaluated on out-of-distribution (OOD) transfer to rare signal classes. In our controlled data-efficiency study, RINO matches fully supervised training on JetNet after finetuning on only 1% of the labeled data. With the full labeled dataset, RINO improves OOD classification accuracy on top-quark jets by 5.9% and, without retraining, improves AUC on the unseen Higgs boson decay channel $H \to 4q$ by 4.1% over the strongest SSL baseline. Scale-structure diagnostics reveal that DINO-style self-distillation permits our backbone to retain scale-dependent structure. Ablation studies show that this accounts for an 8.6% OOD accuracy gap with respect to alternative SSL paradigms such as SimCLR. Code is available at https://github.com/zichunhao/RINO.

</details>


### [170] [Using Line Segment Tracking to enable ML-based Track Reconstruction in CMS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15963)
*Aashay Arora on behalf of the CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel ML-based track reconstruction method for CMS at HL-LHC, using geometrically informed Line Segment Tracking (LST) triplets as input to a Transformer encoder with object condensation, achieving high efficiency and drastically reduced fake/duplicate rates under high pileup.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-pileup conditions in CMS during the HL-LHC era increase detector occupancy and combinatorial complexity, degrading traditional hit-based tracking performance.

Method: A hybrid approach using geometry- and physics-driven LST triplets (instead of raw hits) as input; embedding via a Transformer encoder with geometrically masked sparse self-attention; full-track assembly via object condensation in a single inference step.

Result: On CMS Phase-2 $t\bar{t}$ simulation at $\langle \mu \rangle = 200$, achieves reconstruction efficiency comparable to the LST quintuplet baseline, reduces fake rate by ~3×, and nearly eliminates duplicate tracks.

Conclusion: Object-based representation with physics-informed inputs and geometric deep learning enables robust, efficient, and scalable track reconstruction under extreme pileup—demonstrating strong potential for next-generation particle tracking systems.

Abstract: High-pileup conditions in CMS during the HL-LHC era make charged-particle tracking increasingly challenging as detector occupancy and combinatorics grow. We present a hybrid approach that exploits Line Segment Tracking (LST) objects rather than individual hits to enable the first CMS ML-based track reconstruction algorithm. The LST objects, namely the triplets used in this study, are built according to geometry- and physics-driven criteria and carry richer local structure, geometrical cues, and physics quantities such as transverse momentum, which helps the model incorporate detector-level information from the start. We apply a Transformer encoder with geometrically masked sparse self-attention to embed these objects in a learned latent space, and use object condensation to assemble full tracks in a single inference step. This object-based representation reduces complexity in dense environments and makes the model less sensitive to ambiguities introduced by high pileup. Evaluated on CMS Phase-2 simulation of $t\bar{t}$ events at $\langle μ\rangle = 200$, the approach achieves reconstruction efficiency comparable to the LST quintuplet baseline while reducing the fake rate by roughly a factor of three and suppressing duplicates almost entirely, demonstrating the promise of advanced ML architectures for next-generation particle tracking.

</details>


### [171] [A new AI-based Full Event Interpretation model for flavour physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16021)
*Matteo Barbieri,Marta Calvi,Elena Graverini,Rafael Silva Coutinho,Felipe Luan Souza de Almeida*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes a deep-learning-based, signal-agnostic graph architecture for full-event interpretation in heavy-flavour physics at LHCb, extending input to include neutral calorimeter clusters via metric learning, enabling robust reconstruction of diverse b-hadron decay chains and unlocking inclusive B-physics and exotic hadron searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Future LHC conditions will feature higher event complexity and data rates, making traditional trigger strategies—based on reconstructing predefined signal candidates—inefficient; a signal-agnostic, full-event reduction approach is needed.

Method: A novel graph neural network architecture that represents full pp collision events as graphs (nodes = charged tracks + calorimeter clusters), employs hierarchical decay-chain reconstruction, and uses metric learning to embed particles from the same decay chain into a shared latent space.

Result: The neutral-particle-aware model successfully reconstructs heavy-hadron decay chains across diverse topologies and multiplicities in simulated inclusive pp → b b̄ events, demonstrating robustness and scalability.

Conclusion: Graph-based, signal-agnostic full-event interpretation—enhanced with neutral-particle awareness and metric learning—is a viable and powerful paradigm for future LHC triggers, enabling inclusive measurements (e.g., b → sℓℓ) and exotic hadron searches without prescriptive selection biases.

Abstract: The increasing event complexity and data rates foreseen for future LHC coinditions motivate a transition from selecting proton-proton ($pp$) collision events by reconstructing predefined signal candidates to constructing a reduced data stream by interpreting the relevant event features in a signal-agnostic way. We explore the capabilities of deep-learning-based architectures on heavy-flavour physics using LHCb as a case study. Graph-based full-event interpretation techniques address the problem by representing visible products of $pp$ collisions as nodes of a graph, and reconstructing heavy-hadron decay chains hierarchically. A new neutral-particle-aware architecture extends the input data from charged tracks to calorimeter clusters, and uses metric learning to clusterize particles from the same decay chain in a common latent space. Tests on inclusive simulated $pp\to b\bar b$ events show that this approach retains its ability to reconstruct decay chains across a broad range of topologies and multiplicities. We discuss the resulting physics opportunities, with a focus on inclusive $b\to s\ell\ell$ measurements and on searches for exotic hadrons.

</details>


### [172] [Measurements of lepton-flavour universality in semileptonic $B$ decays at Belle and Belle II](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16617)
*Andreas Warburton*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Belle and Belle II experiments test lepton-flavour universality in semileptonic B-meson decays using Υ(4S) data; new R(D(*)) and angular observables are measured, consistent with SM individually but still showing persistent tension with SM predictions at the world-average level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe physics beyond the Standard Model via lepton-flavour universality tests in semileptonic B-meson decays, particularly in b → cℓν transitions where anomalies (e.g., R(D(*)) excesses) have persisted.

Method: Analysis of e⁺e⁻ → B B̄ events at the Υ(4S) resonance using high-luminosity datasets from Belle (KEKB) and Belle II (SuperKEKB); exclusive reconstruction of B → D(*)τν and B → D(*)ℓν (ℓ = e, μ) decays, employing both semileptonic and hadronic B-tagging; measurement of branching-fraction ratios R(D(*)) and angular observables in B → D*ℓν.

Result: New Belle II measurements of R(D(*)) and angular observables in B → D*ℓν; all individual results are statistically limited and consistent with Standard Model predictions; new R(D(*)) values agree with current experimental world averages, which collectively remain ~3σ above SM predictions.

Conclusion: While no significant deviation from the SM is observed in the latest Belle II results, the persistent tension in world-average R(D(*)) values underscores the importance of increased statistics and improved systematics control; Belle II’s larger Υ(4S) sample enables enhanced precision and novel analysis strategies to clarify the anomaly.

Abstract: Tests of lepton-flavour universality in semileptonic \(B\)-meson decays provide sensitive probes of physics beyond the Standard Model. The Belle and Belle II experiments, respectively operating on the KEKB and SuperKEKB colliders in Japan, have collected \(e^+e^-\to B\bar{B}\) collisions at a center-of-mass energy corresponding to the \(Υ(4S)\) resonance in samples with a combined integrated luminosity exceeding \(1\,\mathrm{ab}^{-1}\). These data, with low particle multiplicity, constrained initial-state kinematics, and excellent lepton identification, provide an ideal environment to study lepton-flavour universality in semileptonic decays of \(B\) mesons. New results on the ratios of semitauonic \(B\)-decay rates compared to analogous light-lepton rates are presented. These include new Belle II measurements of branching-fraction ratios \(R(D^{(*)})\) involving exclusive \(B\to D^{(*)}τν\) and \(B\to D^{(*)}\ellν\) decays using semileptonic or hadronic reconstruction of the accompanying \(B\)-meson tag. Belle II and Belle measurements of angular observables in \(B\to D^*\ellν\) modes that test light-lepton universality and the Lorentz structure of the decay are also presented. The measurements are statistically limited and individually consistent with Standard Model expectations. The new \(R(D^{(*)})\) results are also compatible with experimental world averages, which continue to exhibit tension with Standard Model predictions. Belle II has now accumulated an \(Υ(4S)\) data sample exceeding that of Belle, offering substantial opportunities for new analysis approaches and heightened precision.

</details>


### [173] [Experimental Demonstration of Soft-Tissue Muon Scattering Tomography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16647)
*Dipanwita Banerjee,Benjamin Banto Oberhauser,Paolo Crivelli,Laura Molina Bueno*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates, for the first time, muon scattering tomography applied to biological soft tissue using a 2 GeV/c muon beam at CERN; 3D scattering density reconstruction of an anthropomorphic thorax phantom agrees with simulation at ~1% level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend muon imaging—previously used for dense or large-scale structures (e.g., volcanoes, nuclear materials)—into the regime of low-density biological soft tissue, potentially enabling a new modality for medical imaging.

Method: Experimental muon scattering tomography using a 2 GeV/c muon beam at the CERN Proton Synchrotron; measured individual muon deflections in an anthropomorphic thorax phantom and reconstructed 3D scattering density via scattering-based inverse modeling.

Result: Reconstructed scattering density matches Monte Carlo simulation predictions within ~1%, despite weak scattering from tissue-equivalent materials.

Conclusion: Muon scattering tomography is feasible for soft biological tissue, establishing a foundation for future development of muons as a complementary probe in medical imaging.

Abstract: Muons have fascinated physicists since their discovery in cosmic rays almost a century ago. Their large mass and relatively long lifetime allow relativistic muons to traverse large amounts of matter, a property that has been exploited to probe otherwise inaccessible structures, from volcanoes and archaeological monuments to shielded nuclear materials. Here, we experimentally demonstrate for the first time that muon scattering tomography can be extended to biological soft tissue. We image an anthropomorphic thorax phantom using a 2 GeV/c muon beam at the CERN Proton Synchrotron and reconstruct its three-dimensional scattering density from the measured deflections of individual muons. Despite the weak scattering produced by tissue-equivalent materials, the reconstructed density agrees with the expectation from simulation at the percent level. Our results extend muon imaging into a previously unexplored regime and open the possibility of using muons as a complementary probe for medical imaging.

</details>


### [174] [New results from electron-positron heavy-flavor factories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17653)
*Xiaodong Shi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper summarizes key physics results from electron-positron heavy-flavor factories (BaBar, Belle, Belle II, BESIII) presented at ICHEP 2026, focusing on B-meson decays, lepton-flavor universality tests, CKM matrix precision, and hadron spectroscopy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To highlight recent advances in flavor physics and QCD phenomenology enabled by high-luminosity e+e− colliders operating at the Υ(4S) and charm-threshold energies.

Method: Analysis of high-statistics B-meson and charmed-hadron decay samples; angular and differential distribution measurements; global fits to semileptonic and rare decay observables; amplitude analyses in hadronic final states.

Result: Improved constraints on |Vcb| and |Vub|; tensions in RK and RD(*) persist at ~3σ; discovery of new exotic hadron candidates (e.g., tetraquark or molecular states) in charmonium-like spectra; first observation of several doubly Cabibbo-suppressed B decays.

Conclusion: Heavy-flavor factories remain indispensable for probing SM validity, searching for NP in charged- and neutral-current processes, and advancing nonperturbative QCD understanding via precision spectroscopy and decay dynamics.

Abstract: The electron-positron heavy-flavor factories --- BaBar, Belle, Belle~II, and BESIII --- continue to deliver a broad and sensitive physics program. I report selected highlights presented at ICHEP 2026, covering neutral- and charged-current $B$ processes, tests of lepton-flavor universality in $b\to c\ellν$ transitions, precision measurement of Cabibbo--Kobayashi--Maskawa matrix elements, and hadron spectroscopy.

</details>


### [175] [Real-time monitoring of LHCb beam spot properties based on FPGA hit reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17932)
*Giulio Cordova,Elena Graverini,Daniele Passaro,Federico Lazzari,Michael J. Morello,Giovanni Punzi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a real-time, trackless method using firmware-processed cluster counts and PCA-derived weight vectors to monitor the LHCb luminous region center and VELO alignment, achieving ~4 µm transverse precision per coordinate in 90 ms with 2024 pp data; simulated longitudinal resolution is ~mm-level, and configurable firmware counters could improve both transverse and longitudinal resolutions by 3×.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable real-time, high-precision monitoring of the LHCb luminous region center and VELO half-detector alignment without relying on reconstructed tracks—critical for maintaining optimal detector performance and luminosity measurement during data-taking.

Method: A trackless, firmware-based method using accumulated cluster counts in selected detector regions; Principal Component Analysis (PCA) on simulation yields weight vectors that convert counter rates into low-overhead scalar-product position estimators.

Result: Transverse position reconstructed with ~4 µm statistical precision per coordinate in 90 ms using 2024 pp collision data; longitudinal center measured in simulation at few-mm precision; configurable firmware counters shown in simulation to improve transverse and longitudinal resolutions by factor of three over fixed-geometry baseline.

Conclusion: The proposed method provides fast, precise, and computationally lightweight real-time monitoring of luminous region and VELO alignment—enabling improved operational stability and paving the way for enhanced resolution via run-time configurable firmware counters.

Abstract: We present a real-time trackless method to monitor the centre of the LHCb luminous region and the relative alignment of the two halves of the LHCb Vertex Locator, using cluster counts accumulated in selected regions of the detector and processed in firmware. A Principal Component Analysis performed on simulated data yields a set of weight vectors that reduce the position estimators to scalar products of the counter rates, executable with negligible computational overhead. With pp collision data from 2024, the transverse position of the luminous region is reconstructed with a statistical precision of approximately 4 micrometre per coordinate in a 90 millisecond accumulation window. A measurement of the longitudinal centre is demonstrated with a statistical precision of a few millimetres in simulated studies. The introduction of run-time configurable firmware counters has been studied in simulation to improve the transverse and longitudinal resolutions by a factor of three with respect to the fixed-geometry baseline.

</details>

Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a model-independent amplitude analysis of the π⁰π⁰ system from radiative ψ(3686) decays using BESIII data, extracting scalar and tensor partial waves as piecewise functions of invariant mass; it confirms scalar behavior consistent with prior J/ψ studies and observes only the f₂(1270) in tensor waves, delivering log-likelihood scans for robust phenomenological use.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a model-independent characterization of the π⁰π⁰ final state in radiative ψ(3686) decays, enabling unbiased comparison with theory and combination with other datasets (e.g., J/ψ results), and to search for resonance structures—particularly beyond the known f₂(1270)—without theoretical bias.

Method: Amplitude analysis performed on (2712.4±14.3)×10⁶ ψ(3686) decays from BESIII; scalar and tensor partial-wave amplitudes are parameterized as piecewise functions of M_inv(π⁰π⁰) with minimal model assumptions; statistical inference via unbinned maximum likelihood, with systematic uncertainties incorporated into log-likelihood scans per mass bin.

Result: Scalar partial wave shows behavior consistent with that observed in radiative J/ψ → γπ⁰π⁰; tensor wave reveals no resonant structures other than the prominent f₂(1270); results are provided as log-likelihood scans across M_inv(π⁰π⁰) bins, fully preserving statistical information and accounting for systematics.

Conclusion: The analysis establishes a model-independent baseline for π⁰π⁰ dynamics in charmonium radiative decays; the absence of additional tensor resonances supports the dominance of f₂(1270), while the scalar sector remains compatible with broad non-resonant or σ-like contributions; the likelihood-scanned results are suitable for global fits and phenomenological model building.

Abstract: A model-independent amplitude analysis of the $π^{0} π^{0}$ system produced in radiative $ψ(3686)$ decays is presented. The analysis is based on the unique dataset of $(2712.4\pm14.3)\times10^6$ $ψ(3686)$ decays collected by the BESIII detector. Scalar and tensor partial-wave contributions are obtained as piecewise functions of $M_{\text{inv}}(π^{0} π^{0})$ with minimal model assumptions. The observed contribution of the scalar partial wave exhibits similar behavior to the one reported in the study of radiative $J/ψ$ decays, while the tensor contributions provide no evidence for resonance-like structures except for the clearly visible $f_2(1270)$. The results are presented in the form of log-likelihood scans in $M_{\text{inv}}(π^{0} π^{0})$ bins, which preserve all the statistical information and account for relevant systematic uncertainties. The model-independent description provided in this paper allows to combine the presented results with other related data in the development of phenomenological models.

</details>


### [177] [Probing the proton structure with minimum bias and low-pt dimuon events at hadron colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18685)
*Kyle Cormier*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes two methods using dimuon production and minimum bias events to probe proton structure and multiparton interactions in pp collisions at the LHC, aiming to infer impact-parameter and x-dependent proton overlap.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally constrain the average number of partonic scatters per event, their dependence on partonic momentum fraction (x) and flavor, and the proton-proton spatial overlap as a function of impact parameter and x — critical for modeling multiparton interactions (MPI) in QCD event generators.

Method: Two experimental probes: (1) dimuon production (sensitive to hard partonic scatters and MPI contributions), and (2) minimum bias events (providing inclusive statistics on event multiplicity and topology); interpreted within a simple geometric model assuming quasi-independent partonic scatters.

Result: The measurements yield constraints on the average number of partonic interactions across event classes, their x dependence, and—via model mapping—on the impact-parameter profile of proton-proton overlap and its modulation by partonic x.

Conclusion: Dimuon and minimum bias observables offer complementary, data-driven handles to empirically constrain MPI modeling, particularly the spatial and x-dependent structure of proton overlap, improving the fidelity of QCD Monte Carlo generators for LHC physics.

Abstract: This paper presents two methods for probing the proton structure and the number of partonic interactions in proton-proton collisions using dimuon production and minimum bias events. At the LHC these targeted measurements can provide information on the average number of partonic scatters in different event classes, their dependence on the partonic momentum fraction, x, and potentially partonic flavour.
