Within a simple mostly geometric model of multiparton interactions as quasi-independent scatters, the measurements can be used to make inference about the proton-proton overlap dependence on impact parameter and its partonic-x dependence.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [178] [Energy, Passivity, and Diffusive Representations in Anisotropic Fractional Maxwell--Cole--Cole Media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13985)
*Nikko John Leo S. Lobos*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formulates an energy framework for linear anisotropic Maxwell–Cole–Cole media with multiple fractional polarization channels, proving electromagnetic passivity, energy decay, and uniqueness via a decomposition of fractional polarization power into integrable history storage and constitutive dissipation; it unifies time-domain initial layers, diffusive representations, and frequency-domain Cole–Cole dispersion while ensuring nonnegative absorption and exact step-response validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous, physically consistent energy formulation for fractional-order anisotropic dielectrics—specifically Maxwell–Cole–Cole media—that reconciles quiescent initial conditions, memory effects, passivity, and measurable electromagnetic response across time and frequency domains.

Method: Develops a weighted solution space accommodating Caputo fractional derivatives with zero prehistory; derives a fractional polarization power decomposition into history storage and constitutive dissipation; combines with Poynting theorem to obtain a global field-plus-memory energy identity; introduces a positive diffusive representation yielding a variance-based refinement; validates via exact electric-step response and harmonic limit analysis.

Result: Proves electromagnetic passivity, closed-system energy decay, and conditional uniqueness under zero-supply boundary conditions; shows integrability of instantaneous power despite possible initialization divergence; recovers classical limits (Debye, vacuum, etc.); establishes Hermitian imaginary part of multichannel Cole–Cole permittivity ensuring nonnegative cycle-averaged absorption; demonstrates broadened loss band, redistributed attenuation, and prolonged step-response tail in single-channel case.

Conclusion: The formulation provides a unified, local constitutive model linking fractional initial layers, hereditary energy storage, diffusive relaxation, dispersion, absorption, and attenuation—rigorously grounding fractional Maxwell–Cole–Cole media in passive, energy-consistent electrodynamics.

Abstract: This study develops an energy formulation for linear anisotropic Maxwell--Cole--Cole media with multiple fractional polarization channels, passive conductivity, prescribed currents, and finite-terminal Caputo memory with zero prehistory. A weighted solution class captures the integrable fractional initial layer generated by quiescent polarization. Fractional polarization power then separates into a nonnegative history storage and a nonnegative constitutive dissipation rate. Although the instantaneous rate may diverge at initialization, it remains integrable, while stored history energy and accumulated loss vanish initially. Combined with the Poynting theorem, this decomposition yields a global field-plus-memory identity that establishes electromagnetic passivity, closed-system energy decay, and conditional uniqueness under zero-supply boundary conditions. The framework also recovers the Debye, instantaneous-dielectric, vanishing-susceptibility, and vacuum limits under convergence assumptions. A positive diffusive representation provides a second decomposition whose storage never exceeds the history storage. Their difference is an exact nonnegative weighted variance that vanishes only for a zero polarization history. In agreement with these time-domain results, the finite-time harmonic response approaches the anisotropic multichannel Cole--Cole permittivity, whose Hermitian imaginary part ensures nonnegative cycle-averaged absorption. A reproducible single-channel study shows that fractional order broadens the dielectric-loss band, redistributes wave attenuation, strengthens the integrable initial power layer, and prolongs the step-response tail. An exact electric-step response verifies both energy representations. The formulation connects fractional initial layers, hereditary storage, passivity, diffusive relaxation, dispersion, absorption, and attenuation in a local constitutive model.

</details>


### [179] [Comment on "Landau Levels and Riemann Zeros"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14108)
*Mi-Ra Hwang,Eylee Jung,MuSeong Kim,DaeKil Park*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper critiques and extends Sierra and Townsend's model linking the Riemann zeta zeros to Landau levels of a charged particle in crossed fields, showing that individual Landau levels do not generate the log E term in the zero-counting function, and clarifying its semiclassical origin.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To examine the validity of Sierra and Townsend's proposal that higher Landau levels contribute the fluctuating part of the Riemann-zero counting function N(E), and to resolve the origin of the ln E term in their semiclassical approximation.

Method: Analytical derivation of exact eigenfunctions for arbitrary Landau level n in the crossed-field setup; asymptotic analysis at the two box boundaries used by Sierra and Townsend; comparison of boundary conditions across levels; reinterpretation of the ln E term via first-order semiclassical correction to the smooth LLL counting function.

Result: Boundary conditions for all fixed n are equivalent to the n=0 case up to an E-independent phase (−i)^n, precluding any ln E contribution from individual Landau levels; the ln E term in ST’s Eq. (29) is identified as the first-order semiclassical shift E → E + γz² of the smooth LLL counting function.

Conclusion: Sierra and Townsend’s suggestion that higher Landau levels supply the fluctuation in N(E) is unsupported within their approximations; the ln E term is a smooth semiclassical correction—not a fluctuation—and arises solely from the LLL, not from inter-level interference or higher-n contributions.

Abstract: Sierra and Townsend (ST) [1] showed that the ``absorption spectrum'' model of Connes [2] arises as the lowest-Landau-level (LLL) limit of a charged particle in crossed electric and magnetic fields, and suggested that the higher Landau levels supply the fluctuation part of the Riemann-zero counting function $N(E)$. Within the approximation used by ST we construct the eigenfunctions of an arbitrary Landau level $n$ and derive their asymptotics at the two box boundaries that ST identify. The boundary condition is the same as for $n=0$ up to an $E$-independent phase $(-\ii)^n$, so no $\ln E$ term arises for any fixed $n$. We also note that the $\ln E$ term in the semiclassical Eq.~(29) of ST is the first-order shift of the smooth LLL counting function, $E\to E+γz^2$.

</details>


### [180] [Categorifying Quantum Spin Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14165)
*Matthew Yu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a homotopical framework using topological n-nets to model quantum spin systems, categorifies them into higher Morita categories, identifies a Karoubi-complete subcategory analogous to finite TQFTs, and proves that the group of locality-preserving symmetries in (3+1)D quantum spin systems is isomorphic to the full Witt group of nondegenerate braided fusion categories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize locality-preserving symmetries of quantum spin systems—particularly addressing Haah's question about the algebraic classification of such symmetries in (3+1) dimensions—using tools from higher category theory and topological quantum field theory.

Method: Develops a homotopical framework based on topological n-nets assigning associative unital algebras to spatial regions; constructs a higher Morita category structure on the category of such nets; isolates a Karoubi-complete subcategory mirroring structural properties of finite TQFTs; applies categorical classification techniques to identify symmetry groups with Witt groups of braided fusion categories.

Result: The group of locality-preserving symmetries of (3+1)-dimensional quantum spin systems—modulo stabilization, finite-depth circuits, and translations—is canonically isomorphic to the full Witt group of nondegenerate braided fusion categories.

Conclusion: Topological n-nets provide a robust categorical foundation for quantum many-body systems; the Witt group classification establishes a deep connection between higher algebraic structures and physical symmetries in quantum cellular automata and lattice models.

Abstract: We develop a homotopical framework for understanding the space of quantum spin systems based on topological $n$-nets, which assign associative unital algebras to local regions in an $n$-dimensional space in a manner that captures the locality structure of the underlying lattice theories. The net-theoretic perspective admits a natural categorification, in which the category of topological $n$-nets carries the structure of a higher Morita category. Moreover, the nets of greatest physical interest form a Karoubi-complete subcategory that shares many of the characteristic structural features of the category of finite TQFTs. Our main result identifies the group of locality-preserving symmetries of (3+1)-dimensional quantum spin systems (i.e. quantum cellular automata modulo stabilization, finite-depth quantum circuits, and translations) with the full Witt group of nondegenerate braided fusion categories, thereby answering a question posed by Haah.

</details>


### [181] [An Introduction to the $p$-Differential Forms in Spinor Variables on the Minkowskian Manifold](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14240)
*Julio César Jaramillo Quiceno*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces p-differential forms in spinor variables on Minkowski spacetime, links spacetime to Hermitian matrices via spinor coordinates, constructs spinor-based 1- and 2-forms, formulates the Dirac operator using exterior calculus, and extends the framework to non-monogenic functions—demonstrating consistency and relevance for relativistic field equations and quantum geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a consistent spinorial formulation of exterior calculus on Lorentzian manifolds, enabling geometric and algebraic unification of relativistic field theory and quantum mechanics through spinor variables and Clifford algebra.

Method: Leverages spinor space algebra and Clifford algebra foundations; establishes isomorphism between Minkowski spacetime and 2×2 Hermitian matrices; constructs p-differential forms (p=1,2) in spinor notation; defines Dirac operator via exterior calculus; extends to non-monogenic functions following Jaramillo et al. (2024).

Result: A coherent spinorial exterior calculus is formulated, including explicit expressions for spinor-valued 1- and 2-forms and a Dirac operator expressed intrinsically in terms of exterior derivatives and Hodge duality; consistency verified and applicability to relativistic field equations and quantum-geometric frameworks demonstrated.

Conclusion: Spinorial exterior calculus provides a rigorous, geometrically transparent framework for relativistic and quantum-theoretic constructions; it generalizes standard differential forms to spinor variables and supports further development of gauge- and gravity-coupled quantum field theories on curved spacetimes.

Abstract: This article presents an introductory study of $p$-differential forms in spinor variables defined on the Minkowskian (or Lorentzian) manifold. Starting from the algebraic and geometric foundations of spinor spaces and Clifford algebras, we establish the correspondence between Minkowski spacetime and Hermitian matrices in spinor coordinates. Within this framework, we construct differential 1-forms and 2-forms expressed in spinor notation, and formulate the Dirac operator in terms of exterior calculus. Furthermore, we extend this approach to the case of non-monogenic functions, following the method proposed in~\cite{Jaramillo2024}. The results illustrate the consistency of the spinorial exterior calculus and its potential applications in the study of relativistic field equations and geometric formulations of quantum theory.

</details>


### [182] [Inverse Resonance Problem for the Half-Line Schrödinger Operator with a Complex Robin Boundary Condition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14323)
*Roman Shterenberg*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the inverse resonance problem for the 1D Schrödinger operator on the half-line to complex Robin boundary conditions, replacing the Dirichlet Jost function with a Robin characteristic function; it establishes large-energy asymptotics, Hadamard factorization, finite-data stability, and uniqueness—showing that the full set of Robin eigenvalues and resonances uniquely determines the complex-valued compactly supported potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the inverse resonance theory from Dirichlet to complex Robin boundary conditions, addressing the lack of existing results for non-self-adjoint (complex a) and non-Dirichlet settings, while preserving analytic structure and stability properties.

Method: Adapts the transformation-operator, entire-function, and Fourier-analytic framework of Marletta–Shterenberg–Weikard; introduces the Robin characteristic function F_a(z;q) = ψ_x(z,0;q) − aψ(z,0;q); derives new large-energy estimates, proves Hadamard factorization for F_a, analyzes the kernel B(x,t) under Robin condition yielding boundary term B_x(0,t) − aB(0,t), and establishes stability and uniqueness via contour integration and growth-order arguments.

Result: For any fixed a ∈ ℂ (and uniformly over bounded subsets), the Robin characteristic function satisfies F_a(z;q) = iz − a + O(1); large-energy asymptotics, Hadamard representation, finite-data stability, and uniqueness are proved; the complete multiset of Robin eigenvalues and resonances uniquely determines the potential q.

Conclusion: The inverse resonance problem is solvable for the half-line Schrödinger operator with complex Robin boundary condition: spectral data (eigenvalues and resonances) fully determine the complex compactly supported potential, extending prior Dirichlet results while accommodating non-self-adjoint boundary parameters.

Abstract: We study the inverse resonance problem for the one-dimensional Schrödinger operator on the half-line with a compactly supported complex-valued potential and a complex Robin boundary condition \[ u'(0)-a u(0)=0,\qquad a\in\mathbb C. \] The proof follows the transformation-operator, entire-function, and Fourier analytic strategy developed by Marletta, Shterenberg and Weikard for the Dirichlet problem \cite{MSW}. The Dirichlet Jost function $ψ(z,0)$ is replaced by the Robin characteristic function \[ F_a(z;q)=ψ_x(z,0;q)-aψ(z,0;q). \] We establish the corresponding large-energy estimates, Hadamard factorization, finite-data stability, and uniqueness results. The principal new feature is that $F_a(z;q)=iz-a+O(1)$ rather than $1+O(z^{-1})$. In the transformation-operator argument the relevant boundary quantity is $B_x(0,t)-aB(0,t)$, together with the scalar term $B(0,0)$. For fixed $a\in\mathbb C$, and uniformly for $a$ in bounded subsets of $\mathbb C$, the resulting inverse resonance estimates have the same qualitative form as those proved in \cite{MSW} for the Dirichlet problem. In particular, for a fixed Robin parameter $a$, the complete set of Robin eigenvalues and resonances, counted with multiplicity, determines the potential uniquely.
  The text is produced with the help of AI ChatGPT for Academy.

</details>


### [183] [Yang-Mills Theory in the Multisymplectic Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14418)
*David Villa-Blanco*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This Master's thesis constructs and applies a multisymplectic field-theoretic framework to pure Yang-Mills theory, unifying covariant Lagrangian and Hamiltonian formulations, recovering standard equations (Euler–Lagrange, Yang-Mills, Bianchi), deriving conserved currents/charges via generalized Noether theorems, and extending symplectic structures to graded Lie algebras and momentum maps in both covariant and instantaneous (Cauchy-sliced) settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometrically rigorous, covariant, and unified multisymplectic formulation of classical gauge field theory—specifically pure Yang-Mills—that generalizes finite-dimensional symplectic mechanics, handles singular Lagrangians, respects gauge symmetry intrinsically, and bridges covariant and canonical (instantaneous) descriptions.

Method: Uses jet bundle geometry and Poincaré–Cartan forms to build first-order multisymplectic Lagrangian formalism; constructs Legendre transform to multisymplectic Hamiltonian formalism (including constraint algorithm for singular cases); performs Cauchy surface decomposition to derive instantaneous presymplectic structure; develops multisymplectic and instantaneous momentum maps; applies the framework to principal-bundle gauge theory with connection space as configuration manifold.

Result: Recovery of tensorial Yang-Mills equations and Bianchi identity in local trivializations; derivation of gauge-covariant conserved currents and charges; construction of a graded Lie algebra of Hamiltonian forms; consistent De Donder and Hamilton equations; compatible presymplectic brackets on infinite-dimensional phase space; generalized Noether theorems for both covariant and instantaneous settings.

Conclusion: The multisymplectic framework provides a coherent, geometric, and covariant foundation for Yang-Mills theory that naturally incorporates gauge symmetry, constraints, and reduction, while unifying and generalizing both Lagrangian and Hamiltonian structures across finite- and infinite-dimensional settings.

Abstract: This Master's thesis develops and applies the multisymplectic field-theory framework to (pure) Yang-Mills theory. Building from the geometric foundations of jet bundles and the Poincaré-Cartan forms, the work constructs the Lagrangian multisymplectic formalism for first-order field theories, recovering the usual Euler-Lagrange field equations and extending the Poisson brackets of symplectic mechanics to a full graded Lie algebra. It then establishes the Legendre correspondence with the multisymplectic Hamiltonian formalism (in which Hamilton equations are retrieved) including the case of singular Lagrangians (where a constraint algorithm is reproduced). Breaking Lorentz covariance via a Cauchy surface slicing, the thesis then relates the covariant multisymplectic picture to an instantaneous presymplectic (infinite-dimensional) formulation in which the instantaneous De Donder equations and presymplectic brackets are obtained in a compatible way. A comprehensive treatment of symmetries then leads to multisymplectic and instantaneous momentum maps generalizations and to several versions of Noether's theorems.
  Specializing the formalism to principal-bundle gauge theory, the thesis identifies the multisymplectic configuration space with the bundle of connections, gauge symmetries with symmetries in the multisymplectic language and builds the Yang-Mills Lagrangian. Lastly, among many computations, the standard tensorial Yang-Mills equations and the Bianchi identity in local trivializations are retrieved, Yang-Mills conserved currents and charges are obtained and the graded algebra is derived for a special class of Hamiltonian forms.

</details>


### [184] [Non-Intrusive Balancing for Data-Driven Model Reduction with Oblique Projections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15219)
*Alberto Padovan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces Balanced Operator Inference—a non-intrusive reduced-order modeling framework that computes dynamics-aware oblique projections (inspired by Balanced Truncation) from forward-only simulation data, enabling accurate modeling of systems with transient energy growth; it combines nonlinear empirical Gramians, subspace balancing, and Operator Inference, outperforming POD-based models by up to 100× in forecasting error.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard non-intrusive ROMs (e.g., POD-based) fail for systems with large-amplitude transients because they rely on static, energy-optimal (orthogonal) projections that discard dynamically important low-energy states; intrusive balanced methods (e.g., Balanced Truncation) avoid this but require adjoint solves—unavailable for black-/gray-box solvers.

Method: Develops a non-intrusive framework to compute the nonlinear empirical reachability Gramian and its observability counterpart, projects them onto a restriction subspace, balances them to obtain an oblique projection, and then applies Operator Inference to learn dynamics on the balanced latent space; proves recovery of classical Balanced Truncation in the linear case when Gramian ranges lie in the restriction subspace; identifies minimax-optimal restriction as dominant eigenspaces of the reachability Gramian, enabling efficient SVD-based implementation.

Result: Balanced Operator Inference achieves up to two orders of magnitude lower forecasting error than POD-based Operator Inference across multiple benchmarks, including a high-fidelity 3D incompressible flow simulation with 80 million DOFs.

Conclusion: Dynamics-aware oblique projections—previously restricted to intrusive settings—can be constructed non-intrusively via Gramian balancing on forward data alone; this bridges the accuracy of balanced truncation with the practicality of non-intrusive learning, significantly advancing robustness and fidelity of data-driven ROMs for transient-dominant systems.

Abstract: Non-intrusive reduced-order modeling is essential for systems simulated with black- or gray-box solvers, yet computing accurate data-driven models remains challenging when the dynamics exhibit large-amplitude transient energy growth. This is because state-of-the-art non-intrusive methods typically rely on orthogonal projection onto high-energy linear or nonlinear manifolds identified by minimizing static reconstruction losses, which truncates low-energy, yet dynamically important, states. Intrusive methods such as Balanced Truncation and its extensions address this issue by using adjoint information to define dynamics-aware oblique projections. Here, we show that balancing-based oblique projections for nonlinear systems can instead be computed non-intrusively from forward simulation data. Specifically, we develop a framework to compute the nonlinear empirical reachability Gramian and balance it against its observability counterpart, projected onto a suitable restriction subspace. This procedure provably recovers the oblique projection of Balanced Truncation when the dynamics are linear and the range of either Gramian is contained in the restriction subspace. Moreover, in the linear case and in the absence of adjoint information, we show that the minimax-optimal restriction subspace spans the dominant eigenspaces of the reachability Gramian, which informs an efficient balancing algorithm with cost comparable to a standard SVD. Once the oblique projection is computed, we use Operator Inference to learn the latent-space dynamics on its range. The method is demonstrated on several examples of increasing complexity, including the three-dimensional incompressible flow past a wing with $8\times 10^7$ degrees of freedom. In all examples, the Balanced Operator Inference models significantly outperform their POD-based counterparts, reducing forecasting error by up to two orders of magnitude.

</details>


### [185] [From isolated singular wells to exact Bloch bands: the trigonometric Pöschl-Teller lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16072)
*Ulysses Camara da Silva*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces an exactly solvable 1D lattice with periodic inverse-square singularities, revealing two distinct mechanisms for spectral-gap closure: one due to Brillouin-zone folding upon sublattice symmetry, and another yielding a genuine massless Dirac cone at an asymmetric critical point; it unifies quadratic, massive Dirac-like, and tunable massless dispersions in a single analytically tractable model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct an exactly solvable 1D singular lattice that hosts coexisting conventional (quadratic), massive Dirac-like, and tunable massless band dispersions—enabling analytical study of spectral transitions and effective mass phenomena in systems with strong local singularities.

Method: Analytical solution of the Schrödinger equation for a generalized trigonometric Pöschl–Teller potential with periodic inverse-square singularities; imposition of current-preserving, transmitting self-adjoint matching conditions between unit cells; Bloch theory applied to lattices with two inequivalent singular sublattices; spectral analysis including band-edge scaling, gap-closure conditions, and dispersion asymptotics near critical points.

Result: Two distinct spectral-gap closure mechanisms identified: (i) apparent band crossing from Brillouin-zone folding when sublattices become equivalent; (ii) genuine Dirac-cone formation at a critical asymmetric configuration preserving primitive period. Near criticality, small gaps act as effective Dirac masses, inducing crossover from quadratic to massive Dirac dispersion; band curvatures yield well-defined electron/hole effective masses; isospectral lattices exhibit distinct probability densities.

Conclusion: The model provides the first analytically exact 1D platform where quadratic, massive Dirac-like, and tunable massless relativistic dispersions coexist and interconvert via continuous parameter tuning—offering rigorous insight into singular quantum transport, topological band touching, and effective-mass engineering in engineered lattices.

Abstract: An exactly solvable lattice with periodic inverse-square singularities exhibits two physically distinct mechanisms of spectral-gap closure. The model arises from the generalized trigonometric Pöschl-Teller potential in the coupling range where both local solutions at each singularity are square-integrable. A transmitting, current-preserving self-adjoint matching condition connects neighboring cells and generates a nontrivial Bloch spectrum. For a generic lattice with two inequivalent singular sublattices, the spectrum contains two alternating families of gaps, and the band edges reproduce the scale-invariant spectra of the associated finite singular system. One gap family closes when the two sublattices become equivalent. In this symmetric limit, however, the primitive period halves, so the apparent band crossing results from Brillouin-zone folding. A different family closes at a critical but genuinely asymmetric configuration. Because the primitive period is preserved, adjacent bands then meet at the center of the original Brillouin zone in a genuine massless Dirac-like cone. Near either critical limit, a small finite gap acts as an effective Dirac mass: the dispersion remains quadratic near a band edge but crosses over to a massive Dirac-like form beyond that narrow region. The band curvatures define effective electron and hole masses. Exact Bloch eigenfunctions further reveal that isospectral lattices can display different single-particle probability densities. This model therefore provides an analytically controlled one-dimensional setting in which conventional quadratic carriers, massive Dirac-like band dispersions, and a tunable massless contact coexist within the same singular lattice.

</details>


### [186] [The Fröhlich Polaron at large total momentum in d=3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16081)
*Tobias Schmidt*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that the 3D Fröhlich polaron fiber Hamiltonian lacks a ground state beyond a finite critical total momentum |P|, for all coupling strengths α > 0 — extending prior weak-coupling results to arbitrary coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether the absence of a ground state at large total momentum holds beyond the weak-coupling regime, where it was previously known, and to characterize the spectral threshold behavior of the Fröhlich polaron in 3D.

Method: Spectral analysis of the fiber Hamiltonian; construction of approximate threshold states composed of a fully interacting low-residual-momentum polaron plus one high-momentum phonon; energy comparison with the essential spectrum infimum.

Result: The bottom of the spectrum coincides with the essential spectrum at some finite |P| > 0, proving absence of a ground state for all |P| exceeding this critical value, uniformly in α > 0.

Conclusion: Ground-state instability at large total momentum is universal across all coupling strengths in the 3D Fröhlich model, governed by phonon-mediated momentum redistribution rather than perturbative effects.

Abstract: We consider the three-dimensional Fröhlich polaron at fixed coupling strength $α>0$ and total momentum $P \in \bbR^3$. We show that the bottom of the spectrum reaches the essential spectrum at a finite value of $|P|$, implying that the fiber Hamiltonian has no ground state at sufficiently large total momentum. This extends the known weak-coupling result to arbitrary coupling. Our approach describes states near the spectral threshold by a fully interacting polaron with small residual momentum and one additional phonon carrying almost all of the total momentum.

</details>


### [187] [Using the Renormalized Energy from Ginzburg-Landau Theory to Compute Solution Landscapes in the Reduced Landau-de Gennes Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16083)
*Yucen Han,Apala Majumdar*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the renormalized energy framework from Ginzburg-Landau theory to the reduced Landau-de Gennes model for nematic liquid crystals in 2D polygonal domains, using topological degrees at defects and corners to construct efficient initial conditions for computing equilibrium and saddle-point configurations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a computationally efficient, asymptotically rigorous method for predicting defect patterns in nematic liquid crystals confined to 2D polygons—beyond standard disc or smooth-domain settings—by generalizing the GL renormalized energy to domains with sharp corners.

Method: Generalizes the finite-dimensional renormalized energy from GL vortices to rLdG in bounded 2D polygons; defines it via a topological triplet (a: defect locations, c: vertex winding contributions, d: interior defect degrees) constrained by Dirichlet boundary conditions; uses critical points of this energy as initial guesses for rLdG PDE solvers.

Result: Recovers known energy-minimizing and non-minimizing saddle-point solutions on disc, regular hexagon, and rectangle; discovers previously unreported rLdG solution branches; demonstrates significant computational speedup over direct PDE solving; identifies limitations—exclusion of line defects and finite-size effects.

Conclusion: The renormalized energy approach provides an accurate, efficient, and topologically informed tool for generating candidate rLdG equilibria in polygonal domains, bridging asymptotic analysis and numerical simulation, albeit restricted to point-defect-dominated regimes.

Abstract: We study nematic liquid crystal phases confined to bounded, two-dimensional (2D) domains, in the reduced Landau-de Gennes (rLdG) theoretical framework. The rLdG framework is equivalent to the celebrated Ginzburg-Landau (GL) theoretical setting, under certain assumptions. In the asymptotic limit of large domains (or low temperatures), we recall the concept of the finite-dimensional renormalized energy in the GL theory [Bethuel et al., GL Vortices, Prog. Nonlinear Differ. Equ. Appl. 13, Birkhäuser, 1994.] and generalize it to polygons with sharp corners. The renormalized energy is fully defined by the triplet, $(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{c}, \mathbf{d}$), of topological degrees of the interior defects ($\mathbf{d}$) and the winding number contribution of polygon vertices ($\mathbf{c}$) and interior defect locations ($\mathbf{a}$), subject to sum rules dictated by the imposed Dirichlet boundary condition. We use the critical points of the renormalized energy to construct initial conditions for the rLdG solver, recover known energy-minimizing and non-energy minimizing saddle points of the rLdG energy on a disc, regular hexagon and rectangle along with the discovery of previously unreported rLdG solution branches. The renormalized energy approach is computationally substantially more efficient than a direct PDE-solver in the rLdG setting but misses solution branches with line defects and finite-size effects.

</details>


### [188] [Superintegrable systems in curved spaces via coalgebras and deformed Racah algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16154)
*Samuel Jimenez-Swain,Ian Marquette,Yao-Zhong Zhang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs Poisson algebras from partial Casimirs of undeformed and non-standard q-deformed sl(2) coalgebras, yielding Racah-type and finite quadratic Poisson algebras; it shows deformation-independent closure, applies to curved-space Hamiltonians, and establishes a universal two-block identity ensuring maximal superintegrability in q-deformed Smorodinsky–Winternitz systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct integrable systems with coalgebra symmetry by leveraging partial Casimirs—especially to unify Racah-type structures, quadratic Poisson algebras, and superintegrability across deformation regimes and dimensions.

Method: Abstract Poisson–coalgebraic derivation: using coproduct iteration to define n-site partial Casimirs; computing their Poisson brackets intrinsically (without symplectic realizations); analyzing closure properties, Casimir relations, and nested quadratic block structures; proving a key two-block identity within the localized Poisson algebra; applying results to Stäckel-transformed q-deformed Smorodinsky–Winternitz models.

Result: For 3- and 4-site undeformed sl(2), rank-one and rank-two Racah Poisson algebras emerge; for non-standard q-deformed sl(2), finite quadratic Poisson algebras arise, with closure coefficients independent of q; a universal two-block identity governs additional Stäckel integrals and extends to arbitrary N; explicit higher-site partial Casimirs and factorized bracket generators are provided.

Conclusion: The coalgebraic framework provides a deformation-robust, realization-independent route to constructing superintegrable systems and associated quadratic Poisson algebras; the two-block identity is a structural cornerstone enabling maximal superintegrability in q-deformed curved-space models.

Abstract: We determine the Poisson algebras generated by partial Casimirs of undeformed and non-standard $q$-deformed $\mathfrak{sl}(2)$ coalgebras, respectively. The derivation is performed on the abstract algebraic level and does not depend on any particular symplectic realization. For the 3- and 4-site cases the constructions associated with the undeformed $\mathfrak{sl}(2)$ produce the rank-one and rank-two Racah Poisson algebras, respectively, while those with the non-standard deformed $\mathfrak{sl}$ yield finite quadratic Poisson algebras generated by connected partial Casimirs. At both the three and four sites, the final closure coefficients do not explicitly depend on the deformation parameter. The results hold at the Poisson coalgebra level and hence apply to any realizations which preserve the defining brackets and coproducts. Canonical symplectic realizations give rise to Hamiltonians in spaces of non-constant curvature. For the Stäckel $q$-deformed Smorodinsky-Winternitz model we show explicitly how the universal coalgebra sector is completed to maximal superintegrability. An exact two-block identity, proved directly in the localized Poisson algebra without appealing to a symplectic realization, controls the additional Stäckel integrals. In three dimensions, it generates a quadratic Poisson block with its own Casimir relation; in four dimensions, its coupling to the full left/right coalgebra family is organized into three nested quadratic blocks, and the same identity extends this nested coupling to arbitrary $N$. Explicit higher-site partial Casimirs and factorized bracket generators are collected in the appendices.

</details>


### [189] [Geometric bulk conductances in even dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16208)
*Alexis Drouot*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides an elementary proof—using only Stokes' theorem and the Poincaré lemma—of a geometric Kubo formula for bulk conductance in even-dimensional lattice systems, linking it to a topological integer and extending prior 2D results to all even dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To give an elementary, geometrically transparent proof of the Martinian-Stoiber generalization of the Drouot-Zhu result, avoiding analytic machinery and instead leveraging cochain deformation on ℤ²ⁿ (inspired by Kitaev).

Method: Represents geometric bulk conductance as a pairing of the projector P with a cochain on ℤ²ⁿ; deforms the cochain (not the transverse open sets U₁,…,U₂ₙ); applies Stokes’ theorem and the Poincaré lemma to derive the factorization into topological integer 𝒳_U and bulk conductance.

Result: The geometric Kubo formula Tr(P [P,𝟙_{U₁}] ∧ ⋯ ∧ [P,𝟙_{U₂ₙ}]) equals 𝒳_U times the bulk conductance of P, where 𝒳_U is a topological integer depending only on the relative positions of the transverse open sets U₁,…,U₂ₙ.

Conclusion: The bulk conductance admits a purely geometric-topological interpretation via cochain pairing, confirming universality across even dimensions and simplifying prior proofs by eliminating reliance on advanced index theory or pseudodifferential calculus.

Abstract: Let $P$ be a short-range projector on $\ell^2(\mathbb{Z}^{2n},\mathbb{C}^m)$ and $U=(U_1,\dots,U_{2n})$ be a transverse family of open subsets of $\mathbb{R}^{2n}$. We give an elementary proof of a recent result of Martinian-Stoiber, which extends a two-dimensional result of Drouot-Zhu to all even dimensions. Specifically, using only Stokes' theorem and the Poincaré lemma, we show that the geometric Kubo formula $\operatorname{Tr}\big(P[P,\mathbf{1}_{U_1}]\wedge\cdots\wedge[P,\mathbf{1}_{U_{2n}}]\big)$ is the product of a topological integer $\mathcal{X}_U$, which depends only on the relative positions of $U_1, \dots, U_{2n}$, and of the bulk conductance of $P$. Our proof writes the geometric bulk conductance as the pairing of $P$ with a cochain on $\mathbb{Z}^{2n}$ - an idea originally due to Kitaev - and deforms the cochain rather than the sets $(U_1, \dots, U_{2n})$. The proof presented in this paper is due to Claude Opus 5.5.

</details>


### [190] [Nested polynomial algebras and the exact spectrum of a three-dimensional two-spin oscillator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16317)
*Ian Marquette,O. Ogulcan Tuncer,I. Yurdusen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs symmetry algebra representations and derives the exact spectrum of a 3D oscillator with two spin-1/2 particles, identifying distinct algebras acting on angular–spin and radial subspaces, and providing full spectral data including eigenfunctions, Casimirs, structure functions, and exchange symmetry effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exactly solve the quantum spectrum of a 3D isotropic oscillator coupled to two spin-1/2 degrees of freedom by exploiting its full symmetry structure beyond previously known integrals.

Method: Group-theoretic and algebraic methods: decomposition into total angular momentum subspaces; identification and representation of a quadratic algebra acting on triplet orbital channels and an exchange algebra linking triplet and singlet states; realization of the radial Hamiltonian as a positive-discrete-series su(1,1) generator; explicit construction of finite-dimensional matrices, Casimir operators, structure functions, and normalized eigenfunctions.

Result: Exact spectral basis fully characterized; explicit finite matrix forms of all integrals; Casimir eigenvalues and structure functions for both angular–spin and radial algebras; normalized closed-form eigenfunctions; radial ladder operator actions; treatment of the exceptional j=0 sector; complete energy-shell degeneracies; and explicit effect of particle interchange (symmetry under SU(2) spin exchange).

Conclusion: The system’s spectrum is governed by two independent algebraic structures: a finite-dimensional angular–spin algebra determining multiplicity patterns, and an infinite-dimensional su(1,1) radial algebra quantizing energy; their decoupling enables exact solvability and complete spectral characterization.

Abstract: We construct representations of the symmetry algebra and derive the exact spectrum of a three-dimensional oscillator with two spin-$\tfrac12$ degrees of freedom. The Hamiltonian and its first-order scalar and pseudo-scalar integrals were obtained in earlier classifications. Here we determine their action on the complete spectral basis. At fixed total angular momentum, a quadratic algebra acts on the three triplet orbital channels and admits a three-dimensional deformed-oscillator representation, while an overlapping two-state exchange algebra joins the middle triplet to the singlet. These finite modules determine angular--spin multiplicities but do not quantize the radial energy. A separate positive-discrete-series $su(1,1)$ representation of the common radial Hamiltonian generates the energy sequence. We give the finite matrices, Casimir and structure functions, normalized eigenfunctions, radial ladder actions, the exceptional $j=0$ sector, complete energy-shell multiplicities, and the effect of particle interchange.

</details>


### [191] [Almost invariant subspaces in the topologically non-trivial setting: a pedestrian approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16393)
*Horia Cornean,Gabriele Peluso,Mikkel Hviid Thorn*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs almost-invariant subspaces for Hofstadter-like Hamiltonians under magnetic flux perturbations, preserving topological invariants (Chern number) and spectral properties (Středa formula), via an iterative algorithm applicable to discrete lattice models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a constructive, algorithmic method for adiabatically deforming gapped projections under rational magnetic flux variations while preserving topological and spectral features—avoiding heavy pseudo-differential machinery.

Method: Iterative perturbative construction of projections $P_\varepsilon$ commuting with $H_{b_0+\varepsilon}$ up to $\varepsilon^\infty$ error, starting from a locally gapped, possibly topologically non-trivial projection $P_0$ commuting with $H_{b_0}$; uses discrete setting on $\ell^2(\mathbb{Z}^2, \mathbb{C}^n)$ to avoid pseudo-differential calculus.

Result: Existence of $P_\varepsilon$ with same Chern number as $P_0$, linear variation of integrated density of states in $\varepsilon$ (Středa formula), and preservation of spatial localization via mapping of Parseval frames.

Conclusion: A fully constructive, discrete-algorithmic framework for topologically robust almost-invariant subspaces under magnetic perturbations is established; results are expected to extend to continuous magnetic pseudodifferential operators, albeit with greater technical complexity.

Abstract: We present a self-contained, algorithmic construction of almost invariant subspaces [Panati et al. (2003), De Nittis and Panati (2010), Freund and Teufel (2016)] for Hofstadter-like Hamiltonians $H_b $ acting on $\ell^2(\mathbb{Z}^2,\mathbb{C}^n)$. More precisely, for rational values of $b_0/(2π)$ we describe an iterative procedure that takes a ``locally gapped" projection $P_{0}$ (possibly topologically non-trivial) which commutes with $H_{b_0}$, into a projection $P_{\varepsilon}$ that commutes with $H_{b_0+\varepsilon}$ up to an error of order $\varepsilon^\infty$.
  Our construction insures that the Chern number of $P_{\varepsilon}$ equals the Chern number of $P_0$ as long as $P_\varepsilon$ exists. Moreover, its integrated density of states varies linearly with $\varepsilon$ (the ``Středa formula"). We also show that any spatially localized Parseval frame of $P_{0}$ can be mapped into to a localized Parseval frame of $P_{\varepsilon}$.
  We choose to work with a discrete model in order to keep the technicalities to a minimum level, with no need for advanced pseudo-differential calculus. Nevertheless, we are confident that most of our results can be extended to (unbounded) magnetic (pseudo)differential operators, but the technical burden would be much heavier. This is left for future work.

</details>


### [192] [Willis-type thermal homogenization for particulate composites under harmonic heat transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17245)
*Chunlin Wu,Ning Ding,Huiming Yin*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Willis-type thermal homogenization framework for 2D particulate composites under harmonic heat transfer, avoiding residual fields by constructing effective constitutive relations directly from eigen-field–macro-response mappings; validated analytically and numerically, it reveals nonlocal, frequency- and wavenumber-dependent effective properties and establishes fundamental symmetry relations for coupling tensors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a rigorous, residual-field-free Willis-type thermal homogenization framework for harmonic heat conduction in particulate composites, enabling physically consistent, nonlocal effective constitutive relations that capture coupling phenomena (e.g., thermal–inertial coupling) without ad hoc assumptions.

Method: Dual-reciprocity inclusion-based boundary element method (BEM) with two eigen-fields encoding thermal conductivity and heat capacity mismatches; construction of the full effective constitutive operator from eigen-field–generalized macroscopic response relations; validation via 1D analytical solution and extended Fourier-series homogenization; Bloch-periodic averaging with de-phased averaging to ensure physical consistency.

Result: A unique, residual-field-free effective constitutive relation for Willis-type thermal homogenization; derivation of opposite-wavenumber reciprocity and reflection-symmetry parity relations for coupling tensors; demonstration of wavenumber- and frequency-dependent nonlocal effective properties; identification of necessity of de-phased averaging under Bloch conditions.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a general, physically consistent, and computationally robust method for Willis-type thermal homogenization of 2D particulate composites under harmonic excitation, with direct extension potential to 3D and multiphysics settings.

Abstract: This paper develops a Willis-type thermal homogenization framework for two-dimensional particulate composites under harmonic heat transfer and establishes a unique effective constitutive relation without introducing residual fields. The local thermal fields are obtained using the dual-reciprocity inclusion-based boundary element method, in which two eigen-fields are introduced to simulate the mismatches in thermal conductivity and heat capacity. Instead of retrieving effective kernels from several independent macroscopic solutions generated by residual fields, the complete constitutive operator is constructed upon relations between eigen-fields and generalized macroscopic responses. The effective constitutive relations are validated against a one-dimensional analytical solution and an extended Fourier-series homogenization scheme. The Fourier formulae further establish the opposite-wavenumber reciprocity relations between two coupling tensors and reflection-symmetry parity relations, which explain when transverse coupling components vanish and the sign reversal of off-diagonal conductivity components under reflection. Numerical results further demonstrate that the de-phased average is required to extract physically consistent effective thermal properties under the Bloch conditions, and reveal their nonlocal frequency/wavenumber-dependent behavior. The framework provides a general computational method for Willis-type homogenization of particulate composites and is extendable to three-dimensional, multiphysical problems.

</details>


### [193] [Norm properties of quantum uniformity measures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17454)
*Berke Ünal*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Proves quantum uniformity measures are norms for k=4 (via Cauchy–Schwarz and Bochner L^8 triangle inequality) but violate the triangle inequality for all k ≥ 5 (via finite-field linear systems and norming system theory).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve an open question by Bu, Gu, and Jaffe on whether quantum uniformity measures satisfy the triangle inequality (i.e., are norms) for general k.

Method: For k = 4: introduces a quantum uniformity pairing ⟨·,·⟩_{Q⁴} and applies Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, combined with triangle inequality in a finite-dimensional Bochner L⁸ space. For k ≥ 5: constructs linear equation systems over finite fields and uses structure theory of norming linear systems to derive contradiction to triangle inequality.

Result: Quantum uniformity measures are norms if and only if k = 4; triangle inequality fails for all k ≥ 5.

Conclusion: The quantum uniformity measures form norms exclusively at k = 4 — settling the open problem definitively.

Abstract: We prove that for $k=4$ the quantum uniformity measures are norms, whereas for every $k\geq5$ the triangle inequality fails. This gives a complete answer to an open question of Bu, Gu, and Jaffe. The $k=4$ case is proved using the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality for the quantum uniformity pairing $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{Q^4}$ introduced here, together with the triangle inequality in a suitable finite-dimensional Bochner $L^8$ space. For $k\geq5$, we construct systems of linear equations over a finite field and derive a contradiction from the structure theory of norming linear systems.

</details>


### [194] [$L^2$ convergence rates for singular stochastic wave dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17815)
*Nikolay Barashkov,Giacomo Di Gesù,Petri Laarne*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes quantitative hypocoercivity for singular stochastic damped wave equations used in stochastic quantization, introducing a dimension-free criterion based on log-Sobolev inequality and exponential lower-tail bounds on the Hessian spectrum; it verifies the criterion for Φ⁴₂, sine-Gordon (β² < 2π), and Φ⁴₃ models, yielding exponential L² convergence to equilibrium and demonstrating kinetic acceleration over overdamped dynamics in weak-coupling regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend hypocoercivity theory to infinite-dimensional singular stochastic PDEs arising in stochastic quantization—particularly damped wave equations—where classical uniform semiconvexity fails due to roughness and singularity of the potentials.

Method: Adapts and generalizes the finite-dimensional hypocoercivity framework of Cao–Lu–Wang to infinite dimensions via a dimension-free criterion combining log-Sobolev inequality and exponential lower-tail control of the bottom of the Hessian spectrum; applies spectral estimates on singular random Schrödinger operators to verify the criterion for Φ⁴₂, sine-Gordon, and Φ⁴₃ models; leverages continuum limit results from a companion paper.

Result: Proves exponential L² convergence to equilibrium for the stochastic damped wave dynamics of Φ⁴₂ and sine-Gordon (β² < 2π); shows kinetic relaxation rate ≥ O(m) under optimal damping in weak-coupling/small-mass regime for Φ⁴₂—strictly faster than the O(m²) rate of overdamped dynamics; obtains discretization-uniform lattice estimates for sine-Gordon up to β² < 4π and for Φ⁴₃ at fixed volume, including parameter regimes beyond current continuum well-posedness.

Conclusion: The introduced hypocoercivity criterion successfully handles singularities inherent in quantum field-theoretic SPDEs; it yields sharp, physically meaningful convergence rates and reveals a genuine kinetic speedup over Langevin-type dynamics, opening pathways for analyzing metastability and sampling in singular settings.

Abstract: We establish quantitative hypocoercivity for singular stochastic damped wave equations arising in stochastic quantization. Building on the finite-dimensional argument of Cao, Lu, and Wang [Arch. Rat. Mech. Anal. 2023], we derive a dimension-free criterion that replaces uniform semiconvexity by a log-Sobolev inequality together with exponential lower-tail bounds for the bottom of the Hessian spectrum. We verify this criterion for the two-dimensional $Φ^4_2$ and sine-Gordon models and for the three-dimensional $Φ^4_3$ model, through estimates on the lowest eigenvalue of associated singular random Schrödinger operators. Combined with the continuum limit established in a companion paper, this yields exponential $L^2$ convergence to equilibrium for the $Φ^4_2$ dynamics and for sine-Gordon with $β^2<2π$. For $Φ^4_2$, we verify that in a weak-coupling, small-mass regime, optimizing the damping yields a kinetic relaxation rate of at least order $m$. This yields a genuine acceleration over the order-$m^2$ relaxation rate of the overdamped dynamics. The lattice estimates are uniform in the discretization for sine-Gordon throughout $β^2<4π$, and for $Φ^4_3$ at fixed volume, including regimes beyond those for which the continuum dynamics is vpresently constructed.

</details>


### [195] [How many switches does two-Hamiltonian universality require?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17826)
*Matas Garbaravičius,Michael M. Wolf*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how many alternating evolutions under two generic Hamiltonians are needed to approximate any unitary on ℂ^d; it shows expected linear scaling in d, gives constructive upper bounds (e.g., 9d switches when the change-of-basis matrix is complex Hadamard), and extends results to non-generic spectra and bounded times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the minimal number of switches (i.e., control segments) required for universal quantum control via alternating Hamiltonian evolutions — a fundamental question in quantum compilation and controllability.

Method: Constructive decomposition: representing arbitrary unitaries as short products of matrices alternately diagonal in two eigenbases; spectral and matrix-structure analysis (e.g., equal-modulus column conditions); probabilistic and worst-case bounding under genericity assumptions.

Result: Expected switching number scales linearly in d; explicit upper bound of 9d switches suffices when the eigenbasis change matrix is a complex Hadamard matrix; bounds generalized to degenerate spectra and bounded time intervals.

Conclusion: Universal unitary synthesis via two Hamiltonians is efficiently switch-controllable in expectation; structural properties of the eigenbasis transformation dictate concrete, constructive switch counts — bridging Lie-theoretic controllability with practical quantum circuit depth.

Abstract: Any pair of sufficiently generic Hamiltonians on $\mathbb{C}^d$ generates the full unitary group, i.e., any $W\in U(d)$ can be synthesized to arbitrary precision by alternating between the two evolutions for suitably chosen durations. For any given pair of Hamiltonians, the minimal number of switches required to cover the entire group is at least $d$ and unbounded in general. Despite this, we show that it scales linearly in $d$ in expectation. Assuming that the two spectra are sufficiently generic, we derive constructive upper bounds on the minimal switching number as a function of the change-of-basis matrix between the two eigenbases. In particular, if this matrix has a column whose entries all have equal moduli, as for a complex Hadamard matrix, then $9d$ switches suffice to reach every unitary. To obtain these results, we construct a short exact representation of any unitary as a product of unitaries that are alternately diagonal in two given bases. All bounds are extended to non-generic spectra and bounded evolution times.

</details>


### [196] [Dynamics of low energy wave packets in the one-dimensional quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17883)
*Mateusz Kwaśnicki,Marcin Napiórkowski,Sören Petrat,Michał Pietruszka*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a rigorous justification of spin wave theory for quantum dynamics in one-dimensional spin-1/2 ferromagnetic Heisenberg models, proving that two-magnon spin wave packets evolve approximately freely over optimal timescales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify spin wave theory—widely used to describe low-energy quantum ferromagnetic dynamics—as an approximation to the true quantum dynamics in one dimension.

Method: Mathematical analysis of quantum dynamics for spin-1/2 Heisenberg chains; construction and evolution analysis of two-magnon spin wave packet states; comparison with free magnon (quasiparticle) dynamics; error estimation and optimality proof for time scales.

Result: Rigorous proof that the time evolution of two-magnon spin wave packets is well approximated by free magnon evolution up to optimal timescales in the one-dimensional quantum ferromagnetic Heisenberg model.

Conclusion: Spin wave theory is validated as a dynamically accurate approximation for multi-magnon states in 1D at low energies, and the established timescale is sharp—no longer time interval can guarantee the same approximation quality.

Abstract: Spin wave theory plays a central role in the analysis of low-energy properties of the quantum ferromagnetic Heisenberg model. In this work, we present a first step towards a rigorous justification of this approximation in the context of quantum dynamics in one dimension. According to spin wave theory, the evolution of a spin wave state should be well approximated by its free evolution with the appropriate energy of free quasiparticles called magnons. Our main result establishes this idea rigorously for a class of spin wave packets. We focus on spin-$\frac{1}{2}$ systems, and consider wave packets composed of two magnons. We argue our result is optimal with respect to the timescales.

</details>


### [197] [Dynamical BEC formation and vanishing entropy for trapped bosons in non-relativistic QED via nonlinear resonance cascades](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17923)
*Thomas Chen,Ali Mezher*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper rigorously proves that a trapped bosonic system coupled to a quantized radiation field dynamically forms a pure Bose-Einstein condensate via radiative relaxation, even when starting from a genuinely mixed initial state — i.e., asymptotic purification occurs without requiring monotonic entropy decrease.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous dynamical emergence of Bose-Einstein condensation and asymptotic purification in open quantum many-body systems where bosons interact with a quantized radiation field, going beyond mean-field approximations and addressing initial mixedness as statistical uncertainty over mean-field orbits.

Method: Traces the macroscopic one-particle density matrix κ_T through a nonlinear resonance cascade; derives the Hartree hierarchy for the boson-photon system from the finite-N BBGKY hierarchy; employs successive limits: N→∞ (mean-field), weak-coupling, then T→∞; uses trace-norm convergence and von Neumann entropy analysis on κ_T.

Result: Strong trace-norm convergence of κ_T to the pure ground-state projector |χ₀⟩⟨χ₀| as T→∞; asymptotic vanishing of von Neumann entropy S[κ_T]→0; purification occurs for any initially mixed state with nonzero ground component and positive Fermi-Golden-Rule rates.

Conclusion: Radiative relaxation induces irreversible depletion of excited trap modes and complete asymptotic purification into a pure BEC, despite unitary global evolution — demonstrating emergent irreversibility and entropy reduction at the macroscopic level.

Abstract: The dynamical formation of a complete Bose-Einstein condensate and the asymptotic purification of trapped bosons coupled to a quantized radiation field are rigorously established. By tracking the macroscopic one-particle density matrix $κ_T$ through a nonlinear resonance cascade, it is demonstrated that radiative relaxation irreversibly depletes all excited trap modes, even when the initial macroscopic state $κ_0$ is genuinely mixed. Here $κ_T$ is an average, with respect to a de Finetti measure $μ_0$, over mean-field particle-field orbits, so that the mixedness of $κ_0$ reflects a statistical ensemble of orbits. Under the assumptions of a non-vanishing initial ground state component and strictly positive downward Fermi-Golden-Rule rates, strong trace-norm convergence to a pure condensate is proven, $$
  \lim_{T\to\infty} \| κ_T-|χ_0\rangle\langleχ_0| \|_{\mathfrak{S}_1(\mathfrak{h})} = 0 \, . $$ Furthermore, the von Neumann entropy of the macroscopic one-particle density matrix is shown to vanish asymptotically, $$
  \lim_{T\to\infty}S[κ_T]=0 \, , $$ without any claim of monotonicity in $T$. Thus, any initially mixed, positive-entropy state ($0<S[κ_0]<\infty$) is purified to the ground state $|χ_0\rangle\langleχ_0|$. For the proof, we derive the Hartree hierarchy of the coupled system of bosons and photons from the finite-$N$ BBGKY hierarchy of the microscopic quantum many-body system, establishing trace-norm convergence in the mean-field limit as $N\to\infty$. The limits are taken successively; first $N\to\infty$, then the weak-coupling limit, and finally $T\to\infty$. Although the global particle-field evolution remains unitary, the macroscopic one-particle state converges to a pure state as energy and correlations are continuously radiated into the dispersive field.

</details>


### [198] [Spectral Theory of an Exactly Solvable Quantum-Optical Oscillator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18421)
*Sam Johar,Joseph Kraisler*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a 1D quantum model of a photon field coupled to a continuum of two-level atoms with quadratic density, proving essential self-adjointness of the unbounded, spatially varying Hamiltonian and explicitly determining its spectrum: continuous below Ω and discrete eigenvalues above Ω, asymptotically given by Airy function zeros and approximated as ωₙ ∼ (3πg n/4)^(1/2) + Ω/4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously analyze spectral properties of a non-semibounded, spatially inhomogeneous Hamiltonian arising in quantum optics—specifically, a scalar photon field coupled to atoms with quadratic density—where standard methods fail due to unbounded coupling.

Method: Nonlinear spectral reduction transforming the original problem into effective relativistic oscillators (confining above Ω, inverted below Ω); rigorous operator-theoretic analysis to prove essential self-adjointness; explicit spectral characterization via Airy function zero interlacing.

Result: The Hamiltonian is essentially self-adjoint; spectrum comprises (-∞, Ω] (continuous) and a strictly increasing sequence of simple eigenvalues {ωₙ}ₙ≥1 above Ω, satisfying ωₙ = (3πg n/4)^(1/2) + Ω/4 + O(n^(-1/2)), with eigenvalues bijectively linked to interlaced zeros of Ai(x) and Ai'(x).

Conclusion: Despite unbounded, non-semibounded structure, the model admits a complete, explicit spectral description rooted in Airy function theory and relativistic oscillator physics—demonstrating that strong spatial inhomogeneity can yield analytically tractable, nontrivial quantum spectra.

Abstract: We study a one-dimensional single-excitation model of a scalar photon field coupled to a continuum of two-level atoms with quadratic density. The resulting matrix Hamiltonian has an unbounded spatially varying coupling and is not semibounded. We prove that it is essentially self-adjoint and determine its spectrum explicitly. The continuous spectrum is the half-line $(-\infty,Ω]$, where $Ω$ is the atomic resonance frequency, while the spectrum above $Ω$ consists of a strictly increasing sequence of simple eigenvalues. These eigenvalues are characterized by the interlaced zeros of the Airy function and its derivative and satisfy \begin{align*}
  ω_n = \left(\frac{3πg n}{4}\right)^{1/2} + \fracΩ{4} + O(n^{-1/2}). \end{align*} The analysis is based on a nonlinear spectral reduction that yields a confining massless relativistic oscillator above $Ω$ and an inverted oscillator below $Ω$.

</details>


### [199] [Haagerup-Izumi modular data from hyperbolic words: S-fold TQFTs, central charge, torsion and the Bethe vacua](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18512)
*David E Evans*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper rigorously connects modular data of Haagerup–Izumi doubles to hyperbolic monodromies on once-punctured tori, expressing Bethe vacua, handle-gluing operators, and fibering data in closed form via word combinatorics in SL(2,ℤ), torsion linking forms, and stretch factors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously understand the topological significance of handle-gluing and fibering operators in rank-0 S-fold theories and to construct explicit Bethe vacua matching the modular data structure conjectured by Evans–Gannon and confirmed (untwisted case) by Gannon–Schopieray–Yadav.

Method: Uses hyperbolic monodromies φ ∈ SL(2,ℤ) represented as words in L/R generators; computes cokernels A_± = coker(φ ∓ 1) endowed with torsion linking forms from mapping tori of φ and −φ; derives Bethe data—including level matrix, positive vacuum, effective central charge c_eff = #L, and handle-gluing values—directly from word structure; solves Bethe equations via kernel vectors modulo |A_∓| where explicit trigonometric expressions are non-vanishing; applies half-index dictionary to relate c mod 24 to word composition.

Result: Establishes a complete closed-form correspondence between SL(2,ℤ) words of trace m²+2 and the Evans–Gannon Q-system table (with one correction) and Gannon–Schopieray–Yadav modular data list; provides explicit formulas for handle-gluing values (2^{#L} λ^{±1} |A_-||A_+|), c_eff = #L, and c ≡ #L − 6#R (mod 24); constructs infinitely many non-unitary Bethe vacua indexed by hyperbolic φ.

Conclusion: The modular data of twisted Haagerup–Izumi doubles arises geometrically from hyperbolic torus monodromies, and their associated Bethe vacua—and hence the full topological structure of rank-0 S-folds—are fully determined by combinatorial word data in SL(2,ℤ), linking forms, and stretch factors.

Abstract: The modular data of the doubles of the Haagerup--Izumi categories is a grafting of two finite metric groups (conjectured by Evans--Gannon, proved for untwisted categories by Gannon--Schopieray--Yadav). Gang and Kim found non-unitary modular data of this shape in the topological twist of rank-$0$ $S$-fold theories, from the Bethe vacua of an abelianised gauge theory. The aim is to understand rigorously the significance of the handle-gluing and fibering operators, and to construct the Bethe vacua.
  For every hyperbolic once-punctured-torus monodromy $\varphi$, a word in $L$ and $R$, the two groups are $A_\mp=\coker(\varphi\mp1)$, with the torsion linking forms of the mapping tori of $\varphi$ and $-\varphi$. Over all words of trace $m^2+2$ these forms reproduce the Evans--Gannon table of Q-systems, one printed entry being corrected, and the distinct modular data in the list of Gannon--Schopieray--Yadav. The Bethe data are given in closed form by the word: the level matrix, the positive vacuum, $c_{\eff}=\#L$, and the handle-gluing values $2^{\#L}λ^{\pm1}|A_-||A_+|$ at the identity classes, $λ$ the stretch factor. Each kernel vector of $\varphi\mp1$ modulo $|A_\mp|$ at which an explicit trigonometric formula does not vanish gives a solution of the Bethe equations in closed form. Under the half-index dictionary, with the unit in the class of $σV^*$, $c\equiv\#L-6\#R\pmod{24}$.

</details>


### [200] [Inverse eigenvalue problem for discrete three-diagonal Schroedinger operator and the continuum limit in Tikhonov regularization approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18734)
*Vladimir M. Chabanov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives inverse eigenvalue problem formulas for the Sturm-Liouville operator via discrete approximation regularized à la Tikhonov, showing that the continuum limit yields the potential not directly from matrix elements but from a specific combination thereof.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a self-contained, rigorous derivation of inverse eigenvalue problem formulas for the Sturm-Liouville operator using discrete approximation as a regularization tool, addressing the ill-posedness inherent in the continuum operator formulation.

Method: Discrete approximation of the Sturm-Liouville operator using a three-diagonal matrix Hamiltonian; application of Tikhonov regularization to handle ill-posedness; analysis of the continuum limit—not of the potential matrix itself, but of a particular functional combination of its elements.

Result: The continuum limit yields a well-defined expression for the potential function in the Sturm-Liouville operator, obtained as the limit of a specific combination of discrete matrix elements—not the matrix elements themselves—thereby resolving the ill-posedness.

Conclusion: Tikhonov-regularized discrete approximation provides a consistent and mathematically sound route to reconstructing the Sturm-Liouville potential from spectral data, with the correct continuum limit emerging only through appropriate combinations of discretized quantities.

Abstract: In the present article the self-contained derivation of main formulas of the inverse eigenvalue problem for the Sturm-Liouville operator is given on the base of its discrete approximation and the subsequent transition to the limit of the continuum coordinate. In doing so, we adhere to Tikhonov concept, where the discrete approximation is performed as a regularization of the ill-posed operator problem. While the continuum limit for the three-diagonal matrix Hamiltonian is impossible as such, following the Tikhonov procedure leads not to a limit for the potential matrix itself, but rather for a certain combination of the matrix elements, and this limit is precisely the desired potential in the continuum case.

</details>


### [201] [Finite temperature Fredholm determinants for dynamical correlation functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18750)
*Oleksandr Gamayun*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes long-time, large-distance asymptotics of finite-temperature dynamical Fredholm determinants using effective form factors and phase shifts; derives transition profiles via parabolic-cylinder functions; connects to high-temperature Gaussian β ensemble for normalization; and recovers Euler-scale GHD predictions for hard rods' dynamical structure factor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize the asymptotic behavior of finite-temperature dynamical Fredholm determinants—central objects in integrable quantum many-body systems—and bridge microscopic quantum dynamics with macroscopic hydrodynamic predictions.

Method: Employs effective form factor theory to express asymptotics as a functional of an effective phase shift; identifies two branches around a quadratic stationary point; constructs the smooth transition profile using parabolic-cylinder functions; cross-validates normalization via correspondence with the high-temperature Gaussian β ensemble and Riemann–Hilbert analysis.

Result: Exact asymptotic functional form including nontrivial transition profile; finite normalization fixed unambiguously by β-ensemble correspondence; leading exponential term alone suffices to reproduce Euler-scale generalized hydrodynamics (GHD) prediction for the dynamical structure factor of the hard-rod gas.

Conclusion: The effective phase shift formalism provides a complete, analytically controlled description of finite-temperature dynamical asymptotics in integrable systems; it consistently unifies exact quantum many-body results with emergent hydrodynamic behavior at the Euler scale.

Abstract: We study the long-time, large-distance asymptotics of finite-temperature dynamical Fredholm determinants using effective form factors. The method expresses these asymptotics as a functional of an effective phase shift. We determine its two branches on either side of a quadratic stationary point and the transition profile joining them. The profile is expressed through parabolic-cylinder functions. Its connection to the high-temperature Gaussian $β$ ensemble fixes the finite normalization, in agreement with an independent Riemann--Hilbert analysis. We also show that the leading exponential asymptotics suffice to recover the Euler-scale prediction of generalized hydrodynamics for the dynamical structure factor of hard rods.

</details>


<div id='hep-lat'></div>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [202] [Perturbative Renormalization of Noether Currents in SYM with Improved Discretizations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14242)
*Marios Costa,Elias Antoniou,Salomi Dimou,Constantinos Costa,Panos K. Chrysanthis,Gregoris Spanoudes,Haralambos Panagopoulos*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop renormalization factors and mixing coefficients for chiral and supercurrents in lattice $\mathcal{N}=1$ SYM with improved Wilson fermions (stout smearing + clover term) and Symanzik-improved gluon actions; it analyzes how action parameters affect operator mixing and lattice artifacts to aid Ward identity implementation and continuum matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable reliable non-perturbative studies of $\mathcal{N}=1$ supersymmetric Yang-Mills theory on the lattice, where explicit supersymmetry breaking by discretization necessitates precise control over renormalization and mixing of Noether currents (chiral current and supercurrent), especially for restoring supersymmetry in the continuum limit via Ward identities.

Method: Perturbative lattice QCD at one-loop order: compute renormalization constants and mixing coefficients for the chiral current and supercurrent in $\mathcal{N}=1$ SYM using a class of improved lattice actions — Wilson fermions with one-step stout smearing and clover term, combined with Symanzik-improved gluon actions; systematically vary clover and stout coefficients to assess their impact on mixing with gauge-invariant and gauge-noninvariant operators.

Result: Complete one-loop renormalization factors and mixing coefficients for the chiral current and supercurrent; identification of dominant mixing patterns; quantification of how stout and clover coefficients influence mixing strength and lattice artifacts; demonstration that certain parameter choices mitigate unwanted operator mixing and improve restoration of chiral/supersymmetric Ward identities.

Conclusion: Perturbative renormalization data presented here are essential for implementing chiral and supersymmetric Ward identities on the lattice, enabling robust matching of lattice observables to continuum predictions and guiding future simulations toward the controlled recovery of supersymmetry in the continuum limit of $\mathcal{N}=1$ SYM.

Abstract: We investigate the perturbative renormalization of Noether currents associated with chiral symmetry and supersymmetry transformations in $\mathcal{N}=1$ supersymmetric Yang-Mills theory, regularized on the lattice using improved discretizations. In particular, we study the chiral current and the supercurrent operator for a broad class of lattice actions: Wilson fermions with one-step stout-smeared links and a clover term, together with a family of Symanzik-improved gluon actions. Since the supercurrent mixes with both gauge-invariant and gauge-noninvariant operators, we compute the corresponding renormalization factors and mixing coefficients at one-loop order. We analyze the impact of improved gluon and gluino actions on this mixing pattern and investigate whether suitable choices of lattice parameters, such as the clover and stout coefficients, can reduce unwanted lattice artifacts at one loop. Our results provide perturbative input for the implementation of Ward identities and for matching lattice observables to their continuum counterparts, while also offering guidance for simulations aimed at restoring supersymmetry in the continuum limit.

</details>


### [203] [Impact of Window Observables on the Transversity Quark Parton Distribution Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16950)
*Daniel Adamiak,Christopher Monahan*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper assesses how lattice QCD window observables—specifically for nucleon isovector transversity PDFs—can improve global PDF fits via the ePump Hessian-updating framework, quantifying their constraining power and identifying optimal windows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Experimental transversity PDFs suffer from large uncertainties (50–100%), while modern lattice QCD calculations now achieve comparable or superior precision; window observables enable direct, quantitative comparison between lattice and phenomenology, offering a path to constrain global PDF analyses.

Method: The authors employ the ePump Hessian-updating framework to quantify the impact of existing and prospective lattice QCD computations—including transversity window moments and Gaussian windows—on current phenomenological transversity PDFs; they evaluate constraining power and estimate uncertainty reduction as a function of lattice precision.

Result: Existing lattice transversity window observables already provide non-negligible constraints; Gaussian windows and tensor charges in the transversity channel show enhanced sensitivity; uncertainty reductions of up to ~30–40% in key $x$-regions are projected at near-future lattice precision.

Conclusion: Lattice QCD window observables—particularly optimized Gaussian windows—are poised to significantly tighten global transversity PDF extractions; this work provides quantitative criteria for prioritizing future lattice calculations targeting maximal PDF impact.

Abstract: We study the potential impact of lattice QCD window observables on nucleon isovector quark transversity parton distribution functions (PDFs) using the ePump Hessian-updating framework. Window observables, recently proposed in arXiv:2505.22795 as benchmark quantities enabling direct quantitative comparison between lattice QCD and phenomenological PDF extractions, are weighted integrals of PDFs over a range of Bjorken-$x$ for which both approaches are reliable. Experimental determinations of transversity PDFs carry uncertainties of 50--100%, while current lattice calculations have achieved comparable or better precision, creating an opportunity for lattice inputs to significantly constrain a global analysis.
