We evaluate the constraining power of existing lattice QCD computations of transversity window moments and Gaussian windows against current phenomenological PDF. We additionally examine prospective tensor charges in the transversity channel and estimate the PDF uncertainty reduction achievable at current and near-future lattice precision. Our results provide quantitative guidance on which window observables will most constrain global PDF analyses.

</details>


### [204] [Time-Domain Observables for Resonant Scattering in Quantum Simulations of Lattice Gauge Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17470)
*Praveen Balaji,Patrick Draper,Enrico Rinaldi*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper benchmarks quantum simulation of glueball scattering in pure SU(3) lattice gauge theory, using classical exact diagonalization and analytic methods to identify continuum resonances and design time-domain observables; it proposes and validates a qubit-efficient toy model, and demonstrates feasibility on Quantinuum H2-2 hardware with 28 qubits and ~25n entangling gates for n Trotter steps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop realistic, scalable benchmark problems for early fault-tolerant quantum simulations of nonequilibrium quantum dynamics—specifically real-time scattering in particle physics—bridging the gap between theoretical QCD and near-term quantum hardware capabilities.

Method: Classical exact diagonalization on a line-of-plaquettes lattice to characterize glueball spectra and scattering; real-time evolution and approximate analytic techniques (e.g., scattering phase shift analysis) to identify resonance structure in the continuum limit; design of time-domain observables (e.g., survival probability, correlation functions) sensitive to resonance parameters; construction and validation of a minimal toy model preserving key spectral features; quantum circuit compilation, optimization, and compression for Trotterized time evolution on Quantinuum H2-2.

Result: Identification of a low-energy glueball resonance in the lattice model; successful extraction of resonance properties via proposed time-domain observables both classically and in the toy model; demonstration of resonant collision simulation on Quantinuum H2-2 using 28 qubits; optimized Trotter circuits requiring ~25n two-qubit entangling gates for n time steps.

Conclusion: Glueball scattering provides a physically meaningful, hardware-feasible benchmark for quantum simulation of nonperturbative gauge dynamics; time-domain observables enable resonance characterization without full state tomography; the methodology establishes a pathway toward quantum simulation of real-time scattering in lattice gauge theories on near-term devices.

Abstract: Quantum computing may allow unprecedented access to nonequilibrium quantum dynamics, including problems of interest in particle physics, like the real-time development of scattering processes. Although fault-tolerant simulations at scale are still in the future, recent advancements toward early fault-tolerant simulations motivate building increasingly elaborate benchmark problems in order to assess state-of-the-art capabilities and test new algorithms and observables. In this work, we study glueball scattering on a line-of-plaquettes lattice in pure $SU(3)$ lattice gauge theory. We characterize scattering of the lowest-energy glueball states using classical exact diagonalization methods, identifying the continuum resonance structure using real-time simulations and approximate analytic methods, and we propose time-domain observables for extracting properties of the resonance from quantum simulations. We furthermore construct a toy model that shares the spectral features of the lattice gauge theory and demonstrate extraction of the observables. Feasibility on near-term hardware is demonstrated through simulation of a resonant collision of this toy model on Quantinuum System Model $\texttt{H2-2}$, with a 14-plaquette chain encoded in 28 qubits. The Trotterized circuit after optimization and compression requires $\sim 25n$ entangling gates for $n$ Trotter steps.

</details>


### [205] [Data Free Training of Diffusion Models for Lattice Field Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18863)
*Dennis Grinwald,Ankur Singha,Khaled Kahouli,Karl Jansen,Shinichi Nakajima*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper proposes a data-free training method for diffusion models in lattice field theory, leveraging the target score identity and unnormalized density to avoid reliance on expensive HMC-sampled configurations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sampling equilibrium field configurations in lattice field theory is computationally expensive; standard diffusion model training requires target samples, reintroducing the bottleneck.

Method: Uses the target score identity with importance-weighted or unweighted formulations to train diffusion models solely from their own generated samples, given access to the unnormalized probability density.

Result: The method achieves efficient training and sampling across all phases (symmetric, critical, broken) of 2D φ⁴ theory and matches performance of HMC-reference-trained models.

Conclusion: Diffusion models can be trained without external reference samples—enabling scalable, data-free generative sampling for lattice field theory.

Abstract: Sampling equilibrium field configurations remains a major computational bottleneck in lattice field theory, motivating the development of generative samplers that can produce independent configurations. Diffusion models are promising for this purpose, but their standard denoising score matching objective requires samples from the target distribution, seemingly reintroducing the original sampling problem. Here, we show that diffusion models can instead be trained from their own generated samples using the target score identity, provided access to the unnormalized probability density. Importance-weighted formulations of this identity yield a consistent target throughout training, while unweighted variants define a self-consistent fixed-point training scheme. We benchmark the different approaches in two-dimensional \(φ^4\) theory and identify effective choices that enable efficient training and sampling across the symmetric, critical, and broken phases. Ultimately, our data-free training approach achieves comparable performance to training on HMC reference data.

</details>
